# Invariants (long form)

Rationale and failure modes behind the one-line rules in `CLAUDE.md`. Read the entry before changing the subsystem it governs.

## Engine and message contract

- pi is the only agent engine; new sessions always construct `PiSession`. There is no harness selection.
- Map pi events to existing webview shapes via `pi-stream-adapter.ts` / `tool-normalization.ts`, never to a new message that mirrors a pi event. Add a message type to `src/shared/types/messages.ts` only for a user-facing capability no existing shape can carry (`steerTargets`, `resumeAccepted`, `sessionUsage`, `assistantRetracted`), with its producer and its webview handler in the same change.
- **`sessionUsage` is the only carrier of the status bar's billing totals.** Spend lands outside a completed turn: a stopped turn's partial request, a manual `/compact`, and the cache warmer's idle `usage` entries. `done` cannot carry it there, because the webview finalizes the streaming message on `done`, and `tokenUsageUpdate` is the context meter's last-request snapshot. Both producers build the message with `sessionUsageMessage` (`pi-session/session-usage.ts`): the adapter on every settle (aborted included), a microtask after each `message_end` whose message the inclusion rule counts (once pi has persisted it), on `compaction_end` with a result and on `entry_appended` for an entry that carries usage, and `history-loader.ts` on reload. `done` carries no totals.
- **An error card is shown only for a model call pi will not re-run** (`pi-stream-adapter.ts` `holdFailure`). pi reports a failed call only on the assistant `message_end` (its agent loop forwards no stream `error` event) and decides whether to re-run it afterwards, so the adapter holds the card. `agent_end.willRetry` (auto-retry) and an overflow `compaction_start` (overflow recovery, whose `compaction_end` reports its own failure) withdraw it, together with whatever the call streamed (`assistantRetracted`, which removes that assistant message), because a reload omits the call; no existing shape can take back a message, since the webview's merge never shrinks one. A `compaction_end` with no start that reports pi could not recover an overflow (the call overflowed again after one recovery) replaces it, since that report explains the failure; the next `message_start`, any other `compaction_start` or `compaction_end`, or `agent_settled` shows it, ahead of what follows. A failure that arrives after a Stop, or one the wind-down record names (below), is an abort's wind-down and shows nothing. While pi waits out a retry backoff the adapter sends `statusUpdate` `retrying` (from `auto_retry_start`), and `ready` at the retried call's assistant `message_start`, because pi sends no event when the backoff ends and sends a successful `auto_retry_end` only at the retried call's `message_end`; `auto_retry_end` sends `ready` only for a retry that ended before its call started (a Stop during the backoff). The status bar names the attempt, and the webview also clears it when processing ends. Both recoveries take the call out of model context with a null-replacement `context_edit` (`_omitRecoveryAttempt`), and `history-loader.ts` shows no card for an error entry whose latest edit is one, except an overflow (pi-ai's `isContextOverflow`, the test pi chose recovery by): pi omits an overflow before it knows the recovery compaction will run, so its card stays, and the compaction that follows a recovery clears it as it clears everything before it. A reload therefore shows one card where the live view showed one; pi persists no compaction failure, so where the live view showed pi's recovery failure a reload shows the overflow's own error. A failed call pi kept in context replays as the live view showed it, what it streamed (text, thinking, tool calls) and then its card, and an omitted call replays nothing it streamed, since the live view took that back. pi's agent loop ends a turn whose call failed or was aborted before it executes any tool the call named (`agent-loop.js:148` in pi-agent-core 1.4.0), and persists the aborted call like any other (`agent-session.js:767`), so those tool calls never ran and every transcript shows them `abandoned` (Not executed), never running and never `unrecorded`, with a `ToolAbandonReason` (`abandonReasonOf`) the card words: `failed` for an error stop, `stopped` for an aborted one, whether a Stop or something else aborted the run (an extension's `ctx.abort()`, a manual compaction, a session replacement or disposal), for the calls a Stop cut short before pi executed them (`markAborted` and the adapter's late end, the turn-stopped record; see "A reload shows a stopped turn as the live view did"), for the nested calls an aborted run settled before they ran (a call waiting at the gate included; the nested turn-stopped entries, same bullet) and for an abort's wind-down error. Live, the adapter and the subagent bridge send `toolAbandoned` with that `reason` for each at the call's `message_end`, or for an error stop at its `turn_end`, where the wind-down record decides it (the adapter once per card per turn), and the team runner marks its `tool_use` blocks `abandoned` with it and counts none of them (`assistantContentBlocks`, which its reload also builds, so the merge matches the live copy); on reload `history-loader.ts` sets `HistoryToolCall.abandoned` and `piMessagesToHistoryAgentMessages` and `memberHistoryMessages` mark the nested `tool_use` blocks. An abort that lands while a batch of tool calls runs, whatever aborted the run, makes pi finalize the call it was running and start none after it (`agent-loop.js:408-410` sequential, `:435-437` and `:455-457` parallel): the skipped calls get no execution event and no result, and the run's next model call, made under the aborted signal after any steer pi drains first (`:191`), ends aborted (`:146-157`), or on an error stop when its request setup rejects under that signal before any stream starts (auth resolution, `model-runtime.js:456-460` in pi-coding-agent and `lazy.js:57-62` in pi-ai 1.4.0, "This operation was aborted"), which is what a Stop during a tool produces. That call is their only record, so every transcript abandons the result-less calls of the assistant message before an aborted one or a wind-down error as `stopped` (`skippedToolCalls`; a Stop also abandons them as in flight), but only when pi recorded a result for at least one of them, which a cut batch always holds and a parallel batch orphaned by a killed process never does. A batch belongs to one run, so an aborted call of a later run never stops a batch an earlier run left orphaned. `history-loader.ts` drops the batch at each prompt entry (`promptTest`): a prompt pi did not queue opened a run, and one pi queued as a follow-up is delivered only once the running run's batch has settled. A steer, a queued batch or a cancel note is no prompt and keeps the batch, whether it was delivered mid-run or, as a note can be, started a run after the earlier run settled. A delivery without its `damocles-mid-stream` marker (a file written before markers existed, or a marker append that failed) reloads as a prompt and ends the batch. A team member's file holds one segment per run of its runner (the launch, then each resume), and `memberHistoryMessages` maps each segment on its own, because the live runner starts every run with no batch. `piMessagesToHistoryAgentMessages` is likewise handed one subagent run: a reload maps the invocation's own segment, and the sealing snapshot the run's own messages. Live, at the aborted call's `message_end` or the wind-down error's `turn_end`, the adapter and the subagent bridge send `toolAbandoned` for each, and the team runner re-sends the cut message's `teamAgentAssistant` under its id with those blocks marked (`resealSkipped`), which the store applies in place; on reload `history-loader.ts`, `piMessagesToHistoryAgentMessages` and `memberHistoryMessages` mark the same calls, the last rebuilding the cut message's blocks as the re-seal did, so the merge matches the live copy. A team agent's run that ends on the cut batch with no aborted call after it (its turn decider ending the turn on a `team_standby` or `team_report_complete` that ran) ends cancelled, so once its loop ends the runner re-seals the batch with those calls `stopped` (`resealSkipped`) and appends a `damocles-turn-stopped` entry naming them to the member session, which `memberHistoryMessages` marks on reload, so the live and reloaded blocks match. Any other run of the main chat or a subagent that ends on a cut batch with no aborted call (a `terminate` batch) leaves those calls pending live and `unrecorded` on reload, unless a Stop's own record names them; a team agent's run that ends on one cancelled gets the same re-seal and turn-stopped entry as above. An error stop pi recorded with no `errorMessage` is named `Unknown error` (`failedCallError`) on every transcript, live and reloaded. An error stop whose `turn_end` arrives while the run's signal is aborted is that abort's wind-down, whatever aborted the run (a Stop, an extension's `ctx.abort()`, a manual compaction, a session replacement): it shows no card, and its calls and the calls its abort skipped are `stopped`, as for an aborted stop. One observation decides it for every transcript: `registerWindDownErrorRecord` (main and nested extension factories) reads the signal there (`isWindDownError`) and appends a turn-stopped entry naming the error, which pi commits before listeners get that `turn_end` (`agent-session.js:514`, `:635-640` in pi-coding-agent 1.4.0). The adapter, the subagent bridge and the team runner hold an error stop from its `message_end` and decide it at its `turn_end` from that entry alone (`windDownRecorded`), never from the signal, and a reload reads the same entry, so a Stop landing between the `message_end` and the record pass cannot make them disagree; with no entry (the abort landed after the record pass, or a later `turn_end` handler discarded the draft) every transcript shows the card. The main chat also hides an error that arrives after a Stop, which the Stop's own record names. The bridge passes the wind-down messages to its sealing snapshot. A session reset or replacement unsubscribes the adapter before it aborts, so the webview receives none of the old run's wind-down, and the record still lands in the old file for its reload. A re-run call's `assistantRetracted` takes its tool cards with its message. The turn outcome stays the latest assistant message's error (`_lastAssistantError`), which a retry that answers clears. Never decide retryability from the error text: pi's `isRetryableAssistantError` and its retry settings are the only judge. A subagent's run reports the error its last model call ended on (`subagent-runner.ts`), read after `prompt()` resolves, when pi has finished retrying. The parent receives that error, the text the run wrote before it (`recordOutcomeText`) and the resume call: a failed subagent can be resumed (`canResumeSubagent`), and the interruption notice still lists only interrupted ones, because a failed one's result already named the call. A subagent card and a team agent's transcript follow the same rule through `NestedCallFailures` (`nested-call-failures.ts`), whose `error`, `assistantRetracted` and retry `statusUpdate` carry `parentToolUseId`, the `Agent` call id or the team agent's id; the webview routes them to that transcript and its status line, and drops one whose owner no transcript holds rather than showing it in the main chat. It differs in one place, so the live view, the subagent's sealing snapshot and a reload agree: a call's error shows only while pi keeps the call in model context. An overflow is withdrawn at its recovery `compaction_start` whatever that compaction reports, and an overflow pi will not recover again shows its own error rather than pi's report. The sealing snapshot reads `session.messages`, pi's projection, and a reload reads the agent file through `parseAgentEntries`, which drops a message whose latest `context_edit` is a null replacement while still counting its usage; both map an error stop to an `error` entry after the call's text (`piMessagesToHistoryAgentMessages`, `memberHistoryMessages`).
- **`/stats` has no session producer.** `requestUsageStats`, `usageStatsProgress` and `usageStats` are produced by `message-router/handlers/usage-stats-handlers.ts` through `UsageStatsService` (`src/core/usage-stats/`), never by `PiSession`, because the figures aggregate every project and belong to no session. `/usage` sets the precedent: its handler posts straight to the webview. The three types exist because no existing message shape carries cross-project aggregates. The handler sends exactly one `usageStats` with `final: true` per `requestId`, including when validation or the worker fails, so the webview never waits on a request that already ended.
- `Edit` cannot create files (empty `old_string` throws). `Write` is the only text-file creation path. `GenerateImage` creates only a new image file, never overwrites, and goes through the same write gate (category `write`, never `GATEABLE_MODULE_NAMES`).
  - It is in `WRITE_TOOLS`, so plan mode and read-only agents block it with `formatPolicyBlockReason`, acceptEdits and YOLO allow it, `GenerateImage(<glob>)` rules match the path it writes (see "File rules" under "Permissions and plan mode"), and the checkpoint baseline wait applies. `GATEABLE_MODULE_NAMES` allows before the plan-mode check, so adding it there would let a planner spend money and write files.
  - The path resolves as `Write`'s does (`resolveToCwd`), must end in `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg` or `.webp`, and is written with `writeFile(..., {flag:'wx'})`. The approved path is never changed: a returned MIME type that does not match the extension fails, writes nothing, and keeps `usage`.
  - It runs only in the main session. `resolveAgentToolset` and `teamAgentBaseToolNames` strip the name and no nested build carries the definition.
  - It is eligible only while `damocles.imageGeneration.enabled` holds, the chosen model resolves in pi's OpenRouter image catalog with finite, non-negative prices and a positive output token price (`isOfferedImageModel`), and `hasConfiguredAuth('openrouter')` holds. The `damocles.explore.apiKey.openrouter` secret reaches pi as the `openrouter` runtime key, which is what both that check and `ModelRuntime.generateImages` read, so the menu equals the loadable set. `syncCustomProviders` refreshes every panel's tools once the key is applied.
  - `damocles.imageGeneration.enabled` and `damocles.imageGeneration.model` are both user scope only (`scope: application` and `restrictedConfigurations`), and Settings writes both at user scope: one turns on spending, the other decides what each image costs. The approval prompt names the model and says the call is billed to the user's OpenRouter key.
  - It is not wrapped in `abortableTool`. pi forwards the tool's `signal` to the HTTP request, so an abort settles the call promptly, and a wrapper would answer first and drop the usage of a response that landed as the turn was aborted.
- **A diff's line numbers come from a recorded patch, never from the view.** An Edit or Write card and the diff it opens render one `FileDiffSource` (`fileChangeSource` in `webview/utils/parseUnifiedDiff.ts`): the `patch` the result recorded (pi's `edit`, Damocles' `write`), or while the call awaits approval the `patch` its `requestPermission` carries (`DiffManager.prepareDiff`), or a new file's own lines numbered from 1. A file is new only when its Write result recorded `created: true` or, while it awaits approval, the request carries neither `patch` nor `patchOmitted`. A denied, failed or running call, or a result with neither a patch nor that marker (every Write recorded before the marker existed), shows the requested change without numbers; a patch jsdiff cannot parse counts as none, and a patch omitted for size or a binary file says so. An approval treats a file as new only when `stat` finds it missing, since neither host's `readText` rejection says why it failed; a file that exists but will not open as text gets `patchOmitted` and no host diff. The approval's patch and a write's patch come from one host function, `filePatch` (`tools/file-patch.ts`), pinned to pi's `generateUnifiedPatch`, so a card's numbers do not change when the result lands. Every producer of a nested transcript carries the result's `details` as the tool's `metadata`: the subagent stream bridge and its sealing snapshot and reload (`message-mapper.ts`), and the team path (`content-blocks.ts`).
- **A tool card's time is the `durationMs` pi measured around the call's `execute()`, never a Damocles clock.** pi puts it on `tool_execution_end` and on the persisted tool result message; the adapter and the subagent stream bridge forward it on `toolCompleted` and `toolFailed`, and `history-loader.ts` and `message-mapper.ts` read it from the result, so a card shows the same time live and after a reload and the wait for approval never counts. A call with no `durationMs` never ran and shows no time. Team transcripts show no tool time.
- Internal sub-calls (memory, structured output) use `PiRuntime.runStructuredCompletion` + the terminating-tool idiom (no `toolChoice`). Every structured sub-call sends an explicit thinking level: its purpose's entry in `SUBCALL_THINKING` (`subcall-model.ts`; `off` for `session-title`, `memory-query-expansion`, `memory-rerank` and `memory-merge`, `low` for `memory-extract`, `memory-profile`, `memory-audit` and `commit-message`) clamped by pi's `clampThinkingLevel` for the model, and `reasoning` is omitted only when the clamped level is `off`. A request with no level is not "no thinking" on a model whose `thinkingLevelMap.off` is `null`: pi sends Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 adaptive thinking at `high`, and those models clamp `off` to `low`, as Step 5 Preview does. Haiku 5.5 can disable thinking, so every `off` purpose that runs on it runs without thinking: session titles and query expansion on the Automatic background model, and memory merge and rerank when the Automatic Memory judge uses its model instead of a classifier, both of which try Haiku 5.5 first. If pi-ai fails to load there is no clamp, so the sub-call resolves `unreachable` without sending. `StructuredCompletionRequest.purpose` is `StructuredSubCallPurpose`, so a purpose without a table entry does not compile. The model comes only from `PiRuntime.subCallModelFor(purpose)`: `memory-merge` and `memory-rerank` follow the Memory judge (`resolveMemoryJudge`, see "Memory judges"), and every other purpose resolves through `resolveBackgroundModel` (`subcall-model.ts`): `damocles.background.model` and `.effort` are application scope, read with no folder, so a project file never sets them, and they never choose the judge. Automatic (`''`) takes the first of `AUTOMATIC_BACKGROUND_MODELS` (Haiku 5.5, GPT-6 Luna, then each `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_DEFS` cheap model in declaration order: Step 5 Preview, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, MiniMax M3.1 Flash, Grok 4.7, Kimi for Coding, Muse Spark 1.3; never GitHub Copilot's, whose def sets `pickedOnly`, see "Catalog values and plan gating") that `resolvePiModel` resolves signed in; a picked model that is outside the catalog or signed out gives no model, never another provider's, so memory reports no model. A picked model's explicit effort replaces the table level for every purpose and goes through the same clamp. The Explore settings never choose a sub-call model. Classifier sub-calls use `PiRuntime.runClassification`, which keeps untrusted text in `state`; see "Memory judges". A structured sub-call resolves a `StructuredCompletionResult` (`structured-completion.ts`): `answered` with the value; `unanswered` when the model saw the request and replied without a valid result, meaning a missing tool call, a non-object payload, or an error stop with no HTTP status that pi-ai's own `isRetryableAssistantError` does not class as transient and that carries positive evidence the model saw it (non-zero `usage` input, output or cache read, or a provider content stop in `rawStopReason`: Anthropic `refusal` or `sensitive`, OpenAI `content_filter` or `incomplete.content_filter`, Google's safety finish reasons), which is where a refusal lands; `rejected` for HTTP 400, 413 or 422, and for a 403 whose body is an OpenRouter content refusal (`isInputRefusal` in `http-status.ts`, which `runClassification` reads too), the provider refusing the request as sent (its content, its size or its shape); `unreachable` for a throw, an abort, a timeout, any other HTTP status, a transport error, an error stop with no evidence the model saw the request (pi turns a credential setup failure such as an OAuth refresh failure, a provider signed out after the auth check, a missing key or a credential store failure into a zero-usage error stop, and a Codex websocket usage limit or quota text arrives without a status), or no usable model, credential or pi-ai. An `unreachable` result carries cause `forbidden` for any other HTTP 403, a refusal of access whatever the credential (the plan, the region or a permission, RFC 9110), where signing in does not help, and cause `credential` for HTTP 401, for a provider with no configured credential at call time, and for an error stop with no status, no transient class and no model contact after which pi's own credential resolution (`ModelRuntime.getAuth`, which `completeSimple` runs first) resolves nothing or throws a `ModelsError` with code `auth` or `oauth` (`isPiCredentialError`): pi's setup error stop keeps only the error text, so that re-resolution, read by code, is the structured signal. Every provider failure, network and HTTP included, reaches the core as an error stop, so the status test (`httpStatusOf` in `http-status.ts`, which imports nothing) reads pi-ai's `formatProviderError` form with a label (`Label (529): …`, the classifiers) and without one (`529: …`, an `openai-completions` SDK error) and a provider SDK's own leading `529 ` (`anthropic-messages`). `isInputRefusal` parses the body after the same prefixes and reads the refusal `metadata` under `error`, or at the top level when the openai SDK kept only the inner error object. The transport test is pi's, never Damocles' own text match. Refusal text is never matched. The log names the outcome and its cause (the status, `transient`, `credential refused`, `no model contact` or the content stop), never the error text, which can echo the request. Only memory extraction acts on the difference (see "Memory consolidation"); every other caller reads every non-answer as no value through `structuredValue`.
- Never mutate `process.env` for per-session config; pi reads a Damocles-owned `agentDir` (`~/.damocles/pi/agent`).
- **pi's install telemetry is off in every pi session, whatever a settings file says.** `withSettingsPolicy` (`pi-session/settings-policy.ts`) pins `getEnableInstallTelemetry()` to false on every pi `SettingsManager` Damocles creates, a trusted project's `.pi/settings.json` and an unreadable agent `settings.json` included. pi reads it per provider request to decide its attribution (`client-identity.js:12`), so a value carried only by a settings file would be lost whenever that file cannot be read. pi's `PI_TELEMETRY` environment variable still outranks it.
- **The agent `settings.json` seed never rewrites a file it cannot read or parse** (`ensurePiAgentDir`, `pi-session/agent-dir.ts`). The file also holds what pi and the user keep there, such as `packages`, so a missing file is seeded, a parsed JSON object gets the seeded keys merged in, and any other file is left as it is, with the reason (never the file's text) in the log. pi decides for itself: it records a load error for invalid JSON, a read failure and a JSON string, number, boolean or `null`, and then loads no settings from that file and drops every write to it until a reload loads it (`settings-manager.js:474-478`), but it loads a JSON array with no error (`migrateSettings` makes only `in` checks, `:281-326`) and rewrites it as an object at its first write. The user is warned, naming the file, once per `PiRuntime` init, only when pi's own load of the user-scope manager recorded that error, so a read failure the seed saw and pi did not warns no one. Each settings step of the subscription plugin (`PiRuntime._writeUserSettings`) refuses before it changes anything while pi's latest load of that file failed, naming the file, so no step reloads or deletes on top of a write pi dropped; `_reloadSettings` keeps that load result, because pi records a load error only once per load. No load or write error's message reaches a log or a thrown message, since a parse error quotes the file. While the file is unusable, `damocles.autoCompact` holds only through `PiSession.applyCompactionConfig`'s in-memory value (pi's default is on), which `PiSession.reapplyPiSettings` sets again, with cache warming, after each re-read of the folder's settings manager (`FolderRuntime`'s loader reload and `session.reload()`), since a re-read of a file fixed meanwhile drops it.
- **Account state has exactly one publisher.** `AccountInfo` (`src/shared/types/settings.ts`) derives from six auth inputs (active model, its catalog entry, Claude auth mode, OpenAI auth status, prefer-API-key, and the kind of each key-provider key pi applied: `PiRuntime.flatFeeKeyProviders()`, read from the applied-key cache in `custom-providers.ts`, where a MiniMax Token Plan key `sk-cp-…` bills flat and any other MiniMax key is metered; a daemon's core applies no key and reads the pinned client's `clientAuth` `flatFee` instead) and carries `dollarBilled`, which the webview's cost label reads, plus `model` and an optional `organization`: no plan name, email or billing mode, and the chat header has no account or plan chip. The adapter's once-guarded `emitSessionStartOnce`, reached only at turn start, cannot carry it: a session restored from history would never receive it, and a model or auth change would leave it stale. `PiSession.publishAccountInfo()` rebuilds and emits on demand, and is called wherever an input changes: session start, `setModel`, history replay (a view's `views.join` included, `HistoryManager.replayLive`), both auth paths, and the end of a custom-provider sync that changed a credential or the flat-fee providers, failed, or was the runtime's first, which is when the picker providers are first known (`PiRuntime.syncCustomProviders` republishes every started session and reports `accountChanged` to `onCustomProvidersSynced`, whose `ChatPanelProvider` listener republishes every panel, one whose session has not started included; a key saved in another window included). An `auth.json` change does the same through `PiRuntime.onAuthFileChange`, before pi starts and after, since a sign-in made outside Damocles (a GitHub Copilot one, for example) need not change what the key sync applies. It caches nothing, so it cannot drift from `buildAccountInfo` (`account-billing.ts`), and a disposed session or a retired runtime publishes nothing. On a daemon's core every input is the pinned client's, else the acting client's (`currentPin`), the rule the broker and the settings store follow: a turn's budget checks and spawned agents' billing read its pin, and a view's account state, published outside every run, reads the client whose next send would pin, so an `actingClient` change republishes it in every panel, one whose session has not started included, and every account-provider status with it (`PiRuntime.onActingClientRefreshed`, once pi's snapshot holds that client's credentials; before the runtime exists, `ChatPanelProvider`'s listener on the remote host's clients). The OpenAI key there is that client's `clientAuth` `hasKey`, read on every call. The Claude mode there is that client's credential type and `allowance` choice, and `allowance` only while the server package's bundled plugin loaded on the host (`clientClaudeAuthStatus`), never anything in the host's pi settings: an allowance client whose requests bill extra usage because it did not load reads `extra` with `allowanceUnavailable`, which `AccountInfo.allowanceUnavailable` carries for a Claude chat and the chat's dock shows as a notice (`AllowanceUnavailableBanner.vue`), so `dollarBilled` reports extra usage and the fallback is never silent. It also carries `pickerProviders` (feature `account.modelPicker`, with the `setModelPickerProvider` setter), the vendors the chat's model picker lists (`PiRuntime.pickerProviders()`): each vendor `damocles.modelPicker.providers` sets, else each with a credential (pi's `hasConfiguredAuth`, `openai` counting `openai-codex` too; on a daemon's core the view's own client's `clientAuth`, or the target's own credential, which pi's snapshot counts where the acting client holds none, so a model that runs on the host's environment stays listed and no view lists another device's sign-in), so a change of that setting republishes every panel (`ChatPanelProvider`), and a sign-in or key change already does. On a daemon's core the setting is the client's (an application-scope key), and pi's snapshot counts only for a provider that client holds nothing for (`hostHasOwnAuth`). The composer's model dropdown and This chat list only those vendors' models plus the chat's current model (`usePickerModels`), and every model while `pickerProviders` is absent; Defaults, Teams, Explore and Background list the whole catalog. A consumer must never paper over a missing publication with a fallback; fix the publisher.

- **The chat's model has exactly one publisher, `PiSession.publishModel()`.** The chat publishes the model it shows, `PiSession.modelValue`. A start makes it the configured or recorded model whether or not that can run, and pi may then be seated on a fallback model (`shownPiModel`) until a send adopts the shown model or refuses (see "Catalog values and plan gating"). A switch changes it only after pi's `AgentSession.setModel` resolved (or a rejection left pi's `model` on the target), because pi's `setModel` rejects when its own `checkAuth` fails and then keeps the old model. `setModel` is a request: it queues behind earlier switches and behind any session replacement requested before it, so rapid picks apply in order and the last one wins. A switch Damocles refuses (`openaiAuthRequired`, or the "Sign in to {0} to use {1}" or "unavailable" notice) and one pi rejects (the same sign-in warning) leave the model unchanged. Those notices name the model by its display name and go through the host's `NotificationService`, never a chat toast, because the pick may come from the desktop settings overlay, which lies over the chat page; `openaiAuthRequired` opens the OpenAI sign-in instead, and is raised only while no OpenAI credential exists: with one, the catalog it unlocks lacks the model, so the refusal is the "unavailable" notice and a sign-in cannot loop. A pick refused with `openaiAuthRequired` is held in core (`PiSession.pendingOpenAIPick`), never in a webview: the end of the next OpenAI sign-in the user runs (`openaiSignInEnded`, called for every chat by the `startChatGPTOAuth` and `setOpenAIApiKey` handlers) applies it when that sign-in succeeded and drops it otherwise, and any later model request (from any view, the model already committed included, and a send's or a `/compact`'s adoption of the shown model, which requests a switch), a prompt that runs, a reset, a clear, a resume switch or a delete drops it, as does a later request superseding the refused one before its refusal landed (`openaiPickEpoch`). No other credential change, an `auth.json` change from another app or a key saved for a judge included, applies a pick. A request for the committed model reaches pi only when pi runs another one, since pi's `setModel` appends a `model_change` even for the same model. After pi adopted a model, the switch applies the panel's thinking level for it (`resolveThinkingLevel`), because pi's `setModel` applied its own default level and recorded it in the session file. Every attempt, refused ones included, and the end of `start()` (where `resolveInitialModel` can move the factory default to a signed-in model) report the model the chat shows through `SessionOptions.onModelChange`, with whether the session committed it. `SettingsManager.adoptSessionModel` posts it as `modelUpdate` and `panelThinkingUpdate` either way, and records a committed one as the panel's model, even one equal to the default, so a later session for the panel (a folder switch, a recovery) starts on it and never falls back. A model is committed once it was configured (the panel's or `damocles.model`), the user picked it (the model already shown included), a resumed record set it, the factory default's fallback chose it, or a run or compaction used the shown factory default (`PiSession.commitShownModel`, from `runStarted` and `compact`). A factory default only shown is not committed, so the panel holds none and its next session still treats it as the factory default: it falls back, or with only picked-only models runnable it refuses with `modelUnavailable` and opens This chat. A resumed conversation's recorded model and level become `modelValue`, `desiredModel` and the panel's level only once pi installed the session the factory built on them and the chat keeps it (`PiSession.adoptBuiltSelection`: in `start()` once the runtime exists, before the bind, or as the last step of the rebind of a switch the panel still holds and is not disposing), never in the factory, so a switch pi refuses, one whose build or rebind throws, one the panel let go of meanwhile (a reset, another target, a lost lease) and one a dispose overtakes leave the previous model, window and effort, because a failed switch sends the chat back to the conversation it held. The level then arrives through `onRecordedSelection`, which only makes it the panel's level for the recorded model, and the model through `onModelChange`, with the account state, in that same step. A start whose bind throws afterwards releases the runtime and keeps the conversation it opened, whose record the next start builds on, so its published model, window and effort stay that record's (see "A start, or a replacement that fails after pi tore its session down, leaves no runtime"). A release changes neither `modelValue` nor `desiredModel`, so the model last published is still the chat's afterwards and nothing publishes it again. `onModelChange` also carries the resolved pi model's context window (`contextWindowForCurrentModel()`, the window compaction and `/context` use), which `modelUpdate.contextWindowSize` posts from then on, because one model id can have a different window per provider (GPT-6.1 Sol has 500K through Sign in with ChatGPT and 272K with an API key). The static `DEFAULT_MODELS` window is only the fallback before the session adopts a model, or when pi reports a window of 0 or less, which counts as none (`piContextWindow` in `pi-models.ts`, the one place that rule lives). Nothing else writes the panel's model once its session exists: the `setActiveModel` handler only forwards the request, and the composer menu and the settings "This chat" row (in the desktop overlay too) never set `activeModel` in the webview and send a pick of the model already shown too, so the label never names a model core did not commit. A prompt waits for the queued switches, so a turn runs on the model the label shows, and a reset waits for them too, because pi's `setModel` appends a `model_change` entry to the session it switches; the reset stops the turn before that wait, because pi awaits a `model_select` hook inside `setModel`. A switch whose session was replaced while pi applied it is applied again to the replacement, which the factory built from the previous `desiredModel`. A consumer must never set the model locally to paper over a missing publication; fix the publisher.

- **`sessionStateChanged` and the panel activity have exactly one publisher, and every input they read has exactly one mutator.** `PiSession.publishSessionState()` derives the state from the turn lifecycle plus every prompt map on `PermissionState` and `WebviewExtensionUIContext.pending`, carries the main chat's wind-down while one shows (`windDown`, "Teams and subagents"), and in the same call reports `ChatActivity { state, pendingKinds, pendingPrompts, background }` to the listener `PanelManager` binds (`chat-panel/activity.ts`). It is the only site that constructs either. `pendingPrompts` and `pendingKinds` come from one read of the prompts (`PermissionState.pendingPrompts()` then `WebviewExtensionUIContext.pendingDialogs()`), so the kinds are always exactly the kinds of the prompts reported; the activity is keyed on the prompt ids, so a new prompt of a kind already pending reports again. Each prompt names its owner, resolved once when it is raised by `promptOwnerOf` (`pending-prompts.ts`, installed on `PermissionState`) and sent on the prompt message as `owner`: a team agent's prompt carries its agent id as `parentToolUseId` and resolves through the running team, a subagent's carries its `Agent` call id and resolves through `AgentManager`, and a host dialog carries its attribution; anything else is the chat's own. The webview places a prompt's card by `owner`, never by what its stores hold: the chat's own marks its transcript card, a subagent's marks that subagent's card or none, and a team agent's marks none, while the dock names the agent and its team. `sessionStateChanged` carries the open prompts' ids and owners (a team owner names its `agentId`), and the team card, team view and agent overlays show "Needs you" on exactly the agents those owners name. Its one-line summary holds paths, commands and model text, so it is never logged. `pendingPromptDescriber` builds each prompt's summary on its own, so one that throws leaves that prompt with no summary and is logged by the error's name only, never the prompt's content, and caches it per prompt id while that prompt is pending. Its other inputs call it as they change: `AgentManager` when a subagent run is created or settles (`onRunsChanged`), `TeamService` when its claim is set or cleared (`setRunListener`), and the first write of the session file and a resume or fork target change, so main can re-read `PanelManager.sessionIdOf` on every activity event. A running turn going idle passes its `TurnOutcome` to `setTurnState('idle', outcome)`, which is the only source of `onTurnSettled`; a completed outcome carries the turn's duration from its start to its settle, and an error that `turnOutcomeOfError` finds to be a rate limit by its wording is a `rateLimit` outcome with optional `windowId`, `windowLabel` and `resetsAt`, the full window the next bullet names (D55). The session state goes idle at once; only a rate limit's `onTurnSettled` report waits for that window (`settleOnRateLimit`), and a session disposed meanwhile reports nothing. Any other settled turn on a subscription account asks `PiRuntime.usage` for a refresh (next bullet). A new prompt map maps to a kind in `PermissionState.kindOf`; the adapter reports the turn lifecycle through `onTurnStateChanged` instead of emitting. Those maps are mutated only inside `permission-handler/state.ts` and `extension-ui-context.ts`, because every mutator also fires the change notifier that reaches the publisher. A direct `state.pendingApprovals.delete(id)` in a manager (the shape seven abort handlers used before this rule existed) type-checks, keeps every test green, and parks the session on `requires_action` for the rest of its life with no dialog on screen, which is worse than the missing signal it replaced. `session-state.test.ts` ("single-writer discipline") walks every `.ts` file under `src/core` and `src/vscode` (`src/__mocks__/source-tree.ts`), allows the mutation only in `permission-handler/state.ts`, and enforces both halves; it also checks that the activity is built only inside `publishSessionState`. The webview's spinner follows the same lifecycle (`processing` from the adapter, and `ChatSession.turnRunning` reads it), so a command the host handles on the send path with no turn (`chat-handlers.ts`) lowers the spinner the send armed only while no turn runs: the composer's Compact now sends `/compact` there mid-turn. A consumer must never infer the state locally to paper over a missing publication; fix the publisher.

- **Subscription usage has one monitor per process** (`UsageMonitor` in `pi-session/usage-thresholds.ts`, held as `PiRuntime.usage`). The refresh after a settled turn runs at most every 10 minutes (`USAGE_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS`) for the whole process and raises a usage threshold (80 or 95) once per window reset through `PanelManager.onUsageThreshold`; `pi-runtime.ts` calls each crossing listener on its own, so one that throws does not stop the others. A crossing names its window by `windowId`, the bar id, and two reset times within `RESET_RESOLUTION_MS` (60 s) of each other are the same window reset (`sameReset`, a distance). A window label built from provider text goes through `oneLine` (`untrusted-text.ts`) and is capped at 60 characters. Plan names (Anthropic's profile `rate_limit_tier` and `organization_type`, ChatGPT's `plan_type`, mapped to a display name and never shown raw) come only from that refresh and feed only the usage warning's title; nothing fetches on account activation. A credential change (`credentialsChanged`: an `auth.json` change or an OpenAI key secret change) bumps a generation, so a fetch in flight with the old credentials observes nothing, and clears the plan names. After a rate-limit settle on a subscription account, `fullWindow` refreshes at once, sharing a refresh in flight, waits at most `RATE_LIMIT_REFRESH_BOUND_MS` (5 s), and names the window at 100% that the chat's model counts against: a model-scoped weekly (`seven_day_<model>`) counts only when every word of its scope is a word of the model id, of several full windows the one that resets last decides, and one with no reset time counts as last.

- `src/shared` must not depend on `src/core` or `src/vscode`, and that includes its tests. `tsconfig.webview.json` compiles `src/shared/**/*` for the webview bundle, so a file under `src/shared/__tests__` that imports extension code drags the extension host into the webview typecheck and fails the gate. A test whose subject is shared but which must read extension state belongs on the extension side; split it rather than moving it.

## pi's run lifecycle

- **pi's run state is read only through `RunLifecycle` (`pi-session/run-lifecycle.ts`).** pi 1.4.0 has windows in which its own flags disagree with what a caller means by "pi is running". `_emitAgentSettled` clears `_isAgentRunActive` (`agent-session.js:685`) before it awaits the `agent_settled` extension handlers (`:689`), so while they run `isStreaming` is false, `isIdle` is true (`:1072-1073`), `waitForIdle()` and `abort()` return at once (`:1983-1985`, `:1971-1981`), and a `prompt()` or a `sendCustomMessage` with `triggerTurn` is deferred and returns at once (`:1632-1634`, `:1866-1867`). pi publishes `agent_settled` only after the handlers (`:690`) and then runs what it deferred (`:695-703`), before it resolves an idle wait made while the run was open (`:702`, `:706`). A run whose preparation throws clears the flag and resolves the idle wait without publishing `agent_settled` (`:1429-1435`). A reader that derives "running, settling or idle" on its own (`isStreaming`, `isIdle`, `waitForIdle`, a phase kept from `agent_start`/`agent_end`) is wrong in one of those windows: a team runner or a subagent's manager lets go of a session while a run a user's pi extension opened is still open, or hands pi a prompt that run refuses ("Agent is already processing") or defers; `queueInput` refuses a message typed while pi settles a run; a Stop during compaction's settle wait does not stop the compaction; a runtime reload runs under the `agent_settled` handlers. So one module answers every such question from pi's public events plus pi's own flags: `phase` (`start`, `run`, `settle`, the agent loop counting as `run` before pi publishes its `agent_start`), `streaming` (a prompt made now joins the open run and needs a `streamingBehavior`), `settling` (a prompt made now is deferred), `mayQueue` (a queued message still reaches the model), `busy` (nothing may rebuild the runtime, a compaction included), `untilSettled(signal)`, `untilRunEnds()` (the `agent_settled` of the run open now, which the wind-down notice ends at) and `untilIdle(signal)` (no run open and the deferred actions run, through the idle wait pi resolves after them, which it captures at each `agent_end` while the run is still open). `abortRunAtStart` stays a separate helper: it reads no state, it stops a run at its start.
- **One lifecycle per pi session, bound where the session is bound and disposed when it is let go of.** The main chat binds in `PiSession.subscribeEvents` and disposes in its unsubscribe; a team member's in `TeamRunner.createMemberSession`, disposed by `forgetMemberSession`; a subagent's in `AgentManager`'s `onSessionCreated`, disposed with its session in `afterComplete` or `dispose`. Consumers that hold only the session (`AgentRunner`, `runSubagent`) read it with `runLifecycleOf`, which throws for a session nothing bound, and a second bind of a live session throws, so no reader can fall back to a tracker of its own. Disposal ends every wait the lifecycle holds, so a session let go of never strands a waiter. The wind-down notice (`noticeWindDown`) and the file owner's notice (`AgentFileOwner.watch`) take the lifecycle, never the session.
- **A guard keeps it that way.** `src/core/__tests__/pi-run-state-reads.test.ts` parses every file esbuild bundles into the hosts (src/core, src/vscode, src/desktop, src/shared, src/platform) with the TypeScript compiler API and fails on any read of `isStreaming` or `isIdle` (direct, optional-chained, by string key, destructured in a declaration or an assignment) and any reference to `waitForIdle` outside `run-lifecycle.ts`. src/webview is out: it holds no pi session and its view models have `isStreaming` fields of their own. It is syntactic, so it ignores comments, strings, object literal keys, type members, same-named locals and calls such as the MCP server manager's `isIdle(name, timeoutMs)`; it flags a same-named getter on a non-pi object as well, and misses a read through a computed key held in a variable, `Reflect.get` or a destructured `for...of` target. A new question about pi's run belongs on `RunLifecycle`, answered from pi's events and flags in that one file.

## Deferred tools / ToolSearch

- Browser/Compass/Web/Image/MCP tools are deferred: registered but inactive, activated only via `ToolSearch` (`tools/deferred-tools.ts` is the leaf that owns the universe). They must stay in pi's `tools:`/eligible set. Dropping a name removes it from the registry and `setActiveToolsByName` silently ignores unknown names, so it could never come back. Deferral is only safe where `ToolSearch` is actually registered; gate on that, never on it merely being allowed. A built-in ToolSearch group equals a Tools-panel subsystem: the whole subsystem defers or none of it does, so there is one rule for the model and one line in the menu. The one exception is `mcp_resources`, last in `BUILTIN_DEFERRED_GROUPS`: the three MCP resource tools (`list_mcp_resources`, `list_mcp_resource_templates`, `read_mcp_resource`) are Damocles tools over every visible resource server, not a Tools-panel subsystem, and they take the widest effective resource exposure among the eligible resource servers (`resourceToolsExposure`). With an Always-loaded resource server they are active from the first turn and off the menu, and a request for the group is answered as already active; otherwise they are deferred as a group. `deferredToolNames` never defers them on its own, so every deferral site passes them with the deferrable MCP names (`mcpDeferrableNames` for the panel, `NestedMcpToolset.deferrable` for a nested agent) only while they are deferred, and `/context` lists them where eligible. MCP defers per tool, by the exposure each descriptor carries (`mcp/exposure.ts`): `off` is subtracted from eligibility through the one `disabledToolSet()` gate (`offToolNames()`), `direct` (Always loaded) is eligible and active from the first turn and never on the menu, and only `deferred` tools are deferrable (`deferrableToolNames()`). An MCP group line lists that server's deferred tools only. Every deferral call site (the panel's active set and snapshot, `FolderRuntime.createSubagentSession`, the subagent and team extension factories) passes the deferrable MCP names, never all MCP names; a site that passes all of them holds an Always-loaded tool back and advertises it. An exact-name request for an active tool outside the deferrable set, such as a direct MCP tool, is answered as already active, never resolved, and so is a request for a server group with no deferred tool whose eligible tools are Always loaded (`directMcpGroups`, `NestedMcpToolset.directGroups`). `buildNestedMcpToolset` skips an Off descriptor itself and grants only `direct` beside `deferred`, whatever eligible set it is handed.
- **A reopened conversation restores its ToolSearch loads from pi's recorded loadout** (`tools/restored-tools.ts`). pi's agent loop records each request's tool changes as system messages with `toolsAdded`/`toolsRemoved` (`agent-loop.js` in pi-agent-core 1.4.0), and pi-ai's `getCurrentTools` replays the system messages of every `sessionManager.getBranch()` entry, those before a compaction included, the record pi 1.4.0 restores a resumed session from (`_restoreToolsFromTranscript`, `agent-session.js:1389-1413`). Damocles replaces pi's active set right after (`applyActiveToolsForMode`), so it seeds its own: the panel on every bind whose session id changed (a resume at start, a fork, a resume switch; a `/clear`'s fresh transcript seeds nothing), and `createSubagentSession` for a reopened agent. A restored name deferrable now joins `toolSearchActivated`; one that can become deferrable (a built-in group's tool, the MCP resource tools among them, or an `mcp__` tool) waits in `pendingRestoredTools`, joins once it becomes deferrable (an MCP server that connects after the chat opened, during the first prompt's Always-loaded wait included), and is dropped when pi opens the session's next run, which is where pi drops its own pending names (`_startRun`, `agent-session.js:1441-1447`): on `preflightResult('started')` of a prompt, a re-steered batch, a re-queued follow-up or a cancel note (`runStarted`). The panel seeds last in `bindSession`, after the panel's gate, checkpoints and UI context are bound, so a throw there (pi-ai not loaded) fails the start without leaving the session ungated; a malformed transcript fails pi's own session creation first, which builds the session context and replays the same branch for a session with pi's default tools, as the panel's is (`sdk.js:84`, `agent-session.js:213-214`, `:1407`). A nested agent's MCP is frozen at spawn, so it has no pending set, and `createSubagentSession` tracks the session before seeding, so a failed spawn's session is still disposed with the folder. Never rebuild the set from ToolSearch results or called tools: that misses every later removal. A rebind onto the same session id keeps what ToolSearch loaded since.
- The deferrable universe composes from leaf spec modules only, never a barrel. `deferred-tools.ts` documents itself as composing the `*_PI_TOOL_NAMES` exports and importing nothing else, and `tool-search.test.ts` ("deferred-tools.ts import discipline") enforces it to keep the eval-time cycle `permission-gate` → `tool-catalog` → `browser-tools` closed. A barrel that re-exports runtime modules (as `web-access/index` does with `./config`, which reads settings through `platform-host`, and `./web-tools`, the tool bodies) forces that guard to be relaxed to "any import is fine", retiring it for every future group. Add a `-tool-specs` declaration leaf instead. The reason is guard preservation, not startup cost.
- A system prompt must never name a deferrable tool without stating how to load it. A deferred tool is not callable on turn one, so a prompt that names one without an adjacent `ToolSearch({tools:["<group>"]})` step is a dead end. The model calls it and gets an unknown-tool error. This held in five places at once (`system-prompt.ts`, `plan-mode-guidance.ts` ×2, `subagents/default-agents.ts`, `compass/system-prompt.ts`); the subsystem's enabled-ness is no defence, since that flag is exactly what makes the names eligible and deferred.
- Each Damocles extension instance attaches to its folder through `FolderRuntime.attachExtensionInstance`: the folder's MCP tools register on it (and every tool that connects later, until it detaches), and its ToolSearch republisher joins the republisher set. Attachments retire deterministically, never by exception, and every attachment has exactly one owner responsible for retiring it. Which owner applies depends on whether an `AgentSession` ever binds the instance, and the two cases are genuinely different. Do not collapse them:
  - **Session-bound instances retire themselves.** `attachExtensionInstance` returns a detach the instance calls from its own `session_shutdown` handler, for every reason, `'reload'` included. pi emits that event before it invalidates the instance (`AgentSessionRuntime.teardownCurrent`, `AgentSession.reload`), and pi's `registerTool` throws on an invalidated instance, so an MCP top-up into an instance still attached after its session is disposed fails with "extension ctx is stale" once per tool. This deliberately diverges from the observe-only lifecycle handlers in `hooks/index.ts`, which skip `'reload'` because an internal rebuild is not a user-meaningful session end; here a reload genuinely retires the instance.
  - **Unbound instances are retired by `PiRuntime`.** `resourceLoader.reload()` also runs the extension factory where no bind follows, at four sites: the `.damocles`/`.claude`/`.codex` asset watcher, the workspace-trust grant, `_hotReloadExtensions`, and `_removeSubscriptionPlugin`. `createAgentSessionServices` also mints one at init before any session exists. Such an instance receives no session event at all, so there is no owner inside it. `_reloadResources` takes a `ReloadBinding` argument stating which kind of reload it is, and the runtime holds the unbound instance's detach in `_unboundInstanceDetach`, retiring it the moment the next successful reload supersedes the instance. `prepareSessionExtensions` is the announcement that a session is about to bind whatever the loader currently holds, so it releases that claim (without retiring) on every exit path. That includes the first-session path that does no reload at all, and the path where the reload failed. Releasing is not optional politeness: a claim kept on an instance that has since gone live would detach a live panel on the next reload, freezing its ToolSearch menu and its MCP tools.
- Never infer an attachment's deadness from a throw: it retires live panels on unrelated failures, and it cannot see an unbound instance at all, whose `registerTool` succeeds into an unreferenced extension object and so never throws. Growth is the visible symptom. A window that never retires an unbound instance invokes a longer list of dead closures on every settings toggle.
- Every bind of the shared services must be announced by `prepareSessionExtensions()`. It is the handover point, so a bind that skips it leaves the runtime claiming a live instance as unbound and the next bare reload detaches a live panel. `PiSession`'s session factory announces; `PiRuntime.createSession` does not announce for you, so any future caller of it must. Nested subagent/team sessions are exempt by construction, not by luck: `createSubagentSession` builds its own `AgentSessionServices` with its own `extensionFactories`, so it never binds this loader's instance and never attaches to the folder.
- Nested (subagent/team) MCP tools are delivered as `customTools` on `createSubagentSession`, never through the shared MCP registrar. And that is what preserves the exemption in the bullet above, not a workaround despite it. `customTools` are read during construction, so a nested session still binds no shared loader instance and still never attaches to the folder. "Simplifying" this into sharing the registrar reopens exactly the bug class the instance-ownership model was built to close; do not, however much simpler it looks.
- A nested agent's MCP set is frozen at spawn: one `getAllToolDescriptors()` read and one `resourceServers()` read per spawn. The resource tools join the agent's MCP names, definitions and exposure split only when that read found a resource server and the name is eligible and not in `disallowed_tools`; their definitions are bound to the servers read then and call with each server's `expectedServerId`, so a server outside the snapshot, or one whose id changed, fails with the permanent "no longer available" wording. `isNestedMcpName` (`mcp/naming.ts`) is the one predicate for names that travel this way, shared by `resolveAgentToolset`, `teamAgentBaseToolNames` and the orphan diagnostic. From the two reads, its `tools:` names, its `customTools` definitions, its gate read-only classifier, its exposure split (`deferrable` and `direct`, so `deferrable ⊆ names`) and its ToolSearch blurbs are all derived. The direct names reach `createSubagentSession` as `directMcpToolNames`, which only subtracts from the deferral: omitting it defers every MCP tool rather than activating any. A second read anywhere in a spawn path reopens the divergence window. An `mcp__*` name in `tools:` with no matching definition is dropped silently (no error, no warning, no log), and a definition whose name is missing from `tools:` is lost as silently: pi unregisters it when `tools:` names any `mcp__` entry, and otherwise keeps it registered but never activatable, because pi's `_isActivatable` refuses a `direct` MCP tool the allowlist does not name unless pi's own `tool_search` is registered. Set-equality between the two is what catches both. Asserted at both composition sites (`pi-session.ts`'s subagent and team engines), because a test that reaches only one of them leaves the other free to regress while the suite stays green. `createSubagentSession` also logs any orphan it is handed, since every nested spawn funnels through there and that is the one place the class is observable at runtime. A server connecting mid-run reaches the next spawned agent, never a running one; there is no live top-up. Each frozen tool also carries the `serverId` it was captured from and calls with `expectedServerId`, because tool names are reassigned live (a folder server's tools are named with the user servers' tool names reserved, so a user server made visible takes a colliding name back and the folder tool moves to a hashed name): a name that has passed to another server fails as vanished rather than running that server's tool under the snapshot's read-only classification. (The freeze is over the descriptor set, not connectivity. A server still connecting but with cached descriptors is in the snapshot and callable, since `callTool` awaits `ensureConnected`.)
- The nested MCP grant is uniform across every agent type and mode. Explore's explicit list, `tools: *`, and team specialists all get the panel's full eligible set. MCP names are dynamic and deployment-specific, so no agent author can name them in frontmatter; intersecting with the agent's `tools:` would give the explicit-list agents none. `disallowed_tools` is the one opt-out, and it is exact and case-sensitive (`mapName` passes unknown names through unchanged). A later "fix" that lowercases `mapName` would silently break every MCP denial.
- Every `_reloadResources` call is serialized on `_reloadSync`, and the ownership rules above depend on that serialization, not just on `extendResources` ordering. A reload identifies the instance it minted through a single scratch slot written by `attachExtensionInstance`; two overlapping reloads would let one adopt the other's instance, and adopting a session-bound instance as unbound freezes a live panel's ToolSearch menu and MCP tools with no error anywhere.
- accepted, documented in `damocles-extension.ts`: `AgentSession.reload()` emits `session_shutdown({reason:'reload'})` before awaiting the loader reload, so a reload that throws leaves the still-live panel detached with no replacement. Silently, since `runMcpReload` catches and logs. It self-heals on the next successful `session.reload()`. Do not add recovery machinery for it unless it is actually observed; the fix would be idempotent re-attachment from a `session_start` handler guarded on the detach being undefined.
- **An extension instance is process-global and outlives any one session, so its `session_shutdown` handler retires only what the instance owns outright: its attachment above. Its event handlers stay registered.** `SessionShutdownEvent` carries no session id (`types.d.ts:637-642` in pi 1.4.0, as are the dist lines below), so an instance-wide response to it answers for every session bound to that instance, including the one that binds it next. That next session is reachable, because a failed `prepareSessionExtensions` reload leaves `getExtensions()` returning the previous array (`resource-loader.js:422`; the catch in `FolderRuntime.prepareSessionExtensions`, `folder-runtime.ts`). Retiring the handlers empties the instance's map (`loader.js:216-233`), and pi then skips the gate outright rather than calling it, since `beforeToolCall` treats `hasHandlers('tool_call') === false` as proceed (`agent-session.js:327-330`), so the session runs with no permission gate, no system prompt injection, no checkpoints and no hooks. Ownership is decided per dispatch by the session-keyed registries, `_panelRegistry` and `_checkpointRegistry`, never by instance-wide state. A tool call that finds no panel is blocked by `gateErrorFallback` rather than proceeding, because only main panel sessions dispatch into this instance and each is registered until its panel closes. The per-subagent instance retires nothing at all, since `createSubagentSession` gives each spawn its own services and loader and a nested session is disposed without an `AgentSessionRuntime` to emit the event. `handler-disposal.test.ts` pins three things: the handler count is unchanged after every shutdown reason, a session bound after a shutdown still gets its gate, prompt, checkpoints and hooks, and an unowned tool call is blocked.
- A tool's `description` is materialized at wrap time, not per request (`wrapToolDefinition` copies it as a plain string). A getter alone does not stay fresh: `pi.registerTool` is the only public re-wrap trigger, so anything whose description tracks live config must be re-registered (`FolderRuntime.republishToolSearch`, driven by `refreshActiveTools()`).
- Whatever `ToolSearch` advertises must be exactly what the session can load. The menu, the retry hint, the activation result and `/context` all derive from one loadable snapshot. Report what pi actually activated (re-read after activating), never what was requested. MCP tool names/descriptions are third-party text rendered into a line-structured menu: strip control chars at build time or a name forges a group line.

## Tool overrides

- A custom tool registered under a pi built-in's name replaces it. `_refreshToolRegistry` seeds the built-in definitions first, then writes every custom tool into the same map, so the last write wins. That is the only seam Damocles has for changing a built-in's behaviour, and it fails silently in both directions: a name that does not match exactly registers a second tool instead of overriding, and a built-in renamed upstream leaves the override in place as a tool nothing calls. `bash` is one live case, owned so a per-call `AbortController` can reach the running command.
- `grep` and `find` are overridden so they never reach pi's tools manager (`tools/search-tools.ts`). pi resolves `rg` and `fd` there, looking in its own bin directory (`~/.pi/agent/bin`) and then on PATH, and downloads whichever it finds in neither from GitHub into that directory: a network connection the README's privacy statement does not name, a write into the pi CLI's directory, and an unsigned executable run by a signed app. pi's `grep` takes no binary path, so Damocles keeps pi's definition and replaces `execute` with a copy that runs the ripgrep `@vscode/ripgrep` ships. `search-tools.test.ts` runs pi's own `grep` against the same rg as the oracle, comparing text, `details` and errors, and pins pi's parameter names to `GREP_PARAMETER_NAMES`, so a pi upgrade that changes the output or adds a parameter fails there. The one deliberate difference: a match whose path is not UTF-8 counts toward pi's limit but not toward ours, since neither prints it.
- `find` goes through pi's `operations.glob` hook instead, with ripgrep listing the files and `fdGlobMatcher` applying fd's rules: smart case, dotfiles, a name match without a `/`, no `!` negation and no extglobs. The pattern is matched in JavaScript because an rg `--glob` include overrides `.gitignore`, which fd's never does. rg runs with the search root as its working directory, because rg matches its globs against the path below that directory and the hook's `node_modules` and `.git` excludes would otherwise reject every file under a root inside either. The hook, not fd, is where those two excludes come from. Directories are derived from the listed files, so unlike fd `find` reports no empty directory, no directory whose files are all ignored, and no symlink. The hook branch of pi's `find` drops the "Use limit=N for more" hint its fd branch gives, and `createFindTool` puts it back.
- An override keeps the built-in's exact name, lowercase `bash` included. The permission gate classifies by the mapped name (`mapPiToolName` then `toolCategory`, `permission-gate.ts:159` and `permission-gate.ts:214`), and `PI_NATIVE_ACTIVE_TOOLS` plus every subagent and team toolset carry the pi spelling, so renaming to `Bash` means changing the gate, plan-mode classification, the read-only shell classifier, the active-set lists and the tool catalog together, and whichever one is missed fails open rather than loudly. `Edit` renamed because its schema had to diverge from pi's; bash's does not, so it has no reason to pay that price.
- `write` is overridden so a write that replaces a file records its patch (`tools/write-tool.ts`). pi's own write still does the write, through its `operations` hook built per call, so the old content is read inside that write's file mutation queue slot and an edit queued before it is already in the old content. A second `withFileMutationQueue` around `execute` would deadlock, since pi's own call then queues behind it on the same key. The schema, prompt text and result text are pi's, and `write-tool.test.ts` compares the bytes on disk with what pi's write leaves. A new file records `created: true` instead of a patch, since the tool input holds every line of it. Old content that cannot be read for the diff (EACCES, EBUSY) is logged and records no details, and `writeFile` alone decides the outcome, so the cosmetic diff never fails a write. The patch is bounded in `file-patch.ts`: a binary old file (a NUL in the first 8000 bytes, git's rule) records `patchOmitted: 'binary'`, and a side over `EDITOR_MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` or a diff of more than `FILE_PATCH_MAX_CHANGED_LINES` (1000) inserted and deleted lines records `patchOmitted: 'tooLarge'`, because jsdiff's cost grows with the square of the edit length and an unbounded rewrite diff blocks the host for seconds.
- `ls` is overridden so its file operations come from Damocles' bundle (`tools/ls-tool.ts`). pi's ls touches the disk only through `ops.exists`, `ops.stat` and `ops.readdir` and stats every child, and pi's default operations run on the patched `fs` an external gets, which lists a user's `.asar` with a trailing `/` and holds it open ("Desktop packaging and updates"). The override passes pi's own defaults written in Damocles' source, which the build redirects to `original-fs`; `ls-tool.test.ts` compares the output with pi's ls. `read`, `Edit` and `write` cannot be fixed this way in pi 1.4.0: `read` resolves its target through pi's own `pathExists` and `Edit` and `write` through `withFileMutationQueue`'s `realpath` (`file-mutation-queue.js:14`), both before any operation runs. An agent `read`, `Edit` or `write` whose target is itself an `.asar` therefore opens the archive and holds it until Restart Engine or quit (a window reload in VS Code). Measured on Windows with Electron 44: `read` and `Edit` then fail with ENOENT, because Electron's `access` reports the archive missing, and `write` replaces the file through Damocles' own operations. earendil-works/pi#11235 asks for `exists` and `realpath` operations (closed by pi's new-contributor bot pending triage); once pi has them, pass Damocles operations to those tools as `ls` does.
- Never pair an override with an `excludeTools` entry. `isAllowedTool` filters custom tools through the same allow and exclude sets as the built-ins, so excluding `bash` drops the replacement along with the original and leaves the model with no shell at all. The `edit` exclusion is not a precedent for it: that one is safe only because the replacement registered under a different name.
- Reading a host service at construction rather than at execute makes that service a precondition of building the session, not of running the tool. `createBashTool` spreads its metadata from a delegate built during `buildCustomTools`, so `createBashToolDefinition`, `getShellCommandPrefix` and `getShellPath` must all answer before a `PiSession` exists at all, and so must `createGrepToolDefinition` and `createFindToolDefinition` for the search overrides, `createWriteToolDefinition` for `write` and `createLsToolDefinition` for `ls`; four test files needed their doubles extended each time an override landed. The search overrides read `platform().paths` only at execute time, through the rg path thunk, so the platform is not a precondition of building a session. The symptom is a `TypeError` several frames inside session construction, naming a method rather than the tool that asked for it, and every later assertion then fails on a null session, so the cascade points nowhere near the cause. Suspect an incomplete double before suspecting the session. The construction-time read is still the right trade, since it is what keeps the model-visible schema and the system prompt identical to pi's, but the cost lands on whoever writes the next session test.

## Shell cancellation and process lifetime

- **A cancelled shell call says so in exactly one place: `details[CANCELLED_TOOL_DETAIL_KEY]` on the tool result** (`src/shared/types/session.ts`). A call of any tool that its aborted run cut short says so in the same place: `registerStoppedToolResultMarker` (`stopped-tool-result.ts`, registered by the main and the nested extension factories) sets it from pi's `tool_result` hook on an error result that ended while `ctx.signal` was aborted, keeping the error flag and text the model sees. pi runs that hook only for a call it executed, so the marker always means the call ran; the webview reads it on a completed or failed status (`resolveCancelledStatus`), and the adapter and the subagent stream bridge send a marked error result as `toolCompleted`, so its output shows as a per-call cancel's does. `withPerCallCancel` sets it on both exit branches, merged over `lastPartial?.details` so the partial's `fullOutputPath` and truncation fields survive rather than being replaced (`tools/cancellable-shell.ts`, `cancelledDetails`). Both branches return a normal result, never an error, because the user asked for the stop and the retry heuristics that treat an error as worth retrying must stay quiet; so a cancelled call always lands as `toolCompleted` and the marker on `details` is the only thing that tells the card apart from a success. It rides on `details` because `details` is persisted and re-read on reload, which view state is not, and because the cancel registry releases its entry in a `finally` that runs before pi emits `tool_execution_end`, so a registry side-channel keyed by tool call id could not answer at the time the card is built. The guarantee is only as good as its producers, and there are three: `pi-stream-adapter.ts` and `subagents/subagent-stream-bridge.ts` emit `toolMetadata` from `result.details`, and the team path carries it on `teamAgentToolResult.metadata` (`src/core/team/agent-runner.ts`), which was added because the team path dropped `details` outright and rendered a cancelled command as a green check. A new producer that does not carry `details` reopens exactly that. The reload path is a fourth producer and needs the marker too: `agent-runner.ts` persists a `tool_result` entry per call carrying `content`, `is_error` and the normalized `metadata`, and `useTeamStore.handleAgentDataLoaded` indexes those by `tool_use_id` and derives the same three statuses the live path shows. Persisting `content` alone would leave a reloaded cancelled call indistinguishable from a success, which is what the hardcoded `status: 'completed'` it replaced did to cancelled and errored calls alike. A call with no persisted result restores as `unrecorded`, a terminal status of its own: `completed` would claim an outcome that was never recorded, while `pending` and `running` are pre-terminal and put a spinner, a Stop control and a "Tool is running..." overlay on a tool that ended long ago. The main-session replay path derives the same status from the same fact (`convertHistoryTools`, `src/webview/composables/message-handler/utils.ts`), and so does the subagent transcript loader (`buildChatMessagesFromHistory`, `src/webview/stores/useSubagentStore.ts`), which is why `unrecorded` lives on `ToolCall['status']` rather than inside any one loader. `handleAgentDataLoaded`, `convertHistoryTools` and `buildChatMessagesFromHistory` all read a transcript whose run is over, except a views.join replay's calls the live run still owns, which core marks `HistoryToolCall.live` and `convertHistoryTools` shows `pending` for the live messages to settle (see "A view detaching never disposes a panel with a run"); a stored call without that mark stays `unrecorded` whatever message names it later. The subagent one is reached only by a resume from disk and by the single `subagentMessagesUpdate` the stream bridge emits from `finish()`, so a block with no recorded result is unrecorded rather than still running. The live subagent path is a different function, `buildToolCallsWithStatus`, which sees a `tool_use` block before pi has started the tool and so defaults to `pending`, never to a terminal status. Team logs written before this landed hold no `tool_result` entries and cannot be repaired. The one cancelled call with no marker is one the user stopped while the permission gate still held it: the gate blocks it and it renders as denied (next point).

- **A shell call can be stopped from the gate's entry until it settles** (`tools/shell-cancel-registry.ts`). pi emits `tool_execution_start`, which marks the card running and shows Stop for a `LIVE_OUTPUT_TOOLS` call, before it runs `beforeToolCall` (`prepareToolCall` in `agent-loop.js`), so the permission gate opens the call's entry (`admit`) at entry, before the PreToolUse hooks, the decision and the checkpoint wait, and execute adopts that entry (`withPerCallCancel`). An approval prompt moves the card to awaiting approval, so once `canUseTool` lets the call through, prompted or not, the gate posts `toolPending` before the checkpoint wait and the card shows running, with Stop and live output, again; the main and subagent gates post it, and a team agent's gate has no `postMessage`, since its cards take no status from a prompt. `LIVE_OUTPUT_TOOLS` is the one set: the card shows Stop, the gate admits and `buildCustomTools` wraps exactly those names. An admitted entry is dropped when the gate blocks or throws, when the run aborts before execute (pi never executes a call of an aborted run, sequential or parallel), at `tool_execution_end` (another extension's `tool_call` handler can block a call this gate allowed) and by `clear()`, so none outlives its call. A Stop while the gate holds the call aborts the entry's signal, which the gate combines with the run's (`AbortSignal.any`) for the PreToolUse hooks, `canUseTool` and the checkpoint wait, so a Stop kills a running hook's process tree and starts no later hook, withdraws a pending approval prompt and ends the wait at once. The gate checks for a Stop after the hooks, before any approval prompt, right after `canUseTool` returns (a withdrawn prompt settles as an unasked deny, so this check comes first) and after the wait, and blocks the call, so a stopped call never runs and never skips approval. The block is the user's act: it is worded with `formatDenyReason` (a block result carries no `details`, so it cannot carry the cancelled marker, and denied is the user-rejection rendering), never sets `terminate` (one Stop leaves the turn running) and never contains the note, which went out as a user message like any other cancel note. A Stop that lands after the gate allowed the call and before execute (a deferred parallel batch) makes execute return the cancelled result without calling the delegate, so nothing spawns. The gate's handle and the tools' handle for one context are separate `forContext` handles with equivalent deliveries; an adopted entry keeps the delivery it was admitted with.

- **A cancel note is user turn content, delivered to the agent that ran the command, and never executed.** It is never appended to the tool result. A tool result is untrusted by construction, so an instruction found in one is prompt injection, and a correctly aligned model refuses it; that was observed, not predicted, before the note moved. The trailer says only that the user cancelled, how long the command ran, and that a reason follows. Delivery is bound per build context (`note-delivery.ts`, one builder per context, one `PiSession` method per builder), so the target is fixed when the tools are built rather than guessed when Stop is clicked: the panel session steers it through `prompt(..., { streamingBehavior: 'steer' })`, a subagent takes the steer channel a typed `/steer` uses, and a team agent takes `deliverUserNote`, the sink its own runner publishes for the duration of its run. A single delivery for all three would land a subagent's note on the main conversation. All three reach the agent at the next tool boundary of the run the command belongs to, so the model's next request carries the cancelled result and the note together. Never deliver the panel note as a follow-up: pi hands a follow-up over only once the model has stopped calling tools, so the model answers the cancelled result, typically by waiting for the reason the trailer promised, before it sees the note. The note arrives while the call is still in flight (held by the gate, or a command still dying), since the call's entry lives until the call settles and the run cannot end before its tools do, so pi accepts it into the running run. `expandPromptTemplates: false` is the whole leading-slash guard: it switches off the extension-command dispatch and the skill and template expansion inside `prompt()`, so a note beginning with `/` is queued as literal text and cannot be executed or throw.
