- **Two writers append after an await, and both re-check liveness first.** `maybeGenerateTitle` waits on an unbounded sub-call (15 s timeout on the small/fast model), and a title that outlives its session is routed to that conversation by the session id captured before the call (`PiSession.routeTitle`), never through a captured manager: through its live holder, this panel or another (`LiveSessionMutator.applyAiTitle`, which writes only while that session is still bound there with its lease and unnamed, and refreshes that panel's header, a panel whose dispose has begun included, until it leaves the registry right before its runtime tears down; a decline is logged), else into its stored file (`titleStoredPiSession`), which in one synchronous section re-checks for a live holder, refuses while a panel of this process holds the session (`isSessionHeldByPanelHere`: a resume target not started yet, a switch in flight, a runtime tearing down), takes the writer lease, checks the file exists and appends `session_info` only when it has no name. A delete holds that lease across its detach and rm, so a late title never recreates a deleted file. A panel whose own title lands while it closes waits for its dispose, which releases the lease after the teardown's last append, and then writes the file. A panel holding the session with no live holder, a refused lease or a missing file drops the title, logged, and nothing is written once the runtime retired at shutdown. So a cleared conversation, or a closed panel's, still gets the title its first turn paid for, unless another panel of this process is opening it or tearing its runtime down when the title lands: writing then would make the store a second writer beside that panel. A turn's memory candidate is likewise keyed by the session id and prompt index read when its prompt started, so a clear during the turn files it under the cleared conversation. `persistTurnStopped` waits on `session.abort()` and appends only while the runtime session still has the stopped session's id and its lease was not lost, the rule `persistCheckpointRecord` uses. A delete or switch during a Stop calls `abort()` again, which waits for the same wind-down, so the record lands in the old file before the replacement installs. `recordMidStreamMarker` and `recordSteer` re-read `this.runtime?.session` and write synchronously; `renameActiveSession`/`setActiveSessionTag` re-read after `ensureStarted()`. `recordPromptSidecars` runs when pi commits a prompt's entry, which for a queued prompt can be long after `sendMessage` returned, so it writes only while the panel is undisposed and the runtime session is the one the prompt was sent on. Both sidecars sit under that one check, so a prompt gets both or neither.

## Checkpoints

- **A chat with no project folder takes no checkpoints.** The home target (`FolderTarget.projectScope` false: no folder open in VS Code, no project loaded on the desktop) runs in the user's home folder, and a baseline of it is a git snapshot of the whole profile: it never finished inside the wait limit, so every file-changing call stalled for the full wait and the turn was not rewindable anyway, while the shadow repo grew by gigabytes. `session-manager.ts` passes `projectScope` to `PiSession` explicitly, and with it false the panel creates no `CheckpointService`, registers none with the extension and hydrates nothing, and no gate context (panel, subagent, team agent) gets a `checkpointBaseline`, so nothing waits. A fork runs no checkpoint git work (no legacy clone, no record carry, no ref copy), so nothing creates or touches a shadow repo for the home folder. Only the conversation can be rewound: every prompt is a rewind point (`checkpointInfo` lists them live and `promptUserIdsOnBranch` on reload), the rewind view lists each prompt with `notRewindable: { reason: 'no-project' }`, which disables the file options, and runs no git (no row carries the Not rewindable badge in `RewindBrowser.vue`; the selected prompt's notes, `RewindCheckpointNotes.vue`, say why once), and a file rewind or undo is refused with a localized message. `ChatSession.fileCheckpoints` carries the rule to the history loader and the rewind view.
- **One shadow repo per folder, shared by every conversation in it** (`pi-session/checkpoints/`). The repo lives at `~/.damocles/pi/checkpoints/folders/<folderId>/`, where `folderId` is the first 16 hex of sha256 of `folderKey(cwd)` (the keying Compass uses). It holds one bare git dir, one index, `folder.json` (`{ path, layout: 1 }`) and the mkdir lock. The fixed-length id keeps `GIT_DIR` under git's `'$GIT_DIR' too big` limit for any project path. The shared index is the point: its stat cache lets a new conversation re-hash only the files that changed. Every git step that writes a folder repo runs under that repo's lock (`checkpoints/lock.ts`): an in-process queue per repo plus the cross-process mkdir lock with owner pid, heartbeat and stale takeover. The lock is not reentrant, so no code awaits a baseline while holding it, and only checkpoint git work (baseline, finalize, compaction snapshot, pre-rewind snapshot, restore, maintenance, ref delete and copy) ever holds it. No tool holds it. It never waits forever: a holder is stale once its heartbeat lapsed past 30 s and its pid is gone, or, whatever the pid says (a pid can be reused), once the waiter has itself polled for 10 minutes without seeing the heartbeat move. The bound counts the waiter's own polls, not wall-clock time, so a system suspend counts as one poll and never makes a live holder look stale. Every wait where the user waits takes an `AbortSignal` (`withRepoLock(dir, fn, { signal })` throws `LockWaitAbortedError`).
- **A git lock file a killed git left behind is removed only when no git of the folder repo can still hold it.** Every git command that can create `index.lock`, `packed-refs.lock`, `refs/**.lock`, `HEAD.lock`, `config.lock`, `shallow.lock` or `gc.pid` in a folder repo runs under the folder lock and registers its pid in `<repo>/git-procs/<pid>` while it runs, from the moment the process launcher reports its start (a holder killed before that leaves its git unregistered, which a stale takeover reaches only after the 30 s heartbeat lapse). When a holder acquires the lock and any registered git still runs, nothing is removed. Otherwise the index, packed-refs, HEAD, config, shallow and gc.pid locks go, logged with their age, and `refs/**.lock` too when there is evidence of a dead holder: a takeover of the folder lock, or a registration whose process is gone, runs another program, or is over 24 hours old. A holder that released normally awaited all its git children, so a git lock left then is from a killed git. This assumes the spawned git process (on Windows the `git.exe` launcher, which waits for the real git) outlives the git work it started unless it is killed on its own.
- **Folder repo state is written atomically.** `size-excludes.json`, `info/exclude`, `info/attributes`, `folder.json` and `sessions/<id>.json` go through a synced temp file and a rename, the two `info/` files only when their content changes. An unreadable or malformed size state is logged and read as none, which costs a full rescan. A repo is built in `<repo>/.git-init-*` (init, identity, tuning, ignorecase, version stamp, attributes) and renamed to `.git` last, so `isInitialized` never accepts a half-built repo; the next init removes a leftover temp dir with a log line. The size state records every file over 1 MiB, the smallest cap the setting allows, so hosts with different `maxFileSizeMB` sharing a folder repo derive their size section without a rescan.
- **Snapshots and restores are byte-exact.** A folder repo's `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes` is `* -text -eol -ident -filter -working-tree-encoding`, written at creation and refreshed when it differs, so no line ending is converted, no `$Id$` expanded and no filter run, whatever the project's `.gitattributes` says. `diff` is left alone so numstat still counts lines. Legacy per-session repos do not get it: their blobs were captured through the project's attributes, so restoring through the same attributes is the closest to the original bytes. `core.filemode=false` is set only on Windows; elsewhere git's own probe applies, so an executable keeps 100755 and a restore brings its executable bit back. The rewind preview uses the checkpoint repo's exact config (`PORTABLE_CONFIG`: autocrlf and safecrlf off, filemode per platform, `core.excludesFile=` so no user-global ignore applies, `core.fsmonitor=false`, `core.hooksPath=/dev/null`).
- **Commits live only through per-conversation refs.** A snapshot is `write-tree` + `commit-tree` with no shared parent, and HEAD never moves, so nothing but a ref keeps a commit alive. A turn writes `refs/damocles/sessions/<sessionId>/<userEntryId>/before` and `/after`, plus `/skipped` for its skipped-file manifest; a compaction snapshot writes `.../<compactionEntryId>/snapshot`; a pre-rewind snapshot writes `.../_rewinds/<id>/snapshot` and `/skipped`. `_` cannot start an entry id, so that namespace never collides with an entry's refs. Ids go into ref names only after `isSafeRefId`. Deleting a conversation deletes its own ref namespace in every folder repo its entries name, under each repo's lock, after every holder detached and after its session file is gone, and nothing waits on it: that lock can be held by another conversation's first baseline for minutes. The folder ids are read from the file before the `rm`. Refs whose removal fails or never runs (the process exits) are left to maintenance's orphan rule below. A fork copies the refs of the checkpoints it inherits (and their manifests) into its own namespace and carries every checkpoint record whose `userEntryId` is on the inherited branch, wherever the record sits in the parent's file. The records are written before the fork opens; the ref copy runs after, without the user waiting on the folder lock, and checks each object under that lock when it runs, so a copy never names an object already gone. Before the fork opens, its registration in each folder repo those records name is written with `pendingCopy: true` (`markForkCopyPending`), without the folder lock, because nothing deletes a new session's registration; the copy clears the flag and keeps the file's mtime, which dates the fork for eviction. A fork's inherited checkpoints become its own refs before any ref deletion or prune in that folder repo: every lock hold that deletes a conversation's refs (delete, age eviction, orphan sweep) or prunes first runs `reconcilePendingCopiesLocked`, which copies each pending fork's refs from the validated v3 entries of its session file and clears the flag, or drops a pending registration whose session file is gone, so a copy the process exited before running is still made. The in-process lock queue is first come first served, so a restore or a delete of the fork queued later runs after the copy. A fork inherits no pre-rewind record or ref: an undo belongs to the conversation whose files it replaced. Maintenance evicts a conversation's refs by `damocles.checkpoints.retentionDays` or when its registered session file (`sessions/<sessionId>.json`) is gone and its newest ref is older than 24 hours; it never removes a folder repo as a whole and judges a conversation only by its own refs and registration.
- **gc runs off the folder lock, and still never drops what a ref or the index reaches.** Under the lock, maintenance evicts refs, rewrites `refs/damocles/index` (a commit of the shared index's tree, so blobs only the index references survive) and runs `pack-refs --all --prune`. It then runs `git repack -A -d -l` without the folder lock, under a separate `<repo>/repack` lock, and finally `git prune --expire=1.hour.ago` under the folder lock again. After a snapshot, when the repo has 50 or more packs, the producer starts `git repack -d -l --geometric=2` under the repack lock, off the folder lock and off its queue. Only `prune` deletes objects, and it runs under the folder lock, where no writer runs. Every writer (add, update-index, write-tree, commit-tree, hash-object) writes its objects and the refs or shared index that reach them within one lock hold, and git treats the shared index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`) as a reachability root. `repack -A -d` without `--unpack-unreachable` never deletes: reachable objects go to the new pack and unreachable packed objects are written loose, and `--geometric` copies every object of the packs it rolls up. So an object a writer reuses during a repack is in the new pack or loose, and the next prune sees what reaches it. The one-hour prune grace protects only the lock-free rewind preview's throwaway objects.
- **Entries say which repo holds them.** A v3 `damocles-checkpoint` entry carries `repo: 'folder'`, `folderId` and `skipped`; new turns, including new turns in an old conversation, always write v3. A v2 entry (no `repo`) resolves to the conversation's legacy per-session repo through `getRepoDir(sessionFile)` exactly as before, for rewind, diff preview, file content and fork clone. Legacy repos are never migrated or widened; their existing age eviction retires them. A turn with no usable baseline gets a `{ v: 3, kind: 'not-rewindable', userEntryId, reason, params: { tool?, waitSeconds?, error? }, createdAt }` record under the same custom type; the webview renders its text from `reason` and `params`, and raw git text appears only as `params.error`. A pre-rewind snapshot gets a `{ v: 3, kind: 'pre-rewind', id, folderId, commit, skipped, target, createdAt }` record. `getCheckpointEntries` and `getCheckpointRecords` return neither, so every consumer of checkpoint entries still sees only entries with commits; the rewind view reads them through `getNotRewindableEntries` and `getPreRewindEntries`. Every validator checks every field and drops any other shape (an earlier unreleased v3 shape, or a v3 entry whose commits are not object ids) as no checkpoint; none throws. A restore or manifest read checks that the entry's `folderId` is cwd's and that the repo's `folder.json` names cwd, and otherwise touches nothing.
- **No pi handler awaits checkpoint git work.** pi awaits extension handlers before it forwards stream events and runs tools (`_handleAgentEvent`), and awaits `agent_settled` before `prompt()` resolves, so an awaited `git add -A` of a large folder held the first streamed text back for minutes. The `message_start`, `agent_settled` and `session_compact` handlers in `damocles-extension.ts` call synchronous `CheckpointService` methods that queue the work on the service's chain and return; there is no checkpoint `turn_end` handler. A turn's baseline starts at the prompt's own user entry, one microtask after its `message_end` (`watchPromptEntry`'s `onCommitted`): pi appends the message synchronously after notifying listeners, so the entry exists and no model request has gone out. A run opened without `sendMessage` (a cancel note, a re-steer) and a steered message start at the next assistant `message_start`, keyed on the branch's last user entry. A turn starts once per entry: the service remembers every entry it started and every entry a resumed or forked branch already has a record for, and the producer refuses a turn it already finalized, so a run keyed on a finished turn gets no second baseline and its gate waiters return at once. Finalize runs on the same chain after a still-running baseline and before the next turn's baseline, so it never delays the next prompt's stream. `checkpoint-stream-latency.test.ts` pins it on the real pi event path: another holder keeps the folder lock for the whole prompt, so the baseline cannot finish, and the first text must still stream and `prompt()` settle; it logs the time from the prompt entry's commit to the first text.
- **Records minted off the handler path are appended by the panel, and only to its live session.** The service hands each record, pre-rewind records included, to `PiSession.persistCheckpointRecord`, which appends it with `sessionManager.appendCustomEntry` only while the panel is not disposed, its runtime session still has that session id, and its lease was not lost. A delete, a switch, a lost lease and a panel close all move the runtime off the session and dispose the service first. A switch, a delete and a close first abort the running turn, then wait up to 2 s (`CHECKPOINT_DRAIN_MS`) for the queued checkpoint work while the session is still installed and the panel still registered. An abort made while the run streams resolves only after pi's `agent_settled` handlers; one made while those handlers run returns at once, since pi counts itself idle from the start of `_emitAgentSettled` (`agent-session.js:685`, `:1983-1985` in pi-coding-agent 1.4.0). Either way the checkpoint `agent_settled` handler, which queues the turn's finalize synchronously, has run first: it is registered before `registerConfiguredHooks` (`damocles-extension.ts`), and pi runs one extension's handlers in registration order, so no handler that can hold the settle precedes it. The drain therefore covers the turn that was running, and no tool call runs after the teardown began or meets the unregistered gate's fallback block. A finalize that is nearly done still records the last turn; work still running after that (a first baseline of a large folder) is dropped, and its before-ref waits for age eviction. `dispose` stops the producer synchronously, so it writes no ref afterwards, and a late record is dropped with a log line. Never use `pi.appendEntry` from a continuation: the extension instance is shared by the folder's panels. A record lands at the leaf, which may be after the next prompt's user entry or between a tool call and its result. That is safe because a custom entry is not model context and every reader matches records by `userEntryId` or pre-rewind `id`, and tool results by `toolCallId`, never by position (`damocles-agent-invocation` entries already land mid-turn the same way).
- **File-changing tools wait for the turn's baseline in the permission gate, bounded by `damocles.checkpoints.baselineWaitSeconds`** (default 30, 5 to 600). `waitsForCheckpointBaseline` in `permission-gate.ts` decides, given the folder the checkpoint captures (`CheckpointBaselineGate.folder`, the session's cwd). Read tools, read-only-annotated MCP tools, shell commands `readonly-shell.ts` proves read-only (in every mode), the task and interactive tools, the in-process coordination tools (memory, Compass, browser, team messaging; none writes into the folder), and an Edit or Write whose target is the session plan file (`isPlanFilePath`) or outside the folder never wait and never mark the turn. The target resolves against the folder, so a path the check cannot place reads as inside and waits. Other Edit and Write, other shell (whatever path it names), MCP tools without a read-only annotation, unknown tools and the spawn tools (`Agent`, `create_team`, `resume_team`, `team_spawn_specialist`, `team_redispatch_specialist`) wait. The wait runs in the gate's single `proceed()`, after approval, so the approval prompt's time counts and a blocked call never waits. Configured PreToolUse hooks run before it, so a hook that itself writes into the folder is not bracketed by the turn's baseline; that is accepted. Past the limit the tool runs (a timeout is not a block, so no `formatPolicyBlockReason`), the turn is marked not rewindable (`markNotRewindable(userEntryId, 'baseline-timeout', { tool, waitSeconds })`), and the user gets one `notification` warning for the turn. The late baseline still finishes and warms the index, but it gets no ref and finalize yields the not-rewindable record, because the tool's changes may be in it. Later waiters in that turn return at once, and a later `message_start` for the same entry starts nothing. An abort, a dispose or a Stop of the waiting shell call releases a waiter at once. The wait does not register in `PermissionState`: the session is working, not waiting on the user. Each wait logs `[perf] checkpoint.wait <ms> outcome=<ready|timeout|aborted|disposed>`. The timer is the only bound; git is never killed on a timeout, which would leave `index.lock` in the shared repo.
- **Subagents and team agents wait on the parent turn's baseline through the same gate.** Their gate context carries the parent panel's `checkpointBaseline`, which reads the panel's live service at call time, so a nested file change never lands before the parent's current baseline, under the same limit, the same folder rule and the same single notice. A background agent still running after the parent's turn settled has no turn to wait for; its changes land in the next turn's window. This cannot deadlock: a baseline waits only on the service chain and the folder lock, no tool holds the lock, and every gate wait ends at the timer, an abort or a dispose.
- **Every restore first keeps the folder's current state, and the user can undo it.** A rewind to a turn and an undo both snapshot the folder exactly like a baseline (same excludes, protections and tracked capture) before anything changes, and log the commit (`pre-rewind snapshot <id> of session <sid> is commit <hash>`). If that snapshot fails, nothing is restored and the user is told no file changed. A v3 target is snapshotted and restored in one folder-lock hold; for a v2 target the snapshot goes to the folder repo of cwd under its lock, then the legacy restore takes the legacy repo's own lock, so no code nests two locks. The panel persists the pre-rewind record whenever there is one, including when the restore failed, and the rewind view lists each as a restore point, "Before rewind at 14:32" or "Before undo at 14:32", with an Undo rewind action (`undoRewind`). Undo (`restorePreRewind`) puts back the files of that snapshot through the same `safeCheckout` protections and takes its own pre-rewind snapshot, so an undo is undoable. It changes files only; the conversation tree is already navigable in pi. When the apply and its rollback both fail, the user's error says the folder may be partly restored and that the files from before the rewind are kept, and names both errors. The user's wait is bounded: `PiSession` gives a restore `RESTORE_WAIT_MS` (30 s) to get past earlier checkpoint work on the service chain and the folder lock; past it the restore resolves `aborted` at once and never runs later, and once it holds the lock it runs to completion. A missing or disposed service (`service-unavailable`) and a failed git probe (`git-unavailable`) are told apart. Every message is localized.
- **Files the project's own git tracks are always captured.** A snapshot captures every file the project's git tracks under the conversation folder, even under a path the exclude set drops (`dist/`, `build/`, `out/`, `coverage/`, `.cache/`, `node_modules/`, `*.log`, `.DS_Store`) or one the project's `.gitignore` matches (a force-added `.env.example`). The list comes from `git -C <cwd> -c core.fsmonitor=false ls-files -z`, which never writes the project's index or refs, cached per process while the project's index keeps its size and mtime. No enclosing repository means no tracked list, and a failed read is logged and read as empty. The security excludes (`.git`, `.damocles/settings.json`, `.damocles/settings.local.json`, `.damocles/mcp.local.json`) always win: a tracked copy is never captured or reported. The size cap, the category excludes and LFS still apply to tracked files, and each file they leave out is a skip in the checkpoint's list. Mechanism: `add -A`, then `update-index --add` of the tracked files the shared index lacks; once there, the stat cache keeps them current and `prepareSnapshot` keeps them, so an unchanged tracked file is never re-hashed. A submodule is tracked only as its gitlink, which is never staged, so files inside a submodule or a nested repository follow the exclude rules like untracked files; an embedded repository with no commit is left out with one log line instead of failing the folder. Only folder repos capture tracked files: a legacy per-session repo never does, in a restore or in the preview, so a tracked file a legacy repo excludes is ignored now and protected, neither deleted nor restored. For a folder-repo target, a tracked file the exclude rules drop is restored when the target has it and left where it is, never deleted, when the target lacks it (the target may predate the project tracking it, or the tracked list may have been unreadable then). When a directory now stands where the target has such a file, that path is protected, so nothing the directory holds is deleted. The rewind preview follows the same rules.
- **Large and binary files stay out of folder repos, and a rewind never touches them.** A folder repo's `info/exclude` holds the exclude set of its version (`FOLDER_CHECKPOINT_EXCLUDE_SET`: the performance excludes plus media, archives and disk images, model weights and array dumps, and Python, JVM and JS build and environment caches; images and PDFs are left to the size cap), every `filter=lfs` pattern of the project's `.gitattributes`, and a generated section listing every file over `damocles.checkpoints.maxFileSizeMB` (default 25, 1 to 2048). Before each snapshot the size section is rebuilt from lstat of the untracked and modified files plus the previous section, never reading contents, and files that are now ignored are dropped from the index unless the project tracks them, because an exclude never stops `add -A` from restaging a file already in the index. Every checkpoint stores its complete skipped list (path, bytes or null for a directory skipped whole, reason `size`, `category` or `lfs`) as a JSON blob in the folder repo, referenced by its `/skipped` ref so it lives exactly as long as the checkpoint, with no truncation. The session entry keeps only the summary: `{ totalCount, totalBytes, byReason: { size?, category?, lfs?: { count, bytes } }, patterns: [{ pattern, reason, count, bytes }], manifest }`. A skip counts under a category or LFS pattern only when `check-ignore -v` names the folder repo's own `info/exclude`; a project `.gitignore` line such as `*.mp4` is the project's own ignore, not a skip. The rewind view shows the summary under "Not restored by this checkpoint" and loads the full list when the user expands it (`requestSkippedFiles` answered by `skippedFiles`, read by `readSkippedManifest` without the folder lock, since a ref keeps the blob alive). A restore never modifies or deletes a file that is protected now: one ignored now (and not a tracked file a snapshot captures anyway), over the cap now, or recorded as skipped by the target snapshot. That includes a file captured small and grown past the cap since, which a rewind leaves as it is. A path that is ignored now but absent is recreated from the target, because that changes no existing bytes. The `clean -fd` after the apply protects every path ignored at the start of the restore by exact lines, so a restore that removes or replaces a `.gitignore` never deletes a file only that `.gitignore` protected. When a protected file or directory stands where the target needs a path, the restore refuses and touches nothing, because `read-tree -u` would delete an ignored file in its way. A turn's change list reports a file that grew past the cap during the turn as a skip, not a deletion. The protection is path-based and applies to legacy rewinds too, without widening a legacy repo's excludes. The rewind preview uses the same cap, so it lists exactly what the restore changes.

## Agent auth store

- **One Claude sign-in at a time.** A second `claudeSignIn` is refused before it touches the running flow's abort controller, which Cancel and Sign out reach it through. A pasted redirect URL is forwarded to pi only as a string of at most 8 KB.
- **pi owns `auth.json` and is its only writer; Damocles reads it without pi's lock** (`pi-session/auth-file.ts`). pi's `FileAuthStorageBackend` rewrites the file in place with `writeFileSync` under `proper-lockfile`; it retries `ELOCKED` for up to 30 s, and on Windows retries `EPERM`, which a lock-dir `mkdir` racing another process's `rmdir` fails with, at most 5 times with a backoff from 10 ms (`MAX_EPERM_RETRIES` in pi-coding-agent 1.4.0 `core/auth-storage.js`). A reader that took the same lock would still contend with pi's own write and could exhaust those retries. A read that lands between pi's truncate and its write sees empty text or an unterminated prefix of one JSON object, which `JSON.parse` rejects, so `readAuthFile` re-reads up to 5 times, 20 ms apart, and then returns the last content this process parsed from that path, which may be older than the file on disk; with no earlier read it throws, and the status reads as signed out. Every Damocles read of auth state (`readClaudeAuthFromDisk`, `readOpenAIAuthFromDisk`) goes through it. A failure is logged by error name and code only, because a `JSON.parse` message quotes the tokens it failed on, and only once until a read succeeds or the failure changes. A missing agent dir or file reads as no credentials and creates nothing.
- **A change to `auth.json` re-syncs the OpenAI and key-provider runtime keys and republishes the account state in every live session.** One debounced `FileWatcherFactory.watch(agentDir, 'auth.json')` per watcher factory and agent dir is shared by its holders (`PiRuntime.watchAuthFile`, `_holdAuthFileWatch`): `ChatPanelProvider` holds it from activation and `PiRuntime` joins it at the end of init, so there is never a second watcher. Once the runtime holds it, a change first awaits pi's offline availability refresh (`modelRuntime.getAvailable()` with no provider, which re-reads `auth.json` into the stored-credential snapshot `getProviderAuthStatus` answers from, with no network catalog refresh; `model-runtime.js:189-214`). A failed refresh is logged and the rest still runs: the OpenAI runtime key rule, `publishAccountInfo()` on each registered session, then `syncCustomProviders`, whose listeners republish every key status (`PiRuntime._applyAuthFileChange`); before that (no runtime, or init not finished) it notifies the memory-judge and auth-file listeners directly, so the settings' Memory judge status, Anthropic row and account-provider rows, which read `auth.json` themselves before pi starts, follow a sign-in made in another app. Either way the auth-file listener (`ChatPanelProvider`) then broadcasts every account-provider status and republishes every panel's account state, a panel whose session has not started included. On a daemon's core the acting client's `clientAuth` is that file: a `clientAuth` or `actingClient` change runs the same listener (`ChatPanelProvider.accountInputsChanged`), before pi starts too. A login, logout or token refresh in another Damocles app or a pi CLI lands only in that file. That keeps account state on its one publisher, called because one of its inputs changed.
- **The OpenAI API key lives in per-app secret storage, and in `auth.json` only on a host without a persistent store; the ChatGPT grant lives in `auth.json`.** pi's `auth.json` holds one credential per provider, and Sign in with ChatGPT is the `oauth` method of `openai`, so a stored key and a ChatGPT grant cannot both live there. The key is the host secret `damocles.openai.apiKey`, applied as pi's in-memory runtime key for `openai` while `openaiRuntimeKeyWanted = apiKey && (preferApiKey || !chatgpt)` holds (`openai-runtime-key.ts`); pi reads a runtime key before the stored credential, so the prefer toggle switches billing with no login. The prefer flag is global state (`readPreferOpenAIApiKey`), at the scope of the Memory judge settings, whose GPT-6 Luna choice it decides; each host moves a value an older version kept in workspace state at startup (`movePreferApiKeyToGlobalState`: the first workspace opened after the upgrade wins, and every workspace copy is cleared). VS Code reports no change another window makes to global state, so `PiRuntime.preferOpenAIApiKey()`, which every runtime and session read goes through, re-applies the runtime key and republishes when it reads a value other than the one the last sync applied. The sync only sets or removes the runtime key and never calls `logout`, which would delete the ChatGPT grant. OpenAI is not in `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_DEFS`: `openai-runtime-key.ts` (`syncOpenAIRuntimeKey`) applies its runtime key, `openai-key-migration.ts` (`migrateOpenAIApiKey`) migrates the legacy key, and `openai-auth.ts` holds the rule (`openaiRuntimeKeyWanted`) and the status reads. At `PiRuntime` init, before the `auth.json` watcher starts and before init resolves (every sign-in awaits it), a plain-text `openai` api_key an older version wrote is copied to the secret (read back before anything is deleted; a different key already in the secret wins), the `auth.json` copy is deleted with `logout('openai')` while the entry is still an api_key, and `~/.damocles/openai-api-key-moved.json` (`{ movedAt, by }`, no secret) is written atomically. A host whose secret store is not persistent (`SecretsStore.isPersistent` false, the desktop app with no OS encryption or Linux `basic_text`) moves and deletes nothing and writes no marker: moving the key there would lose it at the next restart. The key stays in auth.json, where pi uses it as the stored `openai` credential, `openaiAuthStatus` counts it as a configured key, clearing the key removes it there too (`logout('openai')`, safe because that entry is an api_key and so no ChatGPT grant), and a later start with a persistent store migrates it. Saving a key on such a host writes it to `auth.json` too (`login('openai', 'api_key')`), or the next restart would bring back the previous key; while that one `openai` slot holds a ChatGPT grant, which the write would delete, the key goes only to the session-long secret. pi has no compare-and-delete, so a sign-in by another process between that check and the delete would be removed; that window exists only at the first start after the upgrade. Each host has its own secret store, so the other host, finding its secret empty, no key in `auth.json` and a marker naming the other host, shows a one-time notice that opens the OpenAI auth panel, recorded in its own global state. `getChatGPTAccessToken()` returns a token only while ChatGPT is the active credential and pi resolves it as OAuth, because pi's `getAuth('openai')` returns the runtime key first and wham/usage must never receive the API key.
- **`logout('openai')` drops the runtime key, so every OpenAI auth change re-syncs it.** pi's `RuntimeCredentials.delete()` clears the override along with the stored credential, and its `login` keeps it. The migration, each key set or clear, the prefer toggle (here or, read later, from another window), a secret change from another VS Code window (`SecretsStore.onDidChange`), the `auth.json` watcher, and every OpenAI login or logout re-apply the rule, all on PiRuntime's OpenAI credential chain (`_openaiCredentialSync`), so each sync reads the state it applies and no sign-in in this process lands between the migration's check and its delete. The sync keeps no last-applied-key cache, which would turn the re-sync after a logout into a no-op. A pending ChatGPT sign-in holds the chain (and pi's own `openai` credential queue) until it ends, so every user OpenAI change (key save or clear, the prefer toggle, both sign-outs) aborts it first, and the auth.json and secret-change resyncs republish the account state before they wait on the chain as well as after.
- **One credential precedence rule for every provider: a stored sign-in beats the key saved in Damocles unless that provider's "Prefer API key" is on.** pi's runtime key is an in-memory overlay that beats a stored `oauth` credential (`runtime-credentials.js:17-21`), so Damocles applies the saved key only while the rule wants it: `openaiRuntimeKeyWanted` for OpenAI, `providerRuntimeKeyWanted = key && (preferApiKey || !signedIn)` for the key providers (`provider-auth.ts`), where `signedIn` means `auth.json` holds an `oauth` credential for the provider (`readProviderSignIns`, read once per sync without pi). A stored `api_key` is never a sign-in. The key-provider flag is global state `damocles.<provider>.preferApiKey` (`providerPreferApiKeyState`), the client's on a daemon's core (`CLIENT_STATE_KEYS`), which applies no key and reads each client's flag from its `clientAuth` (`ClientAuthEntry.preferApiKey`), so there `PiRuntime.preferProviderApiKey` and `preferOpenAIApiKey` start no resync. VS Code reports no change another window makes to global state, so `PiRuntime.preferProviderApiKey` re-syncs the keys when it reads a value other than the one the last sync applied; every key status read and every account and billing read (`PiRuntime.flatFeeKeyProviders`, for the providers with a sign-in) goes through it. It starts no resync while that provider signs in: the sync skips the provider then, so each resync would end with the flag still unapplied and its listeners' status reads would start the next one; the sign-in's final sync applies it. Key syncs run one at a time, since each decides from the secrets and `auth.json` it read before its awaits: a call during a run queues one follow-up, which every such call shares and resolves with, and each run keeps its own `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS` bound. The flag covers only the saved key: pi resolves a stored credential before an environment or `models.json` key (`pi-ai/dist/auth/resolve.js:24-38`), so neither ever outranks a sign-in. `syncCustomProviders` (local core only; a daemon's core registers for the broker) applies a wanted key unless pi already holds that exact key as its runtime key (the cache alone is not enough, since a logout in this process, a sign-out or the sweep, drops the runtime key), and for a key it does not want removes the runtime key it applied and unregisters a fresh-registered provider only when no key is saved. It never logs out on that path, which would delete the sign-in. Its one logout is the one-time sweep of the plain-text keys Damocles 2.6 and earlier wrote into `auth.json`, which covers only the providers 2.6 wrote keys for: StepFun, DeepSeek and OpenRouter (`legacySweep` on their key defs; 2.6 also wrote Gemini, which is gone). A stored `api_key` of any other provider is the user's own and is never swept. While `~/.damocles/provider-key-sweep.json` (`PROVIDER_KEY_SWEEP_MARKER_PATH`) is absent, each sync that reads `auth.json` logs out a stored `api_key` of each of those providers once, whether or not a key is saved (the saved key outranks it anyway), after that provider's secret was read and before its saved key is applied, since the logout also drops the runtime key. A provider whose secret read failed, or whose sign-in is running (checked again after every await), waits for a later sync. The process remembers which providers are settled (swept, or holding no `api_key` at the first look) and that it saw the marker, so it checks the marker once and a retry repeats only what is left: a failed marker write repeats only the write, never a logout. A logout pi committed before a `CredentialSynchronizationError` counts as swept. The marker is written once all three are settled; an `oauth` credential is never swept. The sweep reads `auth.json` again right before each logout and skips a provider whose entry is no longer an `api_key`, but pi has no compare-and-delete, so a `pi login` landing between that read and the logout is removed; that window exists only at the first start after the upgrade. After the marker, a stored `api_key` (a `pi login` of the user's) counts as an external key and stays. A key the sign-in outranks is neither `wired` nor `notWired`, so it raises no startup warning. Billing does not follow the credential kind: xAI, Meta and OpenRouter bill per token with every credential, a sign-in included, and Kimi Code, StepFun and GitHub Copilot bill flat with every credential (their defs' `flatFee`, which each of their catalog entries matches), so `piModelDollarBilled` bills one of their models outside the catalog, such as a template pin, flat too.
- **One sign-in flow per sign-in provider; the key sync leaves a provider alone while its sign-in runs** (`provider-account-handlers.ts`, `PiRuntime.signInProvider`). A provider in `SIGN_IN_PROVIDER_IDS` has a `signIn` def in `ACCOUNT_PROVIDER_DEFS`, and each `KEY_PROVIDER_IDS` provider a `key` def; the defs' mapped type refuses either on any other provider. The key sync and `registerForBroker` go through the defs with a key only (`KEYED_PROVIDER_DEFS`), so a sign-in-only provider (GitHub Copilot) has no key, no prefer flag and no runtime key. A second `providerSignIn` for a provider whose flow runs starts nothing; flows of different providers run side by side, and saving or clearing that provider's key, or signing out, aborts its flow first and waits for it to settle. pi serializes each provider's credential operations, so `syncCustomProviders` skips a provider in `PiRuntime._signingIn`, checked before and again after each of that provider's awaits (its sync would wait behind the sign-in until the sync's timeout and starve the providers after it); the sign-in's `finally` removes it from the set and syncs, which applies the precedence rule to the runtime key pi's login kept, then republishes the account state. A `CredentialSynchronizationError` after the login counts as signed in. Sign-out calls `logout` only while the stored credential is `oauth`, then syncs, which applies a saved key again; while `auth.json` cannot be read it fails, so the handler shows its error instead of reporting the sign-in gone. Only core opens a sign-in page, and only an `https:` URL pi named for that flow (Kimi and Meta accept `http`, so the check is Damocles'); `providerSignInOpenPage` carries no URL, and the webview opens none. Core also copies the device code it holds (`providerSignInCopyCode`, which carries only the provider, answered by `providerSignInCopyCodeAck`), because the desktop settings overlay, where the account rows render, holds no clipboard permission ("Desktop host"). pi's error text for these flows can embed an OAuth response with tokens, so the state carries a fixed localized message per failure class (cancelled, expired, denied, network, provider, the last with an HTTP status when pi's text names one) and the log gets only that class, the error's name and the status. The class comes from pi's own words only, read at the start of its text before any punctuation, so a provider body pi quotes after it never classifies: expired is pi's device-flow timeout or a provider's "device code expired" or "device authorization expired" (never OpenRouter's timeouts, which have no code, or Meta's "session expired"), denied is pi's "authorization was denied" or "login was denied", and the status is a leading one (GitHub Copilot's "404 Not Found: ...", through `httpStatusOf`) or one of pi's own "(HTTP 400)", "(status 400)" and "with status 400" forms; pi's progress and info events are logged by type only, since their text can name OpenRouter's loopback callback path; the user code and an answer are never logged, and an answer past 8 KB, or an enterprise domain that is not a hostname of at most 253 characters (blank means github.com), is refused with `invalid: true` on the flow's state, which the webview shows as the reason. A successful sign-out, key save or key clear ends any error the provider's last flow or sign-out left, and broadcasts the idle state when one was shown. Disposing the handlers (the host's shutdown) aborts every running flow. On the wire the sign-in and sign-out messages (`providerSignIn` and its `Answer`, `Cancel`, `OpenPage` and `CopyCode`, `providerSignOut`, `providerSignInState`, `providerSignInCopyCodeAck`) are feature `account.providerSignIn`. A daemon's core refuses before any pi call or secret write: a sign-in, a sign-out or a code copy (which would write the target's clipboard) with kind `remote` in the state it answers only to the view that asked and never keeps, and a key save or clear in its ack.
- **`authFailure` names the account whose credential the provider refused** (`pi-stream-adapter.ts` `showPendingFailure`, classified by `accountFailureOf` in `provider-account.ts`, which the replay reads too). `anthropic` maps to `anthropic`, `openai` and `openai-codex` to `openai`, and each account provider (`ACCOUNT_PROVIDER_IDS`, GitHub Copilot included) to its own id; another provider gets the plain error card. It fires with `reason: 'rejected'` when `isAuthError` matches: HTTP status 401, read by `httpStatusOf` (so a number in the body, such as a token count, never matches), or the words "unauthorized", "authentication", "invalid api key" or "oauth", which pi's own "OAuth auth derivation failed for <provider>" and "OAuth refresh failed for <provider>" carry; and for `xai` also on HTTP status 402 (read by `httpStatusOf`, never from the body), with `reason: 'noApiAccess'`. It fires with `reason: 'missing'` (feature `account.authFailureMissing`) when pi made no request because the provider had no credential, read only from pi's exact texts naming the call's own provider, since pi exports no code for it: `ModelRuntime.prepareRequest`'s `Provider is not configured: <provider>` and `Unknown provider: <provider>` (StepFun, which Damocles unregisters with its key), which a model call gets when the credential went during the run, and pi's `formatNoApiKeyFoundMessage`, which `AgentSession.prompt` throws when it went after Damocles' send check. That check runs before `isAuthError`, which pi's login help would match. A failed compaction is classified by the error inside pi's wrappers, matched only at the start of the text: `Compaction failed: ` (`AgentSession.compact`), `Auto-compaction failed: ` and `Context overflow recovery failed: ` (`_runAutoCompaction`), then `Summarization failed: ` or `Turn prefix summarization failed: ` (`getSummarizationFailure`); pi compacts on the session's model, so the adapter's `compaction_end` card and `PiSession.compact` name that model's account. A prompt pi refuses gets its card the same way (`failureCard`, in `PiSession.promptPi`), and so does a `/btw` aside, thrown or ended on an error stop, as `btwError` with `authFailure` (feature `chat.btwAuthFailure`), which the aside shows as the transcript's notice. A prompt pi refused before committing its user entry, read from Damocles' own record of the commit (`watchPromptEntry`, never the error text), goes back to the composer as `promptReturned` `promptRejected` with the `correlationId` of its echo, which the transcript drops, and the card's `account` when it names one, which also opens that row (feature `chat.promptRejected`); a failure after the commit leaves the message in the chat. A missing credential of a provider with no Accounts row, which a template's `provider/modelId` pin reaches, gets no card: `failureCard` gives an error card with Damocles' text naming the provider and the model (`missingCredentialText`), live and on replay. For a row's provider that text says what the row takes, from the account facts (`accountTakesKey`, `accountSignsIn`): an API key, a sign-in, or either. A subagent's or team member's transcript has no card, so every missing credential there, a row's included, shows that text in the UI language (`nestedCallError(message, 'user')`), and pi's text with its CLI login help and install paths never reaches the user. Text the model reads is never localized: a subagent's `record.error`, which reaches the parent's `Agent` result, the keep-alive delivery and the status entry, and a team member's `failure.message`, which reaches the lead's bus broadcast, `finalResponse` and the team-failed header, carry the English form of the same text (`nestedCallError(message, 'model')`, and `nestedRunError` for a run that threw, such as a prompt pi refused). A reload renders the transcript in the current UI language; the stored copies the model read stay English. The missing card says no credential is saved, and its replay has no Details, since that text is pi's, with pi's CLI login help, never the provider's. A change to that account's credential (a sign-in completes, a sign-out, a key saved or cleared, a Prefer API key toggle that applied) sends `authFailureCleared` with the account, which clears only that account's card; a reload sends it without one and clears any card. A reload replays such a failed call from the same classifier as `errorReplay` with `authFailure` (feature `history.authFailureReplay`), which the transcript shows as a past error with the live card's provider-named title and message, no settings action, and the provider's text behind Details (`AuthFailureNotice.vue`); the live card and the notice share their wording (`useAuthFailureText`).
- **The account publisher's inputs include the ChatGPT grant and the key secret.** `openaiTokenSource` orders preferred key, ChatGPT (`chatgpt-oauth`), Codex (`codex-oauth`), key, exactly as `resolvePiModel` does, and `openai` bills dollars only while the runtime key is wanted; sub-call billing (`describeSubCallModel`) reads the same prefer flag, because the provider alone no longer names the credential. Key presence is cached in `PiRuntime` at init and on every secret change, so the status stays synchronous for the settings panel; the sync records it from the secret read before it calls pi, so a pi failure cannot leave it stale, and until init's first sync has run the settings panel reads the secret itself. pi's `CredentialSynchronizationError` comes after pi committed the change, so a key sync or a ChatGPT login that throws it counts as done.
- **Catalog values and plan gating.** A catalog value names one model on one provider and is unique across `DEFAULT_MODELS`. GitHub Copilot serves pi ids other entries use (`gpt-6-luna`) or that a retired id's substring matches (`claude-opus-5.5`), so its entries are valued `github-copilot/<pi id>` with the pi id in `piModelId`. Resolution (`resolvePiModel`), billing (`piModelDollarBilled`), a recorded model's value (`modelValueOfPiModel`) and the system prompt's model line compare `piModelId ?? value`, and a pi model maps to its own provider's entry before any other (`defaultInfoForPiModel`, `cheapModelValueForProvider`, which probes pi's registry only for a value outside the catalog). `LEGACY_MODEL_MAP` is keyed by catalog value, never by pi id, so retiring `gpt-6-luna` cannot rewrite a `github-copilot/gpt-6-luna` session. A provider whose def sets `plan` (GitHub Copilot) limits its models per account plan. pi's provider filter applies only to pi's available lists (`pi-ai/dist/models.js:316,334` in pi-ai 1.4.0), while `getModel` and `AgentSession.setModel` ignore it (`models.js:121-123`, `agent-session.js:2008-2011`), and pi's snapshot is briefly unfiltered after `updateModelSnapshot` (`model-runtime.js:181-188`), so pi would adopt and call a model outside the plan. Damocles gates from the stored sign-in's own `availableModelIds` through `ModelLookup.planModelIds` (`PiRuntime.modelLookup`): a local core caches one `readProviderSignIns` read, cleared by the `auth.json` watcher and by each sign-in and sign-out; a provider's reads bypass it while that provider signs in, since pi's login writes the new list before it returns, and an unreadable `auth.json` is never cached and refuses every model of a plan provider until a read succeeds, so it never turns the gate off; and a daemon's core reads the pinned, else acting, client's `clientAuth` entry, which carries the list (feature `client.availableModelIds`). Every reader and sender takes the list through `planModelIdsOf` (`src/shared/remote-user.ts`): whole when it is at most 512 non-empty ids of at most 128 characters, else as no list, so a local and a remote core gate one credential alike and the wire never carries part of one. A model outside the list resolves `notInPlan` and not signed in, so the chat, Teams, Explore, Background, the memory judge, a template's direct `provider/modelId` pin and a resumed subagent's recorded model (`FolderRuntime.assertResumableModel`) all refuse it (`modelInPlan`), and the model list `PiSession.publishAvailableModels()` posts on every account republish marks it `unavailable: { reason: 'not-in-plan' }`. A later list can drop a model the chat already runs, so the chat checks its plan again at each send and compaction, and an agent inheriting the chat's model at each spawn. A sign-in with no list gates nothing. Damocles never chooses a Copilot model on its own: its def sets `pickedOnly`, so `resolveInitialModel`'s fallback walk and Automatic (Background and the memory judge) skip it, and its `cheapModel`, Copilot's Haiku 5.5, is the Explore default only for a chat already on Copilot and only while the plan includes it. A chat always runs the model it shows. The model a chat starts on is the configured one (the panel's, else `damocles.model`), whether or not it can run; only the factory default, with `damocles.model` unset in every scope and no model the panel holds (`SessionOptions.model` absent; a panel holds only a model its session committed, never a factory default it only showed), falls back to its substitutes and then the first signed-in catalog model the walk may use (`resolveInitialModel`). A configured or recorded model that cannot run stays the chat's model. pi is still given a model explicitly, the one the chat shows, or the fallback model when pi's registry lacks it (`shownPiModel`), because pi given none seats the session on one it picks itself (`findInitialModel`, which prefers Copilot's GPT-6.1 Sol). Every send and compaction, on every send path, checks the shown model against today's credentials and plan (`PiSession.modelRefusal`, which never trusts the model it adopted earlier, so a key cleared since then refuses too, and which refuses every call while no model runtime exists, as once the runtime retired at shutdown; a key cleared between this check and pi's own throws pi's "No API key found", whose prompt goes back as `promptRejected`, see "`authFailure` names the account"). So does every message Damocles hands pi that would open a run of its own, which is any it hands pi while no run streams: a re-steered batch of queued messages, a re-queued follow-up and a cancel note, each after the shown model was adopted when it can run, as for a send (`whenShownModelRuns`, `runOpeningRefusal`). A refused batch or follow-up goes back to the composer as a refused send does, and a refused note is dropped with a notice; one pi throws on before committing it shows its card and goes back as `promptRejected`, as a send does. A refusal goes back to the composer as `promptReturned` `modelSignInRequired` when no catalog model can run, else as `modelUnavailable` with the reason in `detail`, and opens settings where it is fixed (`openSettingsPanel`): the provider's row in Models & accounts (`account`) when a sign-in to it would let the model run; Models & accounts when no catalog model can run and no provider's sign-in is named (a model pi does not serve, or no model runtime); else This chat, where another model is picked (outside the plan, no model pi serves, or the factory default, which the user never chose, while another catalog model can run, a GitHub Copilot one included). `promptReturned` carries that `account` too (feature `chat.modelRefusalAccount`), and the toast's title then tells the user to sign in to that provider; a refusal without one asks for a pick in This chat, or for `modelSignInRequired` a sign-in in Models & accounts. Every refusal of a send names the echo the transcript may hold (`correlationId`), so the echo the plan's fresh start shows from `sessionCleared.pendingMessage` is taken out too, and that send's Stop returns it as `interruptRecovery`. A team role or an agent that would inherit the chat's model checks it against today's credentials too and fails naming why: a signed-out model names the provider to sign in to. A shown model that becomes runnable (a sign-in since the start, another credential of the same vendor) is adopted at the next send or compaction, as a pick of it is (`adoptShownModelIfRunnable`). A pick still being applied goes first and the adoption reads the model it left, so the latest pick wins, and a Stop during a compaction's adoption stops the compaction (`compactionAborted`). `/btw` refuses a model that cannot run with a send's text, and with its account as `btwError.authFailure`.

## Memory store

- **The integrity check and every salvage run on a worker, never on the thread that opens memory** (`memory/integrity.ts`, `memory/integrity-worker.ts`). `openAndMigrate` (`memory/database.ts`) only opens the file, sets pragmas and migrates. A corruption error from the open or a migration leaves the file in place: init writes `<db>.needs-repair`, the worker repairs it as below, and the open runs again. `MemoryService` then queues the check as the write queue's first item (`hold`). Writes wait for it up to `MEMORY_WRITE_HOLD_MS` (5 s; the check takes about 0.2 s on a large store, docs/perf.md), so a quick check keeps every write off an unchecked full-text index. A slower check keeps running while writes go ahead, and its verdict still applies: a late `corrupt` makes the run read only from then on, and a late `fts` queues the rebuild, which holds writes because it takes the write lock. Reads never wait. The check runs on every start, not behind an unclean-shutdown marker, because the store is shared and this app's clean exit says nothing about another app or the disk. A store whose schema is newer than this build is never checked, rebuilt or repaired.
- **What the check finds decides the repair.** The worker opens the file read only and runs `PRAGMA integrity_check`, which also runs FTS5's own check (SQLite 3.44 and later; `node:sqlite` has shipped 3.46 or later since it was added, and Damocles' Electron 44 and VS Code 1.142 ship 3.53.4). Neither that nor FTS5's `'integrity-check'` without rank 1 compares the external-content index with `memories`, so a logical desync is not detected. The findings are `fts` only when the `integrity_check(TABLE)` of `memories_fts` and its shadow tables reports every one of them. A table check skips the freelist and pages no table uses, so damage there, which can hand out a page still in use, is `corrupt` like damage in any other table. On `fts` the worker rebuilds the index (in place, else drop, recreate and rebuild in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, so a stop never leaves the store without the table the write triggers update) and checks again; a finding the rebuild leaves is `corrupt`. `corrupt` makes the process read only for its run (`MemoryReadOnlyError` with reason `damaged`), writes the marker (never over an existing one) and shows one notice. A worker error or no answer in 60 s is logged and lets writes through, since open and migrate succeeded.
- **A repair runs before the open, on the worker, and survives a stop at any step.** With the marker present, init awaits `repair`, which holds `<db>.repair-lock` (an SQLite exclusive lock the OS drops with its process), so apps starting together repair once; one that waited and finds the marker gone answers `intact`. The repair checks again, rebuilds an `fts` finding, or quarantines and salvages. Before it moves the store aside it replaces the marker with `{"salvageFrom": "<db>.corrupt-<ms>"}`. It then moves the main file, then its `-wal` and `-shm`, copies every readable durable row into `<db>.salvage`, folds that store's WAL into its file and moves it to the path. A repair that finds the marker's quarantined file resumes from it: it moves sidecars the stop left at the path (a new store there would replay the old WAL), rebuilds from scratch when the path is empty, and copies into the store at the path otherwise (`INSERT OR IGNORE`, so a repeat changes nothing). The path therefore never holds a partial salvage, and an empty or missing store is never read as intact while a salvage is pending. A run whose repair did not finish stays read only and imports no legacy v2 file. Only `intact` and `salvaged` remove the marker, under the lock. The move fails on Windows while another Damocles app holds the file open (`failed`); the marker stays and a later start repairs it. The host stops a check or rebuild at once on dispose, and lets a repair finish for `REPAIR_DISPOSE_GRACE_MS` (2 s, inside desktop's 3 s core shutdown and VS Code's 5 s deactivate) before it stops the worker; a stopped repair resumes at the next start.
- **A write is refused once the store's path names another file than the one this process opened.** On macOS and Linux another app's quarantine can move the file while this one holds it, and its writes would land in the moved file, which the salvage has already read. Before each queued item `MemoryWriteQueue` compares `stat` of the opened file's path (`PRAGMA database_list`) with the device and inode recorded at open; on a mismatch the run goes read only (`MemoryReadOnlyError` with reason `moved`) with one notice to restart. A write that passed the comparison just before the move can still land in the moved file.
- **Every write to the memory database goes through `MemoryWriteQueue`,** the observation path normalization at init included. The v2 import and the worker's salvage write outside it: into a store they just created, or, for a resumed salvage, into the store at the path under SQLite's own locking, before this process opens it.

## Memory injection

- **Injection is a delta against the session projection** (`pi-session/agent-start.ts`, `memory/managers/injection-manager.ts`). Each `before_agent_start` dispatch reads `ctx.sessionManager.buildSessionProjection().messages` once, folds it with `readLiveInjections` (`pi-session/live-injections.ts`), and sends only memories not already live, plus notices for live ones that were superseded, edited or forgotten. The projection is what the model will receive, except that the model-switch fold (below) merges system messages into the leading one for the request; it never touches an injection message. So rewind, fork, compaction and context edits are handled for free: a message compacted away or omitted by an edit reads as absent and its memories become eligible again. When nothing is new and the Compass change key is unchanged, no custom message is sent at all.
- **In-context ids come only from `details`, never from the message text** (`memory/injection/details.ts`). pi stores a custom message's `details` and never sends them to the model. Every injection message carries a `ContextInjectionDetailsV1` listing each memory it rendered (id, content hash, tier), its notices, whether it carried the profile, and the Compass key it sent. Parsing ids out of the text would make a memory that quotes another memory's id look injected. A message without valid details (a session recorded before this existed) is ignored, so its memories are re-injected once.
- **Nothing about injection is held on the extension instance.** The pi extension is process-global and outlives any one session, so there is no first-message set, no per-session cache and no rename bookkeeping. Profile presence (`profileInContext`) and the live Compass key also come from the projection: the profile returns after compaction, a fork already carries it, and a restart re-reads the projection from disk and re-injects nothing.
- **The Injected Context record is written after an await, so the build re-checks liveness first** (`pi-session/agent-start.ts`). A `before_agent_start` still in its memory build is not awaited by the abort a session delete runs, so the delete can finish first. The handler therefore asks the extension's panel registry, which every holder of a deleted session leaves before the delete, whether a panel still holds the session, and posts and persists nothing when none does; persisting would recreate the deleted session's injection database.
- **No `context` handler, and past injection messages are never rewritten.** History stays byte-stable so the prompt-cache prefix survives; `damocles-extension.test.ts` pins that no outbound context handler is registered. The model-switch fold (`context_with_system`, below) is the one request-time rewrite: it changes system messages only, and moves the prefix only at a model switch.
- **The system prompt's preamble is invariant, and each runtime input owns its own section** (`pi-session/agent-start.ts` `assembleDamoclesSystemPrompt`, `pi-session/system-prompt.ts` `SYSTEM_PROMPT_PREAMBLE`). Before each prompt pi diffs the sections the `before_agent_start` handler wrote against the transcript's current ones and appends every changed section, in full, as a mid-conversation system message placed before the prompt (`pi-coding-agent/dist/core/agent-session.js:1323-1328`, `:1525-1529`, `system-prompt.js:140`). pi-ai renders it as `Updated system prompt section "<name>"` (`pi-ai/dist/utils/text.js:24`). For a model whose compat sets `supportsMidConvoSystemMessages` (Sonnet, Opus and Haiku 5.5, Opus 4.8 and 5, Fable 5 and 5.1), Anthropic receives it as a `role: "system"` message after the user's message, directly before the next assistant message: pi-ai holds every later system message back to that point (`pi-ai/dist/api/anthropic-messages.js:1047-1056`, flushed at `:1121` and `:1215`). That is the only position Anthropic accepts: a system message with content must directly follow a user turn (one carrying `tool_result` blocks counts) or an assistant turn ending in a server tool result, and directly precede an assistant turn or end `messages`, so one placed before a user message is a 400 (Anthropic's "Mid-conversation system messages and tool changes" page, "How it works" and "Limitations"). OpenAI Responses and Chat Completions send it in place, as a developer or system message before the user's message (`openai-responses-shared.js:194`, `openai-completions.js:930`). Damocles' sections change only at a prompt, never between the steps of a run, because pi's step refresh rebuilds the prompt from that run's `before_agent_start` options (`agent-session.js:534-565`); so a Damocles patch never lands right after tool results, where Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5 can take system text for an injection. A model with no such support, and every Google and Bedrock model, gets every system message collapsed into the leading prompt (`utils/transcript.js` `resolveTranscript`, `collapseSystemMessages`), which moves the cached prefix at every change. A runtime input in the preamble (`customPrompt`, section `preamble`) therefore re-sends the whole Damocles prompt whenever it changes, and a model can read that as injected text. So the preamble is one constant and every input-dependent piece is its own section: `damocles_model` (model name and knowledge cutoff, from the model's `DEFAULT_MODELS` entry, then `MODEL_SWITCH_NOTE`; only a retired id outside the catalog reads `system-prompt.ts`'s tables, and an unknown cutoff states none), `damocles_environment` (cwd, git, platform, shell, OS), `damocles_output_form` (thinking off), `compass` or `damocles_explore` (Compass on or off; the key `compass` renders the `<compass>` tag `buildAgentPrompt` looks for in an inherited parent prompt), `damocles_web_verification` (web search), `damocles_team` (teams), next to `damocles_memory` and the plan sections. A model or thinking switch then patches only `damocles_model` and `damocles_output_form`. Environment, model and output form stay at the end, before `damocles_tone`, which is last in the prompt `before_agent_start` builds. A leading prompt replayed from patches (the model-switch fold, or pi's compaction head) puts a section added mid-chat after the sections it already holds, so plan mode or thinking turned off mid-chat comes after `damocles_tone`; the replay gives that order on every request, so the cache holds. `system-prompt-sections.test.ts` pins one section per input and a single preamble across every input combination, and `system-prompt-switch.test.ts` pins pi's recorded patch after a switch.
- **A model switch reaches the new model in its leading system prompt** (`pi-session/model-switch-prompt.ts`, registered only by the main chat's factory in `damocles-extension.ts`; subagents, team members and `/btw` never register it). Its `context_with_system` handler folds every system message up to the latest one that sets `damocles_model` to a text other than the one it last held into the leading one with pi-ai's own replay (`getCurrentSystemMessage`, the fold pi's compaction applies at its boundary), for the request only; the transcript keeps pi's patches. A removal of the section (`null`, which pi writes when a run's `before_agent_start` produced no Damocles prompt, because the panel was gone or the build threw) is no switch, nor is its return with the text it held, so neither moves the prefix. The model section must change only with the chat's model, because any other change to its text re-folds the whole prompt: an update that changes that text (the move from 3.7.0's preamble, an edit to `MODEL_SWITCH_NOTE`, a catalog name or cutoff edit) re-folds once per chat, on its first message after the update. A switch starts a new prompt cache anyway, so the new model reads its identity and every section as the leading prompt rather than as a patch after the user's message, which Sonnet 5.5 was seen to take for untrusted user text when the patch named a new identity. The fold drops the record of the switch, so the model section says that earlier replies may have come from another model (`MODEL_SWITCH_NOTE`, `system-prompt.ts`); without it a model reading an earlier reply's identity calls that reply wrong. The fold point moves only at the next switch, so every later request on that model sends the same leading prompt and keeps the cached prefix, and a section that changes under an unchanged model (plan mode, the environment, thinking) still arrives as pi's patch, a system message pi-ai places after the user's message, the only position Anthropic accepts (entry above). Damocles never moves that patch in the request, and never tells the model to trust text in the user's turn, which a tool result or a page could imitate. A return to a model used earlier re-folds too. When every system message after the leading message that model last received changed only `damocles_model`, the re-folded leading message is byte-identical to that one, so the prefix that model cached can still be read while its cache lives; any other change makes a new prefix. An append-mode agent inherits the chat's prompt without `damocles_model` (`subagents/prompts.ts` `inheritedParentPrompt`), since that section names the chat's model and says it can be switched. `model-switch-prompt.test.ts` pins the fold, the switch back, folded tool changes, the removal and the fallback to pi's transcript when the fold throws; `system-prompt-switch.test.ts` pins them through real pi, with the agent's inherited prompt; `damocles-extension.test.ts` and `handler-disposal.test.ts` pin the registration.
- **Stored text cannot open or close any tag the injection message carries** (`memory/injection/render.ts`). Every tag name in that message (the renderer's `damocles_memory`, `memory`, `observation`, `title`, `facts`, `compact`, `memory_updates`, the profile's `user_profile`, `project`, `global`, `static`, `dynamic`, and the Compass status's `damocles_compass`) gets a zero-width joiner after `<` wherever its opening or closing form appears in memory content, titles, facts, notices or a profile section, so a stored memory cannot close its block or forge another, and model-written profile text cannot fake a section. A notice is always one line, so stored text cannot forge a notice line. The pattern is a fixed-string alternation anchored on `<`, which keeps it linear-time. `tagNeutralizer(names)` is the one neutralizer: `neutralizeTags` is its memory instance, and chat attachments use it over their wrapper tags (`damocles_terminal_output`, `damocles_commit`, `mcp_resource`) plus every memory tag. Attribute values, the Compass `error` included, go through `escapeAttribute`: `&`, `"` and `<` are escaped and line breaks flattened.
- **The gate searches only what the user typed** (`pi-session/agent-start.ts`). The opened-file or selection block Damocles prepends to a message is model-only context: `splitIdeContext` (`src/shared/ide-context.ts`, which also holds the `formatIdeContextBlock` the producer uses) takes it off the prompt before `queryTerms`, and its path is the file gate's only editor file: a message sent without the block gives the gate none, never the live editor, which the user chose not to attach and may have changed since Send. Its boilerplate words would otherwise match memories as if the user had written them. Anything else that reads the block back must use `splitIdeContext`, never its own copy of the format.
- **Lexical matching reads only a memory's own text** (`memory/managers/injection-manager.ts`). Per-term match sets and the df/N statistics use the FTS column filter `{content title facts}`; `search_terms`, `tags` and `summary` are model-generated and would admit a memory on a synonym it never states. The file gate's candidate query uses `{content files_read files_modified}`, the fields its proximity matcher confirms against. `search_memories` still searches every column.
- **Typed paths feed the file gate, not the query words** (`memory/injection/gate.ts`, `splitTypedPaths`). A path's parent directories locate a subject rather than name one, and a user or folder name in them (`C:\Users\<name>\…`, `C:\GameDev\…`) would otherwise admit memories on its own. A token with a separator and an extension is a file path: it goes to `query.files` and adds no term. A directory path (rooted, holding a backslash, or of 3+ segments) keeps only its last segment; two-segment slash words such as `app/Http` or `and/or` stay words. A bare file name such as `OrganizationScope.php` keeps its name as a term and drops the extension, which names a format, not a subject.
- **Term specificity comes from the store and from general vocabulary** (`memory/injection/gate.ts`). Two generated references, regenerated only by `npm run generate:reference-words` with a SHA-256 check on every download and credited in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`: `english-reference.generated.ts`, the 49,104 single-token words of david47k's top-english-wordlists 50k list from Google Books Ngram (CC BY 3.0), ranked; and `software-reference.generated.ts`, 3,682 more words from cspell-dicts' software-terms and filetypes dictionaries (MIT), unranked and ranked after every English word. Book English alone missed software words (`repo`, `json`, `php`) and chat words (`hmm`, `yep`), which then admitted memories on their own. Lookup is by Porter stem (`memory/porter-stem.ts`, a port of FTS5's `porter` tokenizer that `porter-stem.test.ts` checks against SQLite). The generator stems both lists into `reference-stems.generated.ts`, which is the only reference the gate imports, so the first prompt builds a map instead of stemming 52k words; `porter-stem.test.ts` fails when the stems and the stemmer disagree. A term takes the rank of the most frequent reference word sharing its stem, because its FTS match is a match on that stem, so `summarization` ranks with `summarize`. Query terms sharing a stem run the same match and count once. A term is common, and dropped, when its store df exceeds `max(20, 0.05·N)` or its rank is within `coreVocabularyRank` (1,000). An `[a-z]+` term whose stem is unlisted is distinctive. There is no separate df cap on distinctive terms: after the vocabulary fix the highest-df distinctive terms in the replay stores were names whose matches were on topic (`oauth`, `iemis`, `toolsearch`, `scratchpad`, `baroulkos`), and store frequency alone cannot tell a name from a general word (`baroulkos` df 6 of 485 against `php` df 8). A term in any other script, and an identifier (3+ characters, starting with a letter and holding a digit, such as `oauth2` or `es2022`), is distinctive when its store df is at most `max(3, ceil(0.005·N))`, the in-store rule. When a prompt has at least 3 letter-only terms, in any script, and most of them miss the reference, the prompt is not English and its unlisted `[a-z]+` terms must also pass the in-store rule. Any other term with a digit (a number, a digit-led piece such as `26t18`, a two-character mix such as `s3`) is never distinctive and never counts toward coverage; two characters are too few to name a subject. When the query has a distinctive term, a memory passes only by matching one (relevance ≥ 0.1), in the manner of a common-terms query: general words would otherwise admit unrelated memories. A query with none is a coverage query, as a common-terms query with only high-frequency terms runs as a conjunction: a memory must match every topical term when the prompt has at most `coverageTerms` (3) of them and that many otherwise, carry at least half of the query's idf, and match at least 2, so a lone general word never admits. On the replay corpora two general words together admitted an unrelated memory far more often than a related one. English contractions are removed before tokenizing, because `doesn't` splits into the non-word `doesn`.
- **Only a strong lexical match renders full** (`memory/injection/gate.ts`, `LexicalMatch.fullAllowed`). A gated memory renders full only when it matched a distinctive term at relevance ≥ 0.4 (`GATE.fullMinRelevance`); a coverage match, one admitted only by the file gate, and one graded `low` by the rerank render compact at most. `assignTiers` treats both entry limits as maxima: over the token budget it demotes the lowest full entries to compact, dropping the lowest compact entry whenever a demotion would exceed the compact limit, then drops compact entries from the bottom.
- **A notice names the user only for a user forget** (`memory/injection/render.ts`, `memory/managers/injection-manager.ts`). A live memory forgotten with `user_forget` or `quality_audit` gets "was forgotten by the user"; decay, a purged row or any other loss gets "was retired from memory", because telling the model the user rejected a memory that merely aged out is false. A dedup merge records a `SUPERSEDES` edge from the primary to each merged row (`memory/dedup-decay.ts`), and the injection follows those edges, so a live merged memory yields a superseded notice naming its primary and a pasted merged id resolves to the primary. A merge with no edge cannot be resolved and stays unannounced.
- **The gate constants are calibrated, not guessed** (`memory/injection/gate.ts`, pinned by `injection-gate.test.ts`). Retune them only with the replay harness (`memory/__tests__/injection-replay.test.ts`, opt-in through `DAMOCLES_MEMORY_REPLAY`), which copies the real store and replays recorded sessions; it must keep at least a 70% byte reduction against what the old engine recorded and a p95 build time under 50 ms.

## Memory judges

- **One resolver picks the judge** (`PiRuntime.resolveMemoryJudge`). `hasClassifier()`, `runClassification`, `describeMemoryJudge()` and the `memory-merge`/`memory-rerank` branch of `subCallModelFor` (with `hasAuthedSubCallModel` for those purposes) all read it with the same breaker predicate, so the status line names what the next merge or rerank runs on, a probe included: a provider due for its probe is named as the judge, and the fallback while the probe is in flight. One resolution reads the OpenAI auth state (`auth.json`, read synchronously with sleeping retries) and the prefer flag once and passes them to every credential check. `damocles.memory.judge` and `.judgeEffort` are application scope, read with no folder.
