Automatic (`''`) is the first classifier of `CLASSIFIER_MODELS` (`classifier-model.ts`: Jev on TypeSafe, `typesafe/jev-latest`; Jev on OpenRouter, `openrouter/~typesafe/jev-latest`; GPT-6 Luna, `openai/gpt-6-luna` on pi-ai's `openai-decisions`) whose credential serves it, whose breaker admits a request and whose model is in pi's catalog, else the first of `AUTOMATIC_BACKGROUND_MODELS` at the `SUBCALL_THINKING` levels; a question the classifier leaves undecided or fails goes to that same small model. A chosen classifier (`MEMORY_JUDGE_CLASSIFIERS`, `shared/memory-judge.ts`) must have its credential and a closed breaker, and a chosen model (any `DEFAULT_MODELS` value) must resolve signed in, with `.judgeEffort` replacing the table level; otherwise the judge is `none` with the reason (`MemoryJudge.forced`; a stored value that is neither Automatic, a classifier choice nor a catalog model, such as a hand edit or a retired id, is `unrecognized`, never `signed-out`), and it never falls back: the merge and rerank model is none too, so a merge defers exactly as with no judge and a rerank keeps lexical order. A chosen classifier hands nothing to a model either. A classifier choice's value must differ from every catalog model id, which shares the enum (`gpt-6-luna` is the chat model, so Luna's classifier choice is `gpt-6-luna-classifier`). The Background model never chooses the judge, and the judge never chooses a writing job's model.
- **GPT-6 Luna judges only on an API key** (`openaiRequestCredentialIsKey`, `openai-auth.ts`; `classifierCredential`, `classifier-model.ts`). The Decisions API rejects a Sign in with ChatGPT token, and pi's `hasConfiguredAuth` and `getModelOfType` do not tell a key from a grant, so `openai` serves a classifier only while it is configured and either no ChatGPT grant exists or `openaiRuntimeKeyWanted` holds; the `rejected` list follows the same rule. A Codex grant is `openai-codex` and never counts. The settings send each provider's credential state (`classifierCredentials` on `memoryJudgeStatus`), and the Memory judge dropdown shows a classifier without one as unavailable with the reason.
- **The judge status reads the credentials.** The TypeSafe settings panel names the active judge from `PiRuntime.describeMemoryJudge()`. That reading is used only once `memoryJudgeKnown` holds (a folder runtime exists and a custom-provider sync has applied the stored keys); before that, `memoryJudgeStatus` (`settings-manager/managers/memory-judge-status.ts`) reads the TypeSafe and OpenRouter secrets (on a daemon's core the client's `keySaved`), a stored OpenRouter sign-in in `auth.json` (counted as `providerAuthStatus` counts it), the OpenAI key secret with `auth.json` and the prefer flag, pi's `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` / `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` / `OPENAI_API_KEY` (`CLASSIFIER_ENV_KEYS`) and the judge setting, and with no usable classifier reports `unknown`, never `none`, because the judge model is not resolved yet; a chosen classifier without its credential, and an `unrecognized` choice, is `none` either way. The Memory judge row shows that status line too whenever the judge is `none` with a reason, and the Background, Explore and judge model choices whose provider has no credential (the account rows' state) are listed disabled as not signed in. The OpenRouter key has its own settings section; its secret keeps its stored name, `damocles.explore.apiKey.openrouter`, as the StepFun key keeps `damocles.explore.apiKey.stepfun` (`explore-providers.ts`); Explore reads neither.
- **A refused credential opens that provider's breaker, never a per-call retry** (`pi-session/classifier-breaker.ts`). A 401, 402 or 403 describes the key or its account, not one request, so `runClassification` opens the provider's breaker and Automatic skips it: TypeSafe falls over to OpenRouter, then GPT-6 Luna, then the small model. A credential change reported by `syncCustomProviders` (`changed`) resets the breaker at once, and the runtime re-runs that sync whenever any `KEYED_PROVIDER_DEFS` secret changes, which includes a change made in another window. Saving any provider's key resets its breaker even when the value is unchanged (`storeProviderKey` calls `resetClassifierBreaker`; a provider with no open breaker is unaffected): the re-save is the user saying the refusal is resolved. The `openai` breaker resets on every user change of the OpenAI request credential (`_openaiCredentialChanged`): a key set or cleared, the prefer toggle (`applyOpenAIPreferApiKey`, even when its sync fails), a ChatGPT sign-in or sign-out, and a key secret or prefer flag change from another window; never on an `auth.json` change alone, which a token refresh also makes. A change from another window resets only after its runtime key sync has settled: a request claimed before then can still carry the old key, and the reset makes its outcome stale. A 402 can also clear with the key unchanged (credit added), so after `CLASSIFIER_PROBE_AFTER_MS` (5 min, on the monotonic `performance.now()` clock, so a wall-clock step cannot lengthen it) the next request is the one probe; the pick and the claim happen in the same tick, so concurrent judges never send a second one. Outcomes carry a generation ticket, so a request sent with the old key cannot reopen a reset breaker, and the ticket records the breaker it probes at claim time: a request claimed before the breaker opened can neither clear it nor end its probe, so parallel batches settling in any order leave one refusal standing and one probe in flight. 429, 5xx, timeouts and malformed answers never open it; each call falls back on its own. An OpenRouter 403 whose `metadata` (under `error`, or at the body's top level) carries moderation `reasons`, guardrail `patterns`, or an `error_type` of `content_policy_violation` or `refusal` declined that input (OpenRouter's documented error shapes), not the key, so it never opens the breaker (`isInputRefusal`); the body is parsed in memory and never kept. A body pi truncated past 4,000 characters no longer parses and is counted as a credential refusal, which only sends the judges to their fallback. `describeMemoryJudge()` reports as `rejected` every configured provider whose refusal stands, except the one the judge routes its probe to, until an answer or a reset clears it. `PiRuntime.onMemoryJudgeChange` is static, backed by a module-level listener set, so `ChatPanelProvider` subscribes at activation without creating the runtime (every `PiRuntime.exists` guard depends on that). It fires on a breaker change (a refusal appearing, changing or clearing, a cooldown ending, which a timer reports, and a probe starting or failing), a published folder, the end of a custom-provider sync, an account republish (`republishAccountInfo` and the runtime's own), and an `auth.json` change before the runtime holds the watch; `ChatPanelProvider` also rebroadcasts on a TypeSafe, OpenRouter or OpenAI key secret change and a `damocles.memory.judge` or `.judgeEffort` change (the one listener for them, on `damocles.memory`, because `judgeEffort` is not under `judge`), the pre-start inputs, and broadcasts only the newest of overlapping reads, so an open panel never shows a stale judge. The log names only the HTTP status (`classifierFailureCause`) and the reason the breaker holds after settling; pi's error text carries the response body, which can echo memory text.
- **`runClassification` is the only classifier seam** (`pi-session/pi-runtime.ts`). It never throws; it returns `null` when no classifier is configured, the request did not stop with `stop`, or any question lacks an answer of its type. Memories and prompts go only in `state`; the questions' instructions and criteria are constants in `memory/classifier-judges.ts`, and rerank questions name candidates by positional keys (`c0`…), never by stored text or ids. It logs the stop reason and `classifierFailureCause` (HTTP status, timeout, malformed answers or no response), never the error text, because a provider error message can echo the request body.
- **The contradiction judge keeps its three values.** The classifier's probability that the facts cannot both be true now decides alone at or above `CONTRADICTION_THRESHOLDS.high` (0.85) and at or below `.low` (0.15). Between them, and when the classifier fails, the LLM judge decides; with no judge model (none on Automatic, or a chosen classifier) that is `null`, which defers the row to the conflict sweep exactly like an outage. Never map the middle band to `false`.
- **Reranks stay inside their caps** (`injection-manager.ts` about 2 s, `retrieval-manager.ts` 8 s). The classifier runs first with the whole cap; on failure the LLM rerank gets only the time left (a chosen classifier has none, so lexical order stands), and with less than `LLM_RERANK_MIN_MS` (300 ms) left it is not started, since it could not finish but could still be billed, and the lexical (BM25) order stands. One classifier request carries at most `RERANK_BATCH_SIZE` (10) score questions; batches run in parallel and the first failed batch fails the classifier rerank as a whole at once, aborting the others through one `AbortController` passed as `abortSignal`, so a doomed rerank never spends the LLM's time.
- **Score semantics.** A score is the probability-weighted index into `RERANK_CRITERIA` (`unrelated`, `partially relevant`, `directly relevant`), so it can fall between levels; it rounds to the nearest level (low, medium, high). The injection records it normalized to 0..1 as `rerankClassifierScore`, and the Injected Context card renders the localized verdict from it, so a persisted record carries no language. A search result's `reason` is model-facing and stays English (`Classifier: <verdict> (<score>)`); the result also carries `rerankClassifierScore`, from which the Memory panel renders the localized verdict.
- **The OpenRouter catalog is the key's, refreshed in the background** (`PiRuntime._refreshOpenRouterCatalog`). pi wraps `openrouter` in `withPersonalizedCatalog` (`remote-catalog-provider.js` in pi-coding-agent 1.4.0): a networked refresh downloads pi.dev's OpenRouter catalog and asks OpenRouter's `/models/user` with the key, drops the chat models the key cannot use, takes the key's context size, output limit and prices, keeps image and classifier models as they are (so image generation and the judge never lose a model), returns the catalog unchanged on 401, 403 or 404, and saves the result to `models-store.json`, which every offline refresh restores. Damocles runs that refresh only when `syncCustomProviders` reports the OpenRouter key both changed and wired (the first sync after start, or a new key), for `openrouter` alone. It passes no `allowNetwork`, so pi's own `PI_OFFLINE` switch decides (`ModelRuntime.refresh` falls back to the `modelNetworkEnabled` it read at creation); while pi is offline it is not started and the log says so, Damocles reading that switch from the runtime (`piModelNetworkEnabled`), never from `process.env`. It is never awaited, so no chat start waits on the network. pi aborts a provider's running refresh, reporting no error and no abort, whenever any refresh of it starts: every `registerProvider` and `unregisterProvider` starts one over all providers, and a credential change of the provider starts one of it. So each attempt waits for `_providerSync` to be idle without holding it (folder creation waits on that queue), and an attempt that ended without an error or an abort but left no `checkedAt` at or after its start in `models-store.json` (`savedCatalogCheckedAt`; pi writes it only when a networked refresh publishes, before the refresh resolves) was superseded. It is logged and retried once, and a second supersede is logged and leaves the saved or shipped list until the next applied key. A catalog another window saved in that time also counts; this process loads it at pi's next offline refresh. A new key, a removed key (both before the credential changes, through `beforeCredentialChange`) and `dispose` abort it. One 30 s clock bounds each attempt, the wait for the queue included, since a plugin install on that queue has no bound of its own; running out logs whether it was waiting or refreshing. Failures log the error name and the HTTP status of pi's `... request failed[ for <provider>]: <status>` form only, never the message, which can quote the response. Every other refresh stays `allowNetwork: false`.
- **Thresholds and batching are calibrated, not guessed.** Retune them only with `memory/__tests__/classifier-eval.test.ts` (opt-in through `DAMOCLES_JEV_EVAL=1` and a TypeSafe or OpenRouter key in the environment), which copies the real store; `classifier-judges.test.ts` pins the chosen values. They were fitted on Jev and apply to GPT-6 Luna unchanged. TypeSafe documents no per-request question cap, only a token budget (64k per request, 32k for the state plus the longest question; pi lists 32k for OpenRouter's Jev).

## Memory consolidation

- **A turn the model keeps declining is set aside, never re-sent forever, and a failure that hits every call sets nothing aside** (`extractInHalves`, `memory/consolidation.ts`). An extraction call is declined when it is `unanswered`, `rejected` or answers with an invalid shape. The decision rule:
  - A declined part holding more than one turn is retried as two halves in the same pass, both halves of a split before either is split again, within `MAX_EXTRACT_CALLS_PER_PASS` calls; turns the cap leaves untried are released uncounted.
  - Once both halves of a split decline and no call of the pass was answered, the failure may be systemic (a model that never calls the tool, a request every turn gets refused), so one queued turn picked at random is sent alone as a probe. If the probe answers, splitting goes on; if it declines, the pass stops and releases the rest uncounted. The pick is random so a run of declining turns cannot stall the queue by always straddling the same split.
  - Only a turn declined on its own call is counted, once per run, at the end of the pass, and only when the model answered another call that pass or the turn already holds a count: the model's answer to other input is the only evidence the failure is the turn's. A run is one manual Run now with the follow-up passes it queues for the remaining turns, or one automatic (idle, start or switch) run. The driver (`MemoryService.runConsolidation`) hands every pass of a run the same `declinedInRun` set; the claim skips the turns in it and the pass adds the turns it declined alone, so a follow-up pass never re-sends a turn its run declined, while turns the call cap released untried still flow, and the follow-up is queued only while a turn outside the set is pending. Merged pending requests unite their sets. A turn that reaches `MAX_FAILED_ANSWERS` (3, `shared/consolidation.ts`) is set aside (`set_aside_at`, released with `consumed = 0`) instead of released. So one declining turn among healthy turns is isolated in its first run and set aside after three separate runs, even alone in the queue, while a failure that hits every call never counts a fresh turn, and a turn alone in the queue with no count waits, uncounted, for a pass that also answers another turn.
  - `unreachable` (a refused credential or access included), `no-model` and a thrown call release every unsettled turn uncounted and stop the splitting, so an outage, a refused sign-in, key or access, or a signed-out model never sets a turn aside.
  - A pass that answered nothing ends `failed`, even when it set a turn aside, so the idle timer backs off and a manual Run does not re-queue itself.
  The claim, the queue preview and the pending count skip set-aside rows (`idx_candidates_claimable`, migration v6), and the set-aside count reads `idx_candidates_set_aside` (migration v7). The extract phase reports progress after each call as turns settled out of turns claimed (`meta.done`, `meta.total`), and its failure reason is a token (`unanswered`, `rejected`, `invalid-shape`, `unreachable`, `credential`, `forbidden`, `no-model`) the stepper localizes. The memory runner turns an `unreachable` result with cause `credential` or `forbidden` into the failure of that name, which reaches the card as its reason. For `credential` the card says the model's sign-in or key was refused and offers Sign in, as it does for `no-model`; for `forbidden` it says the provider refused access to the model and offers only Retry: a 403 refuses access whatever the credential, so Sign in would not help, and the remedy (another Background model, plan or region) lies outside the card.
- **Retry is the only way back** (`retrySetAsideTurns`, routed like the other memory actions in `memory-handlers.ts`). The set-aside count is store state, never part of a pass result: `consolidationPendingCount` carries it beside the pending count, read from the database at send time, after every pass (a thrown one included), after a Retry, after a captured turn and when the overlay opens, so the notice survives a reload and the last publication always follows the last write. Retry clears `set_aside_at` and `failed_attempts` on every set-aside row, publishes the counts and arms the idle timer when it returned a turn.
- **The panel shows one result per run, never per pass** (`accumulateRunResult`, `memory/consolidation.ts`; `MemoryService.runConsolidation`). A run is as defined above: one manual Run now with its follow-up passes, or one automatic run, which is a single pass. A pass that queues its run's next pass hands it the pass number it runs and the run's result so far (`run`, a `ConsolidationRunPass`, the shape of `MemoryService.currentRun` too, kept across `mergePendingConsolidation`), and only the run's last pass publishes `consolidationResult`: the extracted memories in pass order, the maintenance counts and `candidatesReviewed` summed, `ranAt` the last pass's end and `trigger` the run's. The run fails exactly when its last pass fails, with that pass's failure, because a run continues only after a pass that did not fail; the card then shows the memories earlier passes kept beside the failure, and Retry starts a new run over the turns still queued. A thrown pass is already a failed pass result, and a run cut short (memory turned off or uninitialized before its next pass) still publishes its result. `consolidationRunning` is sent true at every pass start with the pass number (`pass`, from 1) and the run's result over its ended passes (`runSoFar`), and false after the run's result, so the toolbar dot and the card follow the run; each pass's `claim` event restarts the stepper, and `consolidationRunning` never restarts a running stepper. `MemoryService.currentRun` holds both from each pass start until `endRun`, and the overlay-open replay sends them (`getConsolidationActivity`). From a run's second pass the card's results section shows "This run" over `runSoFar` and the status strip names the pass; during a run's first pass, so for every automatic run, and once the run ends, the section shows "Last run" over the published result, and the store clears both on `setResult`. `lastConsolidationResult`, replayed when the overlay opens, is the published run result. The idle back-off still counts passes: a non-failed pass resets it and a failed one adds one.

## Usage accounting

- **One inclusion rule decides what counts as spend** (`src/shared/usage-accounting.ts`). It mirrors pi's `getSessionStats()`: every assistant message whatever its `stopReason` (aborted and error turns were billed), a `toolResult` message that carries usage, `usage` entries (the cache warmer writes `kind: 'cache_warm'`), and `compaction` and `branch_summary` entries that carry usage. The status bar (live and on reload), the subagent meters and reload, and the `/stats` worker all import this module, so a figure never differs between the live view and a reload. The module is imported by the webview bundle and the worker thread, so it imports nothing from pi, `vscode` or Node. `pi-parity.test.ts` builds a session with pi's `SessionManager` and asserts the indexed cost equals a real `AgentSession.getSessionStats()`.
- **Image cost travels as toolResult `usage`.** `GenerateImage` returns `usage: result.usage` on its result, a failed call included, and never throws once usage exists, because pi replaces a thrown result with one that has no usage. It writes no ledger line, which would count the same spend twice. The recorded cost is what OpenRouter billed. pi prices image models by token from its catalog, at the text rate for image tokens, and drops the `usage.cost` (USD) OpenRouter returns on every response. So the tool hands pi a per-call `fetch` that reads `usage.cost` from a clone of the same response (no second request), accepts it only as a finite, non-negative number, and records it as `cost.total`, with `cost.output` taking what pi's prompt-side estimate leaves. When the field is absent or invalid it keeps pi's estimate and logs the model id only; the body, prompt and image are never logged. Only models with a positive output token price are offered, so the fallback is never $0. `details` carries `provider` and `model` on every outcome, which `/stats` uses to attribute the toolResult row to the image model.
- **A fork's inherited spend is not its own.** A fork copies its parent's entries verbatim (same `id`, same `timestamp`) into a new file whose header carries a later timestamp, so a copied entry always predates its file's header. `isOwnEntry(entry, headerTimestamp)` is that test; a conversation's totals count only its own entries. They are derived from `sessionManager.getEntries()` on every read, never cached, so a session replacement or a switch cannot leave a stale baseline.
- **The budget counts the conversation's own spend plus its subagent and team spend.** `ownSessionUsage` (`session-usage.ts`) is the figure for the status bar and for every budget check (`checkBudgetAtTurnEnd`, `enforceBudgetInFlight`, `addExternalCost`, the pre-prompt gate), so a fork is never stopped by what its parent spent. Subagent and team cost is added from the adapter's external total, which resets when a new conversation is seeded. The status bar shows the conversation's own spend only; subagent and team spend show on their own cards. The in-flight check runs a microtask after `message_end`, because pi appends the message only after notifying listeners, and it uses the same reading the status bar receives in that microtask.
- **Never bill from `history-loader.ts`'s context snapshot.** Those fields describe the last request's prompt size for the context meter. Cumulative spend comes from summing entries under the rule above, and the two sets of fields are kept apart in the webview store. The snapshot follows pi's `getContextUsage`: it skips an aborted or errored message and one with no tokens, and a compaction resets it to zero until the next response, live (`compaction_end`) and on reload (`contextSnapshotOf` in `context-snapshot.ts`).
- **Every billed internal model call appends one ledger line.** Titles, memory work (extraction, rerank, merge, profile, query expansion, quality audit), desktop commit messages (`commit-message`, attributed to the project folder, a nested repository's included) and `/btw` run outside any session file, so their spend lives in `~/.damocles/usage/subcalls.jsonl` (`usage-stats/subcall-ledger.ts`). `PiRuntime.runStructuredCompletion` appends the line for titles, memory and commit messages, and `PiRuntime.runClassification` for every Jev request that reports usage, a failed one included (purpose `memory-merge` or `memory-rerank`; TypeSafe's direct `jev-latest` has no catalog price, so its rows are unpriced tokens). The `/btw` aside runs on an in-memory pi session, so its own `message_end` subscriber in `pi-session.ts` appends it. A call site that does neither is spend `/stats` never sees. `appendSubCallUsage` skips a call with no tokens and no cost (`carriesUsage` in `usage-accounting.ts`). It never reads `totalTokens`, because a provider may leave that at 0 while it reports tokens. The ledger is append-only JSONL, one `appendFileSync` per line, which is safe across VS Code windows.
- **Formulas.** Every `/stats` card shows its formula in a tooltip and must match these.
  - Cost is the recorded `cost.total`. An entry with tokens and a zero cost is unpriced: its tokens go into a separate "unpriced tokens" figure and never read as $0.
  - Total tokens is `input + output + cacheRead + cacheWrite`, pi's `totalTokens`.
  - Cache hit rate is `cacheRead / (input + cacheRead + cacheWrite)`, and has no value when the denominator is 0.
  - Net cache savings is `(cacheRead + cacheWrite) × inputRate − (cost.cacheRead + cost.cacheWrite)`: what the cached tokens would have cost as plain input, minus what they did cost. `inputRate` is `cost.input / input` from the same entry, which follows pi's pricing tiers. When `input` is 0 it falls back to the registry rate the host passes to the worker (pi's `model.cost.input` is USD per million tokens, the worker takes USD per token). Unpriced entries are excluded. The figure can be negative when 1-hour cache writes are rarely read.
  - The cost label always reads "API-equivalent" and adds "est." when the current account is not dollar-billed, because the billing mode at request time was never recorded.
- **The `/stats` index is a cache, and it never forgets spend** (`src/core/usage-stats/`). `~/.damocles/usage/usage.db` is a `node:sqlite` WAL database owned by one worker thread per window, because the first scan can read more than 1 GB. The worker imports neither `vscode` nor pi and takes every path from the host. The host closes it after 2 minutes idle, and never while it is making progress. When the worker sends nothing for 5 minutes with a request in flight, the host terminates it and rejects the waiting requests. The next request starts a fresh worker. Each file commits in its own transaction, so at most the file in progress is lost.
  - The first worker in each extension host runs `PRAGMA quick_check` on open. Later workers skip it, because it reads the whole file. SQLite still reports corruption from real statements, and the worker then closes the index, so the next open checks it and moves it aside.
  - Rows upsert on `entry_key = "<id>|<timestamp>"`. A copied entry belongs to the oldest top-level session whatever the scan order. For session-file rows, `entries.session_started_ms` is the start of the top-level session the row is attributed to, not the file's own header time: a fork copies subagent files into its own folder (`fork-agent-data.ts`), and the copies' own headers cannot order them. Ties break on session id, agent session id and path, a strict total order, so every scan order gives the same rows.
  - Nested subagent and team files attribute to their parent's session id and keep their own id in `agent_session_id`. Team event logs and `.json` checkpoints are not sessions and are never indexed.
  - Rows keep the raw provider and model. `model_key` maps a dated id to its base id when only the base is in the registry. `meta.model_registry` holds a fingerprint of the registry keys the rows were keyed with. When a scan sees a different registry, it re-keys every row in one `UPDATE`, so the model filter, `byModel` and the fallback rate join never split one model across two keys.
  - Files are read from a stored byte offset, complete lines only; a trailing partial line waits for the next scan. A shrink or a changed tail hash reparses from byte 0, which is idempotent. Each file is indexed in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. The worker re-reads the file's row and its size inside it, so a second window's worker serializes rather than resuming from a stale offset. A migration re-reads `user_version` under the write lock, so two windows opening an old index migrate it once.
  - Marking vanished files and recording the scan time fail soft. When another window holds the write lock too long, the worker logs it, retries on the next scan, and still answers with the rows it indexed.
  - Spend rows are never deleted. When a file vanishes its rows stay, its conversation is marked missing and its title is cleared, so it shows as "Deleted conversation". A file counts as vanished only when a stat inside the marking transaction finds it gone, because another window may have indexed a file created after this scan listed the directory. The index runs with `secure_delete` and truncates its WAL after it clears a title, so the title leaves the database files too. Rebuilding the database would lose that spend, so schema changes are additive migrations under `PRAGMA user_version` and the code never rebuilds a readable database.
  - A corrupt database is moved aside as `usage.db.corrupt-<ms>` and the loss is logged. Only the newest aside copy is kept, and it can still hold titles from before the corruption. Windows refuses the move while another window's worker holds the file open, and the request then fails with an error that says so.
  - Filter values reach SQL only as bound `json_each` parameters. Time buckets use the webview's zone, not the extension host's. Under Remote-SSH, WSL or a container the two often differ, and the webview matches bucket keys as strings. The webview sends its IANA zone with each query. The host rejects a zone that is not a name of at most 64 characters or that `Intl.DateTimeFormat` does not accept. The worker cuts days, weeks, months, heatmap weekdays and hours, and active days in that zone through the `usage_local` SQL function in `queries.ts`, with the zone as a bound parameter. The function formats each 15-minute UTC slot once per zone with `Intl.DateTimeFormat` and caches it. That is exact because every UTC offset and DST change since 1972 falls on a 15-minute UTC boundary. An offset zone such as `+05:37` breaks that, so the host accepts zone names only. Weeks start on Monday in every locale. The chart never drops a point whose bucket it did not expect. `chartRows` gives it its own row in key order, so the chart still sums to the KPI.
  - A run billed to a remote host's own credential is the host's spend, never the user's sign-in. A daemon's core appends `damocles-credential-source` (`{ clientProviders }`, the providers the run's pinned client holds a credential for, never a key) to each pi session a panel builds, at a run's start and at each reply's whenever that set differs from the one the session last recorded, so the first run of every loaded session records it and a turn Continue with my sign-in moved to another device records that device's set before the reply it billed; a sub-call or `/btw` ledger line the host paid for carries `credentialSource: 'host'`. The indexer keeps a file's latest entry across incremental reads (`files.client_providers`) and stamps `entries.credential_source = 'host'` on a row whose provider (a compaction's, the file's latest model's) is not in it; a file with no entry, every local core's, is all the user's sign-in. `byCredential` splits the rows into `host` and `signIn`, which `/stats` shows as its By credential breakdown once any row is the host's (`StatsBreakdown.vue`). This attribution is not an inclusion rule: `usage-accounting.ts` alone decides what counts, and the entry travels inside the session file, so any index that reads the file attributes its rows the same way.
  - Every breakdown (`series`, `byModel`, `byProject`, `bySource`, `byCredential`) groups the same filtered rows as the totals with the same unpriced rule, so each sums to the KPI total; `queries.test.ts` asserts it under every filter kind. `buildReport` reads every figure in one read transaction, so another window's commit never lands between the totals and a breakdown. Sources are exclusive: a ledger row is a background task by purpose; otherwise a `compaction` or `branch_summary` entry is compaction and summaries, and a `usage:cache_warm` entry is cache warming, whichever file it sits in; only the rest take the file's origin (main chat, subagent by agent type, team by role). A new source must keep that one-branch-per-entry `CASE` in `queries.ts`, or a row is counted twice or dropped.

## Platform boundary and shared stores

- **Core reaches the host only through `src/platform`.** Classes take the services they use (`SettingsStore`, `TrustService`, `FileWatcherFactory`, `NotificationService`, ...) by constructor, wired from `activate` through `ChatPanelProvider`. Core has two globals, and the first is the accessor in `src/core/platform-host.ts`: the host calls `installPlatform` at the start of `activate`, before any core code that reads it runs (only `perf.ts` `markActivationStart`, which records a timestamp and reads nothing, comes first, so the platform's construction counts toward activation), and `platform()` throws if nothing is installed. Only functions with no owning object read it: `perf.ts` `perfDebugEnabled`, `asset-sources.ts` precedence, `pi-session/agent-dir.ts` `cacheWarmingSetting`, `web-access/config.ts`, `team/index.ts` `isEnabled`, `subagents/enabled-models.ts`, `skills/utils.ts`, `tools/shell-sentinel-client.ts`, `l10n.ts` `t`, `mcp/mcp-auth-flow.ts` (`authenticate` and the auth provider's `onRedirect`), `voice/sidecar/output-channel.ts` `getVoiceOutputChannel`, and `PiRuntime`, a lazily created process singleton that reads it once in its constructor and hands the services to its folder runtimes by constructor. Worker threads (compass, usage stats, memory integrity, session metadata index, voice archive extraction) and the sentinel process never call it: nothing installs a platform there, so it throws. Tests get a default fake from `src/__mocks__/fake-platform.setup.ts` and reinstall one with `installFakePlatform` for specific values. The second global is the worktree registry lookup in `worktrees/personal-config.ts`, which `ChatPanelProvider` installs from `Platform.worktrees` only when `capabilities.worktrees` is set (desktop); while none is installed (VS Code, tests) its readers (`personalConfigRoot`, `identityFolder`, `worktreeSessionFolders` and the rest) answer as for a folder outside any worktree. `logger.ts` writes to an installed `LogSink`; the host installs its output channel at activation and the compass and memory integrity workers install one that posts to the host.
- **On desktop the engine reaches the host only through the core protocol** (`src/desktop/core-protocol/`, `src/desktop/core-host/platform/`, `src/desktop/main/core-services/host-services.ts`). Damocles Core's entry (`src/desktop/core-host/index.ts`) builds the engine's `Platform` with `createCorePlatform(transport, init, createLocal, onViolation)` over the one ordered channel to main, its parent channel (`parentChannel()`, `src/desktop/local-services/parent-channel.ts`), and installs it for `platform()`; `startCoreService` gives it to `ChatPanelProvider`. Main's `DesktopPlatform` never reaches the engine. Every service is one of three kinds:
  - **Local**, an instance per process: settings, file watchers, confinement, localization, log sinks, app paths and the capabilities, a function of the OS core runs on, read on every handshake. Both processes build theirs from `src/desktop/local-services/` (core in `core-host/local.ts`, over its mirrors of main's folders, trust and state), and each converges on a settings file another wrote one watcher event later. App paths come from main in the init, since only main maps worker names to bundles. The worktree registry, `ScmService`, the git runner and the askpass server are core's alone ("Desktop host").
  - **Mirrored**, synchronous reads of a copy main pushes as versioned deltas (`MirrorTopics` in `messages.ts`), writes by RPC: trust, key-value state, workspace folders, panel view state, the active editor context, the terminal pane's state, and app info (init only). `isTrusted` reads the trusted folder keys plus core's own worktree registry, and `requestTrust` is an RPC. A memento update changes core's copy at once and then goes to main, as VS Code's extension host does; a delta for a key with a write of core's in flight is not applied, since that write lands after it, and once the last write in flight for a key settles, a key any of them failed for goes back to main's value (its last delta, else the value before the first write), so a failed write changes nothing.
  - **Proxied**, async RPCs main serves: secrets, notifications, the folder and file pickers (native dialogs, which take no signal: Electron cannot close an open one), clipboard, shell, editor, window, host lifecycle and `resolveProxy`. Text input and quick picks render in a chat's webview, so core asks them itself through the running provider's `WebviewPrompts` (`createDialogsProxy`); main's own platform answers neither. A question main shows for core (`dialogs.ask`, a modal notice, `worktrees.reviewSetup`, `confirmHandOff`, `showRefusal`, `askpass.prompt`) takes its request's signal and is withdrawn with its cancel answer when it aborts, which main's endpoint does to every request in flight when core ends. Every panel core holds is a proxy named by its view's id, the same id on both sides: `createPanel` returns one with a core-issued id at once, and main makes the view with that id when the create envelope arrives (`createPanelInOwnColumn` too, since a desktop chat's own column is a new chat); a view main refuses disposes the proxy, and core logs why. A chat view main creates is attached by its id (`CorePanels.attach`, from `chats.restore` and `chats.startInWorktree`). A diff's title function crosses as a template with `DIFF_TITLE_NAME` where the file name goes, so the title must contain the name verbatim.
  - **Ordering rule:** main publishes a delta from the listener that saw the change, on the same channel, so it goes out before the reply of any call whose work caused it (a trust grant before `requestTrust`'s answer, a landed memento write before `update`'s).
  - Each side parses every message it receives against the tables in `messages.ts`, keeping known fields only and capping sizes; webview messages and memento values must be JSON-shaped (plus `undefined`, and binary for a browser page's frames). A payload the engine owns (catalog rows, review diffs, attachments) is checked as bounded JSON data, and the ids, keys and paths either side acts on field by field; a chat's activity and worktree state, whose prompt ids, phase and worktree fields main acts on, are parsed field by field in full. A violation in either direction ends core: main's endpoint kills it (`[core] protocol violation (...)`) and core's exits with code 3, and either way main takes the crash path. A response's error crosses as its message, and an error class main acts on (`GitError`, `GitUntrustedError`, `RemoteCancelledError`, `StashKeptError`, `SyncBranchChangedError`, and `WriteRefusedError` with its `refusal` on the `workspace` channel) also by name with its own fields (`ERROR_CODEC`, `core-protocol/errors.ts`), parsed field by field and rebuilt only from that list; any other name is a violation. The loopback (`loopback.ts`) is the unit tests' transport: it structured-clones each message and delivers it synchronously. `src/desktop/core-host/**`, `src/desktop/core-protocol/**` and `src/desktop/local-services/**` import neither electron nor desktop main (eslint, `tsconfig.core.json`).
- **Trust is asked per folder.** Every site that loads repository-authored input calls `TrustService.isTrusted(folderPath)` with the folder it acts for (a folder runtime's `cwd`, a nested session's `opts.cwd`, a `FolderTarget`, a workspace path argument); a site that loads only user-scope input does not ask. `onDidGrantTrust` delivers the granted folder paths; a listener either reloads only the listed folders (`PiRuntime` and `CompassService`, matching by `folderKey`) or re-reads `isTrusted` for its own folder on any grant. The VS Code implementation ignores the path and answers `workspace.isTrusted`, and a window grant fires with every open folder, or with `homeDirectory()` when none is open, so the extension behaves as a single window-wide gate. A site must never cache the answer across a grant. A live chat worktree's working folder takes its project's trust, as "Worktree chats" states.
- **Damocles-owned JSON config is written through `config/json-config-write.ts` only** (`~/.damocles/settings.json`, the project and local settings files, `~/.damocles/mcp.json`). `writeJsonConfig` serialises per path in process, then takes a `proper-lockfile` lock (`<file>.lock`, stale after 20 s against a 2 s heartbeat, a compromised lock fails the write), reads, applies the mutation, and returns without writing when the text is unchanged (a rewrite would only wake every watcher). Otherwise it removes any `<file>.<pid>.tmp` a crashed writer left (only a lock holder writes one, so any found under the lock is an orphan), writes a sibling temp file, `fsync`s it, and renames it over the target, retrying the rename on Windows for EPERM, EBUSY and EACCES (`renameWithRetry`, which the hot-exit backups share). The `fsync` comes before the rename, or a power loss can leave the renamed target zero-length. The temp file gets `fileMode` when the caller passes one, on every write and not only on creation, so `~/.damocles/mcp.json` and the desktop secrets file are 0600 even when the user created them wider; otherwise it copies the existing file's mode. A lock that fails to release is logged and left to go stale, since the write already landed. Readers take no lock: the rename means they see the old file or the new one, never a partial one. A reader that holds the file open delays the rename on Windows, so poll-reading a config file in a loop slows every writer. A second writer that skips the helper reintroduces the lost update between windows.
- **The lock's stale takeover is not exclusive, and that residual risk is accepted.** `proper-lockfile` checks staleness and removes the lock directory in two steps, so two waiters that both see a stale lock can both remove it, the second removing the first's fresh lock; and a holder stalled for more than 20 s (a suspended laptop, a blocked event loop) is superseded while it still believes it holds the lock. The losing holder learns so only at its next heartbeat, up to 2 s later, and a rename before then overwrites the other writer's file. It needs a dead or stalled holder plus a second writer inside that window. Comparing the lock directory's mtime before the rename does not close it: the library does not expose the mtime it wrote, and two holders created by the race can carry the same mtime (each process's first lock is stamped with the next whole second plus 5 ms).
- **Every reader of a user- or tool-authored config file passes its text through `stripBom` (`core/config/bom.ts`) before parsing.** A file saved as UTF-8 with a byte-order mark (as Windows PowerShell 5.1's `-Encoding UTF8` writes it) starts with U+FEFF, and JSON, TOML and frontmatter parsers reject it; pi's readers strip it the same way (`utils/text.ts` `stripBom`), so both read one file alike. A UTF-16 file, which PowerShell 5.1's `>` and `Out-File` write by default, is not UTF-8 and still fails to parse. Position helpers such as `locateJsonParseFailure` get the stripped text, so a reported line and column match an editor's, and a rewrite serializes with `JSON.stringify`, which drops the BOM. A file only Damocles writes (registries, caches, locks, IPC) needs no strip. A check that mirrors another tool's lookup follows that tool's parser instead: `prettier-resolve.ts` leaves the BOM on a `package.json` it searches for a Prettier config, because both majors parse it with `parse-json`, which rejects it. Prettier 3's search skips that file; Prettier 2's search stops at it with a JSON error, so Damocles hands Prettier 2 that file and Prettier reports the error, never formatting with a parent folder's config. It strips the BOM from `package.yaml`, which Prettier 3's YAML plugin reads as no content, and from the `package.json` it reads for npm's dependencies. The Edit tool and the diff views split the BOM off only to match and diff, as pi's edit does (`edit-tool.ts`, `file-patch.ts`); pi's edit and the desktop document service write it back.
- **A key provider is data, and its status has one publisher** (`KEY_PROVIDER_IDS` and `ACCOUNT_PROVIDER_IDS`, `shared/settings-sections.ts`; the defs in `pi-session/custom-providers.ts`, `ACCOUNT_PROVIDER_DEFS`, a mapped type keyed by `AccountProviderId`; `settings-manager/managers/provider-keys.ts`). The status is published for every `ACCOUNT_PROVIDER_IDS` provider, GitHub Copilot included; a sign-in-only provider's status carries `configured: false`, `preferApiKey: false` and `active` of `signIn`, `external` or `none` (on a daemon's core `external: null` and no `active`, which are the client's to say). A key provider's key lives in the host secret store and reaches pi only in memory. `setProviderApiKey` and `clearProviderApiKey` name the provider, store or delete under its def's unchanged `secretKey`, and ack only after the custom-provider resync (at most `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS`). The set and clear handlers never post `providerAuthStatusChanged` (`requestSettingsState` posts it only as the view's first snapshot): `ChatPanelProvider`'s secret-change listener does, for every panel, because both hosts' secret stores fire it for this window's own writes as well as another window's and a host prompt's. The status carries `configured` (a key is in the secret store, read bounded by `providerKeyStored`; a read that did not answer posts nothing) and `external` (where pi's credential for the provider comes from when it is not Damocles' runtime key, `PiRuntime.externalKeySource`: `environment` for a variable such as `MINIMAX_API_KEY`, `piConfig` for pi's own `models.json` or a stored `api_key` in `auth.json`; null while `auth.json` holds the provider's sign-in, which pi resolves first), never a key; Accounts and the model choices count either as signed in, as pi and Automatic do, and name the source apart from "Key saved". GitHub Copilot, which has no key, reports `external` the same way (`COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN` or a `models.json` key), with `active` `external` when no sign-in is stored. One status reads `auth.json` once and passes that read to `externalKeySource`. It also carries `signedIn` and `signedInExpiresAt` (an `oauth` credential in `auth.json`, read by `provider-keys.ts` itself), `preferApiKey`, and `active` (`signIn`, `apiKey`, `external` or `none`), which core computes with the precedence rule ("Agent auth store", `providerCredentialActive`), so an environment or `models.json` key beside a sign-in reports `signIn`; the webview never derives it. `setProviderPreferApiKey` writes the flag once init succeeded, so a failed init writes nothing, and acks after the resync, whose listeners post the statuses. A def's `secretKey` and `mode` decide where its key lives and whether a removal unregisters it (`register` only, and only with no key saved); `flatFeeKey` reads only the key pi was last given, so an outranked key never sets billing. Image generation settings, the Tools status and every key status refresh from `PiRuntime.onCustomProvidersSynced`, which fires at the end of every sync that ran, a failed one included, after the folders' tools were refreshed (a call before init or after dispose runs none), each listener isolated so one that throws neither fails the sync nor skips the rest; it is static, like `onMemoryJudgeChange`, so subscribing never creates the runtime and the runtime's first sync is not missed.
- **A retired stored credential is deleted at startup** (`config/retired-secrets.ts`). Each host runs `deleteRetiredSecrets` right after `runLegacySettingsMigrations`; it deletes each `RETIRED_SECRET_KEYS` entry the store holds, best-effort per key and logged by key name only. Retiring a credential means appending its secret name there, as a retired model id is appended to `LEGACY_MODEL_MAP`; never remove an entry, since an app not launched since still holds the secret.
- **A symlinked config file is written through, and a repository's file is confined to its `.damocles`.** `writeJsonConfig` follows a symlink, dangling or not, and writes the file it names instead of replacing the link, so a user's `~/.damocles/settings.json` may live in a dotfiles repository. Project and local files pass `confineTo: <folder>/.damocles`: the write is refused unless the file resolves directly inside that directory with the directory itself not a symlink, so a repository cannot aim a permission rule or a settings save at another tool's file such as `.claude/settings.json`.
- **`proper-lockfile` has process-wide side effects at import**, in the extension host (shared with every other extension) and in Electron main: its `signal-exit@3` installs listeners for the exit signals and wraps `process.emit` and `process.reallyExit` to remove held locks on exit, and `graceful-fs` replaces the global `fs.close` and `fs.closeSync` with queued versions. Both are widely shipped and benign. `json-config-write.ts` and `session-store/session-lease.ts` import it; `auth-file.ts` must not, see "Agent auth store".
- **A host affordance the webview may lack is a `HostCapabilities` flag** (`Platform.capabilities`, type in `src/shared/types/messages.ts`). The `ready` handler posts `hostCapabilities` right after it re-posts pending dialogs and before any state, so the webview hides a missing affordance before rendering it. The webview store's defaults equal the VS Code values (`VSCODE_HOST_CAPABILITIES` in `messages.ts`, which the VS Code host sends as is: everything but `settingsSources`, `monaco` and `damoclesTheme`, plus `settingsInPanel`, `historyInPanel` and `folderPickerInPanel` true), so the extension's UI is the same before and after the message arrives; a new desktop-only or VS Code-only affordance adds a flag with a VS Code default that keeps the extension unchanged, never a host sniff in the webview. Each flag has a webview consumer; a flag nothing reads is removed. `hostSpeechExtensions` lives only here (the VS Code Speech conflict check in `voice/service.ts` reads it). Desktop builds its value in `src/desktop/local-services/capabilities.ts`, in core's process (`voice` is false on macOS, where an ad hoc signed app gets no microphone input). `settingsInPanel` says where the settings modal lives: true (VS Code) mounts it in the chat page from the header gear; false (desktop) makes every settings link (the header gear, sign-in links) post `openAppSettings { section? }` (`composables/useOpenSettings.ts`), whose core handler calls `WindowService.openAppSettings`, which desktop answers with the overlay settings request. That routing is `openAppSettingsFor` (`chat-panel/open-app-settings.ts`), the one path for a settings link core raises itself too (the Voice settings action of a reply that could not be spoken): with `settingsInPanel` it posts `openSettingsPanel` to the chat, else it calls `WindowService.openAppSettings`. Core validates the section against `SETTINGS_SECTION_IDS` (`src/shared/settings-sections.ts`), and so does main for the shell's gear; the chat header shows its gear only with `settingsInPanel`, since the desktop title bar has one. `historyInPanel` shows the chat header's session history dropdown (search, rename, tag, delete, `chat-header/SessionHistoryDropdown.vue`); desktop lists chats in the sidebar instead. `folderPickerInPanel` makes the chat header's folder label a picker when two or more folders are open (`WorkspaceFolderChip.vue`, posting `setPanelWorkspaceFolder`, which asks and starts a new conversation, D39); desktop chooses the folder in the Projects list, so its header folder is always plain text.
- **Migrations to shared stores are additive only.** The memory database, the per-session memory injection databases, the Compass indexes and the `/stats` usage index are shared by every Damocles app on the machine, and those apps update on their own schedules. A release may add a column or a table; a removal or rename waits until no supported version reads it. An app that finds a store migrated past its own schema version (Compass also checks the extraction format, `isSchemaAhead` in `compass/migrations.ts`) opens it read only (`node:sqlite` `readOnly`), turns off every write path, keeps serving reads, and tells the user once per store per session through `notifySchemaAhead` (`schema-skew.ts`) to update. A newer app can also migrate a store while an older window holds it open, so three stores re-check before writing and switch to read only on the spot: memory reads `MAX(version)` inside each write-queue transaction, under the write lock (`MemoryWriteQueue`'s schema guard), and refuses that write with `MemoryReadOnlyError`, the one write that fails; the Compass worker re-checks at the start of every index write request (`fullBuild`, `incrementalUpdate`, `postprocess`, `mcp:build`) and of `webview:validation`, whose stale-file cleanup and FTS rebuild are writes; the usage worker re-checks `user_version` before it starts a scan. Compass and usage reopen the file read only. The Compass check runs per request, not per transaction, so a migration landing in the middle of a running build is caught at the next request. A per-session memory injection store is checked only when it is opened. Compass and the usage index open inside worker threads, which post `schemaAhead` for the host to show. Never let an older app migrate down, rebuild or write into a newer store.

## MCP client and config sources

- **The client layer is `@earendil-works/pi-mcp`; the host layer is Damocles' own** (`pi-session/mcp/`). pi-mcp speaks the protocol (stdio and streamable HTTP transports, JSON-RPC, OAuth discovery and grants). Damocles decides which servers run, shares one connection per server across every chat of a folder, exposes tools through ToolSearch, applies permissions, routes elicitation and drives the panel. Never load pi's built-in MCP extension (`pi-coding-agent/src/extensions/mcp/`): it keeps all server state on the extension instance, rebuilds it on every `session_start` and closes every connection on `session_shutdown`, and Damocles shares one extension instance across every panel of a folder, so a second panel would orphan the first panel's servers. There is no SSE transport; an SSE entry is an error row.
- **Sources and precedence**, lowest first: `codex`/`claude` (tie broken by `damocles.assetSourcePrecedence`, user-only; a change reloads the MCP config and every folder's resource loader, and re-sorts the slash menu), `pi` (`~/.pi/agent/mcp.json`, user scope), `pi-project` (`<folder>/.pi/mcp.json`, repo-authored, read only in a trusted folder), `claude-local`, `damocles`, `workspace` (`.mcp.json`), `damocles-local` (`SOURCE_ORDER_*` in `src/shared/types/mcp.ts`). The Claude Code, Codex and pi imports rank below `~/.damocles/mcp.json`; the project's `.mcp.json` ranks above it, and only `.damocles/mcp.local.json` ranks above `.mcp.json`. In an untrusted folder `demoteRepoAuthored` (`mcp-manager.ts`) folds the repository-authored sources (`workspace`, `damocles-local`) below every other source, keeping their relative order. Names equal after `[^A-Za-z0-9_]` → `_` (`mcpServerNamespaceKey`) are one server: the highest-ranked entry keeps it, and every other becomes a failed `nameCollision` row; the write path refuses such a name up front. ToolSearch groups by the descriptor's `serverName` namespace key, never by parsing a tool name.
- **Tool names are pi's** (`naming.ts` `createMcpToolName`, an exact port): `mcp__<server>__<tool>` with every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_]` replaced by `_`, capped at 64, and an 8-character hash suffix on every colliding or over-long name. The old prefix algorithm survives only as `legacyServerPrefixMap`, for migrations. Two migrations follow from the rename. Legacy `mcp__…` entries in `damocles.tools.disabled` that resolve to exactly one (server, tool) and differ from that tool's current name become `damocles.mcp.toolExposure[server][tool] = "off"` at the user or local scope they came from; an entry already equal to the current name stays, because `damocles.tools.disabled` matches current names exactly. Legacy prefixes are rebuilt over the enabled servers, as `legacyToCurrentToolNames` does, without an untrusted folder's repository-authored servers, and nothing moves while a config file is unreadable. Project-scope entries stay, because Damocles never writes the committed `.damocles/settings.json`, and those naming a renamed tool go into the notice below. Permission rules naming a renamed tool are rewritten (keeping the `(…)` part, idempotent, inside `writeJsonConfig`) only in `~/.damocles/settings.json`, `~/.damocles/settings.local.json` and a trusted folder's `.damocles/settings.local.json`; a file that cannot be rewritten is logged by path and error code and the rest carry on. The repo-committed `.damocles/settings.json`, hook matchers (whole tool names only, one entry per matcher) and agent `disallowed_tools` are never rewritten: their affected entries go to the folder's panels once as a `mcpRenamedToolRules` notice. A shown entry is remembered by its location, rule and replacement (`renamedRuleNoticeMemory`), so the same rule with another replacement shows again: in workspace state for a file inside the folder, the workspace's setting, or an entry whose replacement holds only in this folder (one `ToolRenames.shared` would not produce, because it names a folder server's resource), and in global state for any other, which reads the same in every workspace. Every replacement shown stays remembered, so the folders of one multi-root workspace never show theirs in turn. An entry earlier builds remembered by its rule alone counts as shown in the state it was written to, and takes the replacement found the next time its rule is; a folder server's entry such a build wrote to global state hides nothing, since it never recorded which workspace's replacement was shown. The panel's list puts a later replacement of a listed rule in its place, as core remembers that one as shown. Claude Code's settings files are neither rewritten nor listed: Claude Code keeps `-` in MCP tool names, so a rule there equal to a legacy Damocles name is correct for Claude Code. A legacy name that is also a current tool name is never renamed. The migration reruns when the folder's tools change and on a trust grant; a run counts as done for its trust state and rename map only once it completes. A second rename source covers the retired `get_<slug>` resource tools (`retiredResourceToolNames`, `toolRenames`): each former name maps to `mcp__<server>__read_mcp_resource(<that resource's URI>)`, so a deny, ask or allow on one resource stays on that resource. The former names are replayed as older builds assigned them (`rebuildDescriptors`, git 054af512 to 6790cdf6): per manager, servers in sorted order, each server's tools then its `get_<slug>` tools named by `assignServerToolNames` with every earlier server's names taken, the first holder of a name keeping it; the user manager over all its enabled servers, the folder manager with the visible user servers' names reserved. Each name maps under its pi name and the server spelled as written and as Claude Code writes it; a name some tool holds now or held then is never retired. Only a server whose tool list is known takes part: its live tools, else the tools of the cache entry its current config wrote, at any age, from the same entry as its resources (`McpClientManager.retiredResourceEntries`); a server not connected whose entry another config wrote is skipped until it connects, because a real tool's deny must never become a resource rule. The owned files get that rule in place of a bare one. A deny or ask `mcp__` glob that covered a retired tool but not a resource read on its server keeps its place, with rules added after it: where its tool part matches every `get_` name (`get_*`, `*`), one `mcp__<its server part>__read_mcp_resource`, which covers every resource of those servers as the glob did, so `mcp__*__get_*` gains one line however many resources are cached; a narrower glob singled out resources by name, so each gains its URI-scoped rule. Each addition is remembered once applied or found present (`appliedRuleAdditionMemory`, per file, rule and addition, stored as notice entries are), so a rule the user deletes is never added again. Places every folder shares (`~/.damocles/settings.json`, `~/.damocles/settings.local.json`, a chat worktree's personal file, which is its project's, and the personal `damocles.tools.disabled` lists below) take `ToolRenames.shared`: a user server's retired tools only, plus the folder-independent `read_mcp_resource` glob additions, because a folder server's URIs are that folder's; a folder server's entries there go to the notice with this folder's replacement. The committed project file, Claude Code's settings files (retired names only, since Claude Code never had those tools), hook matchers (suggested `mcp__<server>__read_mcp_resource`, which matches through the server identity, also for a matcher pattern that matched a former name) and the exact-name lists Damocles does not own (agent `disallowed_tools`, the project's `damocles.tools.disabled`) go to the notice, the lists with the URI-scoped entry as the replacement. A list the host keeps in no file (VS Code's workspace settings, and its user settings) is a notice under the setting, whose button opens the host's settings editor at it (`openSettings` with a `damocles.` query), located as `setting:<key>` for the workspace's and `setting:user:<key>` for the user's (`renamedRuleNoticeKey`); the memory picks the state from the location's scope, never by resolving the key as a path, so the workspace's setting is remembered per workspace and the user's by its replacement, as a home file is. The user scope's `damocles.tools.disabled`, and the local scope's in a trusted folder, are Damocles' own setting on both hosts, so a retired tool's pi name there, the one spelling such an entry ever matched, is rewritten in place into the URI-scoped entry, entry by entry, keeping every other entry where it was (`PiRuntime.rewriteDisabledToolLists`, through the settings store at that scope, from the list as it is when the write runs). Both are window-level, read and written with no folder, so every folder of the window shares them (the local one is the default project's file on desktop; VS Code reports no local value for a window-level key), and only a user server's entries are rewritten there. A run whose list rewrite failed is not done, so the next one retries it. Never turn such an entry into a deny rule: a deny does not hold under YOLO, and the entry did.
- **A stdio server starts on the process launcher** (`mcp/launcher-transport.ts`, a port of pi-mcp 1.4.0's `StdioTransport`; keep the two in step but for the post-exit kill on Windows and stdin errors, below): newline-delimited JSON-RPC, started as cross-spawn starts it (`crossSpawn`, so `npx` reaches `npx.cmd` on Windows), in its own process group on POSIX. Close ends stdin, then after 500 ms stops the tree (SIGTERM to the group on POSIX; on Windows, which has no graceful signal, its call job is terminated, where pi runs `taskkill /T /F` on its pid: the job also reaches descendants whose parent exited, which the pid walk cannot), and 2 s later SIGKILLs the group. When the server exits during a close, what it left running is killed too. On POSIX its group gets SIGTERM, which reaches it only until the server's output closes (pi signals the group at the close). On Windows its call job is terminated, killing every descendant, detached daemons and a browser it launched included; pi skips this on Windows, because by then it holds only a pid, which `taskkill` could find reused, while the launcher holds the job's handle until the close. Without a job (koffi failed to load) nothing is killed after the exit. This matches the MCP Python SDK, OpenAI Codex (PR #37366) and Zed (PR #58885), which also end the server's whole tree. A host that exits sends SIGTERM to every live server group, as pi does. A send resolves once the message reached the server's stdin and rejects with the write error, as pi's does; pi also reports that stdin error to the transport's error listeners, which this port does not. The npx resolver asks npm for its cache folder once per process (an answer not given within 15 s is asked for again on the next connect) and warms the npx cache through the launcher, both with npm's absolute path (`npm.cmd` or a Volta-style `npm.exe` on Windows, from the first PATH folder holding either); a config value's `!command` runs there too.
- **stdio servers never receive the host environment.** The transport's environment is `mcp/stdio-env.ts`, an exact port of the MCP SDK's `getDefaultEnvironment()` allowlist (the platform's process variables, no credentials), plus the server's own expanded `env` (`stdioEnvironment`; on Windows an own key that differs from a default only in case, such as `Path`, replaces it). The extension host holds provider keys in its environment; a server gets one only when its config names it. A failed stdio row shows the last `MCP_STDERR_TAIL_CHARS` (2048) of the server's stderr with control and bidi characters stripped, cut on a character boundary, and never for an untrusted row; stderr is not logged (`McpServerConnectError.stderrTail` is not enumerable and the error carries no `cause`, so even `%O` cannot print it).
- **Long tool results are cut in the middle** (`content.ts`, a port of pi's `limitMcpContent`/`truncateMiddle`): model-facing text over 20 KB (20480 bytes) becomes one block cut in the middle (`…N chars truncated…`) under a truncation warning, and the full text goes to `os.tmpdir()/damocles-mcp-<16 hex>.txt`, created exclusively (`wx`) with mode 0600, whose path is in the result so the agent can Read it. `PiRuntime.dispose` removes every such file the process wrote (`removeSavedMcpOutputs`).
- **MCP resources reach the model through three tools, never one tool per resource** (`tools/mcp-resource-tools.ts`, ported from pi 1.4.0's `extensions/mcp/resources.js`: names, descriptions, parameters and JSON keys, pinned by `mcp-resource-tools.test.ts` against pi's file). `list_mcp_resources`, `list_mcp_resource_templates` and `read_mcp_resource` take a `server`; a list with one gives a page plus `nextCursor`, without one every page of every server plus `errors: [{ server, error }]`, and `cursor` without `server` is an error. MCP App entries (`ui://`, `text/html;profile=mcp-app`) and icons are left out, and an item's own `server` key cannot replace the tag. A read returns text as text (a blob of a text MIME type decoded, `image/svg+xml` among them through `+xml`), an image blob as an image block only when `classifyInlineImage` (`src/shared/inline-image.ts`) accepts it, so the `imageCount` path keeps its bytes out of every message, and any other blob, a refused image included, as a file from `saveMcpBinaryToTempFile` (`os.tmpdir()/damocles-mcp-<16 hex>.bin`, `wx`, 0600, never named from server output, removed by `removeSavedMcpOutputs`). **One image classifier serves every MCP path**: the agent's read, an `image` content of an MCP tool result (`transformMcpContent`, which shows a refused one as a one-line placeholder) and the user's attach at send. It takes a label only if it names a type providers take (`image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/gif`, `image/webp`, and `image/jpg`, which pi 1.4.0 maps to `image/jpeg` in `dist/utils/image-process.js:6-20`), or no label, checks the data is padded base64 (a linear-time pattern) within `MAX_IMAGE_BASE64_LENGTH`, and takes the media type from the bytes with a port of pi's `detectSupportedImageMimeType` (`dist/utils/mime.js:4-21`, BMP left out), never from the label, because a provider rejects a JPEG sent as `image/png` and the block stays in the transcript, failing every later request. The `server` and `uri` are used exactly as sent, untrimmed, because the gate and the hooks match those strings. They run over a port on `McpToolSource` (`resourceServers()` and the page, all-pages and read calls), which awaits the connection, uses the server's timeout and routes the caller's elicitation UI; `FolderMcpView` routes by server owner, so a user server not visible in the folder, or shadowed by a folder server, is unknown, and an error that names the servers with resources names the ones the view reaches (`McpToolCallOptions.reachableResourceServers`), never a hidden user server or an Off one. A connect lists tools only and records the `resources` capability (`ServerConnection.hasResources`); nothing lists resources until a tool asks, and `resources/list_changed` is not followed. The metadata cache stays `CACHE_VERSION = 1` with an additive `hasResources` (read as `hasResources ?? resources.length > 0`, the capability before a connect), and the writer carries an existing entry's `resources` list forward unchanged while its config hash holds and never writes one, because another Damocles build sharing the cache still makes its `get_*` tools from it. The retired-name migration reads the newest list an older build wrote: `resources` when not empty, else the additive `retiredResources`, which keeps that list when a config edit (any `computeServerHash` input) empties `resources` and through a sign-out, which leaves only `retiredResources` in an entry whose `configHash` no config matches, so no build reads tools from it (`cachedRetiredResources`, `clearServerCache`). The file is per server name, so a user and a folder server of the same name share one retired list. Each manager reads a server's entry once per server set (`cacheEntries`) and replaces it with what it writes, so a connect or a tools `list_changed` reads nothing from disk. An exact-name tool list, `damocles.tools.disabled` or an agent's `disallowed_tools`, turns resources off with `mcp__<server>__read_mcp_resource(<uri pattern>)` entries (`disabledResourceMatcher`, matched by `uriRuleCovers`, the one matcher the URI-scoped permission rules use too): a list leaves them out with no notice, as a turned-off tool is absent from the tool list, a directory read drops them, and a read of one fails before any request reaches the server. It is configuration, not a permission rule, so it holds in every mode, YOLO included, and never restricts the user's own browse or attach. The panel reads its list per call; a nested agent's is the panel's entries read with `eligible` plus its own, frozen at spawn. A `resource_link` in any MCP result names the server and `read_mcp_resource` only where the calling agent can read it: with the `ToolSearch({tools:["mcp_resources"]})` step while the tool is loadable but not active, with none once it is active, and with no hint at all where the tool is not eligible for that agent, the server offers no resources to it, its lists turn the URI off or a deny rule covers it (`ResourceReadReach.unreadable`). The server name in the hint is quoted with `quotedServerName`. The reach is computed only for a result holding a `resource_link`, after the call has succeeded and outside the code that reports a failed call, from the caller bound before the call while pi's context is live (`ResourceReachResolver`). The folder's MCP tools serve every panel in the folder, so each call asks the panel whose session made it (`PanelGateContext.mcpResourceReach`, looked up by `ctx.sessionManager.getSessionId()`), and a call from a session with no registered panel gets no hint. A nested agent's tools answer from its frozen grant and its live tools (`ctx.tools`, pi's callable tools, which are the active ones since no Damocles tool sets a pi `exposure`): direct when `read_mcp_resource` is active, which it is from the start for an agent without ToolSearch, the ToolSearch step when ToolSearch is active instead, and no hint otherwise.
- **Resource exposure: Off, On (deferred), Always loaded (`direct`), per server.** The config default is Off for `exposeResources: false` or an `exposure` that maps to off (`hidden`), Always loaded for `exposure: "direct"`, otherwise deferred (`configResourceExposure`); `damocles.mcp.resourceExposure` (`{ [server]: value }`) overrides it per scope with the tool overlay's rules (`resolveResourceExposure`, `resourceExposureMapForScope`, project and local only in a trusted folder), overlaid in `FolderMcpView`. `resourceServers()` lists the visible servers that offer resources and are not Off; with none, or with `damocles.mcp.enabled` off, the three names are not eligible, and `damocles.tools.disabled` still subtracts them. The panel's Resources row writes the setting through `mcpSetResourceExposure` with the tool rows' save scope, a server rename moves its entry at every scope, `PiRuntime` refreshes every folder on a change, and `pendingDirectServers` includes a connecting server whose resource exposure is Always loaded unless its cache says it has no resources.
- **Resource exposure shapes the agent's tools, never the user's own browse and attach (D13)** (`FolderMcpView.userResources()`, `McpUserResourcePort` in `tool-source.ts`, `USER_RESOURCE_REQUEST` in `mcp-client-manager.ts`). The MCP panel's resource picker reaches resources through a separate port that `FolderRuntime.mcpUserResources` returns and the session exposes as `mcpUserResources()`; it routes by owner as the agent's port does and keeps every check but exposure (unknown server, no resources after the connect, a changed server id), so a server whose resources are Off or `exposeResources: false` stays browsable and attachable. No agent tool holds it: the resource tools take `McpToolSource`, whose methods always apply exposure. A server whose capability is unknown connects on the picker's first request, as VS Code starts one when its picker opens. Only a deny rule restricts the user: it disables Attach with the rule named (`McpResourceRuleFilter.resourceDenyRule`), refuses `attachMcpResource`, and refuses the send that would read the resource; an ask rule restricts nothing here, and under YOLO no rule applies, as `matchRule` gives the agent none. A deny rule does not restrict the user's own browsing: a listing shows a covered resource with the rule, and Open still reads a covered directory to list its children (`requestMcpResourceChildren`), because a listing goes to the user and never to the model; only Attach and the send are refused. Template completions go through `completion/complete` only to a server that declares `completions`; core forwards a completion value of at most `MAX_RESOURCE_FIELD_LENGTH` characters (empty included) and flattens and caps each value the server returns (`oneLine`). Every picker request with a usable request id gets its reply, an invalid server name, URI or cursor an `error`, and a server cursor longer than `MAX_RESOURCE_FIELD_LENGTH`, which core would refuse back, ends the listing. A directory is VS Code's (`mcpResourceFilesystem.ts`), except that a MIME type decides: an `inode/directory` MIME type, a URI ending in `/` with no MIME type, or a read with no content under its own URI; its children are the contents one path segment below it, a child with no MIME type and no content shown as a directory and a deeper one as its sub-directory, and a directory is never attached. Opening a row shown as a directory whose read returns content under its own URI answers `notDirectory`, and the picker offers Attach on that row, so a resource is never left neither openable nor attachable. Whether a resource is text is decided by the read at send, which is authoritative: `attachMcpResource` refuses only an `inode/directory` MIME type, an MCP App (`isMcpAppResource`, refused again at send), and the clearly binary families of `isBinaryMimeType` (`application/octet-stream`, `application/pdf`, `application/zip`, `audio/*` and images providers do not take), which also disable Attach in the picker, because text resources carry many other labels (`application/javascript`, `application/xml`, `video/mp2t` for `.ts`). At send an image goes as the type its bytes carry (`classifyInlineImage`), and one it refuses, or a text blob whose bytes already exceed the character cap (UTF-8 decodes at most three bytes into one character), refuses the send with the reason before anything is decoded. Untrusted repository servers never connect, so they never reach the picker.
- **The health check pings a supervised server only when it has no call in flight** (`lifecycle.ts`, `McpServerManager.answersPing`), because a server that handles one request at a time cannot answer mid-call and closing it kills the call. Any JSON-RPC reply proves the server alive, an error reply such as `-32601` from a server without `ping` included; only no reply within `HEALTH_PING_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s, not the call timeout, because servers are pinged one after another) or a closed connection closes and reconnects it. A spec edited under the same name, including a trust grant flipping `trusted`, closes the old connection and clears its failure backoff in `doReconcile`, then connects at once.
- **Config values follow their file's format** (`mcp/config-value.ts`, `resolveMcpServerValues`). pi-format sources are pi's `~/.pi/agent/mcp.json` and `.pi/mcp.json` and Damocles' own `~/.damocles/mcp.json` and `.damocles/mcp.local.json`; there `env`, `headers`, `bearerToken` and `oauth.clientSecret` follow pi's `resolveConfigValue`: a value starting with `!` is a shell command whose trimmed stdout is the value, `$VAR` and `${VAR}` interpolate, `$$` and `$!` are escapes, and an unset or empty variable fails the server with an error naming the field and the variable. Other sources (`.mcp.json`, Claude, Codex) keep `${VAR}` and `$env:VAR` with a missing variable read as empty, and a leading `!` stays literal, because their formats do not define it. `bearerTokenEnv` naming an unset or empty variable fails the server in every source. A stdio server's `cwd` is resolved by the host when it builds the spec (`McpManager` `serverSpec`): relative to the folder for a folder-scoped entry and to the home directory for a user entry, and that directory is the default when `cwd` is absent, as pi's `createDefaultTransport` does, so no server inherits the host process's working directory. A legacy entry's `cwd` keeps `${VAR}`/`$env:VAR`; pi does not expand `cwd`, so a pi-format entry whose `cwd` holds `${` or `$env:` is an `invalidConfig` row naming `cwd`. A resolved header or `bearerToken` holding a line break or NUL, or an `env` value holding NUL, fails the server by field name, because Node and undici quote the offending value in their own errors. Values are expanded on every connect, so a rotated token applies on the next one. Commands run asynchronously (pi's own `spawnSync` would block the extension host): on Windows through pi's `getShellConfig(shellPath)` with the bash tool's `shellPath`, falling back to the default shell only when that shell cannot start, elsewhere in the default shell, with a 10 s timeout that kills the command's process tree. Command output and resolved values are credentials and are never logged; errors and `errorInfo` params carry only field names, variable names and source ids.
- **Commands never run from an untrusted folder's file.** `.pi/mcp.json` is repo-authored and loads only in a trusted window. A `!` value in `.damocles/mcp.local.json` of an untrusted folder fails the server (`commandUntrusted`) before any command runs; it is never run and never sent literally.
