Each is also a registry command, listed in the palette with the chord as its hint and in the menu with no accelerator, which runs it on the active tab through `damocles:shell:editor:command` (`EditorCommand`).
- **Shown folder (D31).** Main keeps one shown folder per project (`worktrees/shown-folder.ts`): the selected chat's working folder, or the project folder after Show project folder, until another chat is selected. The shell gets each change on `damocles:shell:shown-folder` and every project's in `ShellState.shownFolders`. Files, Search, Quick Open (for the selected project), source control, the Repositories section and new terminals follow it; running terminals and open editor tabs keep their own root. A running search and source control's selected repository restart only when the folder shown changes, never when only its worktree's state does (a chat's turn starting or ending, the branch behind); the consumers keyed by root (terminals, the file tree, Quick Open's lists, formatter hosts) follow only a root joining or leaving main's root list (`RootSet` in `roots.ts`). A worktree tab names its branch from the registry record (`WorktreeRoots.branchOf`), so the chip stays while its worktree is being deleted.
- **Roots (AD5, slice 12a rule 19).** A renderer names files by a root key, `rootKey({ projectKey, worktreeId? })` from `preload/folder-root.ts`, in every channel field that names files or a folder view; for a project folder it is the project key itself. Main resolves every key in `WorktreeRoots`: an open project, or a live chat record of that same project. Adopted, deleted and another project's worktrees are refused, and so is a worktree whose delete has stopped what runs there (`WorktreeRoots.close`, taken after the delete's last question, so a Save answered in it writes and no terminal or formatter host stops before then); confinement then applies inside the resolved folder as before. Source control opens a chat worktree as its own `ScmService` project, keyed by its root key, while a project shows it, an editor tab names it or a flow needs it, so quick diff and blame keep working on its tabs. A working folder's trust follows "Worktree chats".
- **The Connect dialog is a reka dialog rendered in place** (`overlay/components/ConnectDialog.vue`, `ui/dialog` `DialogContent` with `inline`). It joins the overlay stack through `useDialogLayer` like every `ui/dialog`, but is not teleported to `body`, so `OverlayApp`'s `TransitionGroup` plays its exit (`.connect-overlay` in `overlay.css`) before the answer reaches main; a teleported dialog would vanish with its popup and main's switch to `hidden` would cut nothing short only by luck. It answers only `dismissed`: a picked folder is added through `pickHostFolder` before it closes. Escape or Cancel on a running attempt calls `cancelConnect` first; any other close leaves an attempt waiting on a prompt to the "Sign in to <name>" banner. A click beside it does nothing on the timeline and Name views and while a passwordless setup or a removal runs in the host card. Every question asked from inside it (the host key dialogs, Forget host, Remove Damocles server) is a reka alert dialog, so it opens as a nested layer that takes the pointer and focus from the dialog. Main rebuilds every host list and step it pushes to the open dialog with the request's own parsers (`parseConnectHosts`, `parseConnectStep` in `overlay.ts`) and drops, with a log line, one past the bounds.
- **A progressbar never wraps controls** (`StepTimeline.vue`). ARIA gives `progressbar` presentational children, so the step timeline's value (the current step, "Installing, 42%", "Failed at <step>", "Connected") sits on its own drawn bar, and the step list with its password field and Retry buttons stays outside it.
- **A danger dialog fills its last action** (`MessageDialog.vue`): with Cancel, Keep running and End terminals, End terminals takes the danger fill and Cancel takes focus (D41). A dialog of another severity fills its first action.
- **Renderer file-name rules follow the project's platform, never this computer's** (`FilesSection` `projectPlatform`, `preload/file-names.ts`): reserved Windows names and characters and case-insensitive clashes apply on Windows and macOS targets; a `\` stays refused on every platform because renderer paths are `/`-separated (`isRelativeFilePath`). A project whose `ShellProject.trash` is false labels Delete "Delete Permanently".
- **A remote host's name and color on this computer reach every place at once** (`hostRenamed` in `main/index.ts`): after a rename main republishes `ShellState`, sends `PANEL_CHANNELS.host` to every chat page of that host (the panel preload updates `getHost()` and calls `onHost` listeners), relabels the entries and showing toasts of its chats (`NotificationCenter.relabelHost`, a toast re-sent under its own id), and pushes the Connect dialog's host list.
- **Removing a remote project whose host runs terminals of it asks first** (`askRemoteTerminals`): a danger `message` "End its N terminals on <host>?" with Cancel focused, Keep running (their tabs close through `TerminalService.detach`, which posts no kill, and they keep running on the host, listed on its card) and End terminals (each ends on the host through `terminals.post` kill). A host that is not connected keeps them and nothing is asked; one that dropped after End terminals was chosen keeps them too, their tabs detached and a log line written, and the removal goes on (`releaseRemoteTerminals` in `remove-project.ts`).
- **A host that stops answering shows nothing for `OFFLINE_GRACE_MS` (2 s), then the same state everywhere it shows** (`src/shared/host-connection.ts`, `webview/composables/useHostConnection.ts`). Main publishes each SSH host's reconnect phase (`ShellHost.reconnect`, read from `Reconnector.phase`) and each remote chat page's (`PANEL_CHANNELS.connection`, also in `PanelInit.connection`), with `since` on this computer's clock: the missed deadline of a suspect connection, the drop of a lost one. Every renderer that shows the host (its status dots, the shell's `ConnectionBanner`, the chat page's banner under its header, the grip gutter's strip, the dimmed Files tree) derives what it shows from `since + OFFLINE_GRACE_MS` and the phase alone, so a drop shorter than the grace changes no dot and shows no banner, and they all change at the same moment. The countdown is whole seconds to `retryAt` in tabular numerals, woken by one timer per view at the next change; the polite live region says each state once (reconnecting, failed, signIn, offline, reconnected) and never counts. A host back after its banner showed shows green for `RECONNECTED_MS` (1.5 s), then the banner collapses (`o-shrink`); a new drop ends the green moment, and a view that moves to another host (`HostConnectionSource.hostId`; the shell keys its banners by host) never shows it. A permanent failure is red with Retry, Show log and Edit host. The banner is part of the chat page under its header, so it never moves the header or the page bounds (`content-bounds.ts` reserves nothing); the shell's own banner shows only while no chat page covers the chat slot. Slow (latency) never shows a banner.
- **A remote chat's sends wait in main while its host is away, and send in order to the conversation they were typed for** (`main/remote/offline-queue.ts`, `holdWhileAway`, `flushHeldMessages` and `takeHeldVerdict` in `main/index.ts`). While the authority has no usable connection (`Authorities.connection` undefined, a suspect connection included), main takes a live remote chat page's `sendMessage` and `queueMessage` before its relay and keeps them by panel token (which survives a fresh attach and a relaunch, where the panel id does not; a token outside `PANEL_TOKEN` holds nothing) in `remote-state.json` `offlineQueue`, at most `MAX_HELD_PER_PANEL` (50) per page and `MAX_HELD_BYTES` (8 MiB) across pages, past which the send goes back to the composer with why (`promptReturned` `heldPromptKept` with a detail): an id, the conversation the page showed (`sessionId`, `sessionStore.sendTargetSessionId`) and the text and image blocks, nothing else the page sent (no attachment ids, no IDE flag) and no credential. A mirrored transcript (offline launch) has no composer and shows its saved chat's held messages as chips, Cancel included. On `connectionGained` each page's held messages go, once that page's rejoin or restore settled, through `DesktopPanel.deliver` and the normal relay, the first as a send unless the chat's turn runs, the rest queued behind it, as the page itself posts them. A flushed message stays in memory until core's verdict names it: core sends no id of main's, so a verdict settles the oldest flushed message of its text, since core answers a page's messages in order. A `promptReturned` `conversationChanged` so matched is kept from the page and turns that message into a not-sent chip (Copy, Discard): main never sends it again, to that conversation or another; any other verdict goes to the page. A flushed message with no verdict after `VERDICT_WAIT_MS` (60 s) of a connection that stayed up got one main does not read (an extension command), since a drop (suspect or lost) stops that clock and a resume restarts it; when the connection it went over ends without resuming (Disconnect Host, a fresh attach) or the core it reached ends on a live connection (a new core epoch, a halt: `RemoteCore`'s `coreEnded`), each unanswered one becomes a not-sent chip, never sent again and never dropped unseen. A `remote-state.json` that does not parse or names another version holds nothing for the run and refuses every write, with a warning naming the file and why, as `remote-hosts.json` does; meanwhile a send is given back (`heldPromptKept`) instead of held, so no held message lives in memory alone. A new chat with no conversation yet that holds messages is saved in `remote-state.json` by its token alone and reopens as a new chat with them. Only Delete takes a chat's held messages with it; every other close keeps them for the chat's next page, and Forget host drops the host's. While the host is away the page's prompts are `inert` and dimmed with a hint, since an answer could not reach core. The page follows main's `PANEL_CHANNELS.connection` whenever main gives one, which only a remote chat page gets and VS Code never does, so it never waits for core to answer its `ready`.
- **Latency moves only when a measurement finishes, and is shown, never inferred** (`ShellHost.latency`, `HostCardInfo.latency`, `host-label.ts`). The dot's tooltip and accessible name and the host card read "prod-1 · 38 ms"; a slow host keeps the `--d-success` center with a `--d-warning` ring and reads "Slow connection to prod-1 (620 ms)"; the card's sparkline (`LatencySparkline.vue`) is an `aria-hidden` SVG of the last ten, keyed on `HostLatency.measurements`, the count of finished measurements, so it draws again (`d-draw`) even when a reading repeats the history. Main tells the latency meter which authority a shown, unminimized window shows (`publishVisibleAuthorities`) on every ShellState publish and window show, hide, minimize and restore.
- **The overlay page gets the theme main pushed while it loaded** (`OverlayHost.sendTheme` keeps the last theme and sends it again on `did-finish-load`), since pushes to a page that is not loaded are dropped.
- **A close or quit while a remote host runs agents asks `closeRemote` first** (`overlay/components/CloseRemoteDialog.vue`, `parseCloseRemoteRequest`, `closeRemoteResult`, `OverlayHost.updateCloseRemote` in `main/overlay.ts`). The request stacks like a message dialog, so no later request dismisses it. Its rows arrive whole on `closeRemoteRows` only while it is open. Keep running takes focus. Escape and the scrim answer Cancel. Don't ask again travels only with Keep running or Stop agents, and the asker writes `damocles.desktop.remote.closeWithRunningAgents`. A token note names a pi provider id (`PROVIDER_ID_PATTERN`), and the dialog shows its proper name from `PROVIDER_NAMES`, else the id.
- **The Sign-in needed card sends nothing without a click** (`webview/components/SignInPrompt.vue`). "Continue with my sign-in" posts `continueWithMySignIn` with the wait's request id only on its own click, and Stop is the chat's `cancelSession`. It is a dock prompt card, distinct from the connection banner's "Sign in to <host>", which is the SSH sign-in.
- **Allow background work renders the admin command from the daemon's user field, never from target text** (`overlay/components/AllowBackgroundWork.vue`): `sudo loginctl enable-linger <user>` as code, shown only on `{ result: 'refused', user }`, whose user the control parser and `linger.ts` both bound to `LINGER_USER` and the component writes as one POSIX shell word (single-quoted unless it is only `[A-Za-z0-9_.@%+=,-]`), since an Active Directory name holds `\`.
- **Damocles writes only under `.damocles`, except where the user runs the write** (the "writes only under `.damocles`" line in `CLAUDE.md`). On this computer: Manage Unsafe Repositories (the path joins git's global `safe.directory`, `ScmService.trustUnsafe`) and Add to .gitignore (the repository's `.gitignore`). On a target: Set up passwordless login (Damocles' own key line appended to the target user's `authorized_keys`, or `administrators_authorized_keys` on a Windows admin, `daemon/authorized-keys.ts`), Allow background work (`loginctl enable-linger` for the daemon's own user, `daemon/linger.ts`), Remove Damocles server (its own server files, and only when their boxes are checked the key line it installed and the linger it enabled, `main/remote/remove-server.ts`) and a host's own install folder (server packages in `<folder>/<user>`; Damocles creates `<folder>/<user>` and removes only its own entries there, never `<folder>`). Each is described in "Remote daemon and wire". Anything else Damocles writes on a target is under `~/.damocles`.
- **The close question counts chat runs only** (`CloseRemoteDialog.vue`, `closeRemote`). Terminals outlive the app on their host ("Remote terminals outlive their clients"), and Stop agents does not end them, so a running terminal alone never asks and its row never shows in the dialog.
- **Choose install folder after a `noexec` or `lowSpace` failure is a typed path** (`InstallFolderField.vue`, `needsInstallFolder` in `overlay/connect-text.ts`). The failure stops the connect before a daemon runs, and only a daemon browses a target's folders, so the field offers Browse only while the host is connected.
- **A host whose daemon of another protocol still runs agents shows the update banner in place of its connection banner** (`webview/components/HostUpdateBanner.vue`, in the shell's chat slot and in the chat page through `PanelHostLabel.update`). Both switches are main's: Switch now asks main's confirmation first.

## Remote daemon and wire

- **The wire is frozen where any app must reach any daemon** (`src/remote/`, `protocol-version.ts`). The codec's tags, the 13-byte PersistentProtocol header, the mux frame (channel id VQL, kind, flags, body), the three authentication messages, hello, welcome, `refused` with `protocol` and `unknown-token`, and the control commands `listRuns`, `shutdownWhenIdle`, `shutdownNow` and `mirror.sync` never change. The welcome advertises `FEATURES`. The app uses a contract a daemon of the same version may lack (a control command, an attach option, resume) only when `welcome.features` lists its name (`RemoteConnection.features`, checked with `features.includes`), and otherwise treats it as unsupported; the daemon checks no name, since hello carries no feature list. Every other contract change is either additive and named in `FEATURES`, or bumps `PROTOCOL_VERSION`. A value added to a closed list the older side parses strictly (a union of string literals, such as a message's `reason`) is a breaking change, so it bumps `PROTOCOL_VERSION` once `PROTOCOL_PRE_RELEASE` is false. While it is true no older peer exists, so a breaking change needs no bump until the first release that ships remote, and it is still named in `FEATURES`. `scripts/protocol-compat.mjs` describes the contracts with the TypeScript compiler (lib aliases such as `Record` and `Pick` by their members, other modules by repository-relative paths) into the committed `protocol-snapshot.json`: the handshake types and `FrozenControl` must equal the previous snapshot's whatever the version, a command, event, mirror or panel-op table may gain members, and any other contract may gain only optional fields. Writing the snapshot refuses a change that breaks those rules unless the committed snapshot is pre-release (`PROTOCOL_PRE_RELEASE`, true until the first release with remote features, never true again once false) and `--allow-breaking` is passed; `--check` fails unless the committed snapshot matches the contracts, and the pull request workflow also runs `--against` the base branch's committed snapshot, which fails an incompatible change once that snapshot is released. `src/remote/**` imports only Node built-ins (eslint), so the same files run in main and on every target.
- **Nothing a peer sends is trusted before its parser ran, and nothing it sends ends the daemon.** The codec checks every count and length against the bytes left and one per-message value budget before it allocates, copies bytes out of the frame and sets keys with `defineProperty`; PersistentProtocol refuses a header whose length passes the bound before buffering the body (64 KiB before authentication, which a welcome with every field at its limit fits); the mux refuses data past the granted credit, empty continued frames, unknown channels, flags on frames that are not data, channel ids not in their shortest form and more than 1024 open streams; every endpoint over a channel has an `onViolation` that closes that connection only. A throw while handling a message closes the connection, never the process. The sending side refuses what its peer would refuse: `channelTransport.post` throws a `CodecError` for a message past the channel's byte bound or the codec's value budget, which fails only that request. A disposed mux wakes every stream writer and message channel producer waiting for a drain and ends every stream reader, and a stream reports `closed`, so no stream user waits on a dead connection.
- **A dropped connection is held for its client, and both sides replay what the other has not acknowledged** (`persistent-protocol.ts`, `daemon/connections.ts`, `main/remote/connection.ts`, VS Code's `ipc.net.ts`). Once a client's `control.attach` landed (a drop before it fails the connect) and the daemon lists `protocol.resume`, a socket that closes or stays silent past the 20 s keepalive timeout (skipped while the event loop stalled) leaves the PersistentProtocol lost instead of closed: what it sends is only held as unacknowledged, the mux queues the rest behind it, and the endpoints, core channel, workspace and terminals stay as they are. The daemon holds such a connection for `RECONNECTION_GRACE_MS` (3 hours); a hello carrying its reconnection token and its client id (compared with `timingSafeEqual`) gets `welcome { resumed: true }` with the same token, after which the handshake's protocol detaches in the same turn (`sayHello`'s `resumedRest`, `acceptConnection`'s synchronous `accepted`) and the held protocol takes the socket and the bytes read past welcome, then resends every unacknowledged message. So no frame can reach the new socket before the held protocol owns it on either side, which is why the reserved Pause and Resume message types (7, 8) stay unused: they are this protocol's local lost and resumed states. The receiver drops an id it already has and acknowledges it within the ack interval (else a resumed peer with nothing new to hear would count its replay unacknowledged and turn suspect), and answers a gap with one ReplayRequest (type 6) per 10 s, which resends the unacknowledged queue; a side answers at most one ReplayRequest per 10 s, however often its peer asks. A connection inside its grace does not count as connected and keeps no daemon alive (`lifetime.ts` `connectionsChanged`). Toward core it reads as detached (`ClientLink.lost`, `core-routes.ts`): the daemon publishes its `clientAuth` without a value when it is lost and with its value when it resumes, so a turn pinned to it waits behind Sign-in needed and is asked again on the resume; the daemon's `clientAuth` and `actingClient` mirrors leave it out meanwhile (the acting client is another attached one, else none), so a core started in that window does not list it as attached; a `user.credentials` ask for it, or one it was answering when it was lost, answers `credential-client-absent`; a request or acting-client event that names it goes to the acting client, else another attached one, else is answered as with no client; and a `views.drained`, keyed to its view or not, does not wait for it, so a join to a device that drops mid-replay ends and frees the panel for the next join. What core posts and publishes to it still queues and goes out on the resume; after the grace, after its held frames passed `MAX_HELD_BYTES` (the next rule) or after a daemon restart, its token is unknown and the daemon answers `refused { reason: 'unknown-token' }`; the client then closes the held connection and attaches fresh over a second transport, the path every attach takes. The daemon closes a dropped connection instead of holding it once it ends its connections for a shutdown.
- **Every connection, live or dropped, holds at most `MAX_HELD_BYTES`, and every transcript replay is paced by the viewing client's channel** (`mux.ts`, `core-host/platform/panels.ts`, `history-loader.ts`, `core-routes.ts` `views.drained`). The mux counts the frames sent and not acknowledged plus every message queued on a channel, except the longest message queued on each message channel, since `MAX_DATA_CHARS` lets one message carry 64 MiB; a queue of ever shorter messages keeps that longest one at constant cost per enqueue. It checks the count on every enqueue and after every flush, since sending moves bytes into the unacknowledged ones. The exempt message is the longest, not the head: a message queued behind small ones that have credit is about to be the one being sent, and counting it made a rejoin's one large reply overflow by flush timing alone (measured: 24 B and 34 B ahead of a 5,400,081-byte message at a 2 MiB bound, nothing behind credit). A connection past the bound, live or dropped, closes, its token names nothing, and the client attaches fresh. That fresh attach always makes progress because nothing it causes is an unpaced burst: its snapshot is the materialized mirrors, and every transcript replay (views.join's live branch and live snapshot, the file replay of a restore, reload or resume) posts each message only after the panel host's `drained()` settled. A core panel proxy asks its host `views.drained` once `PACE_CHARS` (1 Mi JSON characters) went out since its last ask; the daemon answers once the viewing client's core channel queue is back within its 1 MiB window (`MessageChannel.drained`), at once for a view no client shows, and when the connection closes; desktop main answers at once. While an ask is out the proxy keeps only the newest streaming `partial` per stream and field set, and posts the kept ones on the answer or before the panel's next other message: each partial carries its stream's whole text so far, so a reply of S bytes in n deltas otherwise costs about n·S/2 bytes on the wire (2.6 MB in 40 deltas: about 53 MB). A slow client whose other traffic still passes the bound is dropped and resyncs through a fresh attach, never buffered without end. `views.drained` with a `viewKey` answers once that view's client channel is within its window; without one, once every viewing client's is; either way a client inside its grace is not waited for (one lost while the ask waits stops counting then); each view host paces its own posts. A failed `views.drained` ends that pause as an answer would, so a replay awaiting `drained()` goes on.
- **The client sees trouble before the timeout: the suspect state** (`persistent-protocol.ts` `onSuspect`). It tracks two deadlines, the next keepalive (5 s after the last frame received) and the ack of its oldest unacknowledged message (2 s after it was written, counted only while nothing arrived after that deadline); `SUSPECT_AFTER_MS` (2 s) past the earlier one with nothing received the connection is suspect with `since` = that deadline, and any frame clears it. The decision runs in the loop turn after the suspect timer's, past the poll phase that reads what arrived meanwhile, so a timer made late by a stalled event loop measures the silence only once those bytes count; it re-arms for the remainder when a frame came. Only the 20 s timeout decides a drop: a connect of a suspect host (a project selection, a launch, the Connect dialog) joins its connection, and only Reconnect now and Retry give up its transport for a new one (`RemoteConnections.connect`'s `force`). A suspect or lost connection is not usable: `Authorities.connection` and `connectedWorkspace` answer undefined, so new work takes the offline paths, while work sent before completes after a resume or fails with the final close, never twice.
- **The daemon proves the token and makes the peer prove it, without either side sending it** (`handshake.ts`). Before the bridge's proof checks out the daemon writes only its challenge answer, and it closes a connection that has not authenticated within 10 s; at most `MAX_UNAUTHENTICATED` (16) connections authenticate at once and another is closed at once (`connections.ts`), since any local user can open the Windows pipe. Proofs compare with `timingSafeEqual` after a length check, and the app refuses a welcome for another protocol version than its hello's. `acceptConnection` attaches the connection's mux and channels synchronously in `accepted`, never after an await, so a message pipelined behind hello is never read before its listener exists.
- **One daemon per user and host** (`singleton.ts`, `machine-id.ts`): proper-lockfile's mkdir lock at `~/.damocles/server/run-<machine key>/daemon.lock`, taken before the daemon writes its log, so a start that loses the race writes nothing there. The machine key is the first 16 hex digits of HMAC-SHA256 keyed by the machine id over a fixed Damocles label (systemd's rule for app-specific ids, so the id itself never lands in the home): `/etc/machine-id` then `/var/lib/dbus/machine-id` on Linux, `IOPlatformUUID` (`/usr/sbin/ioreg`) on macOS, `MachineGuid` (`reg.exe`, 64-bit view) on Windows, and the hostname only on a Linux system with neither file, as some containers are. It is read once per process, and the bridge, the daemon and the e2e support all get the folder from `localRunPaths`; the hostname, which macOS changes with the network, would let a bridge miss the running daemon and start a second one. The pid file names the hostname for another machine sharing the home to show. A start that finds the lock held exits at once with `EXIT_LOCK_HELD` while the holder answers on the endpoint its pid file names and proves the token (`daemonAnswers` in `attach.ts`, the bridge's own authentication), as single-instance apps probe the running instance. While the holder does not answer, and while Windows refuses the lock folder's mkdir with EPERM because a release is still removing it (measured: 48 of 2,000 races of mkdir against rmdir of one folder), the start waits for the lock up to `LOCK_WAIT_MS` (`LOCK_STALE_MS + LOCK_UPDATE_MS`, by when a crashed daemon's lock is stale, plus `SHUTDOWN_MAX_MS`) and probes again before each retry. A holder may be silent because it crashed, because it is still starting (it binds its endpoint after the login environment and git probe), or because it is shutting down. So the bridge's one start succeeds after a crash or beside a shutdown. Residual: a holder that answers the probe and then begins a shutdown before the bridge's hello (its idle exit, or `shutdownNow`) leaves that bridge with no daemon until the client attaches again. A shutdown closes the endpoint and deletes the pid file first, only while the file names this process, and releases the lock as its very last step before exit, so the waiting start never runs beside it. A compromised lock may already be another daemon's, so the daemon stops at once (`Daemon.lockLost`, `EXIT_LOCK_LOST`): it closes its endpoint and socket folder, removes its agent and relay folders, kills core, the pty host and the formatter hosts without their shutdown, closes its watchers and connections, and skips every shutdown step not yet run; it writes no state and leaves the pid file, the run folder's token (Windows) and the lock to the new owner. A rebind of the endpoint and its close run one at a time (`listen`): a close waits for a rebind in flight and closes what it bound, and no bind starts once the close began. The logs, state and pid file live in that run folder, never in a file another machine sharing the home writes. A stale socket folder is removed only under the lock and only when the pid file names a `damocles-*` folder whose socket is the endpoint, file by file then `rmdir`; a folder already gone counts as removed, and one it cannot empty is logged and left, since the new endpoint gets a new folder. On POSIX the socket and its token live in a mkdtemp folder (0700, the user's uid, lstat-checked before every connect, refused on NFS, SMB or 9P); on Windows the pipe name has 128 random bits and the run folder grants only the user's SID. Every POSIX socket folder the daemon owns (the endpoint's, the agent proxy's and the git credential relay's, each its own folder, so a removal empties only the names it made) is kept fresh by one touch every `TOUCH_INTERVAL_MS` (`keepSocketFoldersFresh`), since tmp cleaners remove old files, and is named in the run folder's `socket-folders` record, rewritten after the bind and after every rebind (`writeSocketFolders`; a failed write is logged), since the pid file names only the endpoint's: the next start removes the folders an earlier daemon recorded, under the lock and by the same rule (`removeRecordedSocketFolders`: a `damocles-*` folder that passes `checkSocketFolder`, only the names a daemon makes there, then `rmdir`), so a crashed daemon's agent and relay folders go too, and the removal stage reads the record; a folder or socket found removed is made again in a new folder (the relay's socket is touched only while a core listens there), and a new agent folder or relay handle reaches a running core in the daemon-owned `hostSockets` mirror (a client's is dropped) and a later core in its init, after which core injects the new agent sockets and its askpass server listens at the new relay handle (`AskpassServer.moveRelay`). A terminal started before the move keeps the old paths, which no longer answer.
- **A daemon's shutdown ends its client connections before it stops Damocles Core** (`daemon/serve.ts` `shutdown`: close the endpoint, close the agent proxy and remove the git credential relay's folder, end the connections, stop core, flush core's state, end the terminals, the formatter hosts and the watchers, release the lock), so a client's request in flight ends with its connection, which main answers as the host going away (a chat list lists what is loaded), never with a stopping core's error. A connection that asked `control.shutdownForRemoval` is kept from the moment its request arrives, also by a shutdown already under way that has not yet ended the connections, and ends only after the lock's release.
- **The daemon starts processes on its launcher, except its forks and the bridge's detached start** (`process-starts.ts`, the one file lint allows; `docs/perf.md` "Starts outside the launcher"). Its own environment has no `ELECTRON_*` variable and no `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` after start, dropped again after the login shell merge (`dropInheritedVariables`), so no device's forwarded agent from the ssh session that started the daemon reaches anything it spawns; `daemonChildEnv('node')` adds `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` for a fork only when the daemon runs under Electron, and `parentChannel()` drops it in the child.
- **A daemon holds no descriptor of the app that started it** (`startDaemonDetached`, `closeInheritedFds`). On Linux the loopback bridge inherits every Electron main descriptor without `FD_CLOEXEC` ("Concurrent starts on Windows" in `docs/perf.md`), and an exec passes them on, so it names its own such descriptors (`/proc/self/fdinfo`) in `DAMOCLES_INHERITED_FDS` and the daemon closes exactly those before it starts anything. A descriptor inherited across an exec keeps its number, so nothing the daemon opened itself can carry one of them. A daemon that kept them passed them to its core, its pty host and every shell in its terminals, which held main's sockets past the quit: its DevTools server and the connection Playwright drives it through never closed, so `app.close()` never returned while the daemon ran, and main's profile files (caches, LevelDB) stayed open in the daemon's processes.
- **Remote terminals outlive their clients** (`terminal-hub.ts`). The daemon's pty host acknowledges each chunk once its screen parsed it (`--self-acknowledge`), so no client paces a pty; each client has its own window, and one past `TERMINAL_HIGH_WATERMARK_CHARS` gets no live data until its acks bring it under `TERMINAL_LOW_WATERMARK_CHARS`, then a fresh `serialize` screen. Detaching or a dropped connection never kills one, and `killAll` and `shutdown` never cross the `workspace` channel; only `kill`, the idle limit (the latest attach's `terminalIdleDays`, 0 never) or `control.shutdownNow` does. A drop (suspect or lost) sets the authority's terminals `reconnecting`, whose input the shell page turns off; keys or a paste that reached main before it did are held per terminal and sent in order on a resume (`connectionResumed`) or after a fresh connection's reattach `serialize`, and dropped, with a log line, only when the terminal ended while its host was away (`TerminalService.connectionLost`); a resume sets them running again, their output carried on by the replay with no gap and no duplicate (`connectionResumed`), and a connection that closed for good leaves them reconnecting. A connection that closed without a drop first (Disconnect Host, a violation) exits its tabs. Every connection that comes up reattaches the terminals `remote-state.json` keeps for it through `serialize` (`reattachRemoteTerminals`), which closing an exited tab while the host is away does not drop; a reattached terminal takes its exited or reconnecting tab's place in its group and keeps the tab's name, icon and color. A terminal's backend is looked up again at each spawn, since each connection has a new workspace, and a spawn while its host is away fails with the reason. A terminal is the host's: any connection may attach a view by posting `serialize`, which sends it the screen and then live output, and any connection may kill it (the host card's Kill); only a connection that views it types into it, resizes it or acknowledges. Closing a tab of a remote terminal posts `detach` for that terminal alone (`TerminalService.detach`), which drops that view's window and starts the idle clock once no view is left; `terminals.detach` drops every view of the connection. The pty host reports no exit for a pty it killed, so the hub sends the exit to every viewer, the killer's connection included (`end`).
- **A device opens a terminal another device started from the host card** (`TerminalService.open`, `RemoteHosts.runs`, `hosts.openRun`). A remote spawn carries a `TerminalView` (the root key's part after the authority key, which every client shares, the profile, the start folder and the shell-integration nonce; features `terminals.view`, `terminals.detachView`), and the daemon lists it with `terminals.list`. `HostRun.canOpen` is true only when no tab of the terminal here has not exited, the daemon recorded its view and its root is open on this device; `open()` uses the same rule, selecting a live tab instead of adding one, else adding the tab as a reattach does. Each device keeps which remote terminals it shows, and their name, icon and color, in its own `remote-state.json`. The nonce crosses to the daemon's other clients, as VS Code's `IProcessDetails.shellIntegrationNonce` does: they are the same account's, and each may already type into the terminal.
- **A target's watcher that runs out of inotify watches is reported, never worked around** (`file-watcher-factory.ts` `startInotifyTree`, `onDidReachWatchLimit`; `daemon/workspace-host.ts`; `main/remote/watch-limit.ts`). A directory whose watch fails with `ENOSPC` is logged and skipped, and the factory reports its watched root once while it stays watched. The daemon sends `files.watchLimit { root, limit }` to every connection and answers `files.watchLimits` with the roots already reported (feature `files.watchLimit`; `limit` from `/proc/sys/fs/inotify/max_user_watches`, null when unreadable); main reads both once per connection. A root inside one of this computer's projects on the host raises one notification per host per connection, naming the project and `sudo sysctl fs.inotify.max_user_watches=524288`, and the host card's `watchLimit` notice for that connection only. The probe's `lowInotify` reading is only logged (`RemoteConnections`). Damocles changes nothing on the host.
- **Another machine running Damocles in the target's home is told, never acted on** (`token.ts` `otherHostsInHome`, `control.sharedHome`, `main/remote/shared-home.ts`). The daemon answers `control.sharedHome` (feature `control.sharedHome`, on the control channel since it concerns the daemon's home, not a workspace) with the hostnames other run folders' pid files name, of those touched within `SHARED_HOME_WINDOW_MS`: each once, sorted, at most `MAX_SHARED_HOME_HOSTS`, and only those `OTHER_HOSTNAME` accepts, which the client's parser checks again, since another machine wrote them. Main asks once per connection; a non-empty answer raises one notification per host per connection, whose Show host opens the host card (`openConnect(hostId, 'show')`), and the card's `sharedHome` notice for that connection only. The hostnames cross as the notice's `hosts` param joined by `SHARED_HOME_SEPARATOR` and render as text. Neither text says NFS, since nothing reads the home's file system type for it.
- **Main builds every page; a core only names it** (`PanelHost.showPage`, the `showPage` panel op, `src/shared/panel-html.ts`, `src/shared/browser-page-html.ts`). A desktop core sends `{ kind: 'chat' | 'error' }` or `{ kind: 'browser', toolbar }`, never HTML: its panel proxy answers `themeCssSource()` with `''` and throws from `setHtml`, and main builds the page with its own theme, nonce, resource URIs and strings through the shared builders, so no HTML a core produced, a remote one included, is ever loaded. The VS Code host keeps `setHtml` and builds through the same builders. `src/shared/__tests__/panel-html.test.ts` pins the three CSPs.
- **A daemon's Damocles Core is the daemon's child, and the daemon alone builds its init** (`daemon/remote-core-host.ts`). The first `control.attachCore` forks `dist/core-host.js` with `daemonChildEnv('node')`; `CoreInit` takes that client's app info, secrets persistence and browser pane setting, remembered for restarts, and everything else from the daemon: its git probe, its package's paths (askpass `node` is the daemon's `process.execPath`, and `credentialRelay` the daemon's git credential relay, which no local core gets), logs and key-value state in the run folder (`daemon-state.ts`), the ended cores and worktree records it recorded, and `remote: { hostId }` with a host id it draws itself, never a client's. A crash restarts core by `local-services/restart-policy.ts`, each start under a new 128-bit epoch, and at most one core runs: restarts and reloads that overlap each stop the old core, and only the first start after its exit forks, and `control.coreState` and its pushed event report it. With `CoreInit.remote` core deletes permanently with VS Code's wording where it would trash, reads no AI login of the target (its credentials come from the token broker), and its settings store reads the acting client's `clientUser` settings as the user layer and `~/.damocles/settings.json` as the machine layer (`machineValue`, never for an application scoped key or a voice key, which run on the client; a machine scoped key from no layer) and writes the file only for a user-home setter reached from the settings overlay's host tab (`settings-view-scope.ts`).
- **A daemon's core takes everything user scope from its clients, keyed by each device's stable client id** (`core-routes.ts` `perClient`, `core-host/platform/clients.ts`). A client is named toward core by its hello's `clientId`, drawn once per app install, never by its connection: the connection only routes an ask, so a device that quits and relaunches, or drops and attaches fresh, comes back under the same id with its pinned turns, broker cache and mirrors, and another device's attach never serves them. The `clientAuth`, `clientUser` and `clientPolicy` mirrors carry the id the daemon stamps (a client's own is replaced); a `clientAuth` delta with a value marks the device attached and one without marks it detached (`ClientSnapshots.attached`). The daemon publishes `actingClient` before the message that made a client act, and core's endpoint runs each served request and panel message under its `actor` (`ClientSnapshots.actor`, `currentActor`); `ClientSnapshots.acting` is the actor while that client is attached, else the client that sent core a message last, since a timer or watcher made inside a served request keeps its actor for life. A turn pins the client whose request or panel message started it (its actor, else the acting client), with that client's user rules and policy, at its start (`client-snapshots.ts` `runPinnedTurn`, an `AsyncLocalStorage`); sub-calls run under `runPinnedSubcall`. A policy check outside a turn reads the issuing client's `clientPolicy`, and a turn's tools read the snapshot pinned at its start, so a deny rule only another device holds never changes it. A chat's settings view is its device's: core names it by the actor of its `settingsView.attach` (`AttachedView.clientId`). Each device's settings view attaches to the same chat at once: `PanelManager` keeps one per device and panel, a device's next attach replaces only its own, `dispatchFromView` runs a request only from a view still attached, and a host prompt shows only in the settings view of the device it asks (`PromptTarget.clientId`). The default model (`damocles.model`) is read per call, never cached (`ModelManager`), so a new chat starts on the default of the device that opened it, and a classifier's credential for the memory judge is the client's `clientAuth` entry or the host's own, never pi's snapshot of the acting client's (`PiRuntime._hasCredential`, as the model picker reads it). Which posts are per device, and how they reach that device alone, is the next rule. The Claude mode a view or settings view gets is `PiRuntime.claudeAuthStatus`, the device's sign-in and allowance choice and whether the bundled plugin loaded, never `readClaudeAuthFromDisk`, which takes the plugin from the host's pi `settings.json`. A detach drops the client's `clientUser` and `clientPolicy`, which a pin holds by value, and keeps its last `clientAuth` (`clients.ts`), which every pinned read takes live (the allowance transport, key presence, account state), so a turn that outlives its client's connection goes on billing as that client chose. The settings store reads the pinned (else acting) client's `clientUser` settings on every read (`DesktopSettingsStore.inspect`), never a copy, so another client acting mid-turn never changes a running turn's settings. pi's credential store is `BrokeredCredentialStore` (`credential-broker.ts`): it never calls `modify`'s `fn`, asks only the pinned client and only inside a run's pin (`user.credentials`, which the daemon routes to that client or refuses with `credential-client-absent`), caches per client and provider while an OAuth token outlives pi's 5 minutes and an API key until a `clientAuth` delta changes that provider, never caches an answer asked for before such a delta (a generation per client and provider), keeps a detached device's cached credentials while a turn is pinned to it (`pinnedClients`: a turn's pin is held until its run, and every run started inside it, settles) and drops every entry of that device once the last such turn settles, so a turn that later pins to it asks again, drops all of them on `dispose`, and holds no answer that arrives after it, and refuses `delete`. A read outside every pin gets the client's `clientAuth` entry without a secret, which is what pi's availability checks of every provider read: they run under `outsidePins`, since `ModelRuntime.create` does, and `PiRuntime._doInit` makes the runtime's `refresh` always run there, which covers the pass pi starts itself on every provider registration or unregistration and session bind, wherever its caller runs; both `createAgentSessionServices` calls in `folder-runtime.ts`, a folder's and a subagent's, which a turn's Agent, team and worktree tools make inside its pin, run under it as a whole. Every pi session a panel builds, main, subagent, team or aside, pins each run it starts outside every pin at its start (`pinRunStarts`; pi starts every run in `prompt` or `sendCustomMessage`), so a cancel note or an extension's `triggerTurn` on an idle session asks as a turn and never sends the status-only placeholder. `clientAuth` crosses machines with each OAuth expiry as `expiresIn`: main keeps its own clock's `expires` and converts at each send (`sentClientAuth`), and `core-host/platform/clients.ts` stamps `Date.now() + expiresIn` as the snapshot arrives (`heldClientAuth`); its `flatFee` marks a key provider whose key bills flat, which `flatFeeKeyProviders` reads there, and its `allowance` an OAuth sign-in the client bills to its subscription's allowance. Sign-ins are the client's: every `PiRuntime` sign-in, sign-out and key setter, the Claude subscription's billing switch (`setSubscriptionBilling`, which would install or remove pi's subscription plugin in the target's settings for every client of the daemon), and the OpenAI key routes before their probe, refuse with "Sign-ins change only in the User tab on your computer." before any pi call or network use, and `resolveCredentialForBroker` refuses. `setProviderPreferApiKey`, route and `PiRuntime` method, refuses too, before any state write, and so do the `setProviderApiKey` and `clearProviderApiKey` routes in their ack, before any secret write, and the Claude auth handlers before they take the core's sign-in lock (`runOp`). A remote chat follows the client's precedence rule because the broker resolves through the client's runtime, whose runtime key already follows it. Its key status (`providerAuthStatusChanged`) takes `configured` from the client's `clientAuth` `keySaved` (feature `client.keySaved`: the key is saved in Damocles there, even one its sign-in outranks, which `hasKey`, pi's applied key, does not say; a client that sent none yet falls back to `hasKey`, which keeps driving the broker's cache), `signedIn` and `signedInExpiresAt` from the client's `oauth` entry, and `preferApiKey` from the client's `clientAuth` `preferApiKey` (feature `client.preferApiKey`), and omits `active` (and `openaiAuthStatusChanged` its `active`), which only the client's core can compute (feature `account.providerPrecedence`). The daemon's core never installs, lists, reads or loads a subscription plugin among the host's pi packages: its pi settings managers hide every subscription plugin source (`withSettingsPolicy`), so pi neither clones nor loads the host's copy, whose `anthropic` registration would shape every client's requests, and its folders skip the plugin reconcile. It bills through the copy its server package bundles (`dist/subscription-plugin`, "Server packages" in `docs/release.md`): at init it loads that copy through pi's extension loader, with the plugin's own folder as cwd and agent dir so nothing else loads, keeps its `anthropic` transport without registering it, and registers its own `anthropic` provider (`allowance-transport.ts`), whose transport decides per request: while the pinned (else acting) client's `clientAuth` marks its Claude OAuth sign-in `allowance`, the bundled plugin's transport, else pi's own Anthropic transport. Every Anthropic request goes through pi's model runtime (the agent loop, compaction, `runStructuredCompletion` and `runClassification`, subagents and team members), each under its run's pin, so two clients' concurrent runs each bill as their own client chose. A bundled copy that fails to load is logged, every request takes pi's transport (extra usage), and the account state says so; nothing downloads or clones a plugin on the host. No key is ever a pi runtime override there (`syncOpenAIRuntimeKey`, `syncCustomProviders`), because pi reads those before any store; key presence and `readAuthFile` come from the pinned client's `clientAuth`. YOLO stays off while the pinned policy refuses it, at every write of `PermissionHandler`'s state and again at each decision. Client user permission rules (one list per client file, naming the file's folder) join the host's home files as extra sources, deny > ask > allow, and a `/path` rule in one is relative to the host's folder of the same name (`.damocles` or `.claude`). A tool whose user-scope service is the client's (the browser) stays eligible and deferred but leaves every loadable snapshot and is blocked at the gate with the user-scope reason (`USER_SCOPE_UNAVAILABLE`, `user-scope.ts`); the browser itself refuses with that reason before it launches Chrome or creates a profile on the target (`BrowserService.ensureContext`), and so do the chat page's open and pick. The memory store's connection carries TEMP triggers that abort any statement adding, changing or deleting a global memory or profile row; every memory read leaves global rows out in its own query (search, so a global row takes no result slot, the panel, details, version history, graph neighbours, extraction's existing memories, and injection's mentioned ids and their chain heads); every write path leaves them out in its own statement (`localOnly`, `memory/local-only.ts`), so maintenance and background work go on for the target's project rows only: episode decay and promotion, the purge, the conflict re-check sweep, the search-term backfill, the stored-path normalization, file-change staleness, a session id rename, the global bucket's retrieval pruning and the quality audit (it grades no global row or profile, proposes no rescope to global, and shows, counts, applies or reverts no proposal another core made about a global row or profile); and an operation that names a global row by id (edit, delete, forget, unforget, pin, history, neighbours, an observation's staleness reset), and listing notes, are refused with the user-scope reason before they reach the write queue. The whole-store copies (the legacy v2 import and an integrity salvage) keep every row, since a store left without the target's global rows would never get them back.
- **Per-device content reaches only its device's views, built as that device's client** (`chat-panel/device-hosts.ts`, `PiSession.emitPerClient`, `ChatPanelProvider.broadcastAccountState`). Each device has its own user layer, account, sign-ins, credentials and policy. A post is per device when it reads any of them or answers one device's request: the settings state (`settingsUpdate`, `panelThinkingUpdate`, `modelUpdate`, `mcpServerStatus`, `toolStatus`, `imageGenerationSettings`, `hostCapabilities`), the account state (`accountInfo`, `availableModels`, and the account rows `claudeAuthStatusChanged`, `providerAuthStatusChanged`, `openaiAuthStatusChanged` and `memoryJudgeStatus`), and a request's ack, error, refusal or progress (every Claude auth error, the ChatGPT sign-in's started, completed and failed notices, a model pick's `openaiAuthRequired`, every `/btw` message, a send's give-back, which `send-senders.ts` routes). Each is built as its device's client and reaches that device's views alone, by one of four routes. `forEachDevice` posts once per device of a panel, each under `asClient`. `PiSession.emitPerClient` builds per view through `SessionOptions.onViewMessages`, and with a client id for that device alone (the actor's, for a model pick's `openaiAuthRequired` and every message of a `/btw` aside). `ChatPanelProvider.broadcastAccountState`, which handlers reach as `HandlerDependencies.broadcastAccountState`, posts each account row per device and keeps each row's newest read per device, so an older read never replaces a newer one and a re-send to one device never drops another device's read in flight. A reply goes to `ctx.reply`, the asking view, and a handler's notice for the asking device to that device's views in every panel (`toAsker`: `deviceHost(panel, reply.clientId)`). Each such read takes a per-client source, `clientUser`, `clientPolicy` or `clientAuth` (which carries the client's prefer-API-key flags), never the `state` mirror's `CLIENT_STATE_KEYS`, which hold the acting client's values alone. A change to one device's `clientUser` settings, whether that device acts or not, fires the settings store's change naming that device (`SettingsChange.clientId`, from `remoteSettings` in `core-host/local.ts`), and each sender re-sends to that device's views alone (`hostForChange` for settings, thinking and the model, `publishAccountInfo(clientId)`, `broadcastMemoryJudge(clientId)`); a change of the acting client fires with no device, and a `clientAuth` delta or a new acting client re-sends every device's account rows, each read as its device. A chat's settings view gets the posts to every view and to its own device's views (`PanelHost.clientId`), never one to another device's, and its requests are answered on its device alone (`settingsViewReply` in `message-router/index.ts`). Each row has one publisher: the Memory judge settings are watched by `ChatPanelProvider` alone, and `PiRuntime.onMemoryJudgeChange` fires only for the runtime's own inputs. What is the chat's or the host's stays one post to every view: the mode, YOLO, the chat's own effort, the conversation and session state, the session list and prompt history, MCP config, Compass, memory, the folders, and the core's single sign-in lock and flows (`claudeAuthBusy`, `claudeSignInWaiting`, `providerSignInState`), which a daemon's core never starts, since every sign-in handler refuses there first. A local core and VS Code have one device, where each route posts once to every view.
- **A turn whose device is away waits visibly, and moves to another device only on that device's click** (`credential-broker.ts` `waitForSignIn`, `permission-handler/state.ts` `pendingSignIns`). When the broker must ask (no entry, or one inside pi's 5-minute minimum) and the daemon answers that the pinned device is not attached (`credential-client-absent`, also for an ask whose connection closed, was replaced or was lost while it was out, and for a device inside its reconnection grace), a turn waits and its chat raises one `signIn` prompt per pin and provider on its own `PermissionState` (`signInRequest`, kind `signIn`), which only `publishSessionState` publishes, so it reaches `chat.activity` (`waiting`) and the run registry. A provider whose last `clientAuth` holds nothing for it answers none at once (the target's own credential), and a structured sub-call never waits: it fails with the cause `credential`. The wait ends when the pinned device attaches again (the broker asks it again before anything else, with an ask of its own: an ask made while the device was away answers absent, so a re-ask never joins one), and each later attach asks again, so the wait lasts until the device answers or the wait ends another way, when that device pushes a token for that provider, on Stop (the run's signal, which pi passes to every credential read), or when the chat closes (`clearAll` cancels it); each end posts `signInResolved`. "Continue with my sign-in" (`continueWithMySignIn`, explicit click only) moves the waiting turn to the device whose panel message carried the click (`ClientSnapshots.actor`, no fallback), and only to an attached one: the turn's credentials, billing and account state follow it (`repin`), its user rules and policy stay as pinned at its start. The card shows on every view, since it exists only while the pinned device is away; no client id reaches a view.
- **A client pushes only its own access tokens, and only to a daemon it is attached to** (`user.credentials.push`, `keep-running-refresh.ts`, `PiRuntime.acceptPushedCredential`). The push is one OAuth access token for one provider, bounded like a broker answer, `refresh` exactly `''` and no key (a cached key never expires, so it crosses only when a run asks); the daemon stamps the pushing connection's client id as its actor, and core lands it only in that client's broker entry and resumes only that client's waits, keeping it only while the client is attached or a turn is pinned to it. At a Keep running close main reads the runs this device started on each connected host (`control.listOwnRuns`, or every run from the frozen `listRuns` of a daemon without it), force-refreshes on this computer exactly the OAuth providers those runs reported (`user.resolveCredential { force }`, which makes pi refresh under its lock), gives a row whose refresh failed its token's minutes left, and pushes each fresh token, as soon as its own refresh is done, only to the hosts whose runs reported it, over this app's own connection, waiting at most 5 s (`PUSH_TIMEOUT_MS`); an OS shutdown or an update's quit does the same with no dialog within the shutdown window (`keepRunningAtShutdown`). No refresh token leaves this computer. This computer checks each ask (`UserRelays.credentials`) against its sign-ins only once the read a local core start makes has landed, so an ask right after launch is never refused against an empty snapshot.
- **Allow background work is a user-run write outside `.damocles`** (`daemon/linger.ts`, `control.linger`). It runs only on Linux. When logind already records linger for the daemon's own user (`/var/lib/systemd/linger/<user>`, which stage 1's `check_linger` reads too) the daemon runs nothing and answers `alreadyEnabled`, and main records `lingerEnabledByDamocles` only for `enabled`, so Remove Damocles server never offers to undo an admin's linger. Otherwise it runs `loginctl --no-ask-password enable-linger <its own user>` at the absolute path `posixToolAsync('loginctl')` finds, through the launcher, never with sudo, in the C locale on that call only; polkit's refusal answers `{ result: 'refused', user }` and the client builds the admin command `sudo loginctl enable-linger <user>` from that user name, never from text the target sent. `LINGER_USER` is systemd's relaxed user name rule, so SSSD's and Active Directory's `@` and `\` names pass: one file name, not numeric, never option-like and never holding a control, format or line separator character. Any other failure answers `failed` with a code (`spawn`, `timeout`, `exit<n>`, `signal-<name>`, `userInfo`), logged. It is one of the user-run exceptions to "writes only under `.damocles`" that "Desktop host" lists.
- **Set up passwordless login is a user-run write outside `.damocles` on a target**, one of the exceptions "Desktop host" lists (`main/remote/ssh/key-setup.ts`, `daemon/authorized-keys.ts`, `ssh.installAuthorizedKey`; D9 layer 2). Main generates Damocles' own key `~/.damocles/ssh/id_ed25519` (never under `~/.ssh`) with `ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -N '' -C damocles@<client>` at the absolute path `resolveSshKeygenExecutable` finds, through its launcher; the daemon accepts only a public key line `DAMOCLES_PUBLIC_KEY` matches (`ssh-ed25519`, its blob and a `damocles@` comment: no options, no line break) and appends it for its own user as ssh-copy-id does: on POSIX `~/.ssh` made 0700 and owned by the user, `authorized_keys` opened for append with mode 0600, its owner checked, the key appended only when no line holds its type and blob (a newline first when the file lacks a final one), and `restorecon` when SELinux tools are present; on Windows an administrator (`whoami /groups` lists S-1-5-32-544) gets `%ProgramData%\ssh\administrators_authorized_keys` and any other user `%USERPROFILE%\.ssh\authorized_keys`, then `icacls <file> /reset` and `icacls <file> /inheritance:r /grant:r` with SIDs only (Administrators and SYSTEM, or the user and SYSTEM, the account sshd reads it as; SIDs since the group names are localized), through `windowsSystemTool('icacls')` on the daemon's launcher, so no explicit grant an earlier file held survives. Between the two, `administrators_authorized_keys` gets `icacls /setowner *S-1-5-32-544`, since Win32-OpenSSH's `check_secure_file_permission` refuses a file whose owner is neither Administrators, SYSTEM nor the user logging in. Only a non-administrator's install asks `whoami /user`, whose SID may be of any authority (Microsoft Entra ID's `S-1-12-1-...` included). Lines are read as sshd reads them: options first unless the line starts with a key type, a quoted option value never counts as the key, and sshd takes the first line holding it, so when that line carries `command=`, `from=`, `restrict`, `expiry-time=` or `cert-authority` the daemon writes nothing and answers `restricted`, which the dialog explains. Every failure is a typed code the dialog maps, logged, never a thrown error: `ownership`, `notFile`, `writeFailed` (a folder or file that cannot be made, read or written), `aclFailed` (icacls failed or did not start) and `accountFailed` (whoami failed, did not start or named no user SID, before anything is written). Before anything else main asks `ssh -G` for the host (`buildSshConfigQueryArgs`, so the saved options and the user's ssh config both count) and refuses one whose PreferredAuthentications lacks publickey or lists password or keyboard-interactive before it (`publickeyNotPreferred`), since every connect would then prompt while the check, which forces publickey, passed; an `ssh -G` that names none means it is unset, so ssh's default order (`gssapi-with-mic,publickey,keyboard-interactive,password`) applies; no ssh, or an `ssh -G` that fails, fails with `configUnreadable` before anything is written. An install whose connection closed before the daemon answered fails with `notConnected` (`HostNotConnectedError`, raised only on the connection's close); any other failed install, a daemon-side error included, fails with `installFailed`, logged. A private key whose `.pub` is missing gets it back from `ssh-keygen -y -f` with Damocles' comment; a `.pub` without its private key fails with `keyPairIncomplete`, naming the file for the user to delete; two setups at once share one generation. Main then verifies in the connection's argument order with `-o BatchMode=yes -o PreferredAuthentications=publickey -o ControlMaster=no -o ControlPath=none -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o IdentityFile=<key>` ahead of the saved host's options (a saved or configured IdentityFile is still offered after Damocles' key, so the check proves the host takes a key without a password, not which key), under a `background` registration of main's askpass server (`SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=force`, every question answered cancel), since ssh does not pass BatchMode to a ProxyJump child, whose question would otherwise reach the desktop's own askpass or a tty. Only then does it record `identityFile` in the host store, which passes it on every later ssh command line through `ssh-args.ts`. The host store records the installed type and blob, file and fingerprint as soon as the daemon reports it appended the line (`installed`, not `present`), before the check, so Remove Damocles server offers to remove only a key Damocles installed, from the file it wrote, even when the check then failed.
- **Remove Damocles server undoes only what Damocles did** (`main/remote/remove-server.ts`, `bootstrap/remove-posix.sh`, `remove-windows.ps1`, `control.shutdownForRemoval`). The daemon answers the run key, shuts down as `shutdownNow` does and ends the asking connection only after releasing its lock, so that close is the exit signal; main then runs the removal stage as stage 0 runs stage 1, its parameters only through `shellQuote`/`psQuote` and the run key validated in main and in the script (exactly 16 hex digits). Main also takes a dropped connection as the end of its wait, and stops waiting after `REMOVAL_CLOSE_WAIT_MS` (15 s), so the stage decides by the lock: it takes `run-<machine key>/daemon.lock` itself (mkdir, as proper-lockfile does) and stops with `daemonRunning` before deleting anything while a daemon holds it (touched within `DAEMON_LOCK_STALE_S`, singleton.ts `LOCK_STALE_MS`; an older one it takes over) or this machine's pid file names a live process, and holds it across that check and the run folder's delete, touching it after each socket folder and each run folder entry it deletes as the daemon touches its own, so no daemon starts in between or takes it as stale (residual: one entry whose delete alone outlasts `DAEMON_LOCK_STALE_S`). A stage that refused with `daemonRunning` is kept, and Try again runs the same script again over a fresh ssh (`RemoteHosts.retryRemoval`), which attaches and starts no daemon, so the lock decides again. It deletes the socket folders the run folder records (the pid file's and `socket-folders`, by the daemon's rule), `run-<machine key>/` (the lock last), and, under each folder's install lock (taken as stage 1 takes it, without waiting), every `<version>-<target>` folder whose version is version-shaped and which holds `server-package.json` and which no other machine's pid file touched within 1500 minutes names (`versionKept` names that machine), `.staging-*`, `.exec-test-*` older than a minute and `install.lock` with a stale takeover lock, in `~/.damocles/server` and in `<folder>/<user>` of the install folder and the previous one; a folder whose install lock a live stage holds keeps its entries but staging folders older than a day (`installInProgress`). An entry it cannot delete is reported (`deleteFailed`) and the stage carries on. `~/.damocles/server` and `<folder>/<user>` go only when empty, `<folder>` never. An empty user name fails it before anything is deleted. The key line goes only when its box is checked and only from the recorded file (a key file it cannot read or rewrite fails with `stageFailed` code `keyFile` on both platforms, keeping the kept lines beside it; main takes only the `stageFailed` codes the scripts send, `keyFile`, `runKey` and `userName`, and adds `protocol`, `killed` and `exit` with `exitCode`, each with its own text), and `loginctl --no-ask-password disable-linger` runs only when its box is checked, at the absolute path `linger.ts` uses (`/bin`, then `/usr/bin`); each box shows only when the host store recorded that Damocles made that change (`installedKeyFingerprint`, `lingerEnabledByDamocles`).
- **Each attached client's policy is the host's, from this computer's host store** (`UserRelays`, `client-snapshots.ts`). Main sends each attach `clientPolicy { allowBypass, defaultPermissionMode?, agentForwarding? }` from the host's settings and republishes it on a change (`UserRelays.policyChanged`). Core starts a new chat in the acting client's `defaultPermissionMode` for the host, else `damocles.permissionMode`; the bypass refusal reads the issuing client's, or a pinned turn's, policy; a YOLO panel's turn for a client whose policy refuses bypass runs in that client's default mode for the host, else the panel's mode (`PermissionHandler.turnPermissionMode`), which every plan mode check reads: the gate's plan blocks and read-only shell rule, ExitPlanMode, and the plan guidance and plan-mode notices the model gets. The active tool set (`PLAN_MODE_EXCLUDED_TOOLS`) follows the chat's own mode, which pi applies when the mode changes, not per turn, so such a turn keeps EnterPlanMode and the team tools; plan mode's write and shell blocks still apply to it. `HostCapabilities.bypassPermissions` is required (VS Code and a local desktop core true, a daemon's core the viewing client's `allowBypass`), and a `clientPolicy` change posts `hostCapabilities` again to that client's views.
- **A target's git reaches this computer's credential manager only through the relay, only for an https `get`, and only after consent** (`core/scm/askpass/credential-relay.ts`, `askpass-server.ts`, `desktop/askpass/askpass-main.ts` `credential`, `resources/git-credential/`, `main/remote/git-credential-client.ts`; D16, D20). Only a daemon's core relays. The daemon makes the relay once at its start, before any terminal or core exists (`serve.ts` `createCredentialRelay`): a fresh 32-byte nonce, the package's helper and a handle (a socket in a private 0700 folder from `createSocketFolder` on POSIX, removed at shutdown, on a lost lock and when the start fails after making it; a pipe with a 128-bit random name on Windows). Every core it starts gets it in `CoreInit.paths.askpass.credentialRelay` (feature `askpass.daemonRelay`; the request below is `askpass.credential`) and its askpass server listens at that handle (on POSIX it first removes the socket an ended core left, since the folder is private and one core runs at a time), so a terminal started before a core, or across a core restart, keeps a working relay. When that listen fails, core withholds the relay from its git calls and agent shells, logs it in the Git log, and listens again when the next one wants it (`core-host/scm.ts`); terminals keep the daemon's variables and their helper fails with "Damocles could not provide credentials." until core listens. The server accepts that nonce only for a `credential` request, drops a prompt that carries it and drops a `credential` request carrying any other nonce. The helper is `credential.helper=!<shQuote'd absolute path>` of the package's `dist/git-credential/git-credential-relay.sh` (the `.cmd` on a Windows target; git runs a `!` helper through its shell, Git for Windows' sh included, so a single-quoted path with spaces works on both), the last `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` pair, after `ENGINE_CONFIG`, the call's own pairs and any pairs the environment already holds, so git asks the target's own helpers first. The git runner adds it, with `DAMOCLES_ASKPASS_NODE`, `DAMOCLES_ASKPASS_MAIN`, `DAMOCLES_ASKPASS_HANDLE` and `DAMOCLES_CREDENTIAL_NONCE`, only to a `CREDENTIAL_SUBCOMMANDS` call (fetch, pull, push, clone, ls-remote, submodule; as `-c` on git before 2.31) the user started (`GitCall.relayCredentials`: a remote operation or a clone), never to autofetch, which like its askpass never asks the user; the agent's Bash and PowerShell (`PiSession.shellOptions`, pi's `spawnHook` and the PowerShell tool's explicit environment) and the daemon's terminals (`workspace-host.ts` `terminals.environment`, `daemonCredentialRelay`) get the same variables (`spawnCredentialVariables`), never `DAMOCLES_ASKPASS_NONCE`, `GIT_ASKPASS` or `SSH_ASKPASS`. While no core listens, the helper fails with "Damocles could not provide credentials.". `process.env` and the target's `.gitconfig` are never written. The helper reads git's description within 64 KiB, drops `store` without connecting, answers a description without a host (git's `protocol=cert` client-certificate prompt) with nothing and exit 0, as git expects of a helper that cannot help, and sends `get` or `erase` as one JSON line `{ nonce, kind: 'credential', op, cwd, fields }` holding only protocol, host, path and username, never a password; an `erase` adds `passwordHash`, the SHA-256 of the password git names in it, which git 2.4x sends and older git may not. Every hop refuses a line break or NUL in a field (`shared/git-credential.ts`). The `.cmd` helper sets `DisableDelayedExpansion`, since a registry `DelayedExpansion=1` would eat a `!` in its paths; residual: git starts it through cmd without `/d`, so a user's cmd `AutoRun` that prints writes onto git's credential output. Core reports whether the target's git has a helper of its own (`git config --get-all credential.helper` and `git config --get-urlmatch credential.helper <url>`: every scope in the helper's folder when `ScmService.trustedProjectAt` names a trusted project for it (the registered project nearest above it, else the project of an open repository rooted there, as for a project opened at a repository's subfolder), else only `--system --includes` and `--global --includes`, since a named file reads no include otherwise, run from a fresh empty folder with its parent as `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, so no repository, a dotfiles home included, is discovered; a probe that fails, and one made while a project's realpath is still resolving (`PROJECTS_RESOLVING`, logged), counts as a helper, so the warning shows) and asks `askpass.credential { op, fields, targetHelper }`, which the daemon routes to the acting client (the request's actor while attached and outside its reconnection grace, else the client that sent core a message last, else another attached client). Unlike the token broker, which stays with a turn's pinned device, the relay deliberately follows the acting client: a request after the turn's device detached reaches another attached device, whose own user consents and whose own credential manager answers; with none attached it answers `notConnected`, and the helper writes exactly "Damocles is not connected. Git credentials from your computer are unavailable." to stderr. Main answers only `https` with a host name or IPv4 address and an optional port, compared lowercased. The first `get` per pair of target host and credential host shows the consent dialog (`gitCredentialConsent`: the host's name and tone from this computer's host store, never from the target, the credential host and the warning when the target has its own helper; Allow once, Always for <host>, Deny; dismissed is Deny; one dialog per pair at a time, which a request needing the warning shares only when it shows the warning, and which is withdrawn only once every request waiting on it went away), Always lands in the host store's `alwaysCredentialHosts` (an Always that cannot be saved is logged and answers as Allow once), and only then main runs this computer's `git credential fill` (`resolveGitExecutable`'s path, main's launcher, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, a sign-in through main's askpass server and the askpass bridge, from a fresh empty folder with its parent as `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` and `childEnv()`'s environment, so neither main's working folder nor an inherited `GIT_DIR` makes it read a repository's config; the folder is removed after) and answers the username and password alone, each bounded. A target's `store` never leaves it, and its `erase` never runs this computer's credential manager. git sends `erase` to every helper, also when one of the target's own gave the refused credential, so core passes an erase on only when its `passwordHash` equals the hash of the password the relay last answered for that credential host, once (`createRelayedPasswords`); an erase naming no password is never passed on, and core logs that once. Main heeds an erase only from a daemon whose welcome lists `askpass.credential.verifiedErase`: it drops that pair's Always and, for a pair this run relayed, raises a notice whose "Remove the saved credential on this computer" runs `git credential reject`, only on the user's click, with what this run relayed (protocol, host, path and the username fill answered, never the erase's). A remote core therefore never changes this computer's credentials. Accepted risk (D20): a helper of the target's own saves a relayed credential there, which the dialog warns about.
- **The forwarded SSH agent reaches only processes Damocles spawns, only while forwarding is on, and only the agent of the device they work for** (`client-snapshots.ts` `agentSocketPath`, `daemon/agent-proxy.ts`, `core-protocol/agent-sockets.ts`). The bridge sends sshd's `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` as `agentSock` in its last authentication message (an absolute one-line path, else ignored). The daemon's agent proxy has its own 0700 folder from `createSocketFolder` (the agent folder, `CoreInit.remote.agentFolder`), made before the endpoint listens and removed at shutdown and on a lost lock, and in it one socket per client, `agentSocketFile(folder, clientId)` (`agent-<24 hex digits of SHA-256(clientId)>.sock`, chmod 0600), listening while that client's latest connection named an `agentSock`. A connection on a client's socket is piped to that client's `agentSock` only, only while that client's `clientPolicy.agentForwarding` is on and the target lstats as a socket owned by the daemon's uid; anything else is refused. So a turn pinned to device A never signs with device B's agent, and B's terminal never gets A's. A Forward SSH agent change reconnects the host and exits Damocles' ControlMaster, since forwarding is the master's option; a master the user's config owns keeps its own. `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` names the pinned (else acting) client's socket on a git runner call in `CREDENTIAL_SUBCOMMANDS` and in an agent shell while that client's `clientPolicy.agentForwarding` is on, and a new terminal's names the socket of the client whose connection asked for the terminal while that client forwards (`AgentProxy.socketFor`, `workspace-host.ts` `terminals.environment`). Otherwise a daemon's spawns get no `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` at all: `gitEnv` and `spawnCredentialVariables` drop an inherited one on a daemon (`SpawnAgent`), and `process.env` never holds it. A remote operation that fails with `Permission denied (publickey` (`gitFailureCode` reads the GitError's stderr, which already crosses, as `publickeyDenied`) on a POSIX host whose Forward SSH agent switch is off, both read from this computer's host store, gets "Forward your SSH agent to <host>" in its SCM error notification (`scm-host.ts` `failed`), which turns the switch on through `RemoteHosts.setSetting`.
- **The daemon names attached devices to clients that ask, and never their ids** (`connections.ts`). An attach with `watchClients` gets `control.clients { clients: [{ deviceName, self }] }` at once and on every attach, close, replacement and grace change; a connection inside its grace is not listed. `control.disconnect` answers and then ends the connection with no reconnection grace (a quit's graceful Disconnect): `PersistentProtocol.end` lets the socket write the answer before it ends, where a close would drop what the socket still holds.
- **An updated app never stops a busy daemon of another version** (`app-update-flow.ts`). A daemon of the same protocol and another app version is attached, then stopped at once (`shutdownNow`) when it runs nothing and is known to serve no other device (`otherDeviceCount`: until the daemon listed this device, as one without the `clients` feature never does, the count is unknown and the daemon is not stopped), else asked to exit once idle (`shutdownWhenIdle`), its terminals delaying it; the next connection starts this version.
