A daemon of another protocol keeps its refused connection for the frozen commands (`connect`'s `onOtherProtocol`, the reconnect loop stops there): with no run it is stopped and this version connects, else the update banner (`ShellHost.update`, `PanelHostLabel.update`) shows its running agents, whose chats stay readable from the session mirror kept live by `mirror.sync` (polled every 5 s with `listRuns`), and Switch when they finish (`shutdownWhenIdle`) or Switch now (after a confirmation listing the agents and terminals, `shutdownNow`) connects this version once the old daemon's connection ends. While the update flow follows a daemon of another protocol on a host, no reconnect loop runs for it; the flows are `FollowedDaemons`, and Forget host and the quit let go of theirs before the connections close, so an old daemon's closing connection starts no connect. Each sync opens the saved chats the mirror now holds, selects the saved selection's, replays changed transcripts and refreshes the host's Chats lists.
- **Every core message crosses the daemon by an exhaustive route** (`daemon/core-routes.ts`). Each table is keyed by its contract (`CoreRequests`, `CoreEvents`, the mirrors, panel ops, `MainRequests`, `MainEvents`, `MirrorTopics`, `MainPanelOps`), so a message added to `core-protocol/messages.ts` without a route fails the typecheck. Each client attach is its own `createCoreEndpoint` whose generation tags every `core` channel message (`attached-transport.ts`), so nothing an earlier generation left in flight reaches the next; a client's envelope is parsed there and sent again on the daemon's own endpoint, never forwarded raw, since core ends on any violation. The core's mirrors are materialized and each fresh endpoint gets them as deltas from version 1, chats only of the panels that client views, under its own view ids. Any number of clients may view one core panel, each view under a key the daemon draws (`view-N`): panel ops fan out to every view, a `post` or `postBinary` with `viewKey` goes to that view alone and one with `except` to every other view, and every `message` reaches core with the sending view's key, whatever the client put there. Each forwarded request and panel message carries the forwarding client's hello `clientId` as `actor` (the daemon's client endpoints have no actor context, so a client's own is dropped); core's requests and events come back with the actor of the work that made them, and a request or acting-client event routed to the client goes to that client while it is attached and outside its grace, else to the client that sent core a message last, else to another attached client. The daemon forks core and runs a `lifecycle.reload` outside any actor: a child's IPC channel keeps the async context it was made in, so every event of a core forked inside a client's request would otherwise carry that client. Panel messages queued behind a `ready` run in the async context they arrived in (`AsyncResource.bind` in `PanelManager`), so each keeps the client (actor) it came from. A send's give-back (`promptReturned`, a send stopped before pi took it (`interruptRecovery`), a queued message put back with `returnToInput`, the settings a refusal opens) reaches only the view of a shared panel that sent it (`send-senders.ts`), and the other views get it with no content, which only drops the send's echo; every view sees the rest. A handler posts shared chat state (a cleared conversation, the mode, YOLO, effort, a queued chip, the session list) through `HandlerContext.host`, the panel's host, which reaches every view, and only what answers the sender (a request's reply, an ack, a refusal, an error, the state `ready` and `views.join` post to the page that sent them) through `HandlerContext.reply`, the sending view's host (`message-router/index.ts`, `multi-view-router.test.ts`). Faults cross through `ERROR_CODEC` only. Each client's `CLIENT_STATE_KEYS` values and `language` are kept apart: core has the acting client's values, published before the message that made it act, and the last attached client's language, which passes back to the client attached before it once that one detaches. The routes refuse `clipboard.writeText`, `shell.trash`, a `CLIENT_STATE_KEYS` write from core and any other key from a client, `core.shutdown` and browser pages from a client, `e2e.*` without the daemon's hooks and provider key secrets; `power.*` stops at the daemon; core's secrets are relayed under the `remote/<hostId>/` namespace of the client whose work core does (its actor, else the acting client), so a token core obtains during one device's turn lands on that device. A violation on a client channel closes that client only, and so does a message to one client that the wire refuses (over its bounds, `deliver`): that client detaches and its connection closes, so its mirrors never silently fall behind core's and its next attach starts from a fresh snapshot, while the other clients still get the message.
- **Main serves a remote core its own services with refusals in front** (`main/remote/remote-services.ts`, `remote-project-keys.ts`, `mention-authority.ts`). No request of a remote core opens, reads, changes or trashes a file of this computer, and no answer to one names its host as this computer names it. Its `editor` mirror carries the active editor's path and selection only while the active document is a file of that core's host (`DesktopEditorService.activeProjectKey`), at attach and on every change, so a local file's selection never crosses. Its secrets live only under `remote/<hostId>/`, and a refused key is logged by name, never its value. A Markdown preview shows the text the core sent (`showMarkdownPreview`'s `content`), never a file main reads, and the host's own settings file (its machine scope, the only settings file a daemon's core edits) opens editable and saves through that authority's workspace (`files.readHostSettings`, `files.writeHostSettings`), whose daemon side names and confines the file itself ("Desktop host", Documents and Confinement). A file of a remote chat's own host dropped into its composer goes to that core through `CoreClient.mention`, which strips the authority prefix; a mention or drop from another authority is refused in main naming both places, while content attachments (pasted images, terminal output, commits) reach a chat on any host. A project key that starts with a known authority prefix and `|` is that authority's whatever follows, and main refuses an empty or relative native key toward the shell and toward core, so no remote key reads back as a local one. A remote chat's model picker marks a provider whose endpoint is a loopback address the client configured (`clientProviderEndpoints`: the endpoints a daemon's core registers for the client's key providers), except for an authority on this computer (`ClientPolicy.sharedLoopback`, the loopback authority); an endpoint the target's own `models.json`, extensions or environment set is on the target's loopback, which its core reaches, and is never marked.
- **A provider the client holds no credential for runs on the host's own** (`client-snapshots.ts` `clientCredentialProviders`, `pi-models.ts` `markCredentialSources`, `credential-broker.ts` `recordCredentialSources`). A client holds a provider's credential when its `clientAuth` entry is an OAuth sign-in or `hasKey`, the rule the broker's status answers follow; without one the broker answers none and pi resolves the target's ambient credential (environment keys, an AWS profile or instance role, Google application default credentials; pi-ai `auth/resolve.js`). A daemon's core then marks each model whose resolved provider the pinned (else acting) client holds nothing for and the host has a credential of its own for (`PiRuntime.hostHasOwnAuth`: pi reports it configured, `hasConfiguredAuth`, and the acting client, whose credentials pi's snapshot also counts, holds none for it, so another device's view never takes the acting device's sign-in for the host's) as `credentialSource: 'host'` in the model list it posts; for GPT, `OpenAIAuthStatus.hostKey` makes `resolvePiModel` resolve `openai` when only the host has a key, so such a model is neither refused for a sign-in nor `unavailable`, and only a model neither side has a credential for needs a sign-in. The check reads presence only: `clientAuth` and pi's availability snapshot, never a key, and nothing writes `process.env`. pi's snapshot is refreshed outside every pin on each `clientAuth` delta and each acting client change, and every account state republish posts the model list again (`availableModels`), so the groups follow a sign-in or out. An acting client change republishes once, after its refresh (`PiRuntime.onActingClientRefreshed`), and a refresh a newer change overtook republishes nothing, so no view gets the previous client's snapshot. Core sends no host name; the view labels the group with the name its own host store gives the host. Spend follows "Usage accounting".
- **A view detaching never disposes a panel with a run** (`daemon/run-registry.ts`, `panel-retention.ts`). The registry's panel part comes only from core's `chat.activity` and `chat.turnSettled`: a running turn, a pending prompt or background agents. The event that starts a run names its owner, the client its actor names, else the acting client (the client `runPinnedTurn` pins), kept until the run ends; `control.listOwnRuns` (feature `control.listOwnRuns`) answers a connection the chat runs its client owns, so one device's close never stops or keeps another's turn. A chat run's title is its panel's title and a terminal's its shell's name, clamped to `MAX_RUN_TITLE_CHARS`, because the frozen `listRuns` parser refuses the whole answer for one longer title. A `disposed { reason: 'detach' }` or a dropped connection keeps such a panel without a view and disposes it once its run ends; an idle one goes at once, to be restored from its session file, and `reason: 'user'` disposes at once. A client rejoins a kept panel through `views.join { viewId, viewKey }`, the key being the page's panel token the daemon learned from its `ready`: core waits for a pending resume switch, replays pi's in-memory branch with the adapter's live snapshot (`PiStreamAdapter.liveSnapshot`, numbered by its event sequence: what the branch does not hold yet, the turn's user messages until each one's `message_end` (one the session gave back to the composer leaves at once, `withdrawEcho`), the running turn only while the session's turn runs (`turnRunning`: a Stop, or a send given back or refused, ends it before pi settles the run, and a retry wait with it), the status line, the streaming message, every ended tool card until its toolResult `message_end`, since pi stores a parallel batch's results only once all of them ended, the turn's abandoned cards, since pi records a Stop's only once the run settled, and the running cards), posts `sessionStarted`, and only then delivers the live events it held (a newer `partial` replaces its stream's older one among the partials held last, since the paced replay can hold them for as long as the link takes), never touching the panel's token. A join waits for the panel's join in flight (`PanelManager.joinView`), so another device's join is queued, never refused, and a post's `except` names at most the joining view and a give-back's sender (`send-senders.ts` posts through `PanelManager.postMessage`, so a give-back too waits behind a join). The branch replay never finalizes a call the live run still owns (`LiveSnapshot.liveToolCallIds`: streamed, started, awaiting approval, or ended with no stored result): `replayLiveBranch` posts it with `live` and no outcome, the webview shows it `pending`, and the snapshot and the live events then move it as they moved a watching view's card, never from `unrecorded`, which no later message revives. A prompt reposted to the joining view marks that replayed card, since `addToolCall` adds no second card for a call the transcript holds. A running subagent's nested cards are still rebuilt from its file, which ends the run (`unrecorded` until the call's own outcome), as on a webview reload: the subagent stream bridge keeps no live snapshot. A view's `disposed`, `detach` or `user`, never disposes a panel another client's view still shows; only that view goes, and so does a `chats.unload` from a client while another client's view shows the panel: the daemon closes that client's view alone and never asks core. A `chats.restore` naming a view another client shows is refused, and a `views.join` that fails or finds no panel unbinds only a view it bound itself. Whenever one view goes while its panel stays, the daemon posts core `viewDetached { viewKey }` (a client's is dropped), and core's panel proxy forgets that key, so no account, model or other per-view message is built for it again. `views.join` names the panel by the page token the daemon recorded from the panel's first `ready` (never re-pointed by a later one, nor taken for a token another panel holds), or, for another device, by the stored session the panel holds (`sessionId`, resolved from the chats mirror); the token goes only to core. Core replays to the joining view alone (`joining`, `PanelHost.viewHost`) while the panel's other views get its posts live (`postMessageExcept`), and holds those posts for the joining view until its replay ends. A joined page's later `ready` is routed by main as `views.join` (`HostServices.rejoinOnReady`), so core never takes a second view's token as the panel's: the lease owner record keeps naming the first view's.
- **SQLite opens with `journal_mode=DELETE` on a network file system** (`core/network-fs.ts`, every opener): WAL needs shared memory a network file system does not share between machines. Linux reads the folder's statfs magic (NFS, SMB, SMB2, CIFS, 9P, FUSE, which sshfs homes are, CephFS, Lustre, GPFS, AFS), Windows treats a UNC path (`\\server\share`, `\\?\UNC\`) as SMB after resolving the folder's real path, which turns a mapped network drive into its UNC path. macOS keeps WAL: Node reads no file system type there, so an NFS or SMB home on macOS is a known limitation.
- **ssh starts with one fixed option set, and nothing user-controlled becomes an option** (`main/remote/ssh/ssh-args.ts` `buildSshArgs`, `ssh-transport.ts`). Main finds the OpenSSH client by absolute path (`resolveSshExecutable` in `src/core/system-tools.ts`: an absolute PATH folder's ssh, else `%SystemRoot%\System32\OpenSSH\ssh.exe` on Windows and `/usr/bin/ssh` or `/bin/ssh` elsewhere) and starts it on its launcher with streaming stdio. The arguments are, in this order, for the connection and the passwordless check alike (`connectArgs`): `-F <file>` only for the e2e hook; `-l <user>` and `-p <port>`; `-T -o RemoteCommand=none -o RequestTTY=no -o ServerAliveInterval=15 -o ServerAliveCountMax=3 -o ConnectTimeout=15 -o PermitLocalCommand=no -o ClearAllForwardings=yes` (`FIXED_SSH_OPTIONS`); the call's own options: on macOS and Linux clients, when Damocles owns the host's master connection, `-o ControlMaster=auto -o ControlPath=<folder>/%C -o ControlPersist=10m` (the next rule), on Windows clients `-o ControlMaster=no -o ControlPath=none`, and for the check `-o BatchMode=yes -o PreferredAuthentications=publickey -o ControlMaster=no -o ControlPath=none`; once passwordless login is set up, `-o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o IdentityFile=<Damocles' key>` (an absolute path; an IdentityFile line, never `-i`, which checks the literal path before ssh expands `%%` and drops it); a saved address host's allowlisted options; exactly one `-o ForwardAgent=`, `yes` only while the host's Forward SSH agent switch is on and the host is known to be POSIX (`forwardsAgent`), else `no`; `--`; the alias or host; the remote command. ssh keeps the first value it reads for an option, so Damocles' values win over saved options and the user's config: `RemoteCommand=none`, the keepalive and connect timeout that decide drop detection, and `IdentitiesOnly=yes` with Damocles' key offered first (a saved IdentityFile is still offered after it); a second ForwardAgent would be ignored. Every `-o` value Damocles passes is one `Name="value"` argument (`optionArg`) that OpenSSH before and after 8.7 read back as written, so a space, `'` or a leading `#` stay literal: a double quote or control character is refused; on Windows clients each backslash becomes `/`, since 8.7 and later read `\\` as one backslash; elsewhere a backslash is refused. ssh expands `%` tokens in ControlPath and IdentityFile, so each path Damocles passes there has every literal `%` doubled where it is built (`sshPercentLiteral`, never in `ssh-args.ts`); a saved IdentityFile's `%` is ssh's token syntax, as in a typed `-i`. An alias, host or user that starts with `-`, holds whitespace or a control character, a user holding a character OpenSSH 10 refuses (`` ' " ` $ \ ; & < > | ( ) { } ``), and an alias holding `@` or starting with `ssh://`, which ssh would read as a user or a URI, are refused before ssh starts (`isSafeAlias`, `isSafeHost`, `isSafeUser`); `ssh-transport.ts` fails an argument `ssh-args.ts` refuses as `unknown` with reason `sshArguments`. A saved address is checked against the parser's current rules before any ssh starts (`checkSavedAddress`, `sshDestination` in `remote-hosts.ts`): a part they refuse (the alias, host, user, an option or its value) fails the attempt with `destinationRefused` naming it, which stops the reconnect loop until Retry, since leaving it out would change who or where ssh connects; a saved `User` or `Port` option an older parse kept is folded into the user and port when it agrees with them, else refused the same way. The one exception is a saved `ServerAliveInterval`, `ServerAliveCountMax` or `ConnectTimeout`, left out with a log line, since Damocles' value comes first and ssh would ignore it. The user's own `StrictHostKeyChecking` and the rest of `~/.ssh/config` are ssh's to read.
- **Reconnects on macOS and Linux share an SSH master connection, and Windows clients never use one** (`ssh/control-master.ts`, `ssh-args.ts`). Each attempt and each removal stage asks `ssh -G` for the destination. When it reports `controlmaster false` and no `controlpath`, Damocles adds its own ControlMaster options with the ControlPath `<folder>/%C`. When the user's config sets a ControlPath, or a ControlMaster without one (which multiplexes nothing), ssh's own configuration decides and Damocles adds nothing. `ssh -G` prints `false` for an explicit `ControlMaster no` and for the default alike, so a user's explicit `no` is overridden; this is a known limit, since only reimplementing ssh's Host and Match evaluation could tell them apart. A Windows client gets `ControlMaster=no` and `ControlPath=none` ahead of the user's config, since Win32-OpenSSH has no master connections and a ControlMaster from dotfiles shared with WSL or macOS would break every connect; a ProxyJump hop's own ssh, which ssh starts with none of these options, still reads the user's config. The folder is the first of `<Damocles' home>/ssh/cm`, `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/damocles-cm` (an absolute `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`), `<temp folder>/damocles-cm-<uid>` and `/tmp/damocles-cm-<uid>` whose socket fits `sun_path` (104 bytes on macOS, 108 elsewhere, NUL included) while the master binds it: ssh binds `<path>.<16 random characters>` first and exits 255 after authentication when that does not fit, so the folder takes at most 45 bytes on macOS and 49 elsewhere (`controlPathFits`). That candidate is made 0700, and used only when it is a directory the user owns, not a link, in a parent the user owns or a sticky one; never under `~/.ssh`. With no candidate left, or when `ssh -G` fails or cannot start, the attempt uses no master and logs why. `ssh -O check` cannot find a dead master: the master answers it from its own loop without touching the network, so after a sleep it answers at once while its connection is gone, and a session over it hangs until ServerAlive ends it (about 45 s). So after a resume from sleep or the network coming back, the next attempt first runs `ssh -O exit` on Damocles' own master, unconditionally, and the attempt starts a fresh one; a master the user's config owns is never checked or ended. The quit also ends Damocles' masters, which would otherwise keep their authenticated connections for ControlPersist's 10 minutes. Every call goes through `buildSshControlArgs` or `buildSshConfigQueryArgs` and the process launcher.
- **"+ New host" is parsed in main against an allowlist, as ssh reads it** (`ssh/ssh-command-parse.ts` `parseSshCommand`). It takes `[user@]host[:port]` (a bare IPv6 address whole, or bracketed), `ssh://user@host:port` or a pasted ssh command: the program as `ssh` or `ssh.exe` in any case, with or without a path, after an optional `$`, `>`, `%` or `&`, the words split as a POSIX shell splits them except that a backslash escapes only whitespace or a quote, so a Windows path keeps its backslashes, and a word starting with `#` begins a comment. Nothing is expanded: `$HOME`, `%USERPROFILE%` and `~` stay as written, for ssh to read. It takes `-l`, `-p`, `-i`, `-J`, `-4`, `-6`, `-C` (bundled as getopt allows) and `-o` for `User`, `Port`, `IdentityFile`, `IdentitiesOnly`, `ProxyJump`, `AddressFamily`, `Compression` and `PreferredAuthentications`, each `-o` value split as OpenSSH 8.7 and later split a config line (its quotes, `\\` as one backslash, exactly one word) and checked (`ALLOWED_SSH_OPTION_NAMES`, `isAllowedSshOption`, which `checkSavedAddress` applies to a saved address before ssh starts and `ssh-args.ts` applies again); a case variant ssh takes (`IdentitiesOnly=TRUE`, `AddressFamily=INET`) is stored in lower case, `true` and `false` as `yes` and `no`. User and port are one setting whichever route gives them (`user@`, `-l`, `-o User`; `:port`, `-p`, `-o Port`) and are stored as the address's user and port, never as options; a second route giving any setting but IdentityFile another value is refused (`conflict`), as are a second `-J` and `-J` beside `-o ProxyJump`, which ssh refuses. An IdentityFile is absolute on this computer, `~`-led, `none`, or led by `%d` or `${}`, with only the `%` tokens ssh lists for it; a relative one is refused (`relativePath`), since ssh would resolve it against Damocles' working folder. `ServerAliveInterval`, `ServerAliveCountMax` and `ConnectTimeout` are refused by name (`fixed`), since Damocles sets them. Everything else is refused with its name (`-F`, `-A`, `-L`, `ProxyCommand`, `LocalCommand`, `KnownHostsCommand`, `Match exec`, `StrictHostKeyChecking=no`, `UserKnownHostsFile` ...), as is a remote command after the destination (`command`). A refusal names the flag or option as typed, the destination part that failed (`host`, `user`, `port`), a word processor's dash or quote (`typography`), or `input` for an unclosed quote or input on several lines or past 4096 characters. A ProxyJump hop is kept exactly as written; an unbracketed jump host with several `:` is refused, since ssh splits it at the first. `connect.start` parses again in main, so a renderer's parse is never trusted.
- **Askpass variables go only on the ssh call, from main's own askpass server** (`ssh/ssh-askpass.ts`, `ssh-transport.ts` `sshCallEnv`). Main builds one askpass server of its own from `core/scm/askpass/askpass-server.ts` (in `MAIN_CORE_IMPORTS`) for its SSH transports; core's serves git. Each attempt registers with its own prompt (`register(mode, label, prompt)`), so a prompt reaches the attempt and host whose ssh asked it, and gets `SSH_ASKPASS`, `SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=force` and the helper's handle and nonce in that ssh's environment only, plus `DISPLAY` when `ssh -V` names OpenSSH older than 8.4 (which uses askpass only with `DISPLAY` set) or names no version Damocles can read, unless the environment already has one; nothing goes into `process.env`. A `ssh -V` that cannot start fails the attempt as `unknown` with reason `spawn`, and the next attempt runs it again. The registration ends when the bridge takes over or the attempt fails. On Windows `SSH_ASKPASS` is the native trampoline `dist/askpass/ssh-askpass.exe` (`resources/askpass/ssh-askpass.cs`, compiled by the desktop build with .NET Framework 4's `csc.exe`), never a `.cmd`, `.bat` or `.sh`: Win32-OpenSSH starts askpass with `CreateProcessW`, which cannot run a `.sh` and runs a `.cmd` through `cmd.exe`, whose parsing a server's keyboard-interactive prompt holding `"` and `&` could break out of (ssh escapes the quote as `\"`, which cmd does not read), running a command on this computer. The trampoline sends the request `askpass-main.js` sends and prints the answer, with no shell anywhere. A registration the server refuses fails the attempt as `unknown` with reason `askpass` and a log line. ssh's unknown-host question becomes the `hostKey` prompt (host as ssh names it, key type, fingerprint) only when the text starts with ssh's own `The authenticity of host`, so a server's keyboard-interactive text quoting it, which OpenSSH 8.4 and later prefix with `(user@host) `, is refused unasked; a question ssh withdrew is never taken for the user's refusal; Connect answers ssh's own question `yes`, so ssh records the key, and anything else refuses it (`hostKeyRefused`). A confirmation that is not a host key question is answered no without asking, a notice answers nothing, and every other question is a `secret` prompt, whose field is masked for every kind with a show toggle, since askpass does not say whether ssh would echo it; its kind only labels it (`pin` for a PIN, `otp` only for word-bounded code wording with no password, PIN, passcode or passphrase in the text). Server text in a log line is `JSON.stringify`ed, so it cannot forge a line.
- **A remembered ssh password or passphrase answers only the one hop its prompt names** (`ssh/remembered-secrets.ts`, `ssh-prompt-match.ts`, `ssh-askpass.ts` `rememberedAttempt`, `ssh-config.ts` `resolveSshHops`; D9 layer 3). At an attempt's first password or passphrase prompt main runs `ssh -G` for the destination and for each ProxyJump host, each a hop of its own (the first jump with its own configuration, its own ProxyJump included; each later one as ssh reaches it with `-J`; a jump without a user or port named as an alias, as ssh names it; a ProxyCommand hop not followed), with the attempt's signal, so a cancel kills the running query. A resolution makes at most 8 `ssh -G` queries, the target's included; a ninth, a query repeating one on its own path (a ProxyJump loop), a failed query or a ProxyJump that does not parse fails the whole resolution, which binds no prompt, and the log names the hop and ssh's stderr. A prompt that ssh withdraws while the hops resolve is given up at once, unasked. A prompt binds to a hop only when its text equals exactly one hop's `<user>@<host>'s password:`, `(<user>@<host>) Password:` (host: the hop's `hostkeyalias` when `ssh -G` prints one, as ssh then names it, else the alias or the HostName `ssh -G` resolved) or `Enter passphrase for key '<IdentityFile>':` (as `ssh -G` prints it, or with `~` expanded; the binding is that key file of the hop). Two conditions narrow that. When any hop's `ssh -G` prints a `proxycommand` other than `none`, no prompt binds, on every version: the command may hold hops `ssh -G` does not list, such as an inner ssh to the same `user@host` on another port. OpenSSH added the `(user@host) ` prefix to keyboard-interactive prompts in 8.4, so before it (or when `ssh -V` names no version) any server in the chain can send another hop's exact text: there a passphrase prompt never binds (a target could send one for the default IdentityFile), and a password prompt binds only on a connection of exactly one hop. A prompt that binds to no hop is logged with the reason. A prompt that matches no hop or several, a verification code and any other text is asked, never answered from the store and never offered "Remember on this computer"; the failure mode is a prompt, never a wrong answer, so a ProxyJump host never receives the target's answer. A stored answer is given once per binding until an ssh of the attempt authenticated: the binding prompting again before that means it was refused, so it is deleted and the user is asked, every typed answer of that binding in the attempt is dropped, and the stored answer is not offered again in the attempt even when deleting it failed. A store that cannot be read is logged and the prompt asked. An attempt may run more than one ssh, each authenticating on its own (a first connection from a client without a master, as Windows' OpenSSH has none, runs the platform detection and then stage 0), so authenticating forgets what was given, and the next ssh's prompt is answered with the latest answer the user typed for that exact prompt and hop in this attempt, whatever the Remember password setting, else from the store. Those typed answers live only in memory and are forgotten when the attempt ends, on every outcome (`RememberedAttempt.end`, `ssh-transport.ts`); a code, any prompt bound to no hop and a host key question are never answered this way. The store is read and written only while the host's Remember password setting is on. An answer typed with Remember checked, which the prompt offers only with the setting on and a binding (`SshPrompt.offerRemember`, `SshPromptAnswer.remember`; `connect.answer` refuses `remember` for any other prompt), is stored only once ssh authenticated (`RememberedAttempt.authenticated()`, at the remote command's first output), so a refused one is never kept. The answers live only in `SecretsStore`, as `damocles.remote.ssh.<hostId>.<sha256 hex of JSON [hostname, port, user]>.password` and `...<sha256 hex of JSON [hostname, port, user, IdentityFile]>.passphrase`; a host id outside `HOST_ID` (`daemon/control-messages.ts`, no `.`) is refused, so one host's prefix never covers another's keys. Switching Remember password off and Forget host delete every one of the host's (`deleteAllForHost`), those the keyring cannot decrypt this run included (`SecretsStore.keys()` lists them). Main holds one `RememberedSecrets`: a host's reads, stores and deletes run in order, and a store from an attempt that began before a `deleteAllForHost` stores nothing, so a sign-in that ends after Remember password went off or the host was forgotten keeps no answer. A changed user, HostName or port leaves the old hop's answer in the store, never offered again, until Forget host or Remember password off. Remove Damocles server's stage (`runSshStage`) answers and offers Remember by the same rules as a connect, since it signs in to the same hops.
- **Damocles writes nothing under `~/.ssh`** (`ssh/ssh-config.ts`). The Connect dialog's aliases are read as OpenSSH's `readconf.c` reads its files: `~/.ssh/config` in the passwd entry's home (`os.userInfo()`, never `HOME`; on Windows the profile folder), or the e2e hook's file alone, as `ssh -F` reads it, then the system file. An account without a passwd entry, for which ssh itself exits, lists no alias and the dialog still opens (`configuredAliases`), and its attempts match a key's `~` only as written; both log it (`passwdAccount`). Lines split as `argv_split` splits them (space and tab, single and double quotes from anywhere in a word, `\'` `\"` `\\` and, outside quotes, `\ `; an unterminated quote logs the line and skips it). `Include` follows ssh: `${NAME}` from ssh's environment and `%d`, `%u`, `%i`, `%%` expanded (an unset variable or a token only a host decides skips that Include with a log line), a relative path in `~/.ssh` for a user file and beside the system file for the system file, `~` from `HOME` else the passwd home (`~user` skipped with a log line), and glob(3)'s rules (no braces, `**` as `*`, `*` not matching a leading `.`, the home put into the pattern unquoted as ssh puts it, a backslash quoting on POSIX and separating on Windows), matches in byte order, an Include inside a Host or Match block read whatever block it stands in, at most 16 levels, a loop stopped at once. A file that is missing is skipped silently; one that cannot be opened, is not a regular file or is past 4 MiB is skipped with a log line naming it and the reason, where ssh would end its run, so the dialog still opens. The system file is the OS OpenSSH's (`/etc/ssh/ssh_config`, `%ProgramData%\ssh\ssh_config`), whichever client `resolveSshExecutable` finds: a Git for Windows, Homebrew or Nix ssh reads its own, whose Host lines the dialog does not list. Only names `isSafeAlias` accepts are listed, dot-prefixed ones skipped, so a listed alias always reaches ssh; the dialog and `start({ alias })` take the same first `MAX_SSH_CONFIG_ALIASES` (1000) of them, logging a longer list. Only ssh writes `known_hosts`, after the user accepted a key; a changed key fails with `hostKeyChanged` and the known_hosts name ssh printed (`changedHostName`: one token of letters, digits, `[]:._-`, never starting with `-`), from which main composes the modal's command (`params.command`, shown and copied verbatim) with `knownHostsRemoveCommand`: `ssh-keygen -R <name>` with a `[host]:port` name double-quoted, since zsh and bash read the brackets as a glob, and nothing retries.
- **Two e2e hooks, development builds only** (`ssh/ssh-environment.ts`): with `!app.isPackaged` and `DAMOCLES_E2E_HOOKS=1`, `DAMOCLES_E2E_SSH_CONFIG` (an absolute path) is passed to every ssh as `-F` and is the file the aliases come from, and `DAMOCLES_E2E_RELEASE_URL` (http or https) replaces the release base the target downloads from. A packaged build reads neither, and nothing else about ssh can be redirected. Under the same gate `remote-e2e-environment.ts` reads `DAMOCLES_E2E_APP_VERSION`, `DAMOCLES_E2E_PROTOCOL_VERSION` and `DAMOCLES_E2E_DEVICE_NAME`, which replace the hello's app version (and the version installed on targets), protocol version and device name. A dev-tree daemon with `DAMOCLES_E2E_HOOKS=1` (`daemonE2eHooks`, never a server package) also reads `DAMOCLES_E2E_RECONNECTION_GRACE_MS` and `DAMOCLES_E2E_MAX_HELD_BYTES` (`e2eConnectionLimits`), which shorten the grace and the held-frame bound; the grace is at most 2^31-1 ms, past which `setTimeout` would fire after 1 ms.
- **Platform detection runs once per host and its result is cached** (`bootstrap/platform-detect.ts` `resolvePlatform`). The host's OS override wins; else a cached detection is reused; else ssh runs `uname -s` alone (csh refuses `2>/dev/null`), where `Linux` and `Darwin` are POSIX and a BSD is refused (`unsupportedOs`), and any other answer runs `echo %COMSPEC%~$PSHOME`, which cmd and PowerShell expand differently, to learn Win32-OpenSSH's default shell (a POSIX default shell such as Git Bash expands neither and is launched as cmd is). A Windows override with no cached shell still runs the echo. The result goes to the host store through `savePlatform`.
- **Stage 0 carries no user data and reads stage 1 exactly** (`bootstrap/stage0.ts`). POSIX: `/bin/sh -c 'IFS= read -r n; s=$(dd bs=1 count="$n" 2>/dev/null); eval "$s"'`. Windows: under cmd `powershell -NoLogo -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -EncodedCommand <UTF-16LE base64>`, under a PowerShell default shell the same one-line script (no double quote) as the command, so no second PowerShell sits between ssh and the script; it reads `<length>\n` and the bytes from an unbuffered `[Console]::OpenStandardInput()` and runs stage 1 dot-sourced, writing only through `OpenStandardOutput()`, never PowerShell's own output, which re-encodes bytes as text. The app writes `<byte length>\n<stage 1>` (`stage1Frame`), and every byte after it stays on stdin for the protocol.
- **Stage parameters enter stage 1 only as quoted literals** (`bootstrap/quote.ts`, `stage1.ts`). The version, each target's file, SHA-256, size and asset URL, the install folder and the previous one replace only the script's parameter block, written by `shellQuote` (POSIX single quotes, `'` as `'\''`) or `psQuote` (PowerShell single quotes, every quote PowerShell ends a string at, U+2018 to U+201B included, doubled). A value holding a line break or NUL is refused before stage 1 exists, and `validateInstallFolder` (absolute POSIX or Windows path, or `~`-prefixed; no control, format (the bidi controls among them) or line separator character, as the probe parser refuses in `install`) is the one rule the host store's `hosts.setInstallFolder` and the transport apply; a refused folder fails with `installFolderInvalid` before ssh starts.
- **Stage 1 speaks only `@@DAMOCLES {json}` lines until the bridge line** (`bootstrap/stage1-posix.sh`, `stage1-windows.ps1`, `probe-parse.ts`). Every tool's output goes to stderr, which main keeps as a 16 KiB tail and logs line by line. Each status line is a step, progress (0 to 1), a notice or failure code with exactly its parameters (bounded strings without control or format characters, or finite numbers; `PROBE_FAILURES`, `PROBE_NOTICES`), the probe's target and `<install>`, an upload request, `settled`, or `bridge`; a marked line outside that protocol ends the attempt (`bridgeFailed`, `stageProtocol`), a line without the marker (a login script's output) is logged, and a line past 64 KiB ends it. The bytes after the bridge line are handed to the protocol unread. Text produced on the target crosses only as these codes, which main and the overlay localize.
- **The prerequisite probe stops before anything is installed** (stage 1's checks, each a function the tests call): CPU (x64, arm64); on Linux NixOS without `NIX_LD` (`nixLdMissing`), glibc from `getconf GNU_LIBC_VERSION` else `ldd --version` at least 2.28, `GLIBCXX_3.4.25` in the first `libstdc++.so.6` found, or on musl an installed `/usr/lib/libstdc++.so.6` (Alpine builds it without symbol versions), kernel 4.18; glibc is decided first (`detect_libc`: `getconf GNU_LIBC_VERSION` answers), then musl when `ldd --version` names it or, without either, when `/lib/ld-musl-*` exists, so a glibc host with Debian's `musl` package stays glibc; macOS 13.5 (`sw_vers`); Windows build 17763; 300 MB free in the home and in `<install>`. Notices that do not stop it: logind `KillUserProcesses` (busctl, else the last assignment in `logind.conf` and its drop-ins) without the user's linger (`noLinger`), `fs.inotify.max_user_watches` below 524288 (`lowInotify`, logged only), no Git Bash on Windows (`noGitBash`), and WMI process creation refused (`wmiBlocked`, tested by starting `cmd.exe /d /c exit 0` through `Win32_Process.Create`).
- **`<install>` is `<folder>/<user>` for a host's install folder, else `~/.damocles/server`, and POSIX tests it in practice** (stage 1 `install_dir`, `prepare_install`). `~` means the target's home. A missing `<install>` is created 0700 (Windows: `icacls /reset`, then `/inheritance:r /grant:r` the user's SID and SYSTEM, since a folder inherits its parent's ACL and a drive root's lets every signed-in user modify what is made in it, and one whose parent hands nothing down holds the creating token's default DACL as explicit entries), and a missing configured `<folder>` above it with the user's umask, since it may be shared; an existing `<install>` of a configured folder that another user may change fails with `installFolderUnsafe` (POSIX: group or other write, or an owner other than the user or root; Windows: an owner, or a write grant through inheritance included, beyond the user, SYSTEM, Administrators, TrustedInstaller and CREATOR OWNER), while the default in the user's home keeps the home's permissions; an empty user name fails before any of this; a two-line script written to `<install>/.exec-test-<rand>`, made executable, run and deleted catches a noexec mount or an execution policy (`noexec`, with `reason: notWritable` when the folder cannot be created or written); Windows skips it. The run folder stays `~/.damocles/server/run-<machine key>/` whatever the install folder.
- **The install verifies the hash before unpacking and replaces nothing in place** (stage 1). Under the mkdir lock `<install>/install.lock` (its `owner` file names `<host> <pid>`; stale when that pid is gone on the same host, or after 30 minutes when another host took it; a stale lock is removed only under `install.lock.takeover`, so two stages that judged it stale never remove a lock the other just took; a stage waits at most 10 minutes) it checks again that `<version>-<target>` is not installed (its launcher and `server-package.json`), removes `.staging-*` folders older than a day, downloads into `.staging-<rand>` with curl, wget or WebClient (progress is the staged file's size against the manifest's), and when no download works asks the app to upload `<length>\n<bytes>` from its artifact cache, read exactly (one bounded `dd` read at a time on POSIX). The SHA-256 from the app's manifest is checked with sha256sum, `shasum -a 256`, `openssl dgst` or `Get-FileHash` before anything is unpacked (Windows stage 1 sets `PSModulePath` to the running PowerShell's `$PSHOME\Modules` before anything else, since a value inherited from the other edition hides `Get-FileHash` from 5.1, and hands the daemon the inherited value): a mismatch stops with `hashMismatch` and no upload retry, since a tampered or truncated download must never be replaced silently, and the staging folder goes. Then it unpacks (Windows: `%SystemRoot%\System32\tar.exe -xf`), runs `self-test` and renames the folder to `<install>/<version>-<target>`, so a new version never touches a running one; tools get no stdin, which is the protocol's. An incomplete folder of that version, which a failed removal leaves, goes first unless a daemon of this machine runs from it (`versionFolderBusy`), and a rename that fails or leaves no install fails with `moveFailed`. Only a version folder counts as one: a version-shaped name and `server-package.json`, the removal's rule, one function the stage and removal scripts of each platform share (`version_of_dir`, `Get-DamoclesDirVersion`), so a `node-v20.11.0-linux-x64` or `MyApp-linux-x64` of the user's is never touched. Pruning keeps the current version, every version a pid file under `~/.damocles/server/run-*/` touched within 25 hours names (another machine sharing the home included), and the newest previous one.
- **A changed install folder cleans the previous one once nothing runs from it** (stage 1 `clean_previous`, `Clear-DamoclesPrevious`). After the install in the new `<install>`, the previous `<install>` (the old setting's `<folder>/<user>`, or the default for `previousInstallFolder: null`) loses Damocles' own entries only, under its install lock: version folders (the rule above), `.staging-*`, `.exec-test-*` older than a minute, `install.lock` and a stale takeover lock, never the folder itself (which may be shared, `/opt/tools`) or anything else in it, `run-*` included. Both sides of every comparison are canonical (POSIX `cd -P`/`pwd -P`, Windows the final path `GetFinalPathNameByHandle` gives, 8.3 names expanded and junctions and links followed, for a path that exists and `GetFullPath` otherwise, without a trailing separator: `Get-DamoclesFinalPath`), so a previous folder spelled differently is the current one, in the running-from check too. A live daemon on this machine running from the old folder, or an install lock a live stage holds there (taken without waiting), defers it to a later connect, and a version another machine's pid file touched within 1500 minutes names stays and defers `settled`; this machine's own pid file is read only by the running-from check (POSIX tells it by `uname -n`, Windows by the run key). Stage 1 then reports `settled`, and the host store drops `previousInstallFolder` (`installFolderSettled`).
- **The POSIX bridge is `exec bin/damocles-server attach`; on Windows stage 1 is the bridge** (`stage1-windows.ps1` `Start-DamoclesBridge`). It reads the run folder's pid file (key: HMAC-SHA256 of `MachineGuid` over `damocles-server-run-folder`, as `machine-id.ts` derives it) and its token, opens the pipe the pid file names with `NamedPipeClientStream` only when it matches the daemon's pipe pattern, and runs the bridge's side of the mutual authentication byte for byte as `authenticateBridge` does: one PersistentProtocol control frame `{ v: 1, nonce }`, the daemon's `{ nonce, proof }` checked with HMAC-SHA256 in constant time within 10 s, then `{ proof }`; a pipe that cannot prove the token is closed with nothing more sent, and the stage starts its own daemon once, through WMI (inside the session when WMI refuses) and waits up to 30 s for its pid file. Only then does it send the bridge line and copy bytes both ways with `CopyToAsync` between the pipe and the raw console streams.
- **The transport hands Authorities a Duplex past the bridge's authentication** (`ssh-transport.ts` `startSshTransport`). It reports `connecting`, then `authenticating` at ssh's first question or, for a key that needs none, once the remote command runs, then stage 1's steps; an abort of the attempt's signal kills ssh and fails with `cancelled`, and an abort during an await before an ssh starts stops the attempt before it, since a listener added to an aborted signal never runs (`RemoteConnections.transport` checks the same after its transport resolved). ssh's failures are read from its stderr (`ssh-stderr.ts`, each pattern anchored to a line ssh itself prints: a changed key, a refused or unverifiable key, `Permission denied (` or too many authentication failures, naming the jump host when the denial came before an identification exchange that never finished, a name that does not resolve, a connect refused, timed out or unreachable (`unreachable`: no route, network unreachable, host down), a ProxyJump host's own connect failure ahead of the close the outer ssh adds, a close or reset during the identification exchange, which a proxy in front of a stopped sshd gives, a banner or ServerAlive timeout), and only when ssh exited 255 and only from what it printed before the remote command's first output, so a tool's or a banner's text on the target never names the failure; else `unknown` with a `reason` (`sshExited` with the exit code, `spawn` for an ssh that could not start). A detection ssh killed by a signal proves nothing, as one that exited 255. stderr is decoded across chunks and logged line by line. When ssh exits, the stream ends after the last bytes and is destroyed once a reader took its end, so bytes a paused reader has not read are kept; ending the stream ends the bridge and leaves the daemon running. Known limit: the launcher acknowledges each chunk once the transport's listener returned, so a paused reader does not pause ssh, and the Duplex buffers what arrives meanwhile; the wire bounds it, since the daemon holds at most `MAX_HELD_BYTES` unacknowledged and closes the connection past it. Pause and resume on `LaunchedProcess` would change the launcher's interface for every caller and fake.
- **Remote features are behind one gate until the release that ships them** (`main/remote/remote-enabled.ts`: `remoteEnabled(app, env) = !app.isPackaged || env.DAMOCLES_REMOTE === '1'`, read once at startup as `REMOTE` in `main/index.ts`). While it is false: `ShellState.remoteEnabled` is false and `ShellState.hosts` empty, `MenuActions.remote` is absent so Connect to Host, Open Folder on Host and Disconnect Host exist in neither menu nor palette, the overlay's Connect channels are never registered, the shell's `hosts.*` and `connect.open` channels refuse, SSH host authorities get no transport, and `remoteProjects()` lists no SSH project from `remote-hosts.json` (their records stay in the file untouched). The loopback authority keeps its own dev-only rule (`LOOPBACK`).
- **Host names are this computer's and have one rule** (`checkHostName` in `main/remote/host-store.ts`, used by `HostStore.checkName`, `addHost`, `renameHost`, the overlay's `hosts.checkName`/`hosts.rename`, the shell's `hosts.rename`, and the file read). A name is trimmed and NFC-normalized, 1..64 code points, has no control character, line or paragraph separator, or other format character (`\p{Cc}`, U+2028, U+2029, `\p{Cf}`: zero-width characters would let a look-alike pass the uniqueness check; reason `controlChars`), and no bidirectional override, isolate or mark (U+202A..U+202E, U+2066..U+2069, U+200E, U+200F, U+061C; reason `bidiChars`), and differs with `toLocaleLowerCase('en')` from every other saved host's name and ssh alias; a new alias host's alias must also differ from every other host's name (both directions). `addHost` and `renameHost` run the check inside the `writeJsonConfig` mutation against the hosts as they are when the write runs, so a stale renderer can never save a clash; a refusal writes nothing. A prefill (`prefillHostName`) drops all of these refused characters and cuts to 64; a record read without a name, or with one the rule refuses, gets its alias or host name made unique with " 2", " 3"... (`uniqueHostName`). Damocles never writes `~/.ssh/config`.
- **`remote-hosts.json` keeps SSH host records beside the per-authority projects** (`host-store.ts`, still `version: 1`, fields added): `hosts: { [hostId]: { kind: 'ssh', destination: { kind: 'alias', alias } | { kind: 'address', host, user?, port?, options: [{ name, value }] }, name, color?, platform?: { os, windowsShell? }, osOverride?, lastUsed?, recentFolders (newest first, at most 10), settings: { installFolder?, defaultPermissionMode?, allowBypass, forwardAgent, rememberPassword, browserLocalNetwork, openLinksWithoutAsking }, previousInstallFolder?: string | null, identityFile?, installedKeyFingerprint?, installedKeyEntry?, installedKeyFile?, lingerEnabledByDamocles?, alwaysCredentialHosts } }` and `groupOrder`, the By host view's group order, which a group header's drag, Alt+Up and Alt+Down, and Move up and Move down write through `SHELL_CHANNELS.projectsReorderGroups` ("Desktop host", the Projects section's order). `color` is a palette tone id; any other value, an earlier `#rrggbb` included, is dropped at parse and logged. The facts record only what Damocles changed, and the file holds no secret. Forget host disconnects (an attempt in flight cancelled too, and `RemoteConnections.forget` drops the host's attempt, notices and stderr tail), lets go of its update flow and ends Damocles' master connection for it, deletes the host's remembered secrets (`deleteAllForHost`), `<userData>/remote/<hostId>/` once its session mirror's sync under way settled, `~/.damocles/browser-profiles/<authorityKey>/` (a link removed as itself), its `remote-state.json` entries and the record; its daemon keeps running. A host's projects and trusted folders stay under `authorities['ssh+<hostId>']`. The host id is drawn by the store (`HOST_ID`). A record whose destination could reach ssh as an option (a value starting with `-`, a newline) is dropped on read, and a saved address the parser's current rules refuse fails its connect with `destinationRefused` before ssh starts ("ssh starts with one fixed option set"). A file that does not parse or names another `version` (a hand edit, a downgrade) shows no hosts or projects and refuses every write for the run, with a warning naming the file and why, so neither replaces the hosts and the facts Remove Damocles server reads; the user fixes the file and restarts, as the worktree `registry.json` refuses writes while it does not parse. `setInstallFolder` keeps the folder in use before as `previousInstallFolder` (null = the default) until `installFolderSettled`, and a second change before that keeps the older one, which still holds the installed versions. Every change lands on disk before memory and listeners change (the desktop store rule) and `flush` joins `flushStores`.
- **One connection attempt per SSH host, shared by every caller** (`main/remote/connections.ts` `RemoteConnections`). `Authorities` stays the connection registry; for `ssh+<hostId>` its transport is `RemoteConnections.transport`, which runs `startSshTransport` for the host's current attempt with that attempt's `AbortSignal`, so Cancel or Escape kills ssh and leaves the host offline (not failed). Steps, progress, notices, the prompt ssh waits on and the failure (`SshFailureCode` + params, or `handshakeFailed` for hello/welcome/attach) are the attempt's, sent whole to the overlay as `connect.step`. A prompt of an attempt nobody watches in the Connect dialog (a launch connect, a project selection) waits and sets `ShellHost.signInNeeded`; no dialog opens without the user, and the banner's Sign in opens the dialog on that waiting prompt. A connect of a host whose connection is up or suspect joins it; no new attempt starts until the connection dropped, except Reconnect now and Retry on a suspect one (`force`), and an attempt on a held connection resumes it before it attaches fresh (`Authorities.connect`). An attempt whose signal aborted (Cancel, Escape, Disconnect Host) fails as `cancelled` whatever the transport or the handshake threw on the way out. A dropped connection marks every project of the host failed until it is back, and Disconnect Host (`disconnectAuthority` in `main/index.ts`: the loop stops, `RemoteConnections.cancelHost` ends a connect attempt in flight and settles once it settled, so a reconnect after it (`reconnectFresh`) starts a new attempt instead of joining the cancelled one, `Authorities.disconnect`) marks them offline, as does a `control.shutdownNow` this client sent once the daemon ended the connection (`shutDownDaemon`), so neither reconnects without the user. `Authorities.disconnect` counts on the entry, and an attempt that began under an earlier count, checked after each await, ends offline and closes or destroys whatever it made (a transport, a connection, an old daemon's connection), resuming nothing and attaching nothing. Every project of a host shares its one connection, keyed by its authority key. A fresh connection records the host's use, syncs its session mirror, settles a daemon of another app version and announces the chats that finished while away once its chats reopened (`AuthoritiesDeps.attached`, `hostConnected`, then `announceAway` at its `ConnectionGain`); a resume or a cleared suspect state does none of it. When the core epoch differs from the one the recorded chats ran under, their core ended meanwhile and stopped them, so the notice says they stopped when Damocles restarted there, never that they finished (`finished-away.ts`). ssh's stderr tail is kept per host for Show log.
- **The reconnect loop runs only for an authority with open views, and never gives up** (`main/remote/reconnect.ts` `Reconnector`, D3). An authority whose connection dropped or whose attempt failed, and which has editor tabs of its files, terminals, chats or the selected project, is attempted after `[0, 5, 5, 10, 10, 10, 10, 10, 30]` s and then every 30 s forever (after a failed attempt made outside the loop, from the second step), each attempt unwatched and timed out after 30 s until stage 1 runs on the target (`checking`, `installing`, `starting`) or a user watches it in the Connect dialog, after which nothing times it out: stage 1's silent steps (the install lock's wait of up to 10 minutes, the hash, unpacking, the self-test) outlast any fixed bound, a dead link still ends it through ssh's keepalive and the handshake's own limit, and the user's Cancel ends a watched one; a stage stuck on a live link holds the loop until Disconnect Host. One that waits on a prompt stops at the sign-in banner with its timeout paused, and no dialog opens. An attempt the loop finds running that it did not start (the Connect dialog's, a selection's) is joined: awaited with no timeout and never aborted by the loop. An attempt that connected while the connection is no longer usable (suspect or lost again) leaves the loop at its next step; one the transport refuses to start (a removal stage runs) fails as an attempt. A changed host key, a removed host, a failed prerequisite, a failed install and a non-interactive authentication failure (`permissionDenied`, `hostKeyRefused`) stop it at `failed` until Retry; a changed key after a drop opens the Connect dialog on its security modal. Reconnect now and Retry attempt at once from the start of the schedule; `powerMonitor` `resume` and `net.online` turning true (sampled every 2 s while a loop waits, since Electron's main process has no network event) attempt every waiting loop at once. A user Cancel, Disconnect Host or the views closing stop it. Each phase is one `ReconnectEvent`; the authority is usable again with `ConnectionGain`, fired for the same connection once it resumed or a suspect state cleared, and for a fresh connection only once its core attach is ready and its chats rejoined through `views.join` (same core epoch) or were restored with their kept permissions (new epoch, `RemoteCore.reattached`, with `restarted` `server` when the daemon's pid changed, else `engine`, for an authority this run had attached before), a reopening that failed included. The offline queue, the offline save uploads and a resume's terminals follow that gain (`connectionGained` in `main/index.ts`).
- **Connection quality is measured only while a window shows the host** (`main/remote/latency.ts` `LatencyMeter`, D34, VS Code's `remoteAgentService.ts`). A measurement is 5 `control.ping`s 2 s apart over a usable connection of a daemon that lists `control.ping`, keeping the smallest round trip halved; the first runs 10 s after a connect and the next 60 s after each, and only while `setVisibleRemoteAuthorities` names the authority: a measurement under way when its window hides is dropped unpublished, its last ping included, a connection's close (Disconnect Host, a violation) stops measuring it as a drop does, and a hidden app sends nothing but keepalives. The baseline is the mean of the first three measurements; the host is slow when the latest is above 1500 ms, or above 500 ms and more than twice the baseline. The latest, slow and the last 10 move only when a measurement finishes.
- **A launch connects only what it restores** (`main/remote/launch-connect.ts`): the authorities whose projects have restored editor tabs or hot-exit backups, terminals or chats kept in `remote-state.json`, and the last selected project's. The loopback authority is awaited before the editor restores; SSH hosts connect in the background, so a host that asks never holds the launch. Other remote projects connect when selected.
- **A host that is not connected shows nothing of this computer's disk in its tabs** (`documents/document-service.ts`, `editor-pane.ts`). A restored tab of its files is an `offline` placeholder document (`ShellDocumentContent.kind === 'offline'`, host id and name); once the host connects (`connectionsChanged`) the file opens and every tab of the placeholder takes the new document id (`onDidReplace`); the placeholder stays in the map until then, and when its tabs all closed meanwhile it replaces nothing and the document service releases the file it opened, unless another open (Files, Quick Open) joined that read or took the document, which then holds it. A hot-exit backup of its file opens dirty in a document `awaitingHost`: its location is the project folder joined with the relative path in the target's flavor, a save is refused until the host connects, and on connect the host confines the file and its disk text becomes the baseline while the buffer keeps the backup's text (a buffer equal to the disk turns clean and its backup goes; a file gone on the host leaves it orphaned). A file that now leads outside its project, whose project folder is gone, or that is an image, binary, too large or not valid UTF-8 takes no backup, as the restore path refuses it, both paths classifying the file by one rule (`fileContentOf`, which makes an SVG an image whatever its bytes): the text and backup move to an untitled buffer that its tabs show, and a warning says why. A file the connected host fails to confine or read keeps the document awaiting: a warning says so once, a save tries again before it is refused, and so does every connection change. One file has one document while its host is away: a second backup of it restores as an untitled buffer ("Another backup of it was restored first"), and a backup of a file whose placeholder is open takes over the placeholder's tabs. A restored remote terminal shows as `reconnecting` (`TerminalService.showReconnecting`) until the host reattaches it, or `exited` when the daemon no longer has it; those placeholders never rewrite `remote-state.json`.
- **A save of a remote file while its host is not connected is kept on this computer and uploads on reconnect** (`documents/document-service.ts` `saveOffline`, `hostConnected`, `upload`; D13). Not connected is every connection phase but connected, suspect included, when `workspaces.forProject` throws `NotConnectedError`, so a save never waits on a replay. The save writes the buffer's backup at once and sets `pendingUpload` (the text saved); a backup that does not land fails the save. The tab is not dirty while the buffer equals that text and shows the cloud-off glyph ("Saved on this computer. Uploads when {host} reconnects.", `ShellEditorTab.pendingUpload`); `disk` and `diskText` stay what was last read from the target, the merge base. While pending, the backup stays whatever the buffer (`takeText`), no watcher or reconnect read replaces the buffer (`reload` leaves it to the upload), a closed last tab keeps the document held until it uploads and then releases it (`release`, `finishRelease`; Don't Save drops only the edits made after the offline save; a reopen shows the same document), and the persisted tab names the backup. On ConnectionGain (index.ts `connectionGained`, after the core endpoint is usable: a resume inside the grace or a fresh attach) `hostConnected` re-reads every other open file of the host and, for each pending document in its save turn, reads and hashes the target's copy through the workspace: equal to `disk` (by existence and SHA-256), the text saved offline is written in place, edits made after it staying unsaved, and a write that fails, or a target copy that is no longer text, is told in a warning toast naming the file and host and leaves a failed save (dirty, backup kept) instead of the pending glyph (`uploadFailed`), in a tab again when its last tab had closed (`DocumentServiceDeps.reopen`); equal to the buffer, it is taken as the disk text; otherwise the document becomes an upload conflict (`uploadConflict`: base = the old `diskText`, current = the target's text or '' for a deleted file, incoming = the buffer), its disk the target's (no conflict bar: the merge editor resolves it, as a git conflict's Result), and its buffer Reset Result's merge (`mergeWithMarkers` in `src/shared/scm/merge-markers.ts`, markers naming the host and "Saved on this computer"), and nothing is written. All of one pass's conflicts reach `uploadsConflicted` together, which opens each in the merge editor (`EditorPane.showUploadMerge`, a merge tab with `upload`, no repository) and shows one summary toast; its inputs come from the document (`editor:merge-inputs`), git computes nothing, and Complete Merge (`editor:complete-merge`) saves the Result through the host's workspace with the normal disk check and ends the merge. A document restored from a hot-exit backup while its host was away (`awaitingHost`) has no known disk state, so its save stays refused until the host connects. The pending state lives in memory: after a relaunch the backup restores as a dirty buffer, saved by the user.
- **A remote chat whose host is not connected shows its session mirror copy read-only, served by this computer's core** (`chats.openTranscript`, `core/chat-panel/transcript-view.ts`, `main/remote/offline-transcripts.ts`). Main names only a file `session-mirror.ts` resolved under `<userData>/remote/<hostId>/mirror/`; core resolves the path again with realpath and refuses one outside that host's mirror root (derived from its own `logs.dir`, never from main), a host id outside `HOST_ID` and a session id that fails `isSafeAgentPathId`; a daemon's core refuses the request and the daemon's route table refuses it from a client. The view replays the file on each `ready` through `replaySessionFileReadOnly`, which reads the bytes itself and hands pi an in-memory session, because pi's `SessionManager.open` repairs a torn last line and migrates old files in place; nothing else the page sends is acted on, and no session, lease, owner record, checkpoint, index or stats entry is made. The page knows from `PanelInit.offline`. When the host connects the transcript views close and the live chats reopen, a selected transcript's live chat selected again.
- **While a host is connecting or offline, its projects' Chats list the session mirror's chats, read the same way** (`chats.listMirrored`, `listMirroredSessions` in `transcript-view.ts`, `listChats` and `searchChats` in `main/index.ts`). Main names only a project's mirror folder (`SessionMirror.projectFolder`); core checks that it is a direct child of that host's mirror root once links are followed, reads each session file's bytes itself into pi's in-memory session and lists it with `sessionFileMeta`, the row the session index would give, so no file is repaired, migrated, indexed or written, and the search narrows the rows with the catalog's `sessionMatchesQuery`. Selecting a row opens its transcript (`OfflineTranscripts.openChat`, `chats.openTranscript`); the host's connect closes it like a launch's transcripts, and the project's remembered chat opens live. A daemon's core and the daemon's route table refuse the request.
- **A remote core's file and folder pickers are the Connect dialog's folder browser, confined to that host's projects and their live chat worktrees** (`RemoteHosts.pick`, `browsePick`, `picked` in `main/remote/remote-hosts.ts`; `dialogs.pickFile`/`pickFolder` in `remote-services.ts`). Main opens the `connect` request with `pick` (mode, the host's name from this computer's host store, the roots, the core's title and button label as text), never a native dialog of this computer's disk. While it is open, every `connect.browse` of that host lists only inside a root, through the target workspace's `tree.list` (the daemon's own confinement), with no parent above the root and files only in file mode, narrowed to the core's filter extensions; a path no root holds answers `outside`, and `connect.pickFolder` adds no project. The answer (`{ kind: 'connect', path }`, accepted only for a request with `pick`) is located in a root and confined again on the host with `confineExisting`, must be a folder for pickFolder and a file of the extensions for pickFile, and the host's realpath is what the core gets; anything else answers `undefined` and is logged. The default path is used only when a root holds it (a file's own folder), else the first project. Cancel and Escape answer `undefined`.
- **The session mirror trusts no name or path a daemon sends** (`main/remote/session-mirror.ts`). Every segment of a mirrored name passes `isSafeAgentPathId` (`src/shared/agent-path-id.ts`), at most 6 segments, and the resolved path stays under that project's mirror folder (`<userData>/remote/<hostId>/mirror/<sha256(folderKey)[0..32]>`); a segment that is a Windows device name (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM<n>, LPT<n>, with or without an extension) is refused on every client; a refused change's stream is drained and nothing is written. A sync runs over the one connection that answered its `mirror.sync`, since stream ids are per connection. A change that fails (a write, a folder that cannot be made, a `receive` that rejects) is logged and its stream drained if nothing claimed it yet, and the next change goes on, so no stream stays unclaimed in the connection's inbox. A full copy goes to a partial file renamed into place with `renameWithRetry` (Windows refuses a rename over a file another process has open), and a partial file a crash left is removed at the next sync; an append that does not follow the bytes held drops the file so the next sync sends it whole; a rewritten file (head or tail hash differs) comes whole. A host's projects sync in last-use order (`recentFolders`), each with `MIRROR_QUOTA_BYTES` (1 GiB) minus what the earlier ones hold as `budgetBytes`, so a file the daemon drops for the quota is deleted and not sent again while the budget stays; a project whose mirror folder cannot be read is logged and counts nothing, so the later projects still sync. A sync requested while one runs runs once more after it, of the latest request's projects, and a request from the turn the running sync read its last one on starts its own (`SessionMirror.schedule`). Stream bytes the daemon opens are buffered per stream id until claimed (`RemoteConnection.receiveStream`), bounded at 64 MiB unclaimed, past which the connection closes.

## Source control

- **Trust is the project folder's, and an untrusted project starts no git process** (`src/core/scm/scm-service.ts`, `git-runner.ts`, D8). `ScmService` asks `TrustService` about the project folder only, so nested repositories and submodules take its trust (`TrustStore` matches folders exactly), except one inside a registered project that is not trusted (next point). While the project is untrusted there is no discovery, status, watcher, check-ignore for the Files tree, decoration or commit-message diff, and the view shows the untrusted state. As a second line the runner refuses any call whose `project` is not trusted before it spawns (`GitUntrustedError`). A trust grant discovers the project with no reload; removing a project disposes its repositories, watchers and queued work, and work still queued is rejected (`RepoQueue.dispose`). `scm.integration.test.ts` arms `core.fsmonitor` in an untrusted repository and checks the hook never runs and the Git log stays empty.
- **The registered project nearest above a folder decides whether git runs there** (`ScmService.gate`, which wraps every runner use). A repository whose root, git dir or common dir lies in a registered project the user has not trusted never runs, whichever project found it: discovery and Open Repository skip it, and the gate refuses at each call, so a project registered or untrusted later stops the next call. Projects match by their registered path and by their realpath, which registration resolves with `fs.promises.realpath`, never a synchronous call on the main thread, where an unreachable network drive would freeze the app. Until every registered project's realpath has settled the gate decides nothing: a git call waits (it is not refused), discovery, Open Repository and safe.directory wait before they decide, and `mayRunIn` refuses, since a link could hide that a folder lies inside an untrusted project; a discovery that saw such a refusal runs again, and removing a project releases the calls waiting on it. `decorations()`, which cannot wait, shows nothing for a project whose own realpath is unknown. A project trusted by name runs although a project above it is not, as the trust store matches folders exactly in both directions. Git gets `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` so it never searches up into a refused folder. While a refused project lies inside a repository, git enters none of it, and every other submodule keeps its changes (`fence` in `scm-service.ts`). Every pathspec call there (status, diff, add, reset, rm, ls-files, and stash push, which otherwise runs a status inside every populated submodule) gets `:(exclude,literal)<refused>` for each refused project, `icase` added on win32 and darwin, after its own paths turned `:(literal)`, since `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1` would switch the exclusion off; a call with no paths gets the exclusions alone, which git reads as the whole tree minus them. A refused project inside a folder that holds a `.git` (a submodule, or a nested repository) cannot be excluded by its own path, since git sees that folder only as one gitlink and runs a status inside it that enters the whole of it, and git refuses a pathspec inside a submodule outright: so the outermost such folder between the repository and the refused project is fenced as if it were the refused project (`enclosingRepositories`, one `lstat` of `<folder>/.git` per folder between them on each fenced call, no git spawn, nothing cached, an `lstat` failing but by absence counted as a `.git`). That gitlink's changes then no longer show in the outer repository (its own repository shows them, the refused project excluded there), and a call naming it is refused. Every call passes `-c submodule.recurse=false`, because a user's `submodule.recurse=true` makes `merge --abort` and `cherry-pick --abort` reset every submodule's work tree. Some subcommands take no pathspec yet enter every gitlink, and each gets the settings that stop it (`COARSE_SUBCOMMANDS`): `commit` passes `-c diff.ignoreSubmodules=all -c status.submoduleSummary=false`, because with nothing to commit it prints a full status, and only that printed status reads them, so a staged submodule commit is still recorded (a user's `status.submoduleSummary=true` makes that status run `git log` inside each submodule whatever `diff.ignoreSubmodules` says); a per-name `submodule.<name>.ignore` from `.gitmodules` or the config beats `diff.ignoreSubmodules` there and in a real merge's `stash create`, which a pull's merge runs too, so every one of these subcommands, `pull` included, also sets `submodule.<name>.ignore=all` for every name of the `.gitmodules` git reads (the work tree's, else the index's and HEAD's; while the work tree's does not parse, as with conflict markers, every blob an abort may restore: the index's stages, HEAD's and ORIG_HEAD's), which one `git config --get-regexp` per source lists before the call (`submoduleNames`). From git 2.31 they go in as `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` pairs (`GitCall.config`), which keep hundreds of names off the command line; below it as `-c`, which splits a name at its first `=`, so there a call is refused while a name holds one. Residual: a name the operation itself brings in or restores (a replayed commit, an applied autostash, or an abort restoring a `.gitmodules` the work tree no longer matches) is not covered; `merge`, `rebase` and `cherry-pick` pass them too, because a pending autostash (`MERGE_AUTOSTASH`, `rebase-merge/autostash`) is applied by `--abort`, `--continue`, `--skip` or the commit that ends the merge with a `stash apply` that prints a status; `rebase` also passes `-c commit.status=false`, because `--continue` with staged changes runs a child `git commit -e` whose message template holds a status, which `GIT_EDITOR=true` means nobody reads. Git's autostash enters every populated submodule (its `stash create` and its `stash apply` without `--quiet`), and so does the `stash create` every real merge runs before it starts (`save_state` in `builtin/merge.c`), whatever the autostash settings. So under the fence `pull`, `merge` and `rebase` pass `-c rebase.autoStash=false -c merge.autoStash=false -c pull.autoStash=false` (git 2.51's `pull.autoStash` would otherwise pass `--autostash` on), any `--autostash` is refused, and a pull, merge or rebase that starts is refused unless it runs after the engine's own fenced `stash push` in the same mutation slot (`Repository.withStash`, `GitCall.fencedStash`). Pull, Sync and Merge or Rebase Branch therefore stash the work tree's changes with that fenced push whenever a refused project lies inside the repository, whatever `autoStash` says, and afterwards apply exactly that stash with `stash apply --index --quiet` and drop its entry, checked by its commit; `--abort`, `--continue`, `--quit` and `--skip` are never refused. A user's `rebase.autoStash`, `merge.autoStash` and `pull.autoStash` are ignored there. Residual: a tracked file saved after the engine's stash and before git's merge step (for a pull, the whole fetch) makes that merge's `stash create` enter the refused submodule. The operands of `remote add` and `remote remove` are a name and a URL, never paths, so the fence does not match them against refused projects. `gitEnv` drops an inherited `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_GLOB_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_NOGLOB_PATHSPECS` and `GIT_ICASE_PATHSPECS`, since `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS` turns the exclusions into file names and the other three change how named paths match or clash with the engine's own `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1`. Stash apply and pop run with `--quiet`, since otherwise git runs a status afterwards that enters every submodule. Hooks are the trusted repository's code and the fence does not reach them: a hook inherits the call's `-c` settings and pairs (so its own `git status` ignores submodules under commit's settings) but never the exclusions. `submodule` with no path, and any named path at, inside or containing a refused project, are refused. Never fence a refused project with per-name `submodule.<name>.ignore`: it misses a gitlink with no `.gitmodules` entry; the per-name settings above only undo a user's own on the subcommands that check gitlinks with no pathspec. `-c submodule.recurse=false` also stops fetch, pull and push from entering a submodule, as command-line config is read last: neither `fetch.recurseSubmodules`, a per-name `fetchRecurseSubmodules` nor `push.recurseSubmodules` makes them recurse. Residual: refused projects match by path, so a link or junction in the work tree at a gitlink path that leads to a refused project outside the repository is not fenced, and Git for Windows follows a junction there. `scm-trust.integration.test.ts` pins each call with an armed `core.fsmonitor` under `submodule.recurse=true` and with an inherited `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1`, a refused project nested in a trusted submodule (a submodule of it, a plain repository in it, two levels down), the status settings above and the recursion settings (a `GIT_TRACE` of each git started inside the refused submodule, since `git log` and fetch run no fsmonitor), and probes that the unfenced commit and `merge --abort` do enter.
- **Every git spawn in `src`, outside tests, runs the absolute path `resolveGitExecutable` found** (`src/core/git-executable.ts`): checkpoints, rewind, Compass, the team fingerprint, the `mcp.local.json` ignore check and source control alike, never a bare `git`. A bare name searches every PATH entry, and a relative entry (`.`, `bin`) resolves against the spawn's working folder, which is often the repository, so a `git.exe` planted there would run. The resolver searches only absolute PATH entries (`searchPathEntries`, `src/core/executable-path.ts`) for `git` (`git.exe` on Windows), caches per PATH value, and reports `GIT_NOT_FOUND` when none holds it; each caller keeps its git-missing path. `git-executable.test.ts` fails on any `spawn`/`exec`/`run` call naming a bare `'git'`. The operating system tools follow the same rule through `src/core/system-tools.ts`: `taskkill`, `tasklist` and `icacls` run from `%SystemRoot%\System32` (`windowsSystemTool`, as VS Code's `killTree` names taskkill; a root that is not a local absolute path falls back to `C:\Windows`), the PowerShell tool's and the voice recorder's Windows PowerShell from `%SystemRoot%\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe` (`windowsPowerShell`), and the PowerShell tool's `pwsh` from an absolute PATH folder (on Windows a Store alias included), then on Windows `%ProgramFiles%\PowerShell\7` (`findPwsh`); `ldconfig` and `bash` from an absolute PATH folder, else from the folders they install in (`posixTool`, through the `findOnAbsolutePath` lookup git's resolver uses too); `ps` on macOS and the BSDs from `/bin/ps` (the checkpoints, as the shell sentinel does) and `swift` from `/usr/bin/swift`;
