a Linux browser is looked up with `findOnAbsolutePath`, never `which`; `nvidia-smi` from an absolute PATH folder, then `System32` and `%ProgramFiles%\NVIDIA Corporation\NVSMI` on Windows and the WSL2 passthrough on Linux (`nvidiaSmiCandidates`); and the voice install's C++ compilers (`g++`, `clang++`, `c++` and their C partners) from an absolute PATH folder (`ensureCxxToolchain`, which passes pip those absolute paths as `CC` and `CXX`), while a relative `damocles.voice.runtimePath` is refused. `system-tools.test.ts` fails on any call in `src`, tests excluded, whose program can be a name that is not an absolute path (a string literal, a template literal by its leading text, either side of `?:`, `??` or `||`, a file constant holding one, a `for...of` variable, used inside its loop, over an array literal holding one, directly, through a file constant or as returned by a file function, or a property read off an object literal such a variable or constant holds) passed to a `child_process` spawner (named, renamed, default, namespace or `require` imports), the checkpoints' `exec`/`execSafe`, a `promisify` alias of either, the `file` of a process launcher start written in place (`launch({ file })`, `runProcess({ file })`), a function in the same file that passes one of its parameters on as such a call's program, at any depth, or a constant that may hold such a function (`const probe = opts.probe ?? defaultProbe`). A program the guard cannot read (a parameter, a value computed at run time, a wrapper imported from another file) is its caller's to keep absolute.
- **Git runs from an absolute path, as argv** (`git-runner.ts`, `shell-env.ts` `probeGit`). `probeGit` takes `resolveGitExecutable`'s path and parses its version; the runner starts that path with `shell: false`, never a bare name, and both start git on the process launcher. Revisions pass `revision()` (`isSafeRevision`: no leading `-`, so `--upload-pack=x` is refused before any spawn, and check-ref-format's forbidden sequences) and follow `--end-of-options` on git 2.24 and later, except on `reset`, which git 2.43 refuses it on (its revision is the constant `HEAD`), and on `blame`, which then reads the path after `--` as a revision (git 2.56; its revision is HEAD's commit as core resolved it); paths follow `--` and are chunked under `MAX_CLI_LENGTH`. Every repo-relative path a renderer names passes `isRelativeFilePath`, and on Windows `hasUnsafeWindowsSegment`, in main's validator (`isRepoPath` in `scm-channels.ts`) and again in core (`checkRepoPath` in `repository.ts`). Those checks are lexical, and a path git gets in its argv has only them; a file operation main or core runs itself (open, reveal, mention, the trash, the conflict-marker scan) also confines the path by realpath (`Repository.confinedEntry`: the entry itself, a link not followed, its parent folder's realpath inside the root). A commit message goes in on stdin through `--file -`. A remote name, URL or branch a user typed or a repository's config holds reaches an argv only through `isRemoteName` (a letter or digit, then letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`; the overlay's `remoteName` validation and main check the same rule), `isRemoteUrl` (no leading `-` or whitespace, no control character, U+2028 or U+2029, no `<transport>::` remote helper, and whitespace only in a local path as git's `url_is_local_not_ssh` reads one, since in a URL or scp-like address ` -oProxyCommand=x:p` reads as an ssh host) and `revision()`, in main and again in core (`remotes.ts`, `sync.ts`, `clone.ts`); `git remote` output passes `isRemoteName` before a name is listed. Remotes and branches follow `--end-of-options` on fetch, pull and push, `remote add` and `remote remove` take `--` before their operands, and clone puts `--` before the URL and the target. A push names full refs (`refs/heads/<branch>:refs/heads/<branch>`), so a branch called `+main` can never read as a forced refspec.
- **The environment** (`gitEnv`): `childEnv()` (no repository-local git variable is inherited) plus `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_EDITOR=true` and `GIT_MERGE_AUTOEDIT=no` on every call, so no git command waits on a prompt or an editor (Continue Rebase reuses git's prepared message); `LC_ALL=C` for parsed output and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` for status. `core.longpaths=true` goes on every call as the first `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` pair, and as a leading `-c` option on git older than 2.31, which ignores those pairs; Compass and the team fingerprint pass it in `GIT_HARDENING_ARGS`: a chat worktree sits deeper than its project, so a path that fits in the project can pass Windows' 260 characters in the worktree, where Git for Windows fails `worktree add`, status and `worktree remove` with "Filename too long" without it. `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1` is set only on calls that name paths (add, reset, rm, mv, checkout-index, ls-files, submodule, diff, a path-limited status), because hooks inherit the environment and a hook's own globs must keep working; a file named `[ab].txt` or `:(top)x` therefore never widens a stage or a discard. check-ignore refuses literal magic, so it gets each path as `./<path>` on stdin instead. Parsed output passes `--no-color`, diff `--no-ext-diff`.
- **One queue per repository** (`RepoQueue`): reads run up to 4 at once; mutations run one at a time, exclusive of status, in arrival order; one status runs at a time and every request made while one waits joins that trailing run. Disposing rejects what waits and kills every running read and status with its process tree (`RepoQueue.signal` on each such call: its own job object on Windows, its own process group on POSIX); an index or ref write never carries the signal, so a running write finishes. The user's remote operations are the exception (next points): their own signal, not the queue's dispose alone, may end a fetch, pull or push. Each such call also joins the one session job `ScmService` holds (`Launcher.createSessionJob()`, passed by `gate` as `GitCall.session`), which the service closes on dispose; it is the service's because its dispose is where every repository's git ends. The job lives in Damocles Core's launcher pool, so a crashed or killed Damocles Core closes it too (main's end ends core), and a hung status and its hooks die with it: kill-on-close on Windows, the sentinel on POSIX. Index and ref writes join no session job, and the call job every Windows start gets has no kill-on-close, so neither path kills one. The cost, not measured: on POSIX each cancellable call writes an `r` and an `x` record to the sentinel, and the `x` makes it read the process table (`ps -A` on macOS); on Windows a daemon a status hook starts (an fsmonitor daemon, a watchman server) is a job member and dies at app exit. `scm-cancel.integration.test.ts` closes only the session job under a hung status and sees git, the hook and its child die. Every read the queue may kill writes nothing: status runs with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and every diff with `-c diff.autoRefreshIndex=false`. There is no timeout, as in VS Code.
- **Status is VS Code's** (`git status --porcelain=v1 -z -uall`, `-uno` at `untrackedChanges` `hidden`), streamed through `GitStatusParser` and stopped past 10,000 entries (`STATUS_LIMIT`, VS Code's `git.statusLimit`); the snapshot then holds the first 10,000 and `tooManyChanges`. The workspace and git-dir watchers feed a 500 ms trailing debounce per repository (`WATCH_DEBOUNCE_MS`); the git dir counts only `HEAD`, `index`, `config` (remotes and upstreams), `refs/**`, `packed-refs`, `FETCH_HEAD`, `MERGE_HEAD`, `REBASE_HEAD`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `MERGE_MSG`, `SQUASH_MSG`, `rebase-merge/**`, `rebase-apply/**` and `worktrees/**`, and a git dir outside the project folder (a linked worktree's, a parent's) gets its own watch.
- **Discovery follows no link and opens no linked worktree** (`discovery.ts`). The repository holding the project folder always opens, even when that folder is a linked worktree; the scan to `repositoryScanMaxDepth` enters only real folders (a link or junction is never a `Dirent` directory) and leaves other linked worktrees unopened (VS Code's `git.detectWorktrees: false`). Each `.gitmodules` path is repository text: it must be a plain relative path whose folder is no link and whose realpath stays inside the repository. A repository whose root or git dir no longer exists closes when one of its git calls fails (`ScmService.closeIfGone`; a spawn in a removed folder fails with ENOENT, which VS Code reads as NotAGitRepository and disposes the repository on), and that call rejects as one of a closed repository (`GitQueueDisposedError`), so a read main runs for the shell answers as for a closed repository (`ScmHost.whileOpen`); discovery opens it again if it comes back.
- **A parent repository opens only after the user's answer** (D9). Discovery runs only the top-level lookup there and posts VS Code's non-modal notice (Yes, Always, Never) through `NotificationService`, because nothing the user did asked and plan section 7 allows a modal only for a user action; dismissing it opens nothing. Plan AD4 lists the parent question among the `ask` questions; the one `ask` is when the user runs Open Repository on that folder, a warning dialog with Cancel focused. Always and Never write `damocles.desktop.git.openRepositoryInParentFolders` (user scope), Yes is remembered per repository in global state. Open Repository refuses a folder outside the project folder by realpath.
- **safe.directory needs the user's consent and main's own path** (`ScmService.trustUnsafe`). A repository refused for dubious ownership is listed only from main's discovery; one above the project folder goes through D9 first, so its ownership check is never turned off before the parent was accepted. After a confirmation naming the path, `git config --global --add safe.directory <path>` runs in argv form from the home folder, with the path exactly as git printed it.
- **The Git log is redacted as it is written** (`git-runner.ts`): each line records argv, duration and exit code; `scheme://userinfo@` loses its userinfo on every argv and stderr line; an argument with anything but plain path characters is JSON-quoted, so a file name cannot forge a log line; stdin (commit messages, path lists) is never logged. Show Git Output opens the log read-only (D36).
- **Every discarded working copy goes to the OS trash first, so no discard loses data** (`Repository.discard`, through the injected `trash`, `shell.trashItem`). This deviates from VS Code, whose tracked discard is an irreversible `git checkout`, and follows GitHub Desktop. Inside the mutation, after the fresh-status re-check: an untracked file is trashed (`confinedEntry`); a tracked file's working copy is trashed whenever lstat finds anything at its path, whatever the status letter says (a folder that replaced a deleted file goes too), then `git checkout-index -u` writes the index version. Without `-f` git creates each file exclusively and refuses one that reappeared after its trash step, so nothing unsaved is ever overwritten. A tracked path whose parent folder is a link is refused, since lstat would follow the link and git never writes through one. A file whose trash step fails keeps its changes, is never written, and is named in the error after the rest are discarded. An intent-to-add file gets back its empty placeholder, as `git restore` gives it; an intent-to-rename gets both paths back from the index; a staged rename edited again (RM, RD) gets only its destination, since the index no longer has the source. On an unborn branch the index is the source too. A submodule, and any gitlink the index records (`ls-files -s`, mode 160000, `.gitmodules` or not), is never trashed: `git submodule update --no-fetch` puts it back at the recorded commit, fetching nothing, and a commit missing locally fails with git's own error. `--no-fetch` does not stop the clone of a submodule whose git dir is gone, so that call also runs with `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=none` (`GitCall.localOnly`), a protocol name that matches nothing and overrides every `protocol.*.allow`. Only the working-tree and untracked groups have Discard. The confirmations keep VS Code's texts with Cancel focused and danger styling, minus "This is IRREVERSIBLE!": a tracked discard says the changed files can be restored from the Recycle Bin (Windows) or the Trash, as the untracked one does; a restore of deleted files only says nothing. Add to .gitignore appends to the root's `.gitignore` after refusing one that is a link, escapes glob characters and a leading `#` or `!`, and refuses a name with a control character.
- **Generate commit message keeps repository text in data position** (`commit-message.ts`, D24): the system prompt is a constant; the staged diff (else every tracked change against HEAD), cut at 30,000 characters, and the last 10 subjects, each cut at 200, travel only in the user message as fenced data. A failure leaves the commit box as it is and the notice names a reason from the result kind, never provider or diff text.
- **A commit's questions go through the injected `ask`, in VS Code's `smartCommit` order, and the index and branches change only after the last one** (`ScmService.commit`, `scm-host.ts` `commit`). Main asks the save-before-commit question (`promptToSaveFilesBeforeCommit`) and saves; core then asks, through `ask` only, Commit Empty's confirmation (`confirmEmptyCommits`), the smart-commit suggestion (Always writes `enableSmartCommit`, Never `suggestSmartCommit`; `smartCommitChanges` picks `add -u`, or `add -A` of the paths the status lists; Stage All at `untrackedChanges` `mixed` stages the listed paths too, as VS Code's `stageAll` does, never `add -A -- .`, which VS Code's smart commit runs and which adds an untracked nested repository, a folder status lists as no change, as a gitlink; while the status stopped at `STATUS_LIMIT` (`tooManyChanges`), Commit All and smart commit that would stage the listed paths refuse before staging anything and name the limit, since they would commit part of the changes, while Stage All stages the listed ones as VS Code does), the No Verify confirmation (`confirmNoVerifyCommit`; refused outright while `allowNoVerifyCommit` is off) and the protected-branch question (`branchProtection` globs through picomatch, `branchProtectionPrompt`), whose branch name comes from main's QuickInput and must pass `git check-ref-format --branch` unchanged. Staging, re-adding the saved staged files and `checkout -b` all run inside the one commit mutation after every answer, so a Cancel at any question leaves the index and the branches as they were. What happens before the last answer stays after a Cancel: the saved files, and the setting an answer writes as it is given (Yes, Don't Show Again; OK, Don't Ask Again; Always; Never), and Never ends the commit unmade.
- **Two settings and the commit variants add arguments to `git commit`** (`Repository.commit`): the `alwaysSignOff` setting adds `--signoff` (as does a Signed Off variant), the `enableCommitSigning` setting adds `-S` (otherwise git's own `commit.gpgSign` decides), a No Verify variant adds `--no-verify`, which skips the pre-commit and commit-msg hooks, and Commit Empty adds `--allow-empty`. An amend with an empty box runs `--no-edit` and keeps HEAD's message. A failed commit's reason comes from the repository, never from git's or a hook's words, because commit runs in the user's locale (`Repository.commitFailure`): `unresolvedConflicts` when `ls-files --unmerged` lists an entry, `missingUserConfig` when `git -c user.useConfigOnly=true var GIT_COMMITTER_IDENT` or `GIT_AUTHOR_IDENT` exits non-zero, `nothingToCommit` when the commit step itself failed, was neither an amend nor an empty commit, no merge is in progress and `diff --cached --quiet` finds nothing staged, else `failed` with git's stderr. After a successful commit `ScmHost.runPostCommitCommand` (the hook's one place) runs Push or Sync as its own flow, with that flow's questions and notices: Commit & Push and Commit & Sync name theirs (`ScmCommitRequest.postCommit`), otherwise `postCommitCommand` decides.
- **Remote operations are cancellable, one per common dir** (`ScmService.remoteOp`, `sync.ts`, `remotes.ts`). Fetch, pull, push, sync and publish take the user's `AbortSignal` and the service's session job through the runner's cancellable spawn, show as the snapshot's `running` and end in `lastRemote`; a second one on any repository sharing the common dir (linked worktrees) is refused. Fetch and push write refs, each under git's own lock, and take no queue slot; a pull writes the index, so it holds the mutation slot, and it is cancellable all the same (AD4), as git's own interrupt is: a cancel may leave a merge or rebase in progress. A kill runs none of git's cleanup, so after a cancel, a kill by the repository's closing, or an autofetch killed by an interactive operation or a stop, the engine removes the `*.lock` files the operation could have created that were not there when it started and were written since (`Repository.removeLocksSince`), and logs each. That scope is every remote operation's `refs/remotes/**`, `refs/tags/**`, `packed-refs.lock`, `config.lock`, `shallow.lock` and `FETCH_HEAD.lock`, the `refs/heads/<branch>` it writes (the fast-forward's target, the pull's branch), and for a pull its own git dir's top-level locks and the `rebase-merge` and `rebase-apply` ones; a fetch or push never removes `index.lock` or `HEAD.lock`. The cleanup holds the mutation slot of every open repository sharing the common dir (a pull cleans up in its own slot before it releases it), so no lock of the engine's own git is ever removed. It follows no link or junction, and `modules/` and `reftable/` are not scanned, so a killed recursive fetch's submodule ref locks and `reftable/tables.list.lock` stay (residual). A removal that fails is logged and the cancel still answers `cancelled`. Residual: a lock in that scope that a git process in the user's terminal took on the same repository during those seconds is removed too. A cancel answers `cancelled` with no notice; any other failure is one notice with Show Git Output, whose text is git's `error:` and `fatal:` lines, redacted and escaped (`gitErrorText`). Pull and Sync read HEAD's branch and upstream inside the pull's mutation slot, never from the snapshot, and pull `<remote> refs/heads/<branch>` as VS Code does; an upstream that is no such pair (a refspec mapping `refs/pull/*`, a remote name `isRemoteName` refuses) is pulled and pushed with a plain `git pull` and `git push`. Sync asks first while `confirmSync` is on, then pushes only when HEAD is ahead, with `--follow-tags` at `followTagsWhenSync`; when HEAD's branch or upstream is no longer the one confirmed, before the pull or before the push, it stops with `SyncBranchChangedError` and pushes nothing. Pull uses `--autostash` from git 2.27 at `autoStash` outside the trust fence. When git cannot apply its autostash back it exits 0 and keeps the entry in the list under its untranslated reflog message `autostash`, so the engine reads the newest stash entry before and after the pull in the pull's own slot (`StashSlot.newestStash`, `keptAutostash`) and a new `autostash` entry throws the same `StashKeptError` below, which also stops Sync before its push; with an older git, and always under the fence, the engine stashes around the operation itself (VS Code's `maybeAutoStash`): it compares `refs/stash` before and after its `stash push`, applies only the stash that push created, by its commit, with `stash apply --index --quiet` after the lock cleanup, and drops that entry. When the stash cannot be applied back (the apply fails or cannot run, or a merge or rebase is left in progress) the operation throws `StashKeptError`, which carries the operation's own outcome and the stash's commit, so the user learns the changes are in the stash list: main shows the operation's own failure or conflict, then names the stash by its index in a fresh list, with View Stash (`ScmHost.stashKept`). The snapshot's `lastRemote` records the operation's own outcome with `stashKept` (`keptStashOutcome`): a pull that went through is `ok`, a sync stops before its push and is `failed`, and a cancel stays `cancelled`. The progress row holds a warning, not a check, for an `ok` one, and its live region names the outcome and that the changes are in the stash list. A `SyncBranchChangedError` is a warning naming the confirmed branch and upstream and the ones found. When an interactive operation or clone cannot register with the askpass server it runs without askpass and logs one line; autofetch does not run then.
- **An engine write resolves only after its status refresh** (`ScmService.wrote`, as VS Code's `Repository.run`). Every operation that writes the index, HEAD, refs, stashes, worktrees or config (commit and the other index writes, checkout, branch and tag operations, stash operations, merge and rebase, and every remote operation) resolves or rejects only once a fresh status has landed in the snapshot, so a caller that reads the snapshot next, such as the post-commit Push or Sync, sees the repository as the operation left it. A failed refresh never fails the operation; it is logged unless the repository was closed or refused meanwhile.
- **Force push exists only while `allowForcePush` is on, and always asks** (`commands.ts`, `ScmHost.push`, `ScmService.push`). The force commands are `hidden` from the menu and the palette and the shell's menus drop them while the setting is off; main refuses a force request then, and core refuses again. Every force push asks through `ask` with danger styling, so Cancel takes focus, and pushes with `--force-with-lease`, plus `--force-if-includes` from git 2.30, never a plain `--force`.
- **Credentials reach git only through the askpass server, and only on the calls that may need them** (`src/core/scm/askpass/askpass-server.ts`, `src/desktop/askpass/askpass-main.ts`, `resources/askpass/*.sh`). One server per run starts on the first registration: a named pipe `\\.\pipe\damocles-askpass-<128-bit hex>` on Windows (libuv creates the first instance exclusively, so a taken name fails and another is tried), a socket in a `mkdtemp` folder on POSIX, under `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` when it is an absolute path, else the temp folder, else `/tmp`, for a `TMPDIR` too deep for a socket path. A base whose socket path would pass `sun_path` (107 bytes on Linux, 103 on macOS) is skipped before any folder is made, since libuv truncates such a path on some versions and binds the socket outside the folder. The folder is checked to be a directory owned by the user with no group or other bits, and removed on dispose and when the socket cannot listen there (`EACCES`), after which the next base is tried. A core that crashed or was killed never disposed its server, so each core's start sweeps those bases (`removeAbandonedSocketFolders`): it removes a `damocles-askpass-*` folder only when the user owns it, it holds nothing but its socket, it was last changed over a minute ago (`ABANDONED_SOCKET_FOLDER_AGE_MS`; a starting server makes its socket moments after its folder) and its socket refuses a connection or is missing. A server still listening in any process answers and keeps its folder, so another running Damocles never loses one and no pid, reused or not, decides. Git's server runs in Damocles Core (main runs a second instance for its SSH transports, "Remote daemon and wire"); core's `core.shutdown` awaits its close, and core's log sinks, the Git log among them, flush after it. Each fetch, pull, push, sync, publish, clone and autofetch call registers and gets, for that call alone, `GIT_ASKPASS`, `SSH_ASKPASS`, `SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=force` and the helper's node, script, handle and a fresh 32-byte nonce in its environment (`GitCall.askpassEnv`); nothing goes into `process.env` or `childEnv()`, so terminals, hosts and other git calls never see them. The scripts set `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` on their own exec line only and pass git's prompt as one argument (`"$@"`). The helper sends one JSON line `{ nonce, kind, argv }`, with ssh's `SSH_ASKPASS_PROMPT` as `sshPrompt` when it is `confirm` or `none`, and the server answers one line `{ answer }` or `{ cancel: true }`. A prompt the user dismisses (Escape or Cancel in the box, No on a yes/no question) cancels its call: the server aborts the registration's `cancelled` signal before it answers the cancel, and the operation's own controller aborts with it, so the operation ends as the progress row's Cancel ends it (VS Code instead answers an empty string and reports git's authentication failure); a withdrawn prompt, a closed notice and a background registration cancel nothing. Both ends allow at most 16 arguments of 4096 characters (`helper-protocol.test.ts` compares the constants), and the server also allows at most 16 KiB per line, a bound arguments within those limits can still pass once JSON-escaped; such a request is dropped. A request that is malformed, oversized, has bytes after its line, names a nonce no live registration holds (every registration compared with `timingSafeEqual`) or is not complete 5 s after connecting has its connection destroyed. At most 16 connections may wait for their request (`MAX_WAITING_CONNECTIONS`); one more drops the oldest of them, so connections that send nothing cannot shut a helper out, since a helper writes its request as soon as it connects. Residual, as in VS Code: libuv creates the Windows pipe with the default DACL and without `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`, so another local user, or a remote one over SMB, can connect. The nonce keeps every answer from them, and to deny prompts they must keep making 16 new connections within the moment between a helper's connect and its request (under 1 ms for the built helper on Windows, measured from accept to its request line). Interactive prompts run one at a time (`askpass-bridge.ts`): git's `Username for '<url>': `, matched only at the start since the URL can hold the word, in the overlay's plain QuickInput; anything else git or ssh asks (a password, a token, a passphrase) in its password box; an SSH host key, or any question ssh marks `SSH_ASKPASS_PROMPT=confirm`, as a danger question whose No takes focus; and ssh's `SSH_ASKPASS_PROMPT=none` notice (a security key's touch request, which ssh ends by killing the helper) as an information dialog with OK, which answers nothing. VS Code ignores `SSH_ASKPASS_PROMPT`. git's text is shown as plain text, without control or format characters (bidi, zero-width, soft hyphen, byte order mark) or URL userinfo, cut at 1000 code points. A prompt is withdrawn when its helper goes away or its call ends: the overlay settles that one request (`OverlayHost.request`'s signal), never another question of its kind; the OS message box fallback cannot be closed, so a withdrawal logs that and ignores its answer. A withdrawn prompt that does not close holds the line for at most `WITHDRAWN_PROMPT_GRACE_MS` (2 s). A background registration (autofetch) is answered cancel without a prompt, logs one line per call, and its call gets `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never` so Git Credential Manager opens no window. No answer, nonce or prompt text is logged or cached; git's credential helper does the storing. Residual, as in VS Code: a hook of a credential-capable call (pre-push) inherits the registration's variables and can ask the user through it while the call runs; hooks are the trusted repository's code. A daemon's core also has the git credential relay ("Remote daemon and wire"), whose nonce this server accepts for `credential` requests only, and never a prompt nonce for one.
- **Branches, stash, tags and worktrees are pulled on demand, and every name a renderer sends is main's own** (`artifacts.ts`, `branches.ts`, `stash.ts`, `tags.ts`, `worktrees.ts`, `scm-host.ts` `artifacts`, `scm/ref-flows.ts`). The snapshot carries no ref, stash or worktree list: the branch picker, the Stashes group and the Repositories section read one group at a time through `damocles:scm:artifacts` (`for-each-ref --sort=-creatordate` with commit details, `stash list`, of which every entry is listed and one whose message is not git's `On <branch>:` or `WIP on <branch>:` (git's `autostash`, `stash store -m`) shows that message with no branch, where VS Code hides it, `worktree list --porcelain` with each HEAD's commit), and main sends `damocles:scm:artifacts-changed { repoId, groups }`, coalesced per repository, after the git-dir watcher reports `HEAD`, `config`, `packed-refs`, `refs/heads`, `refs/remotes`, `refs/tags`, `refs/stash`, `logs/refs/stash` (dropping `stash@{1}` rewrites only the reflog) or the common dir's `worktrees/**` (after the 500 ms debounce) and after every engine write to refs, stashes or worktrees (at once). A ref, stash or worktree path a renderer names (a full refname, a stash's commit hash, an absolute worktree path in this computer's or a remote repository's host's path rules; never a UNC path) passes the validator and is then looked up in a fresh read of main's own list before git sees it; pickers answer with ids main issued. Short names pass `revision()`, so a branch `update-ref` named `refs/heads/-x` never reads as an option; `checkout` takes no `--end-of-options` (git 2.43 reads it as a path in every form), so its name sits between `-q` and `--`, and a remote branch is checked out by its full refname with `--track`. `stash@{n}` is built from the integer only after the hash matched the list; apply takes the stash commit itself, and pop and drop check that `stash@{n}` still names it in the same mutation slot. A tag's message goes in on stdin. View Stash and Compare tabs are pinned to commit hashes, so a shifted stash index or a moved ref never changes what they show; main answers a file of such a tab only while it has that tab open and lists that file, and closes a stash's tabs once the stash leaves the list. A View Stash tab stays open exactly as long as its stash exists: its Apply keeps it, and a successful Pop or Drop closes it, while a pop that leaves the stash (a conflict) keeps it. Checkout, branch delete, stash push, worktree remove and pullBeforeCheckout's fast-forward run with `LC_ALL=C`, because main tells their refusals (`Please commit your changes or stash them`, `is not fully merged`, `You do not have the initial commit yet`, `contains modified or untracked files`, `(non-fast-forward)`, `refusing to fetch into branch`) from git's words; their hooks print in English. A branch checkout git refuses while another worktree of the repository has that branch checked out, read from a fresh `worktree list` after the refusal and never from git's words, asks VS Code's `WorktreeBranchAlreadyUsed` question with Open Worktree, which runs the worktree row's Open; every checkout path (Checkout to..., a Repositories row, the checkout after Stash & Checkout or Force Checkout) and Create Worktree from such a branch ask it. A conflict after merge, rebase or stash apply is read from the index (`ls-files --unmerged`) in the operation's own mutation slot, never from git's words (`stash apply --quiet` prints no `CONFLICT` line, where VS Code reads one). Merge Branch, Rebase Branch, Apply Stash and Pop Stash refuse before git runs while the index already holds unmerged entries, which git cannot merge, rebase or apply a stash over, so the unmerged entries found after git ran are always that operation's own. Stash Staged needs git 2.35, so the palette and menus hide it and main refuses it below that.
- **Ref pickers list every ref the overlay's bounds hold, newest first** (`ref-flows.ts` `pickRef`, `MAX_QUICK_PICK_ITEMS` and `MAX_QUICK_PICK_BYTES` in `overlay-channels.ts`). Local branches, remote branches and tags are each a group in core's `--sort=-creatordate` order, as VS Code's `git.branchSortOrder` default `committerdate` lists them. A quick pick holds at most 50,000 items and 12 MiB of item JSON (a ref item averages about 180 bytes on VS Code's own repository): measured with the overlay's filter at 200,000 refs, a keystroke took 150 to 800 ms (happy-dom, a fast desktop CPU), and at 50,000 at most 90 ms, so the bound is the count that stays responsive. Past it a ref picker keeps every local branch, fills with the newest remote branches and tags by commit date, and ends with an info row naming how many refs it left out, which shows whatever the filter and that no answer may name. Any other list past the bounds is refused with its own notice, never "Try again" (`createQuickPickAsker`). Main shows a quick pick's label, description, hint and group with each control (tab aside, which is whitespace), bidi and zero-width character as its `\u{…}` escape (`parseQuickPickRequest`, with the table the shell's source control views use, `src/shared/visible-text.ts`), so a ref name cannot read as another, and the byte budget counts the escaped text. The Graph's ref picker offers at most `MAX_REF_PICKER_REFS` (2000) refs in the listing's order, always keeping the refs chosen, and says how many it left out and when a group of refs could not be read, whatever the filter (`pickerRefs` in `graph-model.ts`).
- **One selected repository per project, which every view follows** (`scm-host.ts` `select`, `ScmView.selectedRepoId`, `window-layout.json` `scmSelected`, D58). Source control's picker and a click on any Repositories row of a repository set it through `damocles:scm:select`, and Source control and the Repositories highlight follow the view main sends; the shell keeps only which row of that repository is highlighted. A row of another repository than the selected one loses its highlight to that repository's row, as VS Code's `updateTreeSelection` replaces the tree selection.
- **A Repositories row action calls the operation Source control's menus call for it** (`scm-menus.ts` `stashRowCall`, `artifactRowCall`, D58). Stash rows of both sections map their actions through `stashRowCall`; a branch, tag or worktree row names itself to main only by its full refname or path, which main looks up in its own fresh list; Compare with... (`compareRef`) and a worktree's Open (`openWorktree`) are the only operations the rows add, and Open selects the project whose folder the worktree holds, else adds the worktree as a project, which asks for trust. Rows are context-menu actions, never registry commands (D28). A group is read only while it shows expanded and the section is open, and again on `artifacts-changed` only then; a group a change reaches while hidden is read when it shows again. Expanded rows persist per project in `window-layout.json` (`repositories`) as ids built from the repository's project-relative path (a parent repository's absolute path), never its run id. The shell reports them within `boundRepositoryViewStates`' bounds (`shell-channels.ts`: ids of at most 2048 characters, the newest 500 per project and the newest 100 projects, all keys and ids within 262,144 characters), so main takes a report only whole within them and trims only the file, logging what it drops. A group main answers `unknown` for is gone, never read again, until the view drops its repository; a failed group is read again when it shows again; and once a Branches or Tags list is read, the next report leaves out the folder rows it no longer holds.
- **Forced deletes always ask first, and so does every worktree delete** (`ref-flows.ts` `deleteBranch`, `deleteWorktree`). `branch -d` runs first; only when git refuses an unmerged branch does main ask (danger, Cancel focused) and then run `branch -D`. Delete Worktree, from its row or the palette's picker, always asks first (danger, Cancel focused), naming the folder and saying its ignored files (`.env`, `node_modules`) are deleted too, since `worktree remove` without `--force` checks only tracked and untracked files; when the folder holds a registered project (`ScmHost.worktreeProject`) the question names it. Before anything closes, core runs the status `git worktree remove` runs (`ScmService.worktreeDirty`: `status --porcelain --ignore-submodules=none` as a status call in the worktree itself, so the gate decides on that folder and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` applies; main composes no git argv), and a dirty worktree asks the force question (danger, Cancel focused), as VS Code does, before `--force`. When git still refuses because files appeared since, main asks again only while nothing has closed; once the worktree's project closed nothing asks, and the failure, like any failed remove after the close, names the path and the closed project with Retry, which runs the whole order again. Drop All Stashes asks the same way. A branch or tag a Repositories row names asks VS Code's delete question first.
- **Every worktree delete closes what runs in the worktree before git removes it** (slice 12a rule 14, `worktree-controller.ts`, `worktrees/close-worktree.ts`). For a chat worktree every question comes first: each dirty editor tab of it asks Save, Don't Save or Cancel (Save saves then, while the worktree is still a root; Don't Save drops nothing yet; `EditorPane.confirmCloseRoot`), then its terminals ask once when one runs a process, and the tabs are asked again only for a buffer edited, and the terminals only for one opened, while a question was open. A terminal opened while the hold was taken is asked about after it, before anything stops, and a buffer edited then stops the delete with a notice that nothing was deleted (`CloseOutcome` `edited`). Cancel at any question, or a chat that starts working before the delete takes its hold, stops the delete with every tab, buffer, terminal, chat and formatter host as it was. After the hold, the root closes, its tabs close (dropping the text answered Don't Save), the chats using it unload, its terminals end, and main waits for its formatter host's process to exit (Windows refuses to remove a live process's working folder; a file watcher does not block a remove) and for `PanelManager.whenFolderReleased`, so core has stopped the folder's runtime, project MCP servers, watchers and Compass; its file watchers and source control repository close with its root. Only then does `removeWorktree` run `git worktree remove`. A removed worktree's root key leaves `window-layout.json` (`forgetRoot`). A worktree open as its own project (Repositories' Delete) gets an adopted record first, then closes that project and its chats the same way, and the record is marked deleted after the remove, so its chats are listed read-only under the main project; with that project not open, they are not listed. A failed remove keeps the worktree registered and names the path, and Retry runs the whole order again. A chat worktree's Delete is refused while a chat using it works, waits on the user or has subagents or a team working, and while another Damocles process holds the lease of one of its chats.
- **Branch, tag and stash remote operations are remote operations** (`ScmService.deleteRemoteRef`, `remoteTags`, `pushTags`, `fastForward`). Delete Remote Branch, Delete Remote Tag (its `ls-remote --tags` listing too), Push Tags and pullBeforeCheckout's fast-forward run through `Repository.network` inside `ScmService.remoteOp`, so they show the progress row with Cancel, get the askpass registration and are one per common dir; they run as `deleteRemoteBranch`, `deleteRemoteTag`, `push` and `pull`. The fast-forward runs only for a branch with an upstream and no outgoing commits, and is `fetch <remote> refs/heads/<upstream>:refs/heads/<branch>` with no `+`, which git refuses unless it fast-forwards; a refusal (non-fast-forward, or the branch checked out in another worktree) is no failure, any other failure leaves the branch as it is and the checkout goes on as in VS Code (main's log records the failure, and the Git log git's own exit line), and a cancel stops the checkout.
- **Autofetch runs for the selected project only** (`autofetch.ts`, D10). While `autofetch` is on, the selected project's repositories that have a remote fetch as soon as the first of them has a status, and then every `autofetchPeriod` seconds (clamped to 1 to 86400 in core), one fetch per common dir, so linked worktrees share it; selecting another project starts it over there, a trust grant that brings back such a repository starts it again, and a project removed or untrusted disposes its repositories, which kills their fetch. Right before each fetch the round asks whether an interactive remote operation runs in that common dir and skips it if so, and an interactive operation kills the autofetch of its common dir and waits for it to end first. It never prompts (a background registration) and shows no progress row. A failure is logged once per common dir and distinct failure, until a fetch there succeeds or autofetch starts over. The fetch runs in English and git's stderr is matched against the credential refusals, ssh's included, so a refused credential stops that common dir until the next start, as VS Code's AuthenticationFailed does.
- **Clone runs whatever the selected project's trust, and its project starts untrusted** (`clone.ts`, `ScmService.clone`, `GitCloner.clone`). Clone runs no code of an existing repository, so it is the one git call no project's trust decides: the runner's `clone` entry runs `git clone` only, from the parent folder the user picks (the OS folder picker always shows, opened at `defaultCloneDirectory`, else the home folder, as VS Code's `cloneRepository`) with `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` there, and the trust gate never wraps it. The folder is the URL's last segment, then `-1` to `-19`, as VS Code picks it, skipping a name that exists or that the trust store still lists (a folder trusted once and deleted since), and it must be a plain folder name; a cancelled or failed clone removes it when `lstat` finds a real directory, which the clone created. Adding the clone as a project (`openAfterClone` `always`, or the question at `prompt`) asks for trust as any added project does. Clone (Recursive) passes `--recurse-submodules` and never sets `protocol.file.allow` or `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL`. Residuals: a clone into a folder inside a trusted project that the user does not add as its own project takes that project's trust, as a clone from a terminal there does; and a recursive clone runs git's own submodule code, so a git with a known recursive-clone flaw (CVE-2024-32002 before 2.45.1, CVE-2025-48384 before 2.43.7, 2.44.4, 2.45.4, 2.46.4, 2.47.3, 2.48.2, 2.49.1 and 2.50.1) can run code from the cloned repository; the engine does not gate it on the version number, because distributions backport those fixes without changing it (Ubuntu 24.04's 2.43.0).
- **A git side reaches the renderer only as bounded text, else as a placeholder** (`ScmHost.gitSide`, `EditorPane.showDiff`, `DiffEditor.vue`). A blob that does not exist on that side, is over `MAX_SHOW_BYTES` (`Repository.show` answers `tooLarge`) or does not decode as text becomes a `NotShownSide` holding its reason and size only, as a working-tree file the document service will not open as text (binary, too large, unreadable, an image) is already a `notDisplayed` document. The diff opens anyway, as VS Code's does: the tab carries `ShellDiffSide` per side, the shell renders a placeholder side as a centred notice beside the other side's plain editor, and only the modified side when it is a file on disk offers Open Anyway, the tab's Open File. A single side (added, deleted, Open File (HEAD)) opens as a placeholder tab, which has no Reveal since it is no file.
- **Undo Last Commit is one compare-and-swap of HEAD** (`Repository.undoCommit`). The confirmed commit's parent replaces it through `git update-ref -m "reset: moving to <parent>" HEAD <parent> <hash>` (a first commit: `update-ref -d HEAD <hash>`, then everything unstaged), which git refuses once HEAD no longer names `<hash>`, so a commit that lands after the question is never dropped; a refusal reads HEAD again and reports the localized "changed after you asked" error. Never a HEAD check followed by `reset`. Like `reset --soft` it moves HEAD's branch only, writes both reflogs and leaves the index and work tree as they are, and it refuses while `MERGE_HEAD` exists or the index has unmerged entries; unlike `reset` it writes no `ORIG_HEAD` and clears no cherry-pick or sequencer state. `scm-mutations.integration.test.ts` lands a commit between the read and the update through a runner wrapper.
- **Commit message history lives in global `KeyValueState`** under `damocles.scm.commitHistory` (`commit-history.ts`): one list per repository root (`folderKey`), newest first, a repeated message moved to the front, at most 100 per root (VS Code's SCM input history) and 100 roots. Core writes it after each successful commit; the shell reads it through `damocles:scm:commit-input`. The commit template is read from `commit.template` as VS Code's `getCommitTemplate` resolves it (`~`, `~user`, relative to the work tree), bounded, with comment lines dropped. The box follows VS Code's input template (`getInputTemplate`): the snapshot's `mergeMessage`, which is MERGE_MSG, else SQUASH_MSG, read whenever git left one whatever the operation, else the commit template; when that value changes (an operation starts or ends, here or in a terminal) it replaces the box only while the box is empty or still holds the previous value (`scmInput.ts`), so an edited draft is never overwritten (`followTemplate` in `scm-store.ts`). A commit's new box text (the input template as the commit left it) and its commit input (template, history, HEAD's message, read after core recorded the commit) come back in the commit's own reply, which the shell applies as the box leaves its busy state; the box is read-only while a commit runs, as VS Code disables its input box, so nothing typed meanwhile is replaced. Text main puts in a box for anything else (Undo Last Commit with its commit input, Restore Commit Template) reaches the shell only through `damocles:scm:set-input`. The folder each root last created a worktree in is kept the same way under `damocles.scm.worktreeRoots` (`worktree-roots.ts`, 100 roots), recorded by core after every successful `addWorktree`, the first included (VS Code records it only once a value exists), and Create Worktree's suggested path and folder button start there.
- **The IPC is the shell page's only** (`preload/scm-channels.ts`, `main/shell.ts` `handleScm`, `main/scm/scm-host.ts`). Every `damocles:scm:*` invoke passes `ShellHost`'s sender check and an exact-field validator before core; repository ids are core's, never reused by another core (each `ScmService` draws a nonce for them), and anything persisted is keyed by root path (the selected repository in `window-layout.json`, closed repositories in global state by project path). An id main no longer has (a closed or removed repository) is answered, never rejected: `unknown` from run and commit, `missing` from file-diff, nothing from select, copy and the amend report, and its Changes and diff tabs close when core reports it gone (`EditorPane.closeChanges`); a diff whose editable working-tree side holds unsaved edits becomes that file's own tab in its place with the same document, unless the file already has a tab, which keeps them. `damocles:scm:amend` is the shell's report of its selected repository's amend mode (the store's `amendingIn`, sent on every change), the one renderer input to the commit commands' titles; main keeps it for that repository only, and clears it itself when the selection moves, after a commit or Undo Last Commit, and when the repository is gone, so the palette never shows a stale mode. The select reply precedes the view naming the selection, so the shell names the repository it acts on and never reads it back from that view. Copy composes its text from a path of the repository's own status, or from the row its value names in main's fresh list: a stash by its commit hash (Copy Branch Name, refused for a stash made on no branch, and Copy Stash Name, the stash's description as VS Code's copies it), a branch or tag by its full refname (its name, or the commit it points or peels to), a worktree by its path (its branch, refused when detached, its HEAD commit or its path), a commit by its full hash once `cat-file -t` says it is one of the repository (Copy Commit ID copies the hash, Copy Commit Message the message `log -1 --format=%B` reads in UTF-8). `parseScmCopy` takes each kind only with the row forms it may name, and only such a malformed copy rejects: a hash that names no commit of the repository is the commit flows' warning, and any other refusal or failure is told as a failed run is, since the page cannot show it.
- **Hunk staging writes the index blob from core and refuses a changed index** (`src/core/scm/staging.ts`, `ScmHost.stageRanges`, VS Code's `_stageChanges` and `unstageSelectedRanges`). Stage Hunk, Stage and Unstage Selected Ranges, Stage Block and Stage Selection send `{ repoId, path, documentId, ranges, indexOid }`, an unstage with `headOid` too: the ranges are `LineChange`s of index to buffer for a stage and of HEAD to index for an unstage, which core applies inverted onto the index. HEAD's side is HEAD's blob at the file's HEAD path, which core takes from its own index-group status, never from the renderer: the source of a staged rename or copy (`INDEX_RENAMED`, `INDEX_COPIED`, `headPathOf`), else the file's own path, as VS Code's `unstageSelectedRanges` reads `resource.original` at HEAD; the quick diff's HEAD side and blame read the same path. An unstage is refused like a changed index when HEAD's blob there is no longer `headOid` (null when HEAD lacks the file). Main reads the document's buffer when the request arrives, before any await, and the shell sends its pending `editor:edit` just before it, so the buffer staged is the one the shell diffed; ranges that do not fit the buffer's or the index's line count are refused. In one mutation slot, inside the trust fence, core reads the index entry (`ls-files -s`), refuses when its blob is no longer `indexOid` (an index with no entry counts as the empty blob, and the file is then added as 100644 with `update-index --add`) or when stages 1 to 3 exist, refuses a link, a gitlink, a `filter=lfs` file or an LFS pointer, a binary blob and one over `MAX_SHOW_BYTES`, decodes the blob in the document's encoding (refusing bytes that do not decode), applies the ranges with the shared `applyLineChanges` (each side keeps its own line breaks), encodes the result with the document's encoding and byte order mark, writes it with `hash-object -w --path=<path> --stdin`, so the clean filter, `autocrlf` and `working-tree-encoding` turn the buffer into the index's form, and records it with `update-index --cacheinfo` under the entry's own mode. Under a `working-tree-encoding` attribute (`check-attr`, any value but UTF-8) the blobs are UTF-8 and are decoded so, and the result is still encoded in the document's encoding, which git converts as it hashes. When the index blob holds no CR, the buffer uses CRLF and git's clean turns a CRLF into LF for the path (one `hash-object --path` of a CRLF without `-w`), the index side's line breaks become CRLF before the ranges apply, since `core.safecrlf=true` refuses a blob mixing a lone LF with CRLFs that clean converts (git's safer-autocrlf condition); with no conversion each side keeps its own. An unstage from a diff tab with the file not open takes the index blob's own encoding, and under `working-tree-encoding` hashes the result as is, with no filters. A path inside an untrusted project in the repository is refused before any git call, ignoring case on Windows and macOS as the fence does, since neither call takes a pathspec the fence could exclude. Each refusal is a notice; nothing is written. Main refuses a document that is read-only (not valid in its encoding, too large) or not displayed. `scm-staging.integration.test.ts` pins CRLF with `autocrlf` true and false and with `safecrlf` true, UTF-16 with a byte order mark, `working-tree-encoding`, an executable mode, a new file, a stale `indexOid`, a HEAD that moved, an unstage of a staged rename and a fenced path in either case.
- **Revert Hunk and Revert Selected Ranges edit the buffer only** (the shell, through `src/shared/scm/apply-line-changes.ts`). The buffer becomes the index side with the unreverted changes applied, as one undoable edit; nothing is saved, and the index and the file on disk are unchanged until the user saves.
- **A Selected Ranges action uses the newest index base and never ends silently** (`quick-diff-model.ts` `current()`, `editor-git.ts`, `diff-git.ts`, `ScmHost.refuseRanges`). The quick diff reads its base again whenever the repository's status revision moves, which every status core lands after a write, a commit or a watcher event moves. An action waits for a base read still scheduled or in flight, the first one of a tab just opened included, and no diff of the older base answers it meanwhile. An action with no change in the selection, on a file main gave no quick diff base, or on a diff tab whose sides are no longer the index and HEAD, sends `refuseRanges`, and main shows the notice, since only main shows notices.
- **Quick diff, selected ranges, blame, the timeline and the merge editor run only where source control runs** (D8). Their reads (`damocles:scm:quick-diff-base`, `blame`, `timeline`, `merge-inputs`, `merge-file`, `resource`) and run ops name a repository id, which an untrusted project never has, so main answers `unknown` and the shell shows nothing; inline conflict lenses read text only and work anywhere. The quick diff base follows VS Code's `quickDiffProvider.ts`: no base for a file of another (nested or closed) repository or of the git dir, a link, a merge-group, untracked or ignored file, a `filter=lfs` file or an LFS pointer, a binary or too large blob, or a blob that does not decode; HEAD's text, at the file's HEAD path, comes only while the file has staged changes. Each text is decoded with the open document's encoding, else the blob's own, and under `working-tree-encoding` as UTF-8. A diff tab of a working tree or index change (`ShellEditorTab.diff.git`) has its git sides read again after every status of its repository (`ScmHost.refreshGitSides`) and replaced in place (`DocumentService.replaceMemory`), so a stage or an outside `git add -p` shows there. While the file is in the merge group its index has no stage 0, so a working tree diff reads HEAD for that side, as VS Code's `sanitizeRef` reads HEAD for a file outside the index group, and an index diff's index side is not read and keeps its text. `ScmRepoSnapshot.revision` grows with every status that lands, so the shell recomputes its quick diff after any index write.
- **A remote repository's files are named only through `remoteFileRef`** (`scm-host.ts` `remoteRef`, `remotePathRef`, `sameFile`, `resourceOf`, `pathsOf`; `remote/remote-files.ts`). A remote core's repository root and every path it answers are the target's, never this computer's: main joins a repository path onto the root in the target's path rules and resolves it with `remoteFileRef` inside that host's projects and live chat worktrees (`remoteFileRoots` in `index.ts`), else refuses with "{host} named a file outside its projects." Open Changes (staged and working tree), a merge row's Open File, Open All Changes, the timeline's and a commit's file diffs, Compare with Selected and Compare with Workspace open the result as that host's project file through its workspace (`EditorPane.showRemoteDiff`, `openFromUser`, `openInBackground`), and stash, ref and group diffs are core's patches. A remote document is a repository's file only by that project place (the project key, which carries the authority, and the relative path), and a remote editor tab gets `ShellEditorTab.git` only when its own path resolves through `remoteFileRef` back to its project place, so the quick diff base and Stage, Unstage and Revert Selected Ranges read the target's buffer and encoding, never a local editor's with the same path; a local repository never matches a remote document either, and the save-before-commit and save-before-stash prompts list local files only. Worktree rows, Open Worktree and Create and Delete Worktree compare a remote worktree path in the target's rules, find its chat in that host's own registry, select or add a project of that host, take a typed path only when it is absolute on the target and offer no folder picker of this computer; a remote host's registry keeps chat records only, so none is adopted there. The merge editor and file moves name the file the same way, before anything opens, saves or runs git: Open Merge Editor opens the Result as the host's project file through its workspace, its inputs and Compute Conflicts read that document's buffer and encoding (`sameFile`), Complete Merge saves it through the host's workspace, stages it on the target and closes its merge tab by the Result's document (`EditorPane.closeMerge(documentId)`), and Apply's conflicts on a remote worktree chat open that host's merge editor (`openWorktreeConflicts` finds the record in the host's own registry). Git: Rename moves the tabs of a host's file with `fileRenamed(projectKey, relativePath)` when both names resolve in one project, and Git: Delete closes the active tab only when it shows that project file (`EditorPane.closeActive(file)`, a project place, or for a local file outside every project this computer's path); a new name `inputProblem` refuses in the target's path rules, or one leading outside the host's projects, moves nothing. Open Repository stays refused on a remote repository with "This source control action is not available on {host} yet.", since its folder picker browses this computer only. Repository roots are realpaths, and a project may be registered through a link, so each project root also carries the realpath the target's core resolved when it registered the project (the `scm` mirror's `realPath`, as local projects use it in `projectFolders`): `remoteFileRef` matches a path under either spelling, and `resourceOf` joins a document's relative path onto that realpath before it looks for the repository. The project keeps the path it was registered with. The match stays lexical: every read and write of the resulting project file is confined again on the target by realpath, so a link inside the project that leads outside it is still refused.
- **The merge editor's inputs are the index stages, labelled by the operation in progress** (`src/core/scm/merge.ts`, VS Code's `git.openMergeEditor`). `show :1:`, `:2:` and `:3:` come from `ls-files -u`, decoded with the conflicted file's document encoding; a file without both stage 2 and 3 opens as itself (slice 10's Merge Changes actions). Current is HEAD (`:2:`) and Incoming `:3:`: `MERGE_HEAD` for a merge, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` for a cherry-pick, `REBASE_HEAD` for a rebase, where Current is the branch being rebased onto and the editor shows it first, as VS Code swaps its inputs; a conflict with no operation whose markers name `Stashed changes` is a stash apply, labelled "Stashed Changes", and a worktree chat's Apply labels Incoming "{branch} (worktree)". Each side's description is its short hash and its detail its ref names. During a rebase the merge editor renders the Current pane and its label first, so the order on screen and the reading order agree. Compute Conflicts runs `git merge-file -p` (`--diff3` or `--no-diff3`), its markers named as Reset Result names them (a side's ref names, else its short hash, else its title; `markerName`) and as long as the path's `conflict-marker-size` attribute makes git's merge write them (`--marker-size`), by `--object-id` from git 2.43, otherwise (and for an AA conflict, which has no base) on three files in a fresh folder under the OS temp folder, removed in a `finally`; an exit code above 127 is git's refusal. Conflict markers count at any length of seven or more, every marker of a conflict as long as its start, so a `conflict-marker-size` file's conflicts count, and a stash apply's longer markers are told too. Complete Merge saves the Result, which is the file's own document, then stages the file with no marker question; the shell asks Complete with Conflicts first while conflicts are left, or while marker text it cannot pair is. Merge editor tabs are never restored (AD8).
- **The Graph reads history from a snapshot, and acts only on commits of its repository** (`src/core/scm/history.ts`, `graph-lanes.ts`, `scm-host.ts` `history`, `commitDetails`, `withCommit`, plan slice 13). A load resolves the ref choice once (Auto: HEAD's branch, its upstream and its base ref, or HEAD's commit alone when detached; All: every local branch, remote branch but a remote's HEAD, and tag, and a detached HEAD, as git's `--all` includes HEAD; else the full refnames picked that still exist, matched against one listing so no renderer refname reaches git's argv, Auto when none does) to the distinct commits they name, and every page runs `-c i18n.logOutputEncoding=UTF-8 log --format=%H%x00%P%x00%aN%x00%aE%x00%at%x00%D%x00%s -z --topo-order --decorate=full --no-show-signature --no-color --skip=N -n50 --stdin` with those commits on stdin, since All can pass more than a command line holds; the cursor carries them (at most `MAX_SCM_HISTORY_TIPS`), and main parses it strictly, so a later page reads the snapshot the load did and never what a ref points to now. An unborn HEAD answers `empty`; a shallow clone's boundary has no parents in `%P` and ends only its own lane (`graphRows`, which in that one case departs from VS Code's `scmHistory.ts`, where every lane ended there). The first page carries the load's HEAD, upstream and base refs with their commits, `rev-list --left-right --count` and `merge-base` of HEAD and its upstream (exit 1: no common commit). The base ref is VS Code's `getBranchBase` read only: `branch.<name>.vscode-merge-base` when it names an existing remote branch, else the branch the reflog's one "branch: Created from" entry names (from HEAD, HEAD's oldest "checkout: moving from X to <branch>"), a local one through its upstream, else what `refs/remotes/<upstream's remote, else origin, else the first remote>/HEAD` points to; it shows only when it differs from the upstream, and Damocles never writes that config key, where VS Code writes it. A base ref that cannot be read (an oversized or corrupt reflog) is logged and left out, as `getBranchBase` catches it, so it costs the badge and never the load. The cursor puts the base ref last (`<skip>;<commits>;<base ref>`), since a refname may hold `;` and `,`. Every commit hash a renderer names passes `isObjectName` in the validator and `cat-file -t` (a commit of that repository) in main before any flow uses it (Open Changes, Checkout (Detached), Create Branch, Create Tag, Compare with..., Compare with Remote and Merge Base, Cherry Pick, Add to Chat and Explain Changes, the timeline's Compare with Selected on both sides, the copy kinds and the expanded commit); one that names no commit is the warning "The commit {0} is not in this repository."; Incoming and Outgoing Changes name no hash, main reads the merge base, upstream and HEAD itself. Cherry Pick... from the palette verifies the typed text with `rev-parse --verify <text>^{commit}`, refusing a leading `-`, whitespace and control characters. A cherry-pick refuses, in its own mutation slot and before git runs, while a merge, rebase, revert or cherry-pick (a sequence included) is in progress, since that state would read as its own and an abort would drop the user's picked commits. Its outcome is read from the repository, never from git's words: `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` with unmerged entries is a conflict (left in progress for the banner and the merge editor), with the index equal to HEAD it is empty and the engine runs `cherry-pick --abort` in the same mutation slot, so none stays in progress; without `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` git refused (a merge commit, local changes in the way) and the error notice with Show Git Output tells it. Ref changes reach the Graph as `ScmArtifactsChanged.refs`: `engine` after an engine operation that may move a ref (it records a digest of every branch, remote branch and tag first), `background` when the watcher finds refs other than the last digest (autofetch, a terminal), and nothing for the watcher's echo of refs already reported. The digest is first read when the repository opens, so the first watcher event compares with the refs the Graph's first load saw, and a listing that finishes after a later one landed is dropped (`recordRefs`). A cherry-pick refused before git ran moves no ref and reports none.
- **Blame reads HEAD's commit and the timeline follows the file through renames** (`src/core/scm/blame.ts`, `timeline.ts`). Blame runs `blame --root --porcelain <HEAD's commit> -- <path>` at the file's HEAD path (a staged rename's source) and keeps each file's result per HEAD commit (`BlameCache`, 50 per repository); a blob git calls binary, or a UTF-16 one, whose 0x0A bytes inside characters git would split lines at, is not blamed, so a commit's blame is read once; the shell maps editor lines onto HEAD's through its quick diff, so an edited line reads "Not Committed Yet" (VS Code's `blame.ts`). The timeline runs `log --follow -z --name-status`, 50 entries a page, naming each author through `.mailmap` (`%aN`, `%aE`) as blame and the Graph do; `--follow` takes no `--skip`, so the cursor is the last entry's commit and the file's path there, and the next page starts at that commit, at that path, leaving it out. Each entry's path is the file's at that commit, from its status line; an entry with none (a merge git lists without its diff) takes the path of the nearest older entry that has one. The Timeline tab reads its first page again only when HEAD's commit moves (once a Load more running then has landed), and Staged Changes follows the file's presence in the status's index group, so other statuses neither read git nor drop the pages loaded. Open Changes on an entry diffs the file at that commit against its first parent at the path it had there (a rename in the commit names its source), and Open Commit compares the whole commit with its first parent, or with the empty tree for a root commit.

## Chat review

- **A project-folder chat's changes come from its tool records only, never from git status** (`src/core/pi-session/chat-changes.ts`, D60). The set is every file a successful Edit, Write or GenerateImage result changed on the chat's branch, and those of its subagents' and team members' agent files (`agent-records.ts`), attributed to the turn of the call that launched them and kept in message order (a parent's results before the agent records of a later call in the same turn); a team's members' messages are read in time order and all count under the team's first invocation, so a team that keeps working across turns shows in Last turn only for the turn that started it. Each path resolves as its tool resolved it (pi's write through `resolveToCwd`). Files changed only by shell commands are not listed, and the tab says so, and another chat's edits are never counted. A fork's branch holds the turns it inherited, so they list too. A turn is a user entry: a steered or queued prompt opens its own checkpoint turn at the next assistant message (see "Checkpoints"), so Last turn is what changed since the latest user entry. A file's baseline is the checkpoint before the first turn that changed it (read through `checkpoints/blob-reader.ts`: one `git cat-file --batch` per checkpoint repo, started on the recompute's first read there and ended in the recompute's `finally` or the review's dispose, never kept longer, since a running git holds pack files a checkpoint repack or prune, possibly in another Damocles process, deletes on Windows; a path holding a line break, which the batch protocol cannot carry, takes the one-off `git show` of `checkpointCommitFile`; each commit is checked once with `<commit>^{commit}`, so a path the batch calls missing is absent only in a commit the repo has; from the repo of the folder that turn ran in, read within `EDITOR_MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` and binary by git's test of the first 8000 bytes; the persisted entry first, and only for the latest turn's before state the producer's in-memory before-commit, `CheckpointService.pendingBaseline`). When no checkpoint holds it (a home-folder chat, a turn that is not rewindable, a checkpoint removed by retention, a path outside the working folder, or a file the checkpoint skipped, which shows as absent although the chat's first change edited it) the baseline is rebuilt by undoing the recorded patches from the file on disk; when they no longer undo but apply forward the file is back at its baseline, and otherwise the row shows the recorded patches, is marked, and takes no line comments (`commentable: false`). A file whose content is back at its baseline drops out, a binary one when its bytes' sha1 equals the checkpoint's. Exceptions: a file past the review's size bound (`EDITOR_MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` on disk or in the checkpoint) lists with no diff and is never compared, so it stays listed, and a binary file with no checkpoint baseline stays listed too. A file whose read fails (locked, denied) lists with no diff and the rest of the set still lists. The chat's last result is the baseline with its patches applied (the last turn's after-state when they do not apply); a file on disk that differs from it shows "Includes edits made outside this chat". Binary files list with no line diff. A diff past `MAX_DIFF_BYTES` is sent as too large. Across a hand-off (D61) records match by their path relative to the folder they were made in, and now is the file in the current folder, which is also the one watched. A worktree chat's set is every change against its base commit, untracked files included, with no outside-edit mark (`worktreeChanges`); its Last turn uses the tool records and says shell changes are not listed.
- **The counter is computed off pi's handlers and only where the host can open the tab** (`src/core/pi-session/chat-review.ts`, `PiSession.publishChatChanges`). A file result of the chat, its subagents (posted through `PiSession.emit`) or its team, a turn state change, a session bind, a reloaded webview, a checkpoint record the panel appends and a change on disk of any file the chat's records changed (a non-recursive watch of each such file's folder, so a file put back by hand drops out and an outside edit is marked with no turn) schedule a recompute, debounced 500 ms and run on one ordered chain, so no pi handler waits on it; a run queued and not started takes every later change, so a slow recompute never builds a backlog. A disposed review starts no run, and the session's disposal waits for the run in flight, so a folder's release and a worktree's removal come after the review's last git read there. A recompute whose worktree folder is gone (removed outside Damocles while the chat works in it) logs that and publishes nothing; any other failure is logged as a failure. Nothing is computed for the counter while `HostCapabilities.chatReview` is false, so VS Code shows no counter. Every read (the counter, the tab's list, a file's diff, an anchor, Send) comes from one snapshot per scope at the review's generation, which each `schedule()` and each change of the session, its branch's last entry, its folder or its worktree bumps; an older snapshot is read again. Opening the tab and a hand-off's file list start a new generation first, since a change no event reports (a terminal's file in a worktree) is in no snapshot. A team member's tool result reaches the review through `PiSession.teamMessage`, as the team posts to the panel directly. `publishChatChanges` is the one sender of `chatChanges`; the host hears the totals with their generation for `MenuState.selectedChatHasChanges`, and an open review tab reads again unless it already shows that generation. Main drops a review once its tab closes (`EditorPaneDeps.reviewClosed`), and a read lands in the tab only while its scope is the tab's and no later read landed first.
- **Core composes the review message from its own diff** (`composeReviewMessage`, `ChatReview.compose`, `src/desktop/main/chat-review-host.ts`). The renderer sends only each draft's path, line, side, scope, text and the revision core served with the diff it showed (`damocles:review:set-drafts`, bounded and exact-field validated); main keeps a chat's drafts for the run, under its stored session id, until they are sent or discarded, and the tab takes main's list when that key changes. When a comment first arrives main records its anchor from core's diff (`lineAnchor`: the line with the lines either side of it on that side, only while a hunk of three context lines shows it), and none when the revision is not core's current one. Sending lists each comment as `path:line` (`path:line (removed)` on a removed line), the line quoted from core's diff of that scope in a fence longer than any backtick run in it, then the comment text; a comment whose anchor in the current diff differs from the recorded one, or whose line no hunk shows, refuses the whole send as stale. That one rule (`ChatReview.staleDrafts`, which `compose` uses) also marks those comments on the tab, after each read of the review and each change of the drafts (`ChatReviewTab.staleComments`); a marked comment whose line the diff no longer shows is listed above the diff. A stale send's notice and the tab's bar show one message, built in main, naming each changed line as `path:line` (`ChatReviewHost.staleMessage`). The message goes to the chat as a normal user prompt, queued while a turn runs; one the chat cannot take now (`unsent`) keeps the drafts, and only the drafts that were sent leave.

## Worktree chats

- **A worktree is opt-in per chat, and only a chat worktree is the chat's** (`worktrees/registry.ts`, `chat-panel/panel-worktrees.ts`). The registry (`~/.damocles/worktrees/registry.json`, one instance, in Damocles Core, whose records main reads from the `worktrees` mirror) holds `chat` records (a worktree a chat created at its first send or a hand-off) and `adopted` records (a linked worktree opened as a project). It is written through `writeJsonConfig` under its cross-process lock, watched so another Damocles process's write reaches this one, and compared with `folderKey`. Its parse, and main's check of each mirrored record, never use a record that fails validation (`validRecord`, `worktrees/records.ts`): a `chat` record is valid only at `worktreeFolderFor(rootDir, projectPath, id)`, its project may not lie in the worktrees folder, its repository root must hold its project, and its working folder must lie as deep in the worktree as the project in its repository, because any process can write the file. The repository root is read in the project's own spelling (`repositoryOf` in `create.ts`, as VS Code's getRepositoryRoot resolves a relative toplevel): git's `--show-cdup` resolved against the project path, taken only when its realpath is git's `--show-toplevel`, since git prints a realpath (8.3 names expanded, links followed) and every path a chat or the registry compares is the project's spelling; a project that leads into its repository through a link, below its root, has no parent that spells the root and is refused with a message naming its real folder. The working folder and every path handed to git below the root use git's `--show-prefix`, because an 8.3 alias of the project's folder need not exist in the worktree. Stored project paths are never respelled: session folders, trust and layout keys are spelled as the project was added. A record this version cannot read is written back unchanged on every write, so two app versions can share the file, and the leftover scan counts its `worktreeRoot` as recorded. After its own write the registry reads the file back, so memory holds only validated records and never an older state than the file's. A file that stops parsing keeps serving the records last parsed, every write is refused until it parses again, and the user sees one notice per break (`unreadable()`, `onDidBecomeUnreadable`), as the settings store keeps a layer it last parsed. That holds across a core restart: a registry that starts while the file does not parse serves the records main last read from the core before (`lastRecords`, from `CoreInit.worktreeRecords`), checked by `validRecord` again, so isolation, trust and the Chats list keep every chat worktree, and it still refuses writes. A chat record is written before `git worktree add`, so another process's leftover scan never reports a worktree being created, and the scan also skips records younger than 15 minutes. `usedAt` marks a chat record once a chat worked in it (its first prompt sent, a hand-off into it, a new chat in it) or the user kept it; a live record past the grace with neither, that no session file names, is reported as `noChat` (a quit during setup). `projectOf`, trust, personal files, isolation and confinement read `chat` records only; adopted records serve the Chats list alone. The VS Code host has no registry: a chat whose latest entry places it in a worktree opens read-only there.
- **Where a chat works is its session's latest worktree entry** (`damocles.worktree` `{ worktreeId, projectPath, place }`, `pi-session/session-worktree.ts`). A new session in a live chat worktree (first send, `/clear`, a fork) records the entry once at bind; a hand-off records the place it moves to. Resume and restore bind to the latest entry's working folder (`PanelWorktrees.resumeFolder`), never to a folder key the webview names and never to the folder that listed the chat. That folder is registered before the switch, and the panel's hold moves to it only once the switch lands (`ResumeBinding.settle`), so a failed switch keeps the hold where the panel still works. A chat whose latest entry names a worktree it cannot bind to (deleted, or no registry) and a chat of a deleted adopted worktree are read-only: core refuses send, rewind, undo, fork and compact for them whatever the webview shows, and they bind to the project folder with no runtime prompts. The refusal is decided after the session starts (`PiSession.readOnlyRefusal`, in `promptPi`, `compact` and `sessionForRewind`), because the latest entry comes from the session's file: a request that arrives before the start starts the session and is then refused. On a host without worktrees (VS Code, or `capabilities.worktrees` off) the refusal says only the Damocles app can open the chat's worktree; elsewhere it says the worktree was deleted.
- **An existing session is found by its file, never by `piSessionDir` of the current folder** (slice 12a rules 1 and 2). New sessions are created in `piSessionDir(cwd)` and their file never moves. `resolvePiSessionFile` and `piSessionDirOf` search the working folder's directory, then the project's and every registry folder's of that project (`worktreeSessionFolders`). A session's subagent, team and fork data lives beside its file (`piSessionDirOf`, `path.dirname(sessionFile)`), and `copyForkAgentData` takes the source's directory and the fork's. Every open of a live session passes `cwdOverride`, the working folder, because pi otherwise takes the folder from the header, which names the folder the file was created in; a start passes the working folder's session directory too, which pi's `newSession` (`/clear`) and fork create the next file in. pi's `switchSession` takes a file's own directory instead, so a switch to a file in another folder's directory opens it again with this folder's (`switchedFromOtherDir`). A delete takes the agent data's directory from the file before removing it.
- **Listing** (AD12).
