A project's Chats list reads its own session directory and the directories of its registry records that are not open as projects: every `chat` record, live or deleted, and every `adopted` record whose folder is gone. Each chat is listed once, under its project, with its latest entry (`StoredSession.worktree`) and the registry folder it was listed from (`listedFrom`). The storage watchers extend to those directories, which they never create, and drop a directory once its record is gone; a registry change that adds, removes or marks a record deleted re-lists (`onListingChange`), and no other registry write does. A deleted record exists only for its chats: after each change of the stored sessions, one no loaded or stored chat of an open project names in its latest entry, whose folder's session directory holds no session file, is removed (`PanelWorktrees.storedSessionsChanged`, `WorktreeRegistry.removeDeleted`), never while `registry.json` does not parse.
- **Owned folders stay out of the projects** (slice 12a rule 5). A working folder a chat binds to is registered in `WorkspaceFolderRegistry` as owned by its project (`acquireOwned`, counted per panel); `resolve()` finds it, `targets()` never lists it, so `StorageManager.folders()`, memory's known workspaces, `folderInfos()`, `isMultiRoot`, `defaultTarget` and `ScmService.setProjects` stay project-only. A user folder switch and a restored folder key resolve only projects, so no chat moves into another chat's worktree. Removing the project drops its owned folders with it. `ownedFolders()` lists them and `onDidChangeOwned` fires on a first holder, a last holder and a project removal. When the last panel lets go of a working folder, core releases its per-folder services once the closing sessions settle; a chat that binds to the folder again before then cancels that release, so a release never tears down a folder a chat holds. `PanelManager.whenFolderReleased` settles once the release is done.
- **A working folder has its own MCP scope.** `McpManager` reads the projects and the owned folders: a working folder's `.mcp.json` and `.pi/mcp.json` from the worktree, its `mcp.local.json` through `localMcpConfigPath` (the project's, D34), all watched. Trust is its project's, and its toggles live in its project's lists; an enable that overrides a config's `enabled: false` is kept under the folder whose config says it and pruned only against that config, and a working folder applies its project's overrides and its own. Its state and watchers exist exactly while a loaded chat holds it (`onDidChangeOwned` reloads).
- **The first send waits for the worktree, never inside a folder switch** (slice 12a rules 3 and 4). The first prompt of an opted-in chat is held per panel while the worktree is created, D32's files are copied and setup runs; the switch to the working folder (`switchPanelFolder` with the `chatMove` reason, which asks nothing and checks the session lease first) ends at once. Setup's exit 0, Send now and Send anyway release the prompt (a hand-off's setup holds nothing until the user sends, so its card offers Send now only for a held send, and Continue without setup on a failure with nothing held); a failed creation keeps it with Retry and Run in project folder. The held prompt is published as `worktreeState.heldPrompt` in every holding phase (`creating`, `settingUp`, `setupFailed`, `createFailed`), and the host keeps such a chat loaded; a window close, a quit and a host reload keep it in `panels.json`, and the next start puts it back as the chat's draft (`PanelManager.heldPrompt`, `restoreDraft`, `held-prompts.ts`), keeping it stored until it is back, so a second quit before then loses nothing. Such a chat has no session file to come back from, so Remove Project asks first, as VS Code asks before closing unsaved changes (`remove-project.ts`): one danger question names the project and lists the waiting chats, each by its title or an escaped excerpt of its message, and takes in the terminal question, leaving out their Setup terminals. Cancel, focused first, changes nothing. Remove Project closes those chats, waits until core discarded each one's unused worktree (`PanelManager.whenWorktreeDiscarded`: its creation ended, its Setup terminal stopped, its session there closed), then removes the project; a project with no waiting prompt asks only the terminal question. A second send while one is held goes back to the composer (`promptReturned`). Only a prompt is held: an empty send and a command core handles itself (`/compact`, `/remember`) run as in any chat. A folder change outside a worktree move drops the opt-in and returns its held prompt. A chat that goes away, or leaves its project, before its worktree was used discards the worktree, its branch and its record (`discardChatWorktree`), after its Setup terminal is stopped (`WorktreeHostServices.stopSetup`) and its session there closed (`whenFolderReleased`); only `git worktree remove` deletes the folder, and when git refuses, the folder, branch and record stay for the leftover scan. A refused `git worktree add -b` deletes the branch it made only while that still points at the start commit, removes the project's worktrees folder when empty, and reports git's error. The setup commands come from the project folder's `.damocles/worktree.json` (`setupWindows` on Windows, `setupUnix` elsewhere, else `setup`; a regular file only, never a link), read once before anything is created: at most 20 commands, each one line, 6,000 characters together, so the script fits one Windows `-EncodedCommand`. The array the review dialog shows is the array that runs; the approval is the SHA-256 of that array, kept per project in global state. Every run, a Retry and the Setup terminal's Restart included, refuses a project that is not trusted. Setup state is per worktree and apart from the held prompt, so the Setup terminal's Restart (`retrySetup(worktreeId)`) reruns it after the prompt was sent; `setupStep` names the command running. A `.worktreeinclude` lists its candidates from its own patterns (`ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-from`), then keeps the ones git ignores, so an ignored tree no pattern names is never listed.
- **A link the OS refuses is skipped, never followed.** Creation, the opted-in uncommitted changes and a hand-off's file move recreate each link on its own; an EPERM or EACCES skips that link, the rest continue, and one notice lists the skipped links ("Windows needs Developer Mode to create symbolic links"). A folder link is recreated as a junction. A hand-off leaves a skipped link in the project folder.
- **Hand off (D61) is the one folder move that keeps a conversation.** It runs only for an idle, writable chat with a conversation (no turn, no prompt waiting, no subagent or team working). To a worktree, the files the chat changed that have uncommitted changes move after the confirmation lists them, in an order that loses no file whichever step fails (`moveFilesToWorktree`): their current content is copied into the working folder, then the project folder's copies git knows (in HEAD or the index, a staged new file included) are restored to HEAD through `git restore --staged --worktree`, and last the untracked ones are removed. A failure reports whether the project folder may have changed (`HandOffMoveError.sourcesChanged`): if not, a worktree the hand-off created is discarded; if so, the worktree is kept and marked used, the chat stays in the project folder, and the user is told to check both folders. A worktree that received the files is marked used even when the resume then fails. Back to the project folder, Apply copies the worktree's changes and refuses, naming the files, when one has local changes there; files it leaves in conflict do not stop the move, and the user is told as after the Apply command (`WorktreeHostServices.showApplyConflicts`). The session is resumed in the new folder and receives the working-folder note as user-role content (`sendCustomMessage` with `triggerTurn: false`), which never starts a turn and never carries the steer prefix; a note given before the resumed session starts is kept and delivered when it starts, and a prompt never goes before it. A rewind to a point before the latest hand-off restores the conversation only (`RewindHistoryItem.crossesHandOff`); its files belong to the other folder.
- **A hand-off changes nothing before it can no longer be refused.** The lease is checked first, idleness is re-checked after the confirmation, and the conversation's lease is held in this process from before the first file or registry change until the resumed session holds it, then let go before the new worktree's setup runs. From the request until the conversation runs in its new folder, the chat and its worktree take no other hand-off and no finish action, and the chat publishes `canHandOff: false`. A refusal after the confirmation tells the user why. The place entry is written by the session resumed in the new folder, first thing when it starts and before it publishes, never by the session it replaces. A resume that fails puts the conversation back in its folder and tells the user where the files are. On a detached HEAD a new worktree starts from HEAD's commit (D61): the worktree options then list `HEAD` (its short hash as the name) as the current start, and main accepts that literal start ref.
- **Worktree isolation (D62) is a guard against wrong-copy mistakes, not a sandbox** (`pi-session/worktree-isolation.ts`, called from `permission-gate.ts`; its rules are in `docs/worktree-isolation.md`). For a chat working in its live chat worktree, and for its subagents and team, the gate blocks in every mode, YOLO included, and before a PreToolUse hook's force-allow (on the input as the hook rewrote it): an Edit, Write or GenerateImage whose path, resolved as the tools resolve it and through links, lies in the project folder or another worktree of the project and outside the working folder; and a Bash or PowerShell command that changes directory into, or points git at, the project folder, another worktree or the project's repository, directly or through text git runs as a command. It reads paths and command text without running anything, and fails closed: a target the text does not prove, and text it cannot parse, block. Reading only what a command proves lets ordinary worktree commands run, and anything the reading cannot follow costs a block, never a wrong pass. Git writes to the shared `.git` directory stay allowed, so commits from the worktree work. A command that writes into the project folder without git, such as `cp` or a redirect, is not caught; it stays under the normal permission rules, which ask for shell commands outside YOLO. The check's input is built per call by `PiSession.worktreeIsolation()` from what the shell tools start with: the command prefix and `shellPath` from `shellOptions()`, and the starting environment, `process.env` with the worktree's variables over it and `PATH` and pi's per-call `PI_*` variables left without a value. A tool that changes the environment or shell it starts must change that method with it. The environment holds secrets: it is never logged, and a block reason never repeats a value from it. A `shellPath` other than bash, sh, dash or ash refuses every Bash command of a worktree chat. A block is a policy block (`formatPolicyBlockReason`) naming the area it reaches and the working folder. When the chat's record disappears or is marked deleted while its session still works in the worktree (registry.json removed mid-turn), isolation keeps going from the record the session was made with, else from the latest entry's project as both project folder and repository (`SessionWorktree.isolation`); the chat is read-only from then on.
- **The worktree environment never touches `process.env`** (slice 12a rule 13). `DAMOCLES_PROJECT_FOLDER` and `DAMOCLES_WORKTREE_INDEX` (`worktrees/environment.ts`) reach the agent's Bash through pi's `spawnHook`, passed in `shellOptions()` (whose cache key covers them) and the subagent build, and its PowerShell through the tool's explicit `env`. The D62 check reads `process.env` per call and only reads it, merging the worktree's variables into a copy of its own. `index` is the smallest positive number no other live chat worktree of the project holds, allocated under the registry lock.
- **Personal files are the project's, live** (D34, `worktrees/personal-config.ts` `personalConfigRoot`). `loadPermissionsByPriority` reads `settings.local.json` from the project folder while relative rule paths match the working folder, "Don't ask again" writes the project's `.damocles/settings.local.json`, and `settingsFolderOf` gives the project as `personalPath`, so the local settings layer and its trust are the project's. `localMcpConfigPath`, the `mcp.local.json` gitignore check and the tool-name migration's owned files resolve the same way. Files in git, the shared `.damocles/settings.json` included, come from the working folder.
- **Identity follows the project.** Memory's project scope stores and injects the project (`identityFolder`; the injection reads it through `PanelGateContext.memoryWorkspace`, while the system prompt keeps the working folder), `/stats` attributes a session to the project its worktree entry names (`usage-stats/indexer.ts` re-keys the session, its entries and its nested files at the first such entry), and the title and commit-message sub-calls record the project in the ledger. A trust grant on a project also names its live chat working folders, and a live chat working folder answers `isTrusted` with its project's trust; adopted and deleted records never inherit (desktop `TrustStore`, `TrustStore.setWorktrees`).
- **Instruction files read as if the worktree sat where its project does** (slice 12a rule 21, `context-files.ts`). pi's walk inside the worktree stays; above the worktree root, the project's repository root's ancestors replace the worktree's own, and `~/.damocles` is never read as an ancestor: its `AGENTS.md` is the global slot.
- **The wrong-copy guard.** A worktree chat takes IDE context only from files in its working folder, filtered again whenever the chat moves to another folder (`IdeContextManager.refresh`), and a mention of a project-folder file is refused with "This file is in the project folder. This chat works in its worktree." (`mention-resolver.ts`).
- **Several chats can share one chat worktree.** `PanelManager.startInWorktree` binds a new panel to a live `chat` worktree of a trusted project before its first session exists, so that session starts in the working folder and records the worktree entry as `/clear` does; nothing is created, set up or held. Isolation, the environment, finish actions and chat review treat it as any chat of the worktree. `worktreeState.sharedWith` counts the other chats of the project whose latest entry places them in the worktree (a loaded chat's live entries, else its stored file's), published again when a chat joins or leaves; a chat review of a shared worktree still lists the whole worktree against `baseCommit` (D60) and carries the count (`ChatReviewSnapshot.sharedWith`).
- **`worktreeState` has one publisher** (`SessionWorktree.publish`, slice 12a rule 20), sent only with `HostCapabilities.worktrees`, for a chat of a trusted project whose repository source control has open (`WorktreeHostServices.projectRepository`, D9) or a read-only chat; it publishes nothing before the session's entries exist. Once a panel binds a session (`setListener`), its first publish without a state sends `noWorktreeState`, since the webview may still show the previous session's state. The webview drops the state it shows when another stored chat resumes (`resumeAccepted` without `continued`) or the panel switches folder, and keeps it through a clear, whose new session works in the same place, and through a hand-off until the moved session publishes. `PanelManager.onDidChangeWorktreeState` carries the same state, with `busy`, to the host. The behind and rewritten facts are read again on every change of the project repository's refs (`onDidChangeRefs`). A resume of a stored session gives the state its file gives (`PanelWorktrees.resumeFolder`) to `SessionWorktree.publishStored` before the history replay and before `sessionStarted`, so the first render of a read-only chat is the read-only one; a restore that opens empty in a project folder gives phase `none` the same way (`placeOf`). A session already running there (a reloaded webview) sends its live state instead, so the file's never replaces it.
- **A finish action holds its worktree** (D33). The host takes `PanelManager.beginWorktreeFinish` after its last question; it holds nothing when a loaded chat of the worktree is busy at that instant or a hand-off moves a chat into or out of it. The hold is the core's that gave it: core runs Apply, Merge into, Update from, Rebase onto and Delete's remove only under a hold it gave out for that worktree and still keeps (`FinishHolds.require`), and main sends them to that core alone, so a core restart between the hold and the action fails it visibly. While held, the worktree's chats publish `finishing`, and every send path refuses in the session (`PiSession.promptPi`, next to its last check before the prompt): a composer send comes back to the composer (`promptReturned`), and the review tab keeps its comments (`submitReview` answers `unsent`).
- **Finish actions run through the GitRunner, argv only** (`worktrees/integrate.ts`). Apply snapshots the worktree's files as a tree through a temporary `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, so the worktree's real index never changes, diffs it against `lastAppliedTree` or `baseCommit` with `--binary` and rename detection into a patch file under the OS temp folder that `git apply` reads (`withPatchFile`, as the opted-in uncommitted changes of a new worktree do), since the runner rejects stdout past its bound rather than return part of it; a binary file whose project HEAD entry is still its base one is left out of that patch and copied as an object (`git restore --source=<tree> --worktree`, VS Code's migrateChanges applies a stash the same way), because `git apply` parses a binary patch in time quadratic in its size, after `git apply --3way --check` passed on the text patch, and only while each copied path's preimage still holds (`changedPreimages`): an added binary needs nothing at its path, an ignored file included, and a modified or deleted one needs its index entry and its file on disk to still be the base blob, or Apply refuses, naming those files, so a refusal writes nothing. The temporary index, the patch and `.worktreeinclude`'s pattern list each live in a `damocles-git-<pid>-` folder (`withTempFolder`) removed when its work settles. Core's dispose interrupts no worktree git work, so a quit waits for those folders within its bound (`tempFoldersSettled`), and startup removes one whose process is gone and that is over an hour old (`removeAbandonedTempFolders`). Apply refuses when a touched file has local changes, applies with `--3way` and leaves the result unstaged, conflicts or not: the cleanly applied files are unstaged before conflicts are returned. Its conflicts are the touched paths it newly left unmerged; a failed apply with none throws and records nothing. Path lists go to git in chunks under `MAX_CLI_LENGTH`, and every patch is made with `--src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/`, so a user's diff prefixes never break `git apply`. Merge into, Update from and Rebase onto refuse while a merge, rebase, cherry-pick or revert is unfinished in a folder they run in. One that succeeds moves `baseCommit` to the commit it took in and clears `lastAppliedTree`, so a later Apply never copies the branch's own commits back. One that stops on conflicts, told from a refusal by git's operation markers (`MERGE_HEAD`, `rebase-merge`, `rebase-apply`, read through `git rev-parse --git-path` so a linked worktree's own are found), only records that commit as `pendingBase` with the folder it must reach (the project folder for Merge, the worktree for Update and Rebase). `settlePendingBase` ends it once no operation is in progress there: it becomes `baseCommit` when that folder's HEAD contains it, and is dropped otherwise (an abort). Every finish action settles first, the base readers (`isRewritten`, `unmergedCommits`, `applyPlan`, the line counts) count a pending base whose operation finished, and `SessionWorktree.refreshGit` settles on each refs change; an abort changes no ref, so its pending base stays until the next one, and meanwhile `baseCommit` is still the right base. A rewritten branch is `merge-base --is-ancestor` failing; unmerged commits are `git cherry -v <branch> <worktree branch> <base commit>`, and Delete pre-ticks "Also delete branch" only when that lists none and the branch's tip is in the project folder's checked-out commit (`branchContained`). Delete removes the worktree with `git worktree remove` (never an `fs.rm` of a worktree git still knows), marks the record deleted, removes the project's folder under the worktrees folder once it is empty (`removeEmptyProjectFolder`, under the registry's lock with no live record there, so a creation that wrote its record keeps its folder; a discard does the same), then deletes its Compass index and checkpoint folder repo; a failure keeps the record live, names the path and offers Retry. A remove git cannot finish (on Windows, a process outside Damocles holding the folder) can leave the folder without its `.git` link, which git then refuses to remove, so a Retry of that confirmed Delete removes the leftover folder itself, prunes git's record and deletes the branch if the user asked, with no second question, and any other Delete of such a folder asks first, as a danger question, whether to delete it and everything in it (`worktreeFolderState` in `integrate.ts`, `deleteChatWorktree`). A worktree folder removed outside Damocles is pruned from git's records and marked deleted, and a resume reads it as deleted. The leftover scan marks a record deleted when its folder is gone, but an adopted one only when the folder's parent exists, so a worktree on a drive that is not mounted stays live, and opening a deleted adopted worktree again revives its record. It reports only the worktrees of open projects, and skips an open project whose folder is gone, where git cannot start. It never deletes or prunes; `git worktree prune` runs only from its Review dialog and from a Delete whose worktree folder is already gone.

## Desktop packaging and updates

- **The packaged layout comes from `app.isPackaged`, never from a path guess** (`src/desktop/main/platform/app-paths.ts`, `app-info.ts`). Packaged, `resourceRoot` is `app.getAppPath()` (`resources/app.asar`), `unpackedRoot` is its `app.asar.unpacked` sibling, the `workerEntry` bundles (`WORKER_BUNDLES`: Compass, usage stats, the sentinel, the process launcher, memory integrity, the session metadata index and voice extraction), the Quick Open worker (`quickOpenWorkerPath`) and the watch worker (`watchWorkerPath`) resolve under `unpackedRoot/dist`, and `AppInfo.version` is `app.getVersion()`. Unpackaged (`electron dist/desktop/main.js` from a checkout), all three roots are the repo root and the version comes from the repo `package.json`, because `app.getVersion()` then reports Electron's version. `package.json` `main` stays the VS Code entry; electron-builder gives the app its own with `extraMetadata.main: dist/desktop/main.js`. electron-updater reads the same version (`app.getVersion()`) and its feed from `process.resourcesPath`, the directory that holds `app.asar`.
- **`asarUnpack` has one source: `ASAR_UNPACK` in `src/desktop/main/platform/unpacked-assets.ts`.** `electron-builder.yml` `asarUnpack` must equal it, and `app-paths.test.ts` fails when they drift. A file belongs there when Damocles hands its path to a worker, a child process or another runtime, or when anything other than Node's module loader, an Electron API that takes a path, an external package's own `fs` or `src/core/resource-fs.ts` reads it, since bundled code reads through `original-fs` (next bullet): the worker scripts (worker threads, the process launcher's among them, and the sentinel, which runs as Node in a separate process), koffi's loader (`node_modules/koffi`, which the unpacked launcher worker requires), the tree-sitter grammars and `web-tree-sitter` (read from the unpacked compass worker), the Python sidecar package (Python cannot import from an archive), ripgrep (`spawn` cannot execute a file inside an asar), the formatter host and the pty host (`dist/formatter-host.js`, `dist/pty-host.js`, which main forks from `unpackedRoot`, though a utility process can load its entry from inside `app.asar`: `dist/core-host.js`, Damocles Core's, is forked from there and is the one utility process entry not in `ASAR_UNPACK`, so it stays under asar integrity and its bare imports resolve `app.asar/node_modules`; `verify-desktop-package.mjs` `checkCoreHost` enforces it), the shell-integration scripts (`resources/shell-integration/**`, which a shell reads), the askpass helper and scripts (`dist/askpass-main.js` and `dist/askpass/**`, which git and ssh execute and which run the helper as Node; the desktop build copies the scripts from `resources/askpass` with mode 0755 and the package check requires exactly that mode), node-pty's target prebuild (`node_modules/node-pty/prebuilds/**`; node-pty's JS stays in `app.asar`, because node-pty rewrites its `spawn-helper` path from `app.asar` to `app.asar.unpacked`) and the native Node-API prebuilds of koffi and `@parcel/watcher`. The Windows package holds no `@parcel/watcher` prebuild, so its glob (`WATCHER_UNPACK`) matches nothing there, and the package check takes each platform's globs from `asarUnpackFor`. A new consumer that hands a bundled file to a worker, a child process or another runtime adds its path to `ASAR_UNPACK`; it never relies on reading that file through the archive. The one exception is node-pty's conout worker on Windows (`lib/worker/conoutSocketWorker.js`), which node-pty starts from `app.asar` because its path rewrite handles only `node_modules.asar`; Electron's worker threads read the archive, and the packaged e2e run proves a shell starts. `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs` checks the unpacked tree of every CI build.
- **Every Node bundle reads files through `original-fs` under Electron; Damocles' own packed files are read only through `src/core/resource-fs.ts`** (`scripts/esbuild-original-fs.mjs`, `esbuild.config.mjs` `nodePlugins`). In every Electron process (main, utility processes, worker threads, `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` children) Electron's `fs` treats any path ending in `.asar` as a folder: the first `lstat`, `stat`, `readdir`, `readFile`, `realpath` or `access` that names one opens the file and caches it, handle included, until the process exits (`lib/node/asar-fs-wrapper.ts` `cachedArchives`; electron/electron#1642 made it deliberate, #6251 and #38236 report the locking, microsoft/vscode#646 is the same bug in VS Code). A user's archive, such as an Electron project's `node_modules/electron/dist/resources/default_app.asar`, then could not be deleted on Windows while Damocles ran, and Node's recursive `rm`, which takes `lstat`, `rmdir` and `unlink` from `require('fs')`, called `rmdir` on it (EBUSY on Windows; on POSIX ENOTDIR, which rimraf treats as done, so the archive stayed). `original-fs` is Electron's copy of `fs` without archive handling. The plugin resolves every `fs`, `node:fs`, `fs/promises` and `node:fs/promises` import or require in all 15 Node bundles (the extension, every worker, the sentinel, main, Damocles Core, the formatter and pty hosts, Quick Open, the watch worker and the askpass helper; the sandboxed preloads have no `fs`) to a shim that picks `original-fs` when `process.versions.electron` is set and `node:fs` otherwise, a runtime choice because one `dist/extension.js` runs under VS Code desktop (Electron) and VS Code Remote (plain Node).
  - `original-fs` cannot see inside an archive, so a file packed in `app.asar` is readable only by Node's module loader, an Electron API that takes a path (`nativeImage.createFromPath`), an external package's own `fs` (below), and `resource-fs.ts`, which the plugin recognizes by file identity and skips, and which re-exports Electron's archive-aware `readFileSync`, `readFile`, `realpath` and `stat`. Its importers are the `app://` protocol (`protocol.ts` `serveFile`), What's new (`release-notes.ts`), the server package manifest (`remote/server-manifest.ts`), the string tables (`localization-service.ts` `readStringTable`), the contributed `package.json` (`read-contributed-configuration.ts`) and pi's `package.json`, whose version keys the session index (`meta-index/worker.ts`); `esbuild-original-fs.test.ts` pins that list. Never pass it a user's path. A packed path need not come from `__dirname`: `piPackageDir` is built from `resourceRoot`. A dev run reads real files from the checkout, so a packed read that bypasses `resource-fs.ts` fails only when packaged; `packaged-assets.spec.ts` checks What's new, every packaged launch reads the protocol's files, the string tables and both `package.json` files, and no packaged launch reads the server manifest yet.
  - Never import `original-fs` (the build fails: plain Node has no such module), and never fetch `fs` with `getBuiltinModule` or a `createRequire(...)` call (lint refuses both in every Node tree and its tests, `FS_BUILTIN_BAN`), which bypasses the plugin. The shim calls `require`, so the plugin refuses a bundle whose format is not `cjs`. A bundled package that imports `fs`, or does its file work through `graceful-fs`, fails the build until it is in `REVIEWED_PACKAGES`, after a check that it reads no file inside `app.asar`; a package that does becomes an external. unpdf's bundled pdf.js fetches `fs` through `getBuiltinModule`, which the gate cannot see; it is unreached, because `extract.ts` passes bytes, not a URL, and the bundle leaves `standardFontDataUrl` unset.
  - In Electron 44, `original-fs`'s recursive `readdir` still descends into an archive (ENOTDIR) and opens it, in every form except `fs.promises.readdir` with `withFileTypes`: Node implements the others with `binding.internalModuleStat`, which Electron patches on the binding the two modules share. Walk a user's folder with that form or with non-recursive `withFileTypes` reads.
  - Externals (`scripts/desktop-externals.mjs`, `scripts/extension-externals.mjs`) load through the module loader and keep the patched `fs`, which pi needs to read its own `package.json` from `app.asar`. So an agent `read`, `Edit` or `write` whose target is itself an `.asar` still opens it in Damocles Core ("Tool overrides").
  - Under Electron each process holds two `fs` instances, so an `instanceof` check on `Stats`, `Dirent`, `ReadStream` or `FileHandle` fails across the bundle and external boundary. Vitest imports source under plain Node, where `esbuild-original-fs.test.ts` runs the shim only against a stub `require`; under Electron only the desktop e2e suites run it, and the `.asar` tests in `agent-tools.spec.ts`, `watchers.spec.ts`, `files.spec.ts` and `worktree-chat-flows.spec.ts` fail if the redirect breaks.
- **Fuses are set by electron-builder and read back from every built binary** (`electron-builder.yml` `electronFuses`, `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs`, which reads the fuse wire with `@electron/fuses` `getCurrentFuseWire` on each CI leg). `runAsNode` is enabled; `enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable`, `enableNodeCliInspectArguments` and `grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` are disabled; `enableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation`, `onlyLoadAppFromAsar` and `enableCookieEncryption` are enabled. Never weaken a fuse to make a test pass and never add an app-side environment hook instead: with `--inspect` disabled, Playwright's `_electron.launch` cannot attach to a packaged app, so tests against the packaged app drive its renderers through `--remote-debugging-port`, which reaches renderers only.
  - **`runAsNode` stays enabled because the POSIX shell sentinel needs it.** `startShellSentinel`, which Damocles Core's launcher worker runs, spawns `process.execPath` with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`: the sentinel must outlive a crash of Damocles Core, and only main can fork a `utilityProcess`. Two more consumers run the binary as Node: the login-environment probe (`shell-env.ts`), and the askpass helper, which git and ssh start through `dist/askpass/*.sh` with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` on the scripts' exec line, outside any process main owns. Residual risk, accepted: a process already running as the user can use the Damocles binary as a Node interpreter and inherit the app's OS permissions, including its macOS privacy (TCC) grants. VS Code keeps the fuse enabled for the same reason.
  - **With `enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` off, the packaged app ignores an inherited `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`.** Electron strips it together with `NODE_OPTIONS` at startup, so `installCaCertificates` trusts the bundled roots and the OS store only. On macOS and Linux the file is still added when the login shell environment merge brings the variable back (it is set in the user's shell profile); on Windows, where there is no merge, it is never read. Corporate CAs belong in the OS certificate store. The VS Code extension and the unpackaged dev app are unaffected. Do not turn the fuse on to restore the variable: it would reopen `NODE_OPTIONS` injection.
  - **Files under `app.asar.unpacked` are outside the asar integrity check.** Electron validates asar integrity on macOS and Windows only, so on Linux `app.asar` itself is unchecked too and the root-owned `/opt/Damocles` is the whole protection. Where it runs, integrity validation covers `app.asar` only, so the unpacked worker scripts (which run inside the app), `sentinel.js`, the formatter and pty hosts (`dist/formatter-host.js`, `dist/pty-host.js`), the Quick Open and watch workers, the shell-integration scripts (`resources/shell-integration/**`, which every integrated shell sources), the askpass helper and scripts (`dist/askpass-main.js`, `dist/askpass/**`, which every credential prompt runs), the Python package, the grammars and the native modules are protected only by the install directory's file permissions: root-owned under `/opt/Damocles` on Linux, the user's own `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs` on a per-user Windows install, and `/Applications` on macOS.
- **Linux never runs without the Chromium sandbox.** Nothing launches the Damocles binary or its Chromium processes with `--no-sandbox` (not the app, its scripts, CI or the docs), and there is no AppImage, whose electron-builder workaround is exactly that flag. Ubuntu 24.04 and later restrict unprivileged user namespaces, so the deb and rpm `afterInstall` script (`build/linux/after-install.sh`) installs `/etc/apparmor.d/damocles`, an unconfined profile that grants `userns` to `/opt/Damocles/damocles` only, and reloads that one profile. It installs nothing when AppArmor is absent or older than 4.0, and it never fails the install on an AppArmor error. `after-remove.sh` removes the profile only on a real removal, never on an upgrade. The script makes `chrome-sandbox` setuid instead on kernels without unprivileged user namespaces, and when AppArmor restricts them (`kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`) but the profile did not load, so Chromium falls back to its setuid sandbox rather than failing to start. Residual, accepted: AppArmor keeps an unconfined profile's label across exec of a binary with no profile of its own, so every descendant (agent shell commands, `git`, MCP servers, the voice sidecar) may create user namespaces too. That is the posture Ubuntu ships for Chrome and VS Code. `build/linux/**` is LF through `.gitattributes`, because a CRLF shebang or profile fails on Linux.
- **The update model differs per OS, and the feed is only the packaged `resources/app-update.yml`** (`src/desktop/main/updater.ts`). The updater starts after the window is up, returns before it loads electron-updater, and is off in the unpackaged app. It never calls `setFeedURL`, never sets `forceDevUpdateConfig` or `updateConfigPath`, and reads no environment variable or argument; `updater.test.ts` guards all of these. `allowPrerelease` is off, so every platform reads the latest stable release.
  - Windows and Linux download in the background, then offer **Restart Now**; a dismissed update installs on quit (`autoInstallOnAppQuit`). Linux deb and rpm install through electron-updater's own `pkexec` path; the app adds no elevation of its own. Restart Now (the notice, the pill and About) runs the app's quit first, with its terminal and unsaved-editor questions, and calls electron-updater's `quitAndInstall` only after the quit tore everything down, as VS Code's `quitAndInstall` does; while that quit runs the state is `restarting`, which the pill, its menu and About show with Restart disabled, and main refuses a second restart; a cancelled quit returns the state to `ready`, and a later restart works.
  - Windows sets `autoUpdater.channel` to `latest-<arch>` because both architectures release from one tag and would otherwise both write `latest.yml`; electron-builder's combined multi-arch NSIS installer is not an option (non-deterministic `files` order since 26.0.13, issue 9745). `allowDowngrade = false` is set after the channel, because electron-updater's channel setter turns downgrades on. Feeds: `latest-x64.yml`, `latest-arm64.yml`, `latest-mac.yml`, `latest-linux.yml`, `latest-linux-arm64.yml`.
  - macOS cannot apply an update without a Developer ID signature (Squirrel.Mac checks the signature against the installed app), so `autoDownload` is off and an available update raises a notice whose one action opens `https://github.com/AizenvoltPrime/damocles/releases/tag/v<version>` through `ShellService.openExternal`. The version must match a plain release version pattern first, so a feed cannot turn the notice into another URL. Adding a Developer ID later is configuration only; see `docs/desktop-signing.md`.
  - `UpdateService` publishes one state (`disabled` in the unpackaged app, `idle`, `checking`, `upToDate`, `available` on macOS, `downloading`, `ready`, `restarting` while the quit that installs a ready update runs, `error`) to the title-bar pill and Settings › About. It checks after the window is up, every 4 hours, on a resume from sleep once the last check (`damocles.desktop.update.lastCheckedAt`, global state) is that old, and on demand from About and Help › Check for Updates…; recording `lastCheckedAt` happens in the background, so a failed write is logged and never stops the check or its download; a check while one or a download runs does nothing. Each state that needs the user posts one `NotificationService` notice. Help › About Damocles, Release Notes and Check for Updates… sit in the app menu on macOS, which has no `role: 'about'`.
  - What's new reads `dist/desktop/CHANGELOG.md`, which the desktop build copies and `verify-desktop-package.mjs` checks inside `app.asar` against the repo's file and the packaged version's section (outside a tag run, `[Unreleased]` while the changelog has it). `release-notes.ts` indexes the `## [x.y.z] - date` headings, skipping any other, and sends one version's markdown per request; a failed read is logged with its path and not cached, so the next request reads again, and the overlay shows a fixed localized reason, never main's error text; the overlay sanitizes it with DOMPurify and opens links through `openExternal`. The first launch of a higher version than `damocles.desktop.lastSeenVersion` posts one "Damocles {0} is installed" notice; a first install, the same version and a downgrade post none.
  - electron-updater both emits `error` and rejects with the same error. The updater handles both and logs each error once through the log sink, so an update failure can neither crash the main process nor raise an unhandled rejection. A failed check (offline, a captive portal, a firewall blocking GitHub) is only logged, because it would otherwise warn on every such launch; once an update was found, the first failure to download, install or offer it shows one warning per run.
  - Update tests point an installed test copy at a loopback feed by rewriting its `resources/app-update.yml` (`scripts/desktop-update-override.mjs`, driven by `e2e/desktop/update/drive-update.mjs`). That file sits outside `app.asar`, so the app needs no test hook; the writer accepts only an `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` generic feed and only over the packaged GitHub config.

## Tests

- Every test file runs under its own temp home: `src/__mocks__/hermetic-home.setup.ts` points `USERPROFILE` and `HOME` at a fresh dir before any module reads `os.homedir()`, so nothing a test writes through `DAMOCLES_HOME_DIR` reaches the developer's real `~/.damocles`. That setup file must never import `paths.ts`, which freezes the home at import. It also points `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP` at a fresh dir, so `os.tmpdir()` sits under the same per-run root, which `test-home.global-setup.ts` removes after the fork workers exit. A test still removes what it creates: the run root only catches what a failed test leaves behind. Each root holds an `owner.pid` with the Vitest process's PID, and the next run's setup removes every root whose owner has exited, which is what an interrupted run (Ctrl+C, a killed terminal) leaves. A root without the file is never removed, because a concurrent run may have just created it.
- Those homes live under one per-run root that `src/__mocks__/test-home.global-setup.ts` removes in its teardown. Cleanup belongs there and not in a per-file `afterAll`: vitest skips a file's hooks when every test in it is skipped or filtered out by `-t`, and the teardown runs after the fork workers exit, when Windows has released their file handles.
- The redirect needs vitest's `forks` pool, pinned in `vitest.config.ts`. In a worker thread, `process.env` writes do not reach `os.homedir()`.
- **The OS clipboard is locked across e2e workers** (`e2e/desktop/support/clipboard.ts`). A test that touches the clipboard takes the `clipboard` fixture first, so the lock spans every app it launches, and reads and writes only through it. `scripts/__tests__/e2e-clipboard.test.ts` fails on any other clipboard access, OS clipboard tool, or copy and paste key, click or menu pick outside the fixture.
- **The OS foreground is locked across e2e workers** (`e2e/desktop/support/foreground.ts`). A test that asserts native focus, relies on hover or presses keys through `pressKeys` takes the `foreground` fixture first: it waits until no other worker has an app open, and no worker opens one until it ends. A worker records each app it opens as a file in `damocles-e2e-open-apps` and refreshes it every 2 s; a record counts only while its worker is alive and refreshed it within 10 s, because a killed run leaves its records behind and Windows reuses their pids. A key needs it because Chromium runs an application-menu accelerator only while its window is active (`View::CanHandleAccelerators`): `pressKeys` focuses the page, which activates the window even in the background, but an app another worker opens takes the OS foreground, and a key still on its way through the renderer is then dropped with no error. While a worker holds the foreground, each app it launches parks the OS cursor beside the main window, inside its display's work area and outside every window of the app (`e2e/desktop/support/pointer.ts`: `SetPhysicalCursorPos` on Windows, `CGWarpMouseCursorPosition` on macOS, `XWarpPointer` on X11), and the fixture puts it back before it releases the foreground. A resting user cursor over the window would otherwise end a hover the test made with Playwright's mouse, because Chromium replays the OS cursor's position into the page when it repaints under it (an element screenshot does). A window that covers its whole work area leaves no spot and fails the launch. The `clipboard` fixture holds the foreground too, since a paste needs keyboard focus, so a test takes one or the other, never both; `scripts/__tests__/e2e-foreground.test.ts` fails a test that reads native focus, relies on hover or calls `pressKeys` (or a support helper that does) with neither, and one that takes both. Both locks serve their waiters in arrival order (`fair-lock.ts`): a waiter first takes a ticket in the OS temp folder, and only the oldest ticket whose worker is alive and refreshed it within 10 s tries the `proper-lockfile` lock, so a worker that releases a lock and asks again queues behind every worker already waiting. A plain lock retry lets the releasing worker win every time, so one running foreground tests back to back starved another worker's launch past its test timeout. A launch's wait for another worker's hold runs outside its test's time (`outsideTestTime` in `fixtures.ts`), as the two fixtures' waits run in their own fixture timeout; `scripts/__tests__/e2e-foreground-queue.test.ts` runs the queue in real worker processes.
- **Nothing on a Playwright worker's event loop starts a synchronous child process** (`e2e/desktop/` and `e2e/remote/`: specs, support and screenshots). A lock holder refreshes its lock and a waiter its ticket every 2 s on the worker's event loop, and another worker takes over a lock or drops a ticket not refreshed for 10 s, so a `spawnSync`, `execFileSync` or `execSync` during a hold or a wait hands the lock to a second worker or loses the place in line. Specs run git through `support/git.ts`. `killTree` reads the process table and runs `taskkill` through the async `run` helper in `app.ts`, which bounds each child at 15 s; when the table read fails or times out, `killTree` still kills the root's tree and then rejects with the read's error, rather than shrinking the tree to its root. `scripts/__tests__/e2e-async-children.test.ts` fails on any synchronous call, by name or string key, except in the files that run in a process of their own (global setup, the update driver, the forked second host, the app's fixtures, the module Electron's main process requires first, the MCP server, the reference server package build and ssh's ProxyCommand connector).
- **Release CI fails on a flaky test** (`playwright.desktop.config.ts`). With `CI` set, `retries: 1` only sorts a failure into flaky or broken and keeps the first attempt's trace, and `failOnFlakyTests` fails the run either way, in `e2e-desktop` and in `package-desktop`'s packaged-app suite. `globalTimeout` (45 minutes in CI, global setup included) ends a hung run while Playwright can still write its report. Each suite step's `timeout-minutes` outlasts it with the step's own build, and each job's `timeout-minutes` outlasts install, that step and what follows, because a job timeout cancels the job and skips the report upload while a step timeout only fails its step (`docs/release.md` "Time limits"). Every `e2e-report-*` upload in `release.yml` runs `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, so a green shard keeps its report too; `release-targets.test.ts` checks the flags, the margins and the uploads.
- **A test that must act before a chat's page is ready holds it** (`e2e/desktop/support/hold-chat-pages.cjs`, `LaunchOptions.require`, dev launches only). The module, required ahead of the main script, holds every `app://damocles/panel/` load until `__e2eChatPages.release()` and counts each view's focus gains. Keys meant for main's input observers go through `pressKeys`, under the `foreground` fixture, because Playwright's CDP key presses never reach `before-input-event`.
- **A desktop launch must quit with exit code 0** (`e2e/desktop/support/app.ts`). `DesktopApp.close()` waits for the main process to exit and rejects, once, when it died of a signal or exited with another code, as when a native module aborts in Node's environment teardown (`FATAL ERROR` in the main output); the fixture closes every launch, so a crash on the way out fails its test. A process killed by a signal keeps `exitCode` null, so an exit check reads `signalCode` too.
- **Desktop end-to-end launches stay off the developer's keyring** (`e2e/desktop/support/hermetic.ts`). Each launch gets a temp home and an allowlisted environment with `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS=disabled:`, because libsecret otherwise finds the session bus through `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/bus` and safeStorage reads and writes the real Linux keyring. `DAMOCLES_E2E_OS_KEYRING=1` opts in, and only then does `secrets.spec.ts` "encrypted at rest" run on Linux. On macOS every launch runs with `--use-mock-keychain`: Playwright's Electron loader appends it (with `--password-store=basic`) to each dev launch, and `support/packaged-app.ts` and `update/drive-update.mjs` pass the same two switches, because the real Keychain's modal prompt blocks the main thread on a runner and would add the app's Safe Storage item on a developer's Mac. Windows DPAPI stores nothing outside userData. Every launch's git sends GitHub URLs to a missing folder, so nothing is cloned, and `seedSubscriptionPlugin` installs a local stub of the pinned subscription plugin. A teardown that cannot delete the temporary home names the leftovers and, on Windows, the processes Restart Manager reports holding them (`support/file-holders.ts`).
- **Review screenshots are their own Playwright project, never part of the suite** (`e2e/desktop/screenshots/*.screenshots.ts`, `support/screenshots.ts`). `dev`, and so `npm run test:desktop` and the WSL script, matches only `*.spec.ts`; after the build `test:desktop` runs, the captures run with `npx playwright test --config playwright.desktop.config.ts --project=screenshots`. Each PNG goes to `DAMOCLES_SCREENSHOT_DIR` when it is set (Playwright never wipes it), else to the test's folder under `dist/e2e-results/screenshots`, and is attached to the HTML report. A capture waits for what it shows (the theme on the page's body, the emulated viewport, the element) and then for `settled` (fonts, two frames, every finite animation except the toast life bar, a countdown), never for a fixed time. The `proto-*` captures render the design reference from the untracked `Damocles desktop UI revamp/export/damocles-revamp` folder, which loads its scripts from the network; they are taken again on every run, and without the folder they are skipped with a `reference` annotation. A whole-window capture goes through `saveWindowScreenshot`, and a capture showing a browser page waits for `pageFramesShown`. `slice6b` alone compares pixels (`toHaveScreenshot`) against a baseline recorded on the same machine with `--update-snapshots` before a change; its `*-snapshots/` folder is gitignored, so a run without one fails and writes the baseline there, never into a commit.
- **The desktop suite runs on Linux from Windows through WSL** (`node scripts/test-desktop-wsl.mjs --distro Ubuntu-24.04 [specs...]`), as the release workflow's Linux leg does: the script syncs the working tree into `~/damocles-e2e` in the distro, runs `npm ci` there only when `package-lock.json` changed, builds, and runs the `dev` project under Xvfb and openbox. The distro needs Node 24 through nvm, xvfb-run, openbox, xauth and rsync, and the script refuses the Windows npm the distro's PATH carries. Its rsync file list lives in the OS temp folder and is removed on exit, Ctrl+C included. Every step runs under the distro's `timeout --kill-after`, which signals the step's whole process group, because killing `wsl.exe` leaves Xvfb, Electron and Playwright running in the distro.
- **The remote suite reaches the fixtures only through the temp ssh config** (`e2e/remote/support/sshd.ts`): `DAMOCLES_E2E_SSH_CONFIG` names a config under the test's hermetic home whose first block is `Host *` with `IdentityFile`, `IdentitiesOnly yes`, `IdentityAgent none`, `UserKnownHostsFile` and `GlobalKnownHostsFile` in that home, so every destination, an address typed as user@host included, reads and writes nothing under the real `~/.ssh`. One case in `ssh-install.spec.ts` signs in through an agent alone (`ssh-agent-stub.ts`): its host block is left out of that `Host *` block (`Host * !prod-1`) and names no key file (`IdentityFile none`, because Win32-OpenSSH reads its default keys from the real profile whatever `USERPROFILE` says). Known hosts are seeded only from the container's own generated key. On Windows the launch environment keeps `ProgramData`, without which Win32-OpenSSH's ssh exits 255 with no output.
- **Servers a fixture container reaches are named host.docker.internal, never a loopback address** (`HOST_FROM_CONTAINER`, `containerReachableAddress`): a remote core marks a loopback provider endpoint unreachable, and on Linux the container's loopback is not this computer's. Docker Desktop forwards the name to 127.0.0.1; on Linux the containers get `--add-host host.docker.internal:host-gateway` and the release mirror and model stub listen on the default bridge's gateway.
- **A fixture goes offline by killing its sshd, never by stopping the container** (`stopSshd`), so the daemon and its terminals survive the outage and a Reconnect proves a reattach.
- **Every remote spec that starts a daemon ends it.** A Docker fixture spec ends with `shutdownHostDaemon`: control `shutdownNow`, then the target account has no process at all (daemon, bridge, core, pty and formatter hosts, shells, the ssh session). An attempt that fails before Starting waits for the same empty process list. A target suite test (`target.spec.ts`) ends with `shutdownTarget`: control `shutdownNow`, then the daemon's process tree is gone; its auto fixture, torn down after the test's app closed, kills the tree of a daemon whose pid file a failed test left (`killDaemonIn`).
- **Target suites run only when named, one test at a time** (`scripts/test-remote.mjs`, `playwright.remote.config.ts`): `@windows-target` and `@darwin-target` stay out of every run whose `--grep` does not name them (`DAMOCLES_E2E_REMOTE_TARGETS` drives the config's `grepInvert`), because they need the runner's own sshd and the key the nightly workflow authorizes. A run that names one has a single worker, because every target test connects as the runner's account, which has one daemon.
- **A remote run removes only its own fixtures and those of runs that ended** (`scripts/test-remote.mjs`, `sshd.ts` `fixtureLabels`): every fixture container and Docker network carries `damocles-e2e-sshd` and `damocles-e2e-run=<pid>`, the pid of the `test:remote` process (under a bare `playwright test`, the Playwright runner's). Before a run, `test:remote` removes the labelled fixtures whose pid is no live process; after it, those with its own pid. A second run on the same machine therefore never removes the first one's live containers.
- **The stage scripts run in real shells in the unit tests** (`src/desktop/main/remote/bootstrap/__tests__/shells.ts`): a POSIX sh (Git for Windows' on Windows) and PowerShell (Windows PowerShell 5.1 on Windows, pwsh elsewhere when installed) run the stage scripts' definitions, the part above `# BEGIN MAIN`, and the tests call the functions. Scripts go through files, a PowerShell one with a BOM, and binary stdin through the launcher (`runWithBytes`), since `runProcess` writes a string input as UTF-8. Tests that need Docker with Linux containers, or a shell a platform lacks, are skipped by `skipIf` with the requirement in their name; `ssh-transport.integration.test.ts` runs the whole transport against an Alpine sshd with this computer's ssh and the built askpass helper.

## Packaging

- **The VSIX node_modules allowlist is generated, never hand-edited** (`scripts/sync-vscodeignore.mjs`, run through `npm run sync:vscodeignore`). `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs the script with `--check` on `ubuntu-latest` while developers generate the block on their own machines, so every pattern it emits has to be identical on every platform. A native binary package therefore collapses to a family glob (`@vscode/ripgrep-win32-x64` becomes `@vscode/ripgrep-*`), and a name that is nothing but platform tokens collapses to its scope (`@esbuild/win32-x64` becomes `@esbuild/*`). A literal platform name in the block fails `--check` on the release runner and aborts the tag push before the package matrix runs. `scripts/__tests__/sync-vscodeignore.test.ts` pins both halves.
- **node-pty ships only the target's prebuild** (`electron-builder.yml`, `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs`). `node-pty` is pinned exactly at `1.2.0-beta.15`, the version VS Code pins, because stable 1.1.0 lacks Linux prebuilds and its macOS `spawn-helper` lacks the execute bit. The package keeps only `prebuilds/<platform>-<arch>/`, with no `*.pdb`, `build`, `deps`, `src`, `third_party` or `scripts`. The verifier asserts exactly one prebuild directory, matching the target, its native binaries unpacked, node-pty's JS inside `app.asar`, and on macOS `spawn-helper` at 0755 with `codesign -v` passing on it and on `pty.node`. The publish job's `npm ci --ignore-scripts` builds nothing, and node-pty's install script only checks that the prebuild exists.
- **Main confirms and confines every terminal link** (`src/desktop/main/terminal/terminal-links.ts`, `src/desktop/shell/terminal/terminal-links.ts`). The shell sends only candidate path text that the link parser (`terminal-link-parsing.ts`) finds, within VS Code's limits, and never reads the file system. Main refuses UNC, device, drive-relative, root-relative and `~` paths before any file system call, and in a WSL terminal any POSIX path outside `/mnt/<drive>/`. It resolves the rest against the terminal's base folder, requires the result inside the terminal's project (a home folder terminal resolves no link), and confirms it with `confineExisting` and `stat`. Opening a link resolves it again. A file goes through `openFromUser`. A folder is revealed in Files only while Files shows its project; otherwise Quick Open opens limited to that folder of that project (`folderLinkAction`, `QuickOpenScope`) and leaves the selected chat and project as they are. Neither path takes an absolute path from a renderer.
- **Main parses shell integration, and the nonce decides trust** (`src/desktop/main/terminal/shell-integration.ts`). Main strips every OSC 633 sequence from a terminal's output before the renderer sees it, trusted or not, and the renderer registers no 633 handler. A sequence is trusted only when its field count is exact for its type and its last field equals the terminal's nonce (128 bits, new on every spawn and restart, passed only in the environment, unset by each script). Main alone owns a terminal's cwd, running command, title and command facts; link resolution, kill confirmation and every menu and dialog read only those, and the renderer gets validated events in-band with the output. Main acks stripped characters to the pty host itself. The pty host's screen only counts OSC 633 sequences (`pty-host/screen.ts`), recording the buffer line of each by its index since the spawn, and never reads a nonce; main's `Osc633Stream` and the screen share that one index, so the stream reads output as xterm's parser does (its transition table and `OscParser`): it counts every sequence xterm hands that handler (one ended by BEL, C1 ST or ESC, malformed or field-less included; one cancelled by CAN, SUB or another C1 control, one whose ident is another number after leading zeros, and one whose payload passes xterm's 10,000,000-character limit not), ignores the C0 controls xterm ignores inside an OSC, and lets a C0 control after a lone ESC through while the ESC still waits. `shell-integration.test.ts` checks the counts and the rows the forwarded text draws against a headless xterm. A restored screen's marks are main's trusted events at those indexes, and a screen carries the count it parsed (`screen.sequences`): one that holds output main never parsed (a reattach, a daemon's screen after this client lagged) restarts main's stream at that count, in ground state. The nonce stops program output, not code running as the user, which can read it as in VS Code.
- **Shell-integration scripts load only from the app's `resources/shell-integration`** (`shell-integration-injection.ts`, unpacked when packaged). A script path travels as its own argv entry or in the environment, never inside shell source, and every escaper hex-escapes all control bytes. zsh's startup files are copied to `<userData>/shell-integration/zsh`. wsl.exe and cmd.exe get no injection, as in VS Code. The bash, zsh and fish scripts are LF (`.gitattributes`), and the package verifier checks them.
- **Every terminal kill goes through one confirmation** (`terminal/kill-confirmation.ts`). Kill, Kill All, a row's kill, closing the window, removing a project and quitting ask through `TerminalService` per `damocles.desktop.terminal.confirmOnKill`, in one D41 dialog that is the only wait. Command marks come only from main's events: a command without a reported command line gets no mark, and Rerun and Copy Command never read screen rows.
- **Terminal output reaches a prompt only as a framed attachment block** (`src/core/terminal-attachment.ts`). Each attachment is its own block before the IDE block and the typed text, says it is data, and is capped by `capTerminalText` in the renderer and again in main. A Graph commit is the other kind of pending attachment (`ChatAttachmentInput`, by `kind`, `src/shared/types/chat-attachment.ts`) and takes the same path: the shell names only `{ repoId, commit }`, main checks it is a commit of the repository, and core reads it through the gated repository (`core/scm/commit-attachment.ts`): its header, message, `--stat` and patch against its first parent or the empty tree, the start kept within the terminal attachment's bounds with an "N more lines not shown" note, every control, bidi and invisible character but tab escaped (`visibleLines`; one presentation selector right after a visible non-ASCII character, as in a gitmoji subject, is kept). Every attachment's text has every attachment wrapper and memory tag neutralized. An MCP resource the MCP panel attached is the third kind, and the one both hosts hold (`mcp-resource-attachments.ts`): the attachment is only `{ server, uri, name, mimeType?, serverId }`, where `serverId` is the server the user port routed the name to at attach (`McpUserResourcePort.serverIdOf`), refused at attach for a directory, binary content, an unknown server, a covering deny rule or a full composer (counted after the attach's last await, since `add` past the bound drops the oldest chip), and `sendMessage` reads it through the user port, passing that `serverId` as `expectedServerId`, before the prompt is dispatched (D12). Its text goes in an `<mcp_resource>` block and each image as one of the message's image blocks, within `MAX_IMAGES_PER_MESSAGE` with the typed ones; the block names its images by their place among the message's images, typed ones first (`[Image 2 of this message is from this resource.]`), after its text when it has both, and its text counts with the other attachments against `MAX_PENDING_TERMINAL_ATTACHMENT_CHARS`. A failed read, a read with no content under its own URI, binary content, a cap overrun, a deny rule that now covers it or a server other than the one it was attached from (one replaced or removed since) refuses the whole send: core puts the taken attachments back and posts `promptReturned` (`mcpResourceUnavailable`, with the reason as `detail`), and nothing partial is sent. Like the other kinds it goes only with a direct send; a queued message leaves the chips pending. Explain Changes adds its prompt to the composer after the draft (`composerAppend`) and never sends. A composer's pending attachments hold at most `MAX_PENDING_TERMINAL_ATTACHMENT_CHARS` (128,000) characters together; an Add to Chat past it is refused with a toast and the chips stay. A `damocles-terminal-attachments` sidecar entry records how many blocks lead a user entry, and as `images` how many of its last images are the attachments' own (an attached image resource, which `sendMessage` puts after the typed images and passes as `attachmentImageCount`); the history leaves those out of the reloaded message's images, so a reload shows the chip alone, as the live transcript does. `images` is written only when the entry pi committed holds every image the prompt was given (`PromptEntry.images`): pi drops an image it cannot normalize (`_normalizePromptImages`, `agent-session.js:1603-1620` in pi-coding-agent 1.4.0), and the last images would then include a typed one, so a reload shows them all instead. A malformed `images` drops only that field. `PiSession.recordPromptSidecars` writes it together with `damocles-original-input` when pi commits the entry, for a prompt that opened a run and for one pi queued into a running run (`queuedPromptEntries`: matched by text at delivery, oldest first, withdrawn when a Stop, the budget, a new chat, a resume switch or the session's disposal drops pi's queue), and never for a prompt that committed nothing. Display readers (the history, list metadata and the rewind list) strip exactly that many, so typed text that imitates a block stays text. Memory queries, memory extraction and title generation drop the blocks. Attachments leave the composer only with a prompt pi takes. The send handler claims the send (`beginSend`) before it takes the chips, and puts them back on every exit that did not hand the prompt to pi (`sendMessage` returned `unsent`, a refusal, a throw from the rule filter or from `sendMessage`, which still reaches the router), once (`TerminalAttachmentManager.restore`). A Stop, a new chat, a resume switch or the session's disposal during the resource read aborts the ticket's signal, which the read gets (`readResource(server, uri, { signal })`), and returns the message at once through `interruptRecovery` and `onWithdrawn` (see "A send holds the session from its first step"). One refused or stopped before pi had it gets `interruptRecovery` with the typed text and images (`contentBlocks`), after whatever the composer holds. The restore puts the taken chips back in their order ahead of any added since; past either bound the ones added since go, newest first, and a notice says how many. One the session's disposal, a new chat or a resume switch returns before pi accepted it, while pi's input handlers hold it included, goes back at once through `interruptRecovery` and `onWithdrawn`, and `sendMessage` then returns `withdrawn`, which the handler leaves alone as it does `sent`. One a Stop overtook while pi held it is sent and stopped instead, and stays in the transcript. For one pi queued into a running run it returns `sent`, and calls the handler's `onWithdrawn` when a Stop, the budget, a new chat, a resume switch or the session's disposal (a folder switch, a cross-folder resume) drops pi's queue before pi delivers it (`dropQueue`, `queuedPromptEntries.withdraw`). The same withdrawal posts `queueCancelled` with `returnToInput` under the prompt's correlation id, so the webview takes the echo out and puts its typed text and images back. A prompt pi queues, after its input handlers ran, into a run already stopped or a session already replaced is withdrawn at once from `preflightResult('queued')`. One a Stop withdrew while those handlers held it, which pi then queues into a newer live run, is withdrawn alone (`queuedPromptEntries.withdrawOne`) and taken back out of pi's queue (`takeBackQueued`). pi acts on a slash invocation only at the start of the prompt, so when blocks would lead one, `chat-handlers.ts` resolves it first (`slash-invocation.ts`, a port of pi's extension command check and its skill and template expansion, with a parity test against pi): a template or skill command goes expanded behind the blocks, and an extension command runs alone while the chips stay pending and a notice says so.
- **A terminal paste never hands the clipboard to a renderer, and happens only for a paste gesture main observed** (`src/desktop/main/terminal/terminal-paste.ts`, `clipboard-gestures.ts`). The shell stops xterm's native paste events in the capture phase. Main issues a one-shot grant, bound to the active terminal and to `clipboard` or `selection`, when it observes the paste: a `before-input-event` keyDown of `isTerminalPasteChord` (the shell's key handler uses the same predicate) while the xterm's input has focus, a Linux middle `mouseDown` in `before-mouse-event`, a pick of the terminal menu's Paste, or Edit › Paste while the xterm's input has focus. The pane reports whether focus is the xterm's input; anywhere else Edit › Paste does what the paste roles do. The grant lasts 2 s in main's timer ticks, clears when the window page loses focus, and answers one `damocles:terminal:paste` naming that terminal and source while it is active and focused; anything else is logged and reads nothing. Main reads PRIMARY only for a `selection` grant, applies the multi-line and control-character warning (D41), strips ESC inside a bracketed paste, and writes the text to the pty itself. Edit › Paste and macOS Paste and Match Style are menu items, not roles.
- **The terminal runs in one pty host process, and main issues every terminal** (`src/desktop/pty-host/`, `src/desktop/main/terminal/`). The pty host is an Electron `utilityProcess` (`dist/pty-host.js`) with the login environment minus `ELECTRON_*`, so a native crash or a blocked read cannot take down main; no `MessagePort` reaches a renderer. A terminal's environment (`terminal-env.ts`) is that login environment minus every variable the integration scripts read or export (`SCRIPT_STATE_ENV` in `shell-integration-injection.ts`) and minus a `ZDOTDIR` naming the app's own zsh folder, so a Damocles started from its own terminal starts clean shells; any other `DAMOCLES_` variable is the user's and passes. A missing `LANG`, or one not ending in `.UTF-8`, `.utf8` or `.euc…`, becomes `<locale>.UTF-8` from `app.getLocale()`, as VS Code's `terminal.integrated.detectLocale` `auto` does, because a macOS app opened from the Dock or Finder inherits none. Main drops any variable the host's parser refuses (`isHostEnvEntry`) or past `MAX_HOST_ENV_ENTRIES` before it posts a spawn, and logs its name, never its value. On Windows only spawns and kills wait for the conpty throttle, in order on their own chain, so a kill sent while its spawn waits finds the pty; input, resize, ack and clearAck run at once, or in order behind their own terminal's waiting spawn. A window close sends one `killAll` and a quit one `shutdown`, both unthrottled, each cancelling every spawn still waiting. node-pty runs a Windows pty's kill only on its first output, so `shutdown` ends the host only once every pty it started has printed or exited (`connecting` in `sessions.ts`), within main's 2 s bound; a host that exits sooner never closes that pseudoconsole, and its shell can outlive the app. A Windows shell that exits with CreateProcess error 193, 267 or 1260 before any output shows a localized line saying why, and a restart starts in the project or home folder once its launch folder is gone; input typed while a restart checks that folder is held in main and sent after the spawn. Main issues ids `term-<n>` and accepts a create only as a detected profile id plus a target (`TerminalTarget`): an open project, the home folder, or the selected project, which is the home folder while no project is open, so a terminal needs no project (the same folder as the chats' home target, `homeDirectory()`); main resolves the cwd itself. New Terminal's home folder pick is a home target, so a project that opens while the pick shows does not move it. A home folder terminal (`projectKey` null) stays in the home folder when a project opens, as VS Code never moves a running terminal, and survives project changes; New Terminal offers the home folder only while no project is open. Toggle Terminal and New Terminal wait for shell detection to settle, as VS Code waits for `profilesReady` (Toggle Terminal also for the launch restore, so it focuses a restored terminal rather than starting one ahead of it), and the empty pane says no shell was found only once it has (`TerminalState.profilesReady`). Every project change republishes the terminal state (`projectsChanged`). The host batches output every 5 ms or 64 KB and applies VS Code's flow control: pause above 100,000 unacknowledged characters, resume below 5,000, acknowledge every 5,000. A host exit marks every terminal exited with its code and a Restart action, except a daemon's reconnecting terminals, which still run on the target and reattach on the next connection. Every pty's output also feeds a headless xterm in the pty host (`screen.ts`, VS Code's `XtermSerializer`) with the shell's Unicode version, its host's Windows build and the spawn's scrollback, which resizes, a later `damocles.desktop.terminal.scrollback` (`TerminalService.settingsChanged` posts `scrollback`; a daemon's shared screen keeps the largest an attached client asked for, a detach leaves it so the client finds its history on return, and a reattach posts the client's own again) and the menu's Clear follow; the Clear runs after the writes already queued, as the view cleared after the output it had shown. A shell page that loads again asks each live terminal's `serialize`; the host posts its pending batch, then `screen` (SerializeAddon's sequences, holding every data message posted before it and none after), then the output since. The screen holds only the scrollback whose longest serialization (80 characters a cell) fits `MAX_TERMINAL_SCREEN_CHARS`, so no larger string is built, its marks moved to the rows it holds; a serialize that fails rejects inside the screen, never in xterm's write callback, and the host answers it with a blank screen, so main stops waiting and no other terminal ends. Main drops and acknowledges a terminal's output until its last asked screen arrives (it still parses that output, so the shared index holds), sends that screen with its marks (`TerminalScreen`) and the state with `restoring` false; the view stays hidden until the screen is written into its fresh xterm at the screen's size, the marks restored on the normal buffer before any alternate screen covers it, then fits its box, so it never shows empty or replays output. A view that mounted before main asked for its screen hides once `restoring` turns true, and one whose screen still waits in the store stays hidden until it is written. An exited terminal's screen is not kept. Main kills a local project's terminals when the project is removed (a remote project asks first: "Removing a remote project whose host runs terminals of it asks first"), a terminal on close, and every terminal at once on window close and quit. The window layout restores the terminal list at launch without taking focus: main reads the saved list when the window attaches, and `restore()` recreates it ahead of any terminal started before the window page loaded, which stays active. The xterm is fitted only while its screen is visible (`TerminalView.vue`), because xterm 6 resizes a paused renderer at idle time and meanwhile takes the clamped scroll for the user scrolling up, after which it stops following output; the terminal pane glides open from no height. The pty learns a size only once it has held for 100 ms (`pty-resize.ts`, VS Code's resize debouncer), and none while the grid's tracks glide or the sidebar slides (`GridContext.gliding`), since a busy renderer's frames can be further apart than that; so a pane gliding open or the sidebar sliding resizes the shell once. Input and a paste send a waiting size first, so the shell reads the user's keys at the width on screen and readline's redraw of a prompt wrapped at the old width cannot erase the output above it. While an xterm's own input has focus (VS Code's `terminalFocus`, `MenuState.terminalInput`) only the commands with `inTerminal` fire; focus elsewhere in the terminal pane (a list row, the tab, Find, the rename field) is the terminal part for F6 and leaves every app key working (Quick Open, Show All Commands, Toggle Terminal, Toggle Sidebar, Settings, New Chat, F6 and Shift+F6, Split Terminal, Resize Pane on Linux and macOS, and Focus Previous and Next Pane only while the active group has more than one pane, as VS Code's `splitTerminalActive` gates them, so Alt+Left and Alt+Right reach the shell otherwise; `passKeys` follows the same `when` clauses through `Command.keyWhen`); every other key reaches the pty, as VS Code's `commandsToSkipShell` does; its menu item stays enabled, and `keyFires` refuses only the key. Links, OSC 8 hyperlinks included (xterm's `linkHandler`, never its `window.confirm` default), open on Ctrl+click (Cmd+click) through `ShellService.openExternal`, http and https only. The link parser finds the path before a suffix by one scan of the line, never by a regex anchored only at its end. A Linux middle click pastes the selection only while the program tracks no mouse (`modes.mouseTrackingMode === 'none'`) or Shift is held, as xterm decides. Clear marks a pending prompt again after `xterm.clear()` (VS Code's `clearBuffer`), and the command model disposes every marker it replaces or drops. `TerminalState.screenReader` (`app.isAccessibilitySupportEnabled()`, republished on `accessibility-support-changed`) sets xterm's `screenReaderMode`, and `TerminalInfo.windowsBuild` is `windowsPty.buildNumber`: the Windows build of the host whose conpty runs that terminal (`TerminalBackend.windowsBuild`, this computer's `os.release()` or a daemon's `terminals.windowsBuild`), null and no `windowsPty` off Windows, whatever this computer runs, so the view and the pty host's screen wrap lines alike. A host exit is logged with its exit code, the reason Electron's `child-process-gone` gives for an abnormal exit (waited for at most 500 ms), and the tail of the host's stderr, which main pipes for this, or V8's fatal-error location.
- **A terminal tab is a group of side-by-side panes, and main owns the groups** (`terminal/terminal-service.ts`, VS Code's `terminalGroup.ts`). Every terminal is in exactly one group; `TerminalState.groups` lists them in order, each with its panes, active pane and sizes (fractions of at least `MIN_TERMINAL_PANE_FRACTION` summing to 1, at most `MAX_TERMINAL_GROUP_PANES` panes). Split Terminal starts a pane right of its source with the source's profile, looked up again among the listed profiles, in the folder the source's shell last reported while that is still a folder (`cwdOf` plus an async stat), else the source's project or home folder, and the new pane takes focus because the user asked. A split, a kill and an Unsplit leave equal sizes (VS Code's Sizing.Distribute); Unsplit moves a pane to a new group at the end of the list; a kill removes a pane, and with the last pane its group, and the active pane passes to the pane at the same index, else the last. Focus Previous and Next Pane wrap within the active group, Focus Next and Previous Terminal Group walk the groups, and Resize Pane asks the shell, which knows the cell width, for VS Code's four-cell step. Main takes a `resizePanes` only for a known group id with one finite size per pane, each at least the minimum, summing to 1 within `TERMINAL_PANE_SIZES_EPSILON`. The window layout keeps each group's pane count, active pane and sizes; saved groups that do not fit the saved list give each terminal its own group, with a log line, and the restore takes no focus. In the renderer the panes sit in one keyed list (`TerminalPanes.vue`), so a split, unsplit or kill never remounts an xterm, and only the active group's panes count as shown.
